# Spec decisions

This document records how Loop reads the Symphony spec: the commit it implements, its answers where the spec is ambiguous, the behaviors the spec asks implementations to document, and where Loop extends or departs from the spec. It is the single place for these answers. Code and other docs link here instead of restating them.

> Ids such as `m3-29` (milestone 3, issue 29) or `bl-134` (backlog issue 134) name Loop's own issues. Open ones are in [`.loop/issues/`](../.loop/issues/). Finished ones were kept out of this repository when it went public, so their ids remain only as history. Pull request numbers up to #119 (for example "pull request #115") refer to the old private repository, not to this one. "The pilot app" is a private iOS app used to prove Loop on real work.

Section numbers such as "8.4" refer to the vendored spec, [`symphony/SPEC.md`](symphony/SPEC.md). Terms follow the glossary in [`../CONTEXT.md`](../CONTEXT.md): an Attempt is one execution of an agent on one Issue, a Run is one working period of Loop, a Project is one `WORKFLOW.md` and its state, and a Parked Issue is not dispatched again until a human resumes it or its tracker state changes.

Started 2026-11-06 in issue m1-01.

## Answers to the five ambiguities

### 1. Workspace key hash

**Spec references:** 4.2 (workspace key), 9.2 step 1, 9.5 invariant 3, 17.2.

**What is ambiguous:** the spec requires "a stable hash suffix of the original identifier with at least 64 bits of entropy" when sanitizing changes the identifier, but names no algorithm, length, encoding or separator.

**Decision:**

- Sanitize `issue.identifier` by replacing every character outside `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` with `_`.
- If sanitizing changed nothing, the key is the identifier itself, with no suffix (17.2: identifiers unchanged by sanitization keep their deterministic key).
- Otherwise the key is `<sanitized>-<suffix>`, where `<suffix>` is the first 16 lowercase hex characters (64 bits) of SHA-256 over the UTF-8 bytes of the original identifier.
- Accepted collision: an unchanged identifier that literally has the form `<sanitized>-<16 hex>` could equal the key of a sanitized one. Loop accepts this case and records it here instead of hashing every identifier, because hashing every key would break the 17.2 rule above, and such an identifier is vanishingly unlikely in practice.

Example: `ABC 12` becomes `ABC_12-` followed by the first 16 hex characters of `sha256("ABC 12")`; `ABC-12` stays `ABC-12`.

**Reason:** SHA-256 is in the JDK, stable across platforms and versions, and 16 hex characters give exactly the 64 bits the spec asks for while keeping directory names short. Hex and `-` are allowed key characters, so the key needs no second sanitizing pass.

### 2. `after_run` after a failed `before_run`

**Spec references:** 5.3.4 (`after_run`), 9.4 (failure semantics), 16.5 (reference worker algorithm), 17.2.

**What is ambiguous:** 5.3.4 says `after_run` "runs after each agent attempt (success, failure, timeout, or cancellation) once the workspace exists", and 17.2 says it "runs after each attempt". The reference algorithm in 16.5 returns on a failed `before_run` without calling `after_run`.

**Decision:** yes. When `before_run` fails or times out, the Attempt fails and `after_run` still runs, best effort, because the workspace exists. A failure or timeout of that `after_run` is logged and ignored, as for any `after_run`.

**Reason:** 5.3.4 and 17.2 are normative prose; 16.5 is a language agnostic reference algorithm. Running `after_run` on every Attempt once the workspace exists also gives hooks one simple contract: whatever `before_run` started, `after_run` gets the chance to undo. Hooks must be idempotent anyway (`before_run` runs before every Attempt, continuations included).

### 3. Priorities outside 1 to 4

**Spec references:** 4.1.1 (`priority`), 8.2 (sorting order), 11.3 (normalization), 17.4.

**What is ambiguous:** 8.2 ranks `1..4` first and puts "all other integers and null" after that bucket, but does not say how values inside the trailing bucket compare. 11.3 lets an implementation document a different mapping.

**Decision:** Loop uses the spec's rule with no remapping. Candidates sort by:

1. Priority bucket: `1`, `2`, `3`, `4` in ascending order, then one trailing bucket holding null and every other integer (0, negative values, 5 and above). Inside the trailing bucket, all values are equal: `0` does not beat `7`, and `7` does not beat null.
2. `created_at`, oldest first, null last.
3. `identifier`, lexicographic, as the final tie breaker.

**Reason:** this is the literal reading of 8.2 and of the 11.3 default. One trailing bucket keeps the behavior identical across trackers, and a priority outside the range a tracker defines is treated as unknown instead of as urgent.

### 4. Cleanup on a terminal retry

**Spec references:** 8.4 (retry handling step 3 and its note), 16.6 (`on_retry_timer`), 8.5, 8.6, 9.4 (`before_remove`).

**What is ambiguous:** 8.4 step 3 says that when a retry refresh finds the Issue terminal, Loop must "clean its workspace and release claim", and its note lists retry refreshes among the places that clean terminal workspaces. The reference `on_retry_timer` in 16.6 only releases the claim when `retry_dispatch_allowed` is false.

**Decision:** when the retry timer fires and the refreshed Issue is in a terminal state, Loop runs `before_remove` (failure or timeout logged and ignored), removes the workspace, and releases the claim. No Attempt is dispatched.

**Reason:** 8.4 is normative and explicit, and 16.6 is a reference algorithm. Cleaning at the retry refresh also matches 8.5 (terminal means stop and clean) and avoids leaving a terminal workspace until the next startup sweep (8.6). For Loop's own Project, workspace removal removes the git worktree and keeps its branch (m1-04), so this cleanup never loses work.

**Built in:** m1-04 builds `WorkspaceManager.remove` (runs `before_remove`, ignores its failure or timeout, then deletes the workspace directory inside the root without following symlinks) and one Orchestrator method, `removeWorkspace(issue)`, that computes the workspace key, runs `remove` with the hook environment and records the `hook_finished` event and log lines. The tick does not call it in m1-04. m1-07's retry path calls it when the refreshed Issue is terminal; whichever of m1-04 and m1-07 merges second adds that one line call.

### 5. Retry cap (`retries.max_attempts`)

**Spec references:** 8.4 (retry and backoff), 16.6 (worker exit and retry handling), 12.3 (`attempt` semantics), 14.3 (in memory scheduler state). The spec retries without a cap.

**What is ambiguous:** nothing in the spec stops an Issue that keeps failing. It is redispatched as long as it stays active and routable, which can burn tokens without limit.

**Decision:** Loop adds `retries.max_attempts`, a top level extension key in `WORKFLOW.md`. Decided by the user on 2026-11-06 (plan decision D15).

- **What counts:** only failure exits count toward the cap:
  - abnormal worker exit (every `fail_worker` path of 16.5, such as an agent turn error, a session startup error, a workspace error or an Issue state refresh error inside the worker);
  - stall (8.5);
  - timeout (turn or read timeout, 10.6);
  - failed or timed out `before_run`;
  - prompt error (`template_parse_error` or `template_render_error`);
  - spawn failure (the agent process could not start).
- **What never counts:**
  - a normal worker exit, and the continuation dispatched after it;
  - a requeue because no orchestrator slot was free (`no available orchestrator slots`);
  - a failed retry refresh (`retry refresh failed` in 16.6, the tracker call made when the retry timer fires).
- **Consecutive:** the counter counts consecutive failures. It resets to zero on a normal worker exit, on `loop resume <issue>`, and when the Issue leaves the active states.
- **Park:** when a failure brings the counter to `max_attempts`, Loop parks the Issue with the reason `max_attempts` instead of scheduling a retry. With the default of 5, the fifth consecutive failed Attempt parks the Issue, and no sixth Attempt starts.
- **Counter reset scope:** the counter resets only on a normal worker exit, on park, and when the Issue leaves the active states. Parking zeroes it and drops the retry entry, so a resumed Issue starts at 0. The counter does not reset because the Issue is not eligible for a tick.
- **Shutdown:** a cancel by shutdown is not a failure and gets no retry.
- **`attempt` template variable:** it is null on the first dispatch after a resume or a restart, and holds the spec's retry `attempt` on a retry. Whether the workspace still holds earlier work is a separate variable, `loop.workspace_reused` (bl-19, see [Workspace reused](#workspace-reused-extension-bl-19)), which leaves `attempt` as it is.
- **Restart:** since m2-02 the retry queue and both counters are kept in the machine level store as well as in memory, so a restart keeps them, with or without a Run id. A queued retry fires at its stored due time with its stored spec `attempt`, and the failure counter goes on from its stored value, so `max_attempts` parks on the consecutive failures of every process together. See [persisted project state](#persisted-project-state-deviation-from-143).
- **Retry fire refresh:** when the retry timer fires, Loop refreshes the Issue by id. Outcomes:
  - refresh fails: reschedule with attempt + 1 and error `retry refresh failed`, not counted;
  - not found: drop the entry, counter kept;
  - terminal: drop the entry, reset the counter. Workspace cleanup runs once m1-04's removeWorkspace is wired at the orchestrator hook `onTerminalRetryDropped`;
  - not active: drop the entry, reset the counter;
  - active but not eligible: drop the entry, counter kept;
  - active and eligible: dispatch with the entry's attempt.
- **Failure counter without a retry entry:** it resets when the issue is no longer among the active candidates.
- **Parked store write failure:** the issue stays parked in memory for the life of the process, the write is retried each tick, and `issue_parked` carries `store_error`.
- **Unpark detection:** on every tick, a Parked Issue that is among the candidates is compared with its parked snapshot (state and normalized label set). Parked ids that are not candidates are fetched by id in one batched call. Any state or label difference unparks the Issue (`issue_unparked`, reason `tracker_changed`); an Issue not found drops the record; a fetch failure skips detection for that tick. A round trip inside one poll interval is not seen, and `loop resume` is the explicit path.
- **Runner unavailable:** since bl-39 a failure the runner marks `runner_unavailable` takes no `max_attempts` slot and holds the whole run instead, see [runner unavailable hold](#runner-unavailable-hold-extension-bl-39).
- **Park reason wire forms:** `max_attempts` (the park is logged at ERROR), `budget` (WARN, m2-20, the `issue_parked` event names the budget, see [Spend budgets](#spend-budgets-extension-m2-20)), `max_continuations` (WARN, see the continuation cap below) `unverified` (WARN, m2-08, see [Unverified park and rerun](#unverified-park-and-rerun-extension-m2-08)), `max_rotations` (WARN, m3-11, see [Soft limit rotation](#soft-limit-rotation-extension-m3-11)) and `split_issue` (WARN, m3-12, see [Hard limit and split issue](#hard-limit-and-split-issue-extension-m3-12)). Since m2-20 `budget` replaces `token_cap`: Loop no longer parks with `token_cap`, and a park an older Loop stored with it still loads, shows and resumes.
- **Validation:** `max_attempts` defaults to 5 when absent. It must be an integer of 1 or more; any other value (0, a negative number, a non integer) is a config validation error, so preflight fails (6.3) and dispatch is blocked until it is fixed.
- **Parked behavior,** as the M1 spec and m1-07 state it:
  - a Parked Issue is excluded from candidate selection and from retry dispatch;
  - its workspace is kept, never cleaned because of the park;
  - the parked state is persisted in the store and survives every restart of Loop, with or without a Run id;
  - it becomes dispatchable again after `loop resume <issue>`, or when its tracker state or labels change.
- **Token thresholds (m1-38):** every `budgets.token_threshold_every` tokens an Attempt uses (10,000,000 by default, cache reads included), Loop records a `token_threshold` event, logs it and runs `hooks.on_token_threshold`, and the Attempt goes on. A threshold never stops or parks anything. `0` turns thresholds off. See [token thresholds](#token-thresholds-extension-m1-38).
- **Token ceiling (opt in), now the per Attempt token budget:** `budgets.per_attempt_tokens` has no default, so by default no token count parks an Attempt. When it is set, crossing it stops the Attempt and parks the Issue directly with the reason `budget`, naming `per_attempt_tokens` (m1-08 parked with `token_cap`; m2-20 replaced it by `budget`). It does not count as a failure and does not consume one of the `max_attempts`. Like every budget it warns once at 80 percent.
- **Why thresholds and not a cap:** nobody can know in advance how many tokens an Issue needs. On Loop's first self run (m1-13, 2026-11-11) Attempt `51975f30` parked on the then default 2,000,000 cap after one 12 minute turn of 5,220,740 tokens (97 percent cache reads, $2.62), and Attempt `00510b3a` finished the same Issue in one turn of 7,166,570 tokens ($3.38). Issue sessions run by hand on two other projects of the user (107 Claude Code sessions) used a median of 5 to 18 million tokens in the main session, a 90th percentile of 45 to 50 million and a maximum above 100 million. No fixed cap fits that spread: a cap parks normal work, a threshold only tells.
- **Token accounting:** Loop keeps the latest absolute total per Attempt (input + output + cache read + cache creation), replaced on each usage report, never summed across turns or session ids. The ceiling, when set, is breached when the total is greater than it, so exactly the ceiling is allowed. On a breach the Attempt stops, `budget_reached` is recorded (`token_cap_reached` before m2-20), and the Issue parks with `budget` (`token_cap` before m2-20) without counting a failure. The ceiling is checked only on real usage reports, never on the mid turn estimate. With Claude Code, real usage arrives once per turn, so a single turn can overshoot the ceiling before it is checked.
- **M2 point, decided in m2-20:** the ceiling was the M2 per Attempt token budget arriving early (PLAN.md safety pulled forward). `token_cap` folds into `budget`: `budgets.per_attempt_tokens` is the per Attempt token budget of the [spend budgets](#spend-budgets-extension-m2-20), beside the dollar budgets of story 84. **Migration:** the key `budgets.per_attempt_tokens` is unchanged, as are its value and its counting, so a `WORKFLOW.md` needs no edit. Only the park reason and the event change: the park reason `token_cap` becomes `budget` with `budget` = `per_attempt_tokens`, `used` and `limit` in `issue_parked` (instead of `attempt_tokens` and `max_tokens`), and the `token_cap_reached` event becomes `budget_reached`. A new `budget_warning` event comes at 80 percent. A script that reads the park reason or the event reads the new names; the store keeps older events and parks as they were.
- **Continuation cap (`retries.max_continuations`, m1-27):** a second counter, the no progress count, stops an Issue whose Attempts keep ending normally without moving it. Decided at triage of m1-27.
  - **What counts:** a normal worker exit after which the Issue is still in an active state, with the same state and the same normalized label set as at dispatch. The baseline is the Issue as refreshed by id at dispatch, so a human label change that lands between Attempts is not progress. Changes to the body, the description or the comments, and workspace or git changes, do not count as progress. A blocked Issue whose state and labels did not change counts, and may park.
  - **What never counts:** a `not_found` exit neither increments nor resets the count. An exit after which the Issue is terminal or no longer active (a workflow reload included) is progress.
  - **Counter rules:** the no progress count is separate from the failure counter. A normal exit resets the failure counter and leaves the no progress count to the rule above. A failure neither increments nor resets the no progress count. Both counters reset on progress, on `loop resume <issue>`, on park (any reason, the budget park included) and when the Issue leaves the active states. The retry fire refresh drops for not eligible and not found keep both. The count is kept in the store with the failure counter (m2-02), so a restart keeps it.
  - **Park:** when a normal exit brings the count to `max_continuations`, the 1 s continuation retry of 8.4 and 16.6 is not scheduled, and the Issue is Parked with the reason `max_continuations`, logged at WARN. With the default of 3, the third normal exit without progress parks the Issue, and no fourth Attempt starts. An Attempt that runs to `agent.max_turns` counts once.
  - **Events:** every continuation `retry_scheduled` carries `no_progress` (the count, 0 after progress) and `max_continuations` (the limit), a slot requeue of a continuation included. `attempt_started` does not carry them. `issue_parked` with the reason `max_continuations` carries both. `loop trace` renders them as `no_progress=<n>/<limit>`.
  - **Validation:** `max_continuations` defaults to 3 when absent. It must be an integer of 1 or more; any other value is a config validation error (`invalid_config`), so preflight fails (6.3) and dispatch is blocked until it is fixed.
- **Backoff:** failure retries still use the spec's delay, `min(10000 * 2^(attempt - 1), agent.max_retry_backoff_ms)`, based on the spec's retry `attempt`.

**Reason:** a cap on consecutive failures stops an Issue that always fails (the M1 spec's safety limit before Loop works alone) without punishing long work that progresses through continuations. Requeues for slots and failed retry refreshes say nothing about the Issue, so they do not count.

**Note for m1-07:** the consecutive failure counter is separate from the spec's retry `attempt`. The spec's `attempt` also grows on failed retry refreshes (16.6, and on slot requeues in the spec, which Loop no longer does since bl-34, see [due retries before new candidates](#due-retries-before-new-candidates-deviation-from-82-and-166-bl-34)) and restarts at 1 after a normal exit, so it cannot serve as the cap counter. Keep both: the spec's `attempt` drives backoff and the template variable, Loop's counter drives parking. m1-07 tests that delays follow the spec formula and that the fifth failure parks; m1-08 tests that the token cap parks without touching the counter.

## Template engine

**Spec references:** 5.4 (prompt template contract), 5.5 (error surface), 12.2 to 12.4.

**The question:** 5.4 requires a strict, Liquid compatible engine where unknown variables and unknown filters fail. The M1 spec said to try an existing JVM Liquid library in strict mode first, and to write a small strict subset for Loop only if a test showed the library could not fail on both.

**The spike:** [liqp](https://github.com/bkiers/Liqp) 0.9.2.3 (`nl.big-o:liqp`), configured with `withStrictVariables(true)` and `ErrorMode.STRICT`, behind Loop's own `TemplateEngine` seam in `spec-core` (`loop.core.template`). The seam has two calls, `parse(source)` and `render(vars)`, each returning a result with a `TemplateError.Parse` or `TemplateError.Render` error, mapped to the spec's `template_parse_error` and `template_render_error`. Nothing outside the seam imports liqp, so the engine can be swapped by touching `spec-core` only.

**What liqp does alone:** strict mode is not enough. liqp 0.9.2.3 accepts an unknown filter at parse and fails it only at render, and its strict variables cannot tell a variable bound to null (such as `attempt` on the first run) from a missing one, so a null `attempt` fails rendering.

**Loop's guards:** the `TemplateEngine` adds three guards on top of liqp:

1. A parse tree scan that rejects unknown filters, so an unknown filter is a `template_parse_error`.
2. Rendering in liqp's WARN mode, followed by a fail closed check: a miss is forgiven only when its dotted path resolves to a key bound to null in the input; every other miss is a `template_render_error`.
3. The `tablerow` tag is removed, because liqp's `tablerow` swallowed misses. A template using it now fails at parse.

**Known gaps:** these cases fail closed with `template_render_error`, even when the value exists and is null:

- a null field reached through a `for` loop variable;
- an index lookup, such as `issue.labels[0]`;
- a template local nil (a variable assigned nil inside the template).

m1-03 checks the template in preflight and leaves these gaps to the Attempt time render (see [the deviation](#prompt-template-checked-in-preflight-deviation-from-55-and-124-m1-03)). If a later issue needs loops over records with nullable fields, Loop writes the small strict subset instead of patching liqp further.

The spike tests live in `spec-core`, in `TemplateEngineTest`:

| Test | Expected result |
|---|---|
| Known variables and a standard filter (`upcase`) | Render the expected text |
| Unknown top level variable | Fails at render with `TemplateError.Render` |
| Unknown nested property (`issue.nope`) | Fails at render with `TemplateError.Render` |
| Unknown filter | Fails at parse with `TemplateError.Parse` |
| `attempt` bound to null | Renders, and `{% if attempt %}` takes the else branch |
| Collection access (`issue.labels \| join: ", "`) | Renders the joined labels |
| `tablerow` tag | Not supported, fails at parse with `TemplateError.Parse` |

**Evidence:** the trail is in `.scratch/m1-01/evidence/`: a red run for each behavior (`red-*.txt`; `red-unknown-filter-at-parse.txt` and `red-null-attempt.txt` show what liqp does alone), mutation reds where a test passed on its first run (strict variables turned off for one run to prove the nested property test can fail), and `first-run-pass-labels-join.txt`, where the labels join passed on its first run as a capability check of liqp.

**Result:** the issue's rule (use liqp only if its strict mode alone fails both unknowns) is not met by liqp alone. The chosen reading for M1 is liqp behind the seam with Loop's guards: it fails closed, no caller imports liqp, and a swap touches `spec-core` only. The user signs this off in the issue's manual QA.

**Note on unknown filters:** Loop's parse tree scan rejects an unknown filter, so Loop reports it as `template_parse_error`, while 5.4 and 5.5 list unknown filters under rendering failures (`template_render_error`). This is acceptable:

- 5.5 itself places `template_parse_error` "during prompt rendering", and both error classes have the same effect: the template errors "fail only the affected run attempt" (5.5) and the orchestrator handles it like any other worker failure (12.4).
- The template still never renders with an unknown filter, which is what 5.4 protects.
- Failing earlier is stricter, never looser.

## Implementation defined behaviors

The spec says implementation defined behavior must be documented by the implementation. This section is that documentation. Each item is either decided here or names the issue that decides it.

| Behavior | Spec | Loop's choice | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hook shell | 9.4 | Hooks run with `sh -lc <script>`, with the workspace as the working directory and `hooks.timeout_ms` (default 60000 ms) as the timeout. The hook environment is the host environment of the Loop process plus the `LOOP_` variables: `LOOP_ISSUE_ID`, `LOOP_ISSUE_IDENTIFIER`, `LOOP_WORKSPACE_KEY`, `LOOP_TRACKER_ID` and, in Loop mode, `LOOP_KIND` (see the extensions), with `LOOP_WORKFLOW` and the other Attempt variables, and, for a kind with `delivery.pull_request`, `LOOP_BASE_REF` (see [Base fetch before every attempt](#base-fetch-before-every-attempt-extension-bl-59)). Tracker credentials are never added (15.3). Stdout and stderr are merged and the last 8 KiB are kept. A hook that exceeds its timeout is stopped with its whole process tree. Each hook run is recorded as one `hook_finished` event with `hook`, `command` (first 200 characters), `exit_code` (omitted when the hook was stopped), `timed_out`, `duration_ms`, `output` (the kept tail) and `output_truncated` (present only when the 8 KiB tail was cut, left out otherwise). Log lines: `event=hook outcome=started hook=<name>` at start, `outcome=completed` with `duration_ms` on success, `outcome=failed` with `exit_code`, `duration_ms` and `reason` (the last non empty output line, or `timed_out=true` and `reason="timed out after <n> ms"`) on failure. A failure logs at error level for `after_create` and `before_run`, at warn level for `after_run` and `before_remove`. When the Attempt was cancelled, `after_run` gets `min(hooks.timeout_ms, 5000)` ms instead of the full timeout, so shutdown is not held up. | Built in m1-04 |
| Non-directory at the workspace path | 17.2 | A file or other non-directory entry already at `<workspace.root>/<workspace_key>` fails the Attempt. Loop never replaces or deletes it, so Loop never deletes a file it did not create. | Decided; built in m1-04 |
| Priority mapping for the `files` tracker | 11.3 | The `priority` front matter value is passed through as is when it is an integer. A missing or non integer value normalizes to null. No remapping, so values outside 1 to 4 sort with null (answer 3). | Decided |
| Workspace population | 9.3 | Loop has no built in population or VCS logic; hooks do it. The walking skeleton uses a plain empty directory (m1-02). Loop's own Project uses `after_create` to create a git worktree on the branch `loop/<issue>` without the `.loop/issues/` folder, and `before_remove` to remove the worktree while keeping the branch ([`workspaces.md`](workspaces.md)). Partial cleanup: when `after_create` fails or times out on a workspace created in that call, Loop removes that directory, so the next Attempt runs `after_create` again instead of reusing a half populated folder. A reused workspace is never removed or reset on failure. Dependency: when `git worktree add` had already run, this removal leaves a stale worktree entry in the main repository; for Loop's own Project, `worktree-after-create.sh` runs `git worktree prune` first, which clears it. | Decided; built in m1-04 |
| Approval and sandbox policy of the Claude Code runner | 10.5 | The runner launches Claude Code with `--permission-mode bypassPermissions`, so it never stops on an approval prompt. For each turn (each `claude -p` invocation, the first and every `--resume`) it generates its own settings file outside the workspace, passes it with `--settings <file>` and deletes it when that turn ends. The file's `permissions.deny` prefix rules, written like `Bash(git push:*)`, block `git push` (forced pushes included), `gh`, and the history rewrites `git rebase`, `git reset --hard`, `git commit --amend`, `git filter-branch`, `git filter-repo`, `git reflog expire`, `git update-ref` and `git replace`. Since m2-24 they also block the operator commands `loop approve` and `loop resume`, by the bare name and by the Attempt's `LOOP_LAUNCHER` path, and a denial of either through any path ending in `/loop` counts as deny-listed. Behind these rules both commands refuse to run when `LOOP_ATTEMPT_ID` is set, see [Delivery policy and approval](#delivery-policy-and-approval-extension-m2-24). This is a guardrail, not a sandbox: a prefix rule can be dodged, for example with `sh -c "gh ..."` or `git -C . push`. Since m2-14 the file also denies `Read` and `Edit` under the holdout folder, see [Holdout checks](#holdout-checks-extension-m2-14). Startup fails when bypass mode is disabled by managed settings (`permissions.disableBypassPermissionsMode: "disable"`) or when Claude Code would run as root. | Decided; built in m1-05 |
| Approval and sandbox policy of the Codex runner | 10.5 | Sandbox `danger-full-access` on `thread/start` and `turn/start`, so a Codex agent has the reach of the Claude Code agent in bypass mode (m3-18). Approval policy `untrusted` since m3-20, `never` before: Codex asks before every command it does not hold as known safe and every file change, and the runner answers each request at once by rule, declining `git push`, `gh`, history rewrites and the holdout folder, so no turn waits on a person. User input ends the turn with `turn_input_required`, an unsupported dynamic tool call gets a tool failure result. See [Codex runner session startup](#codex-runner-session-startup-m3-18) and [Codex runner approvals, user input, tool calls and guards](#codex-runner-approvals-user-input-tool-calls-and-guards-m3-20). | Decided; built in m3-18 and m3-20 |
| User input requests | 10.5, 14.1 | A runtime that needs input it cannot get fails the Attempt, with the reason traced, so it is retried and eventually Parked instead of hanging. With bypassPermissions the Claude Code runner meets no approval prompt. A turn that ends after a denial of a tool that is not deny-listed fails with category `turn_input_required`, and the reason is traced. An error `result` whose only permission denials are deny-listed commands is `turn_failed: <subtype>`, because no input can unblock a policy denial. A denied deny-listed command on its own is not a failure: the agent sees the denial and continues. A denied file tool call under the holdout folder counts like a deny-listed command (m2-14). How a denial is classified (bl-33): each entry of the result's `permission_denials` is a policy denial or a request for input. A Bash denial is a policy denial when its command holds a deny-listed command anywhere in its text, not only at its start, since Claude Code matches its rules on the whole command text and so also denies a command that only quotes one: a heredoc body with `git push` in backticks, a quoted string, a command after `&&`, `;` or `|`, or a `python3 -` script that quotes `loop approve`. The runner splits the command into words at blanks, quotes, backslashes, the shell's operators and list punctuation, and a deny-listed command matches as a run of whole words at any place, so `git status && git push` and `echo "gh pr view"` match while `git pushd` and `echo github` do not. An operator command through any path ending in `/loop` counts the same way. A Bash denial that names the holdout folder, or a file tool denial under it, is a policy denial too. Any other denial needs input. The turn then ends by its denials and its result: one denial that needs input makes it `turn_input_required`; with only policy denials, an error result is `turn_failed: <subtype>` and a success result completes the turn, so the move, comment or submitted result it queued is applied as for any successful turn. Matching more than Claude Code does only keeps a turn going after a denial the agent already saw; matching less would fail a finished turn and drop what it queued, as it did for m2-14 attempt 1 and m2-24 attempt 3. | Decided; built in m1-05 and m1-07, classification widened in bl-33 |
| `files` tracker adapter profile | 11.2 | Published in [`plugins/tracker-files.md`](plugins/tracker-files.md): provider keys (`tracker.provider.dir`, default `.loop/issues` relative to `WORKFLOW.md`), scope, `id` and `identifier` mapping, the six states and their roles, normalization (`created_at` is always null), sort order, malformed records, writes through `loop tool move` and errors. | Decided (m1-02) |
| Human readable status surface | 13.3, 13.4 | `loop status`, built from the store and the run file only, never required for correctness. See [`loop status` runtime snapshot](#loop-status-runtime-snapshot-extension-of-133-and-134-m1-14). | Built in m1-14 |
| Continuation guidance | 7.1, 16.5 | The input of turns 2 to n of an Attempt, the same text for every runner, with the tags filled per turn: "Continue working on <identifier>. This is turn <n> of <max>. The issue is still in <state>. Build on your previous turns, do not start over. When the work is done, move the issue with loop tool move review. If you are blocked, comment and move it to needs_info." Turn 1 gets the full rendered prompt. In Loop mode, when the checks refuted a result the turn before submitted, a blank line and the verify findings follow (m2-06): "Your last result was refuted: Loop ran its checks and one failed, so the issue did not move. Fix what the check found without changing or skipping it, then move the issue again.", then the lines "Check: <command>", "Exit code: <code>" (a timed out check gives no findings since m2-08, it is `unverified`) and "Last <k> lines of its output:" with those lines (or "Its output was empty."). See [Verify findings](#verify-findings-extension-m2-06). A refuted holdout check gives a fixed text instead, with no command, exit code or output (m2-14, see [Holdout checks](#holdout-checks-extension-m2-14)). When the drain after the turn before could not apply a queued `loop tool` request (a `tool_request_failed`), a blank line follows, then one line per failed request, after the findings when both are present (m2-07): "Loop could not apply your last request (move to <state>): <reason>. Run it again if it is still needed." A failed comment reads "(comment)", the failed move of a `done` result file "(move to review)" (m2-15), a `submit_result` call in Symphony mode "(submit_result)" (m2-16) and an undecodable request or an unreadable queue "(unknown request)". The agent can choose part of that text (the state of a move, the name of a request file it wrote), so the state and the reason are redacted, each put on one line (every run of whitespace, line breaks or control characters becomes one space), the state cut to 64 characters and the reason to 200. The reason gets a final period unless it already ends with a period, a question mark or an exclamation mark. A move to the same state that fails twice in one drain, a queued move and a `done` result file, gives one line. Only the very next turn of the same Attempt gets it; turn 1 of a new Attempt stays the rendered prompt. See [host side tracker writes](#host-side-tracker-writes-extension-m1-23). | Decided; built in m1-06, findings in m2-06, failed requests in m2-07 |

## Loop extensions and deviations from the spec

The spec allows new top level front matter keys, so a Loop `WORKFLOW.md` stays readable by other Symphony implementations. Items below marked "deviation" change spec behavior; the others extend it or state how Loop applies a spec rule.

### `blocked_by` enforcement in the `files` tracker (extension, m1-09)

The `files` adapter sets `dispatchable` to `false` for an Issue with an unfinished blocker, that is, any Issue listed in its `blocked_by` front matter whose state is not `done`. The orchestrator never reads blockers itself.

This follows the spec's split of responsibilities: 4.1.1 lists blocker semantics among the provider rules behind `dispatchable`, 11.2 forbids the orchestrator to branch on provider blocker semantics, and 11.3 lets an adapter derive them when it can represent them reliably, which the `files` tracker can because every Issue is a file in the same folder. Side effect from 8.5: since a blocked Issue is not routable, a running Issue whose blocker leaves `done` is stopped without workspace cleanup. Blockers across trackers, a body line such as `Blocked by lin:MKT-12`, came with several trackers in m4-16, see [Cross tracker blockers from a body line](#cross-tracker-blockers-from-a-body-line-extension-m4-16).

**Decision (m1-09):** only `done` resolves a blocker. A `canceled`, `review`, `needs_info` or any other state blocks, and a missing or malformed blocker file blocks too, with a warning once per issue and missing blocker. A blocker whose file exists but cannot be parsed also blocks, with the warning `<issue>: blocked_by <id> has an issue file that cannot be read, so <issue> stays blocked.` The adapter sets `Issue.blockedReason` (`blocked_by <id> (<state>)`, `blocked_by <id> (missing)` or `blocked_by <id> (malformed)`) and derives `dispatchable` from it. The orchestrator records a `candidate_skipped` event when a candidate's skip reason changes, so `loop trace` shows why an Issue waits.

**Reason:** fail closed. A blocker that cannot be confirmed done must not let work start, SPEC.md L1278 forbids inventing blocker semantics, and user story 16 wants a blocked Issue to stay blocked until its blocker is done.

### `loop stop` mechanism (extension, m1-09)

For M1 the Run is a `loop run` process that holds a run file, `<LOOP_HOME>/runs/<projectId>.json`, locked for as long as it lives. `loop stop` writes a stop marker, sends SIGTERM to the pid in the run file, waits up to 15 s for the process to exit and never sends SIGKILL, so agent processes are stopped by the run itself and workspaces are kept. Since m1-34 the stopped `loop run` exits 0 once it unwound within its 10 s shutdown grace (the attempts canceled and recorded, the process trees killed, `run_stopped` recorded, the store closed, the run file released), and keeps 143 when the grace runs out. A plain SIGTERM or SIGINT from an operator, without the stop marker, is a handled stop too (reason `signal`): it exits 0 the same way, and keeps 143 or 130 only when the grace runs out. A store row that the run polls is the M2 path, once `loop status` reads running state from the store (ADR 0001).

### Stall detection (deviation from 8.5, m1-09)

Loop measures stall as one receive timeout per wait for a runner event inside a turn. The timer restarts on every runner event. 8.5 starts the clock at dispatch, but before the first turn only hooks run, and they have their own timeouts. The Claude Code runner's own read timeout may end a silent turn before the stall timeout does. A stalled Attempt is stopped and handled as a failure (it counts toward `retries.max_attempts`).

### Reconciliation and startup cleanup (clarification of 8.5, 8.6 and 16.3, m1-16)

**When.** Every tick reconciles first, before the preflight (8.1, 16.2), so a broken `WORKFLOW.md` still stops what the tracker ended. With no running Issue there is no tracker call. `loop run --once` waits for its Attempts inside its one tick, so only the daemon reconciles a running Attempt. An Attempt that reconciliation is already stopping is not read again.

**What.** One `fetch_issues_by_ids` call for every running Issue, classified like the retry refresh and the turn loop:

| Issue as read | Attempt | Workspace | Retry entry and counters |
|---|---|---|---|
| Terminal | Stopped, reason `terminal` | Removed once the Attempt closed (`before_remove` runs) | Dropped, counters reset |
| Neither active nor terminal, for example `review` or `needs_info` | Stopped, reason `not_active` | Kept | Dropped, counters reset |
| Active without a required label, or blocked | Stopped, reason `not_eligible` | Kept | Dropped, counters kept |
| Not returned by the tracker | Stopped, reason `not_found` | Kept | Dropped, counters kept |
| Active and routable | Keeps running | Kept | Unchanged; the state read replaces the running state used by the per state slots |

A failed read logs `level=warn event=reconcile outcome=failed` and keeps every Attempt running until the next tick (8.5, 14.2). Each stop logs `level=info event=reconcile outcome=completed` with `issue_state`, `workspace=remove` or `workspace=keep`, and the reason.

**How a stopped Attempt closes.** Reconciliation cancels the Attempt's turns only. The Attempt then closes like one that ended: the agent session is stopped, `after_run` runs with the 5 s cap of a canceled Attempt, queued tool requests are dropped with `tool_request_dropped`, the Attempt directory is deleted. It records the phase `CanceledByReconciliation` (7.2) with `reason` and `issue_state`, and `attempt_finished` with outcome `canceled` and the reason. It is neither a failure nor a normal exit, so no retry is scheduled and no counter grows. It is not counted in the `attempts_canceled` of `run_stopped`, which counts what `loop stop` or a signal canceled. `loop trace` prints the reason as `issue terminal`, `issue not active`, `issue not eligible` or `issue not found`.

**Startup cleanup (8.6).** `loop run` and `loop run --once` fetch the Issues in the terminal states once, after the Attempt directory sweep and before the first tick, and remove the workspace of each one that has a workspace, `before_remove` first. A failed fetch logs `level=warn event=startup_cleanup outcome=failed` and startup goes on (11.4). With Loop's own worktree hooks, removal removes the worktree and keeps the branch `loop/<issue>` ([workspaces](workspaces.md)).

### Abandoned Attempts at startup (extension, m1-36)

A `loop run` killed without a chance to unwind (`kill -9`, a crash, a power cut) leaves its Attempts with an `attempt_started` and no `attempt_finished`, and itself without a `run_stopped`. When the next `loop run` holds the run lock, no live process of the Project runs an Attempt (ADR 0001), so every such Attempt belongs to a dead process. Right after the Attempt directory sweep and before startup cleanup and the first tick, `loop run` and `loop run --once` close each one:

- `attempt_finished` with outcome `canceled`, reason `abandoned` and `detected_by_run` (the id of the new Run), in the Run and for the Issue of the Attempt's `attempt_started`. Its time is when the new Run detected it. No terminal phase is recorded.
- A warning in the operator log, once per Attempt: `level=warn event=attempt outcome=failed ... previous_run=<dead run> reason=abandoned`.
- `run_stopped` with reason `abandoned`, `attempts_canceled` (the Attempts of that Run closed now) and `detected_by_run`, for each Run of those Attempts that recorded no `run_stopped` after them, so a Run that spans restarts shows where the dead process ended. A dead process that left no open Attempt is not detected.

An abandoned Attempt is not a failure: it counts toward neither `retries.max_attempts`, the backoff, nor the no progress count, like `stop_requested`. The workspace is kept, and the Issue is dispatched again as usual when still eligible. A failed store read or write logs a warning and startup goes on; what was not recorded is closed by the next run.

`loop trace` stays read only: before a restart it shows the dead Attempt as running. After it, the header says `canceled in <s>, abandoned`, counted to the detection time, and the timeline ends with `attempt_finished  outcome=canceled reason=abandoned detected_by_run=<run>`. `loop status` no longer lists it (only Attempts of the live run are running), and `loop show <attempt> --events` prints its `attempt_finished`.

### Slot exhaustion on a due retry (clarification of 16.6, m1-09)

A due retry that finds no free slot is requeued with the error `no available orchestrator slots`. It is not counted as a failure, as decided in [answer 5](#5-retry-cap-retriesmax_attempts). Since bl-34 the requeue keeps the spec `attempt` and the retry stays due from its first due time, see [due retries before new candidates](#due-retries-before-new-candidates-deviation-from-82-and-166-bl-34).

### Due retries before new candidates (deviation from 8.2 and 16.6, bl-34)

**Spec:** 8.2 sorts every dispatch candidate by priority, creation time and identifier, and 16.6 requeues a due retry that finds no free slot with `attempt + 1`, so with the failure backoff of 8.4.

**What went wrong:** in run 4672d0bf a retry due at 18:41 started at 20:21, after 24 requeues with `no available orchestrator slots`. Each requeue doubled its delay up to `agent.max_retry_backoff_ms`, and each slot that freed went to a new candidate that became eligible later (four of them, the first at 19:00). With two slots and a long queue a retry can wait for hours behind newer work.

**Decision,** confirmed by the maintainer at triage of bl-34:

- **Order of a tick:** issues with a due retry are considered for free slots before issues without one, even when a new candidate has a higher priority. Among due retries the order is the time each retry first fell due, earliest first, then the spec order of 8.2. New candidates follow in the spec order of 8.2.
- **First due time:** a slot requeue moves the due time of the entry and keeps the time it first fell due. A retry counts as due from its first due time, so a retry that waits for a slot stays due at every tick and keeps its place ahead of a retry that falls due later. Any other schedule (a failure, a continuation, a failed retry refresh) starts a new first due time. Only the process keeps it, in memory: after a restart a queued retry takes its stored due time as its first due time.
- **Slot requeue:** a due retry that finds no free slot keeps its spec `attempt`, and its due time moves one poll interval (`polling.interval_ms`) ahead. Its `retry_scheduled` still carries the error `no available orchestrator slots`, and it still counts no failure, no `max_attempts` step and no backoff. The moved due time only wakes the daemon. It moves by the poll interval rather than the 1 s continuation delay because the daemon sleeps until the next due time, and a 1 s move would poll the tracker every second while every slot stays full. Since m4-15 a retry that keeps waiting records its requeue once per reason and is refreshed by id at most once per poll interval, see [the requeue volume](#per-tracker-concurrency-limit-and-a-quieter-retry-requeue-extension-m4-15).
- **Earlier ticks:** the retry stays due from its first due time, so any tick decides its slot, also a tick that comes before the moved due time. Such a tick comes from a 1 s continuation, from the 10 s or 20 s backoff of a failed attempt, or from the poll. The free slot then goes to the due retry that first fell due earliest, never to that continuation, that failure retry or a new candidate. Before this rule, with a 30 s poll, a waiting retry was invisible to those earlier ticks and could lose every freed slot (send back of bl-34).
- **Unchanged:** per state caps still apply, so a due retry whose state is at its cap is requeued and later issues in other states still get slots (`a due retry blocked by its state's cap is requeued and another state's candidate takes the free slot (bl-77 finding 2)` in `OrchestratorRetryTest.kt`). Blocked or not ready issues are skipped as before, with no special case for a due retry. A failed retry refresh still reschedules with `attempt + 1` and its backoff. Failure counting, `max_attempts` and the backoff of a failed attempt do not change. No slot is reserved across ticks.

**Reason:** a retry is work Loop already started on, and the requeue says nothing about the Issue, so neither its place nor its backoff should suffer from waiting for a slot. Reserving the next free slot for the oldest due retry gives the same result but needs state across ticks.

**Tests:** `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/OrchestratorRetryTest.kt` (`with one slot a due retry of priority 3 is dispatched before a new candidate of priority 1`, `with two due retries and one slot the retry due first is dispatched`, `a slot requeue keeps the attempt and is due at the next tick`, and with a 30 s poll `the daemon gives the freed slot to the waiting retry before the holder's continuation and a new candidate` and `the daemon gives the freed slot to the waiting retry before the holder's failure retry and a new candidate`), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/RetryQueueTest.kt` and `conformance/src/test/kotlin/loop/conformance/SchedulingConformanceTest.kt`, whose comments name this deviation.

### Persisted project state (deviation from 14.3)

14.3 keeps scheduler state in memory: after a restart, retry timers are gone and every active Issue is eligible again. Loop keeps the Project state in the machine level store (ADR 0001) instead, so a restart, with or without a Run id, resumes where the last process stopped (user story 82 of [the M1 to M3 spec](../.loop/specs/loop-m1-to-m3.md)). This also covers the spec's own open item about persisting the retry queue and sessions. Startup cleanup (8.6) and fresh polling still apply as the spec says. What is persisted, one row per Issue and Project:

| State | Table | Since | What a restart does with it |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Parked Issues | `parked_issues` | m1-07 | A Parked Issue stays Parked across every restart. Restarting Loop never undoes a park. |
| The retry queue and its counters | `retry_states` | m2-02 | Each Issue's queued retry (spec `attempt`, due time, last error, null for a continuation), its failure counter (`retries.max_attempts`) and its no progress count (`retries.max_continuations`). The row is written each time a tick or an Attempt end changes it, and deleted when the queue keeps nothing for the Issue. The first tick of a new process reads every row back before it dispatches anything: a queued retry fires at its stored due time, never at once, and is recorded again in the new Run as `retry_scheduled` with `restored=true` and the remaining `delay_ms`. A store that cannot be read on that tick logs `event=retry_state outcome=failed` and dispatches nothing until it can. A failed write logs the same line at warn and is written again at the next change or tick. A row left for a Parked Issue (a park deletes it, so only after a failed delete) is dropped on the next tick, never dispatched. |
| Claims | `claims` | m2-02 | Written when an Attempt starts, with its Attempt id and Run, and deleted when it ends, a cancel included. The decision to claim stays in memory (single threaded dispatch, plan D16); the row tells the next process what a dead one held. At startup, with the run lock held, every row belongs to a dead process: it is deleted and recorded as `claim_released` with the reason `abandoned`, in the Run, Attempt and session of the claim, with `detected_by_run`, and logged as `level=warn event=claim outcome=released`. The Issue is then dispatched once by the usual paths: through its stored retry when it has one, else as a candidate, unless it waits (m2-04), in which case its checks run again first. The abandoned Attempt is not a failure (m1-36). A failed claim write logs `event=claim outcome=failed` and the Attempt goes on. |
| Waiting Issues | `waiting_issues` | m2-04 | One row per Issue Loop waits on, with the reason, the Attempt and Run that started the wait, since when, and what the wait needs to go on (for `verify`, the target state `to`; for `approval` since m2-24, also the verified `fingerprint`, then `approved_by` and `approved_at` once `loop approve` ran; with pull request delivery since m2-25, also `checks`, `judgment_calls` and, once commented, `pull_request`). The first tick of a new process reads every row back before it dispatches anything, and an Issue with a row is never dispatched while it waits. See [Waiting](#waiting-claim-state-extension-m2-04). |
| Ledger rows | `attempts` | m2-19 | One row per finished Attempt, never changed afterwards. A restart keeps them all; the last row of an Issue tells the next Attempt whether a human moved it back. See [Ledger](#ledger-extension-m2-19). |
| Frozen acceptance command lists | `acceptance_commands` | m2-09 | One row per Issue whose kind declares `acceptance_commands`: the digest and count of its command list at its first dispatch, and the changed list a later verification found, waiting for `loop resume`. Never the commands, which can hold a secret. A restart verifies against the row as the dead process would have. See [Acceptance commands](#acceptance-commands-extension-m2-09). |
| Sessions | `claims` | m2-02 | The claim row carries the session id of its Attempt and the runtime session id when the runner reports one, updated on every turn, so `claim_released` names the session that died. Resuming a dead session (Claude Code `--resume`) is not done here: it lands with session rotation (M3), and a restart starts a new Attempt with a new session. |

### Store access: plain JDBC kept (m2-02)

**Question:** the spec stack section dropped SQLDelight in m1-02 and asked to revisit it past three tables or a migration beyond `user_version`. m2-02 brings the store to four tables (`events`, `parked_issues`, `claims`, `retry_states`).

**Decision:** keep plain JDBC through sqlite-jdbc. Judgment call made in m2-02.

**Reason:** the store is one class with a dozen short statements, each covered by a store test against a real SQLite file. Every migration so far only adds tables with idempotent `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` statements gated by `PRAGMA user_version` (now 3), and an old store opens and upgrades by running them all, which a test covers for versions 1 and 2. SQLDelight would add a Gradle plugin, generated code and its own migration files to a build that does not need them yet, and its JVM driver runs on the same sqlite-jdbc anyway.

**Revisit when:** a migration has to change an existing table (rename, drop or retype a column, or copy rows), or queries start joining tables, or the store goes past about eight tables. Any of these is when generated, compile checked queries and verified migrations pay for themselves.

### Template `attempt` keeps spec semantics (clarification)

The template variable `attempt` is exactly the spec's (5.4, 12.3): null on the first run of an Issue, then a 1 based retry or continuation count, with no distinction between the two. Loop's Attempt (the glossary term) is a different thing: every Attempt has its own id, carried in every event, log line and trace record next to the Run, Project, Issue, session and turn ids. The Attempt id is never exposed as the template's `attempt`, and the template's `attempt` is never used as an id.

### `files` tracker refresh fails on malformed records (clarification of 11.1)

11.1 lets a state list call omit a malformed record and requires an id refresh to fail instead. The `files` adapter applies both:

- `fetch_issues_by_states` skips an Issue file it cannot normalize (missing or empty `id`, `title` or `state`, or unparseable front matter) and logs the omission.
- `fetch_issues_by_ids` fails the whole call when a requested Issue file is malformed, because omitting it would mean "no longer visible" and release the claim.

### `files` adapter profile defaults for active and terminal states (extension of 5.3.1)

5.3.1 makes `active_states` and `terminal_states` required unless the adapter profile documents a default. The `files` profile documents these defaults:

| Setting | Default |
|---|---|
| `active_states` | `todo`, `in_progress` |
| `terminal_states` | `done`, `canceled` |

`review` and `needs_info` are handoff states, neither active nor terminal: an Issue moved there stops its worker and keeps its workspace (8.5).

### Retry cap and Parked (extension, m1-07, m1-08 and m1-27)

`retries.max_attempts` (default 5) and the Parked claim state, with the rules in [answer 5](#5-retry-cap-retriesmax_attempts). The spec retries without a cap. The opt in token ceiling per Attempt (m1-08, no default since m1-38, the per Attempt token budget since m2-20) also parks when it is set, and so does `retries.max_continuations` (default 3, m1-27), after too many normal exits without progress. M2 extends parking to every reason (budgets, landed in m2-20, see [Spend budgets](#spend-budgets-extension-m2-20), an `unverified` verdict, landed in m2-08, see [Unverified park and rerun](#unverified-park-and-rerun-extension-m2-08), "split this issue") and adds the `Waiting` claim state, landed for verification in m2-04 (see [Waiting](#waiting-claim-state-extension-m2-04)).

### Prompt template checked in preflight (deviation from 5.5 and 12.4, m1-03)

The spec says template errors "fail only the affected run attempt" (5.5), and that a rendering failure fails the Attempt and is handled like any other worker failure (12.4). m1-03 asks that a template with an unknown variable or filter launches no Attempt at all. Loop follows m1-03: the prompt template is part of [preflight](#preflight-on-each-tick-clarification-of-63-m1-03), and a broken template blocks dispatch.

Preflight checks the template in three steps:

1. A parse, with the unknown filter scan of the [template engine](#template-engine). An unknown filter is a `template_parse_error`.
2. A static lookup check of the parse tree. Every lookup root must be `issue`, `attempt`, `forloop`, a loop variable or a name assigned or captured in the template, and every `issue.<field>` must be a field Loop binds for the Issue. This catches unknown variables in branches the sample render does not take.
3. A sample render against a sample Issue with every field set (except `created_at`, which the `files` tracker always yields as null), once with `attempt` null and once with `attempt` 1, so both branches of `{% if attempt %}` are rendered. An unknown variable found here is a `template_render_error`.

**Effect:** a failure blocks dispatch for every Issue for that tick, not only for the Issues the template would fail on. No Attempt starts, so a template error never counts toward the retry cap (answer 5). Dispatch resumes on the first tick whose preflight passes.

**Backstop:** the render at Attempt time keeps its spec behavior. If it fails anyway, the Attempt fails with `template_render_error` (12.4) and counts toward the retry cap.

**Residual gap:** the sample render cannot reach every case. These still pass preflight and fail the Attempt at render time, as listed in the template engine's known gaps:

- a null field reached through a `for` loop variable;
- an index lookup, such as `issue.labels[0]`;
- a template local nil (a variable assigned nil inside the template).

**Reason:** a template is shared by every Issue, so a template error is almost always a config error, not a property of one Issue. Blocking dispatch with an operator visible error costs no tokens and no workspace, while following 5.5 would start, fail and retry an Attempt for every eligible Issue until each one is Parked.

### Preflight on each tick (clarification of 6.3, m1-03)

Preflight runs once at startup and then at the start of every tick, before the tracker fetch (16.2). It re-reads `WORKFLOW.md` from disk and runs the whole pipeline: parse, defaults, `$VAR` resolution, typed coercion and validation, the tracker check (`tracker.kind` supported, and the adapter accepting `tracker.provider` and the states after defaults and `$VAR`), the runner check, and the [template check](#prompt-template-checked-in-preflight-deviation-from-55-and-124-m1-03).

- **At startup:** a failure exits `loop run` (and `loop run --once`) with code 1 and the error on stderr. Nothing is dispatched and no Attempt is recorded. On success, the workflow preflight loaded is the one the Run uses, so the file is not read twice.
- **On each tick:** a failure skips dispatch for that tick only (6.3). The Run keeps going and checks again on the next tick.
- **Reload (m1-15):** a passing preflight whose file differs from the effective workflow becomes the effective workflow, so the next dispatch and the next Attempt use it. A failing one keeps the last good workflow and still skips dispatch. See [workflow reload](#workflow-reload-clarification-of-62-m1-15).
- **`codex.command`:** 6.3 lists "`codex.command` is present and non-empty" among the checks. In M1 the check applies to the selected runner's command (`runners.command.command`), because the Codex runner does not exist until M3. An empty `codex.command` is not a preflight failure in M1, see [validation rules](#config-validation-rules-clarification-of-53-and-61-m1-03).

### Workflow reload (clarification of 6.2, m1-15)

Loop detects a change of `WORKFLOW.md` with the preflight it already runs at the start of every tick: the preflight reads the file from disk, and a passing preflight whose SHA-256 differs from the effective workflow's is a reload. There is no file watcher. Every dispatch, retry and Attempt start goes through that preflight first, so no Attempt starts on a stale config, and a change is seen within one `polling.interval_ms`.

- **Config version:** the SHA-256 hex of the `WORKFLOW.md` text. It is the same across runs and restarts for the same text. Every `attempt_started` stores it as `config_version`, and `loop trace` prints its first 8 characters in the Attempt header as `config <version>`, in full with `--full-ids`. Attempts recorded before m1-15 have none.
- **What applies when:** an Attempt reads the effective workflow once, when it starts, and keeps it to its end: prompt, `agent.max_turns` and `agent.max_wake_turns`, the active and terminal states of its continuation turns, budgets, `codex.stall_timeout_ms`, hooks, `workspace.root`, the runner and its settings, the agent env's removed names, `agent.env`, the tool config snapshot and `kinds:` with its checks (m2-03). A reload never changes a running Attempt (6.2 does not require it). Dispatch, eligibility, concurrency, the polling interval, retry scheduling and workspace removal use the effective workflow of the moment.
- **Needs a restart:** the tracker adapter is built once per run. A reload that changes `tracker.provider.dir` is applied to the config, and the adapter keeps reading the old folder; Loop logs `level=warn event=workflow_reload outcome=completed reason="tracker.provider.dir changed to <new>, Loop keeps reading <old> until loop run is restarted"`. States and required labels apply live.
- **Invalid edit:** the preflight fails, dispatch is skipped for the tick as in [preflight on each tick](#preflight-on-each-tick-clarification-of-63-m1-03), and the effective workflow stays the last good one. Running Attempts go on with their own. When the file is fixed, or reverted to the last good text, dispatch resumes; a revert is not a reload, since the version did not change.
- **`config_reloaded`**, a run level event (Project and Run ids only): `config_version` (the new hash), `previous_version`, and `diff`, a unified diff of the two texts with 3 lines of context and `--- WORKFLOW.md <previous 8>` and `+++ WORKFLOW.md <new 8>` headers. The diff is redacted, then cut at a line end to 16,000 characters, with `diff_truncated=true` when cut. It is recorded before the tick's `preflight` event and before any Attempt the tick starts. The operator log says `level=info event=workflow_reload outcome=completed config_version=<new 8> previous_version=<old 8>`, the human log `WORKFLOW.md reloaded, config <new 8> replaces <old 8>`.
- **`config_reload_failed`**, run level, recorded once per rejected text, and once per stretch for a file that cannot be read: `config_version` (the hash of the rejected text, absent when it could not be read), `active_version` (the last good one), `error` and `message` (the preflight's code and redacted message), and `diff` from the active text, absent when unreadable. The operator log says `level=error event=workflow_reload outcome=failed config_version=<8> active_version=<8> error=<code> reason="<message>"`, the human log `WORKFLOW.md edit rejected, config <8> stays active: <message>`. The `preflight` event and its log line still come on every failing tick.
- **Secrets:** the redactor learns the secrets of every passing preflight before anything of the tick is recorded, so a diff never shows a new credential. A new reference whose value is under 4 characters is named once, at the reload that adds it, as a `workflow_reload` warning.

### The `preflight` event (extension, m1-03)

Every preflight on a tick records a run level event in the store, pass or fail, so `loop trace` shows when dispatch was blocked and why.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `kind` | `preflight` |
| Ids | Project and Run only. No Issue or Attempt id, since preflight runs before any Issue is picked. |
| `data.result` | `ok` or `failed` |
| `data.error` | On failure, the error code: `missing_workflow_file`, `workflow_parse_error`, `workflow_front_matter_not_a_map`, `invalid_config`, `missing_env_var`, `template_parse_error` or `template_render_error` |
| `data.message` | On failure, the operator message. It names a `$VAR` but never prints its value. |

stderr follows the same events without repeating a healthy state:

- a failed preflight logs on every tick: `level=error event=preflight outcome=failed error=<code> reason="<message>"`;
- a passing preflight logs nothing, unless it follows one or more failures, then it logs once: `level=info event=preflight outcome=completed skipped_ticks=<n>`.

### Loop config error codes (extension of 5.5, m1-03)

5.5 lists the workflow error classes, and 6.1 asks for typed values without naming their error. Loop adds two codes to the 5.5 list:

| Code | When | Example message |
|---|---|---|
| `invalid_config` | A typed value fails coercion or validation, see [validation rules](#config-validation-rules-clarification-of-53-and-61-m1-03) | `agent.max_turns must be a positive integer, got 0` |
| `missing_env_var` | A `$VAR` value names a variable that is unset or empty | `workspace.root uses $WS_ROOT, which is not set` |

Both behave like the spec's workflow file errors: they block dispatch until fixed (5.5), fail startup, and skip dispatch on a tick.

### `$VAR` resolution scope (restriction of 5.3.1 and 6.1, m1-03)

6.1 resolves `$VAR_NAME` in values that "explicitly contain" it, and limits expansion to local filesystem paths. Loop applies it narrowly:

- **Whole value only:** a value is resolved only when it is exactly `$NAME`, matching `^\$[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$`. A `$` inside a longer string, such as `$HOME/work`, is not expanded and stays literal. Loop does no token expansion inside strings.
- **Two fields only:** `workspace.root` (a core path) and `tracker.provider.dir` (the `files` adapter's path, see its [profile](plugins/tracker-files.md#configuration)). Other adapters document their own `$VAR` keys, secret keys included, when they arrive (the GitHub tracker in M2 is the first, see the [conformance test matrix](#conformance-test-matrix)).
- **Never in shell strings:** `runners.*.command`, `hooks.*` and `codex.command` are kept verbatim, `$` and `~` included.
- **Empty is missing:** an empty value fails like an unset one, with `missing_env_var`. 5.3.1 states this rule for secrets; Loop extends it to these path fields, since an empty path would silently resolve to the folder of `WORKFLOW.md`.
- **Order:** `$VAR` resolution comes before `~` expansion and path resolution, so a variable may hold `~/work` or a relative path.
- **Source:** the environment of the `loop` process, as given by the host. Every resolved reference is kept with its key path, so m1-11 can redact the values.

### Workspace root default (deviation from 5.3.3, m1-03)

5.3.3 defaults `workspace.root` to `<system-temp>/symphony_workspaces`. Loop's default is `<system-temp>/loop_workspaces`.

**Reason:** startup cleanup (8.6) removes workspaces of terminal Issues under the root. A shared default would let Loop and a real Symphony install on the same machine sweep each other's workspaces. An explicit `workspace.root` behaves as the spec says.

### Config validation rules (clarification of 5.3 and 6.1, m1-03)

- **Integers:** a quoted integer (`"30000"`) coerces like a plain one. These keys must be integers above 0, else preflight fails with `invalid_config` and the message gives the key and the value read:
  - `polling.interval_ms`
  - `hooks.timeout_ms`
  - `agent.max_concurrent_agents`
  - `agent.max_turns`
  - `agent.max_wake_turns` (m4-03, see [Wake turns](#wake-turns-extension-m4-03))
  - `agent.max_retry_backoff_ms`
  - `codex.turn_timeout_ms`
  - `codex.read_timeout_ms`
  - `budgets.per_attempt_tokens` (a positive integer, read as a long, the per Attempt token budget; `budgets` is a Loop top level extension section)
  - `budgets.per_issue_tokens` and `budgets.per_day_tokens` (m2-20, read as longs, see [Spend budgets](#spend-budgets-extension-m2-20))
  - `observability.retention_days` (m2-18, a Loop top level extension section, see [Attempt file retention](#attempt-file-retention-extension-m2-18))
- **Dollar keys (m2-20):** `budgets.per_attempt_usd`, `budgets.per_issue_usd` and `budgets.per_day_usd` are positive decimal numbers (`10`, `25.5`); zero, a negative number or text fails preflight with `invalid_config`: `budgets.per_day_usd must be a positive number of dollars, got 0`.
- **Non negative integer keys:** `budgets.token_threshold_every` (read as a long; `0` turns thresholds off, a negative or non numeric value is a config validation error).
- **`context` (m1-17):** `context.soft_limit`, `context.hard_limit` and `context.prompt_limit` are fractions above 0 and at most 1, with `prompt_limit <= soft_limit <= hard_limit`; `context.tool_output_tokens` and `context.max_rotations` are positive integers; `context.windows` is a map whose keys are `provider/model` (a provider without a slash, then a non empty model) and whose values are positive integers. Anything else fails preflight with `invalid_config`, naming the key and the value. See [prompt slot sizes](#context-config-and-prompt-slot-sizes-extension-m1-17).
- **`agent.env` (bl-01):** a map of environment variable names to literal string values. A value that is exactly `$NAME`, a key that is `PATH`, starts with `LOOP_` or is an Anthropic key, a key that is not an environment variable name and a value that is not a string fail preflight with `invalid_config`. See [`agent.env`](#agentenv-extension-bl-01).
- **Stall timeout:** `codex.stall_timeout_ms` of 0 or less is valid and disables stall detection (5.3.6, 8.5).
- **Per state concurrency:** in `agent.max_concurrent_agents_by_state`, state names are trimmed and lowercased, and entries that are not positive integers are ignored, not rejected (17.1).
- **Per tracker concurrency (m4-15):** `agent.max_concurrent_agents_by_tracker` is a map of tracker id to a positive integer. Unlike the per state caps, a key that is no tracker id of the workflow, a value below 1 or not an integer, and a key that is not a map fail preflight with `invalid_config`: `agent.max_concurrent_agents_by_tracker.gh names no tracker of this workflow, use one of local, side`. See [per tracker concurrency limit](#per-tracker-concurrency-limit-and-a-quieter-retry-requeue-extension-m4-15).
- **`codex.command`:** kept as the raw shell string, never expanded and not checked in M1, so `codex.command: ""` passes. The command of the selected runner is checked instead (see [preflight](#preflight-on-each-tick-clarification-of-63-m1-03)).
- **Defaults:** a missing optional key takes the spec default: `polling.interval_ms` 30000, `agent.max_concurrent_agents` 10, `agent.max_turns` 20, `agent.max_wake_turns` 30 (m4-03), `agent.max_retry_backoff_ms` 300000, `hooks.timeout_ms` 60000, `codex.stall_timeout_ms` 300000, `codex.turn_timeout_ms` 3600000, `codex.read_timeout_ms` 5000, `codex.command` `codex app-server`, `budgets.token_threshold_every` 10000000, `context.soft_limit` 0.20, `context.hard_limit` 0.21 (0.25 before m4-02, see [default limits and unknown windows](#default-limits-and-unknown-windows-extension-m4-02)), `context.prompt_limit` 0.05, `context.tool_output_tokens` 2000, `context.max_rotations` 5, `context.windows` `anthropic/claude-opus-5-5: 1000000` and, since m4-02, `openai/gpt-6.1-sol: 475000`, `observability.retention_days` 90. `budgets.per_attempt_tokens` has no default: absent means no ceiling (m1-38). No other spend budget has one either (m2-20): absent means no budget.
- **Hooks:** `hooks.*` are parsed and validated in m1-03 and run from m1-04. `hooks.on_token_threshold` (m1-38) and `hooks.on_budget_warning` (m2-20) are Loop's own optional hooks.

### Command runner shell (deviation from 10.1, m1-02)

The spec launches `codex.command` with `bash -lc`. Loop's command runner (`runner.kind: command`) launches `runners.command.command` with `sh -c`, with the workspace as the working directory. A login shell reads the user's profile, which can reset `PATH` and hide the `loop` launcher Loop puts first on it. Hooks keep `sh -lc` (see the hook shell row above).

### Runner configuration keys (extension, m1-02)

The runner is chosen by `runner.kind`, and each runner's settings live under `runners.<kind>`. An unknown `runner.kind` is a startup error. Since m3-04 the runner is a plugin selected by its kind, which reads its own section, see [runner plugins](#runner-plugins-extension-m3-04). The command runner, the only runner of the walking skeleton, takes one setting:

```yaml
runner:
  kind: command
runners:
  command:
    command: ./agent.sh
```

The spec's `codex` key keeps its meaning for the Codex app-server runner in M3.

### Session id for the command runner (clarification of 4.2, m1-02)

The spec composes `session_id` as `<thread_id>-<turn_id>`. A shell command has no thread or turn of its own, so the command runner mints both as UUIDs before it starts the process, passes them as `LOOP_SESSION_ID` and `LOOP_TURN_ID`, and carries them in its events. Each event, log line and trace record of the Attempt therefore has the same session and turn ids as the agent saw.

### Command runner continuation (extension, m1-06)

The command runner supports continuation turns, which the spec does not define for a shell command. Each turn re-invokes the command with the turn input on stdin. All turns of an Attempt share one `LOOP_THREAD_ID` (new, minted once per Attempt) and each turn gets a new `LOOP_TURN_ID` and a `LOOP_TURN_NUMBER` (new, 1-based). `LOOP_SESSION_ID` is `<thread_id>-<turn_id>`, so it changes every turn while the thread stays the same. `session_started` is emitted once per thread, and the runner emits `RunnerEvent.TurnStarted` for turns 2 to n (turn 1 is `SessionStarted`), while the orchestrator records a `turn_started` event for every turn.

### Claude Code runner configuration keys (extension, m1-05)

`runner.kind: claude-code` selects the Claude Code runner. Its settings live under `runners.claude-code`:

```yaml
runner:
  kind: claude-code
runners:
  claude-code:
    command: claude
    model: sonnet
    turn_timeout_ms: 3600000
    read_timeout_ms: 120000
    strict_mcp: true
```

| Key | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `command` | none, required | The Claude Code executable, for example `claude`. |
| `model` | none, Claude Code's own default | Passed as `--model` when set. |
| `turn_timeout_ms` | 3600000 (1 h) | Silence timeout: the turn fails when the agent writes no stdout line for this long during the turn. It does not run between turns (m3-07). |
| `read_timeout_ms` | 120000 (2 min) | Time to the first line: the turn fails when Claude Code writes no stdout line within this time once the turn's input is written. Until m3-07 it was the time to the `system` `init` event. |
| `strict_mcp` | `true` | Passes `--strict-mcp-config`, so Loop's MCP server is the agent's only one (bl-72, see [strict MCP](#strict-mcp-extension-bl-72)). |

Since m3-04 the section is read by the plugin, `ClaudeCodeSettings.of` in `plugins/runner-claude-code`, no longer by the loader (until then the sealed `RunnerSettings.ClaudeCode`). `runner.kind: claude-code` without `runners.claude-code.command` is rejected with `invalid_config`, so is a timeout that is not a positive integer or, since m3-05, over one day (its [profile](plugins/runner-claude-code.md#profile)), and an unknown `runner.kind` is still a startup error. The runner launches `<command> -p [--resume <id>] --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose --settings <file> --permission-mode bypassPermissions [--strict-mcp-config] [--mcp-config <file>] [--model <m>]` in the workspace, once per Attempt, and writes each turn's input on its stdin as a stream-json user message line (m3-07, see [Claude Code long lived process](#claude-code-long-lived-process-extension-of-103-m3-07)).

### Session and turn ids for the Claude Code runner (clarification of 4.2, m1-05)

As with the command runner, Loop mints the thread and turn ids as UUIDs before it spawns Claude Code, and sets `LOOP_SESSION_ID` and `LOOP_TURN_ID` in the agent environment, so `session_id` is `<thread_id>-<turn_id>`. Since m3-07 the process lives across turns, so its environment, and the MCP config of Loop's server, carry the ids of the turn that spawned it: a `loop tool` call made in a later turn records those ids, while Loop's own events of that turn carry the turn's. `session_started` is emitted once, and first: when Claude Code's `system` `init` event arrives, or right before `turn_failed` when the turn ends before init. The session id Claude Code reports in `init` is recorded as `runtime_session_id` in the `session_started` data, so a trace can be matched with Claude Code's own logs.

### Preflight for the Claude Code runner (extension, m1-05)

An unreadable managed settings file or an unknown uid is a startup warning, not an error.

Since bl-210 the preflight also checks the Claude Code version, as the Codex runner (bl-128) and the `acp` runner under `preset: agy` do, since a Claude Code release of another major may change the stream JSON format the runner reads:

- **The pinned version:** Claude Code 2.1.330, major `2`, the release installed on 2026-11-25. `claude --version` prints `2.1.330 (Claude Code)`. The version is `ClaudeCodeSettings.PINNED_VERSION` in `plugins/runner-claude-code`, a constant like the agy pin, since no build step reads it.
- **The check:** `loop run` runs `<runners.claude-code.command> --version` at startup, with the host env, its stdin closed and a 10 second limit, and reads the first `<major>.<minor>.<patch>` of its stdout and stderr. Only the major is compared, so a newer minor or patch release starts.
- **Another major:** a startup error, `loop run: Claude Code 3.0.1 is not of the pinned major version 2 (pinned 2.1.330).`, and no attempt runs.
- **No readable version:** a startup warning, and the run goes on. That covers an output with no version, a nonzero exit, a command that cannot start and one that does not end within 10 seconds, for example `could not read a version in the output of claude --version, so the claude-code runner did not check that Claude Code is of the pinned major version 2 (pinned 2.1.330)`. A command that cannot start still fails each attempt with `agent_not_found`, as before.
- **The record:** the version read, of the pinned major or not, is the `runner_version` field of the operator log line `event=run outcome=started`.
- **Tests:** the `bl-210` tests of `plugins/runner-claude-code/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/claudecode/ClaudeCodePreflightTest.kt` (a matching version, another major, an unreadable output, a nonzero exit, a command that cannot start, the real probe) and, end to end with a fake `claude`, of `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ClaudeCodeRunCommandTest.kt` (another major stops `loop run` before any attempt, the pinned major runs with `runner_version` on the run started line).

### `usage` event and token accounting (extension of 13.5, m1-05)

Runners report token usage with a `usage` event, recorded with these fields:

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `input_tokens` | Input tokens. |
| `output_tokens` | Output tokens. |
| `cache_read_input_tokens` | Input tokens read from the prompt cache. |
| `cache_creation_input_tokens` | Input tokens written to the prompt cache. |
| `total_cost_usd` | Cost as reported by the runtime, when it reports one. |
| `attempt_tokens` | The Attempt's token total so far: input + output + cache read + cache creation. |
| `max_tokens` | The per Attempt token budget (`budgets.per_attempt_tokens`), absent when none is set. |

The `budget_reached` event (m2-20, `token_cap_reached` before) is recorded once when a budget is passed, for example `attempt_tokens` above `max_tokens`. Its data are `budget`, `used` and `limit`.
