The turn is cut at the event after the breaching usage, so the runner's real terminal event is still recorded, and the turn events of that turn carry `capped` = `true`.

Accounting rule: the values are absolute totals for the Attempt's agent session so far. A new report replaces the last one, it is never added to it. Since m1-06 an Attempt can run several turns (see the next section), so the session sums every `result.usage` and every `usage` event is absolute for the session so far. Since m3-07 the turns share one long lived process and each turn ends a segment with its own `result` line, summed the same way. Claude Code's `result.usage` excludes the tokens of its subagents, so before bl-71 Loop did not count them; since bl-71 each `result` line's tokens come from its `model_usage_since_last_result`, which counts them, see [Claude Code subagent spend](#claude-code-subagent-spend-extension-bl-71).

Since m2-19 (bl-25), one invocation can print several `result` lines: an agent that starts a background task (a build with `run_in_background`) ends a segment with a `result` line, is woken by the task's notification and ends the next segment with another. Each line's `usage` is its own segment's, so the invocation's usage is the sum of all its `result` lines; the last line still decides the turn. `total_cost_usd` is not summed: Claude Code reports the session's cost so far, across the lines of one invocation and across `--resume` invocations alike, so the latest one is the session's cost. A probe with a real `claude -p` showed both (usage 106,260 then 55,752 tokens, cost 0.00700 then 0.00764, and 0.00841 after a `--resume`). Before m2-19 only the last line counted, so a woken turn reported its last segment only (m2-22: 1.95M recorded, 13.6M used), and the runner added the costs of its invocations, counting the earlier ones twice. Since bl-37 a turn or response timeout after at least one `result` line with usage emits that sum, on top of the earlier turns, as a `usage` event before the `TurnFailed`; a timeout with no such line emits none. Since m3-07 a turn ends at its first `result` line, so the woken segment of a background task comes while the process is idle: the next turn reads it first and adds its usage, and a failure of that turn still reports it. A segment that comes after the last turn is not counted, see [Claude Code long lived process](#claude-code-long-lived-process-extension-of-103-m3-07).

Since bl-108 the runner emits that `usage` event as soon as it reads each `result` line with usage, right after the line: the session total so far, this invocation's lines summed on top of the earlier turns, with the latest `total_cost_usd`. The end of the turn, an exit or a timeout, emits no second one. A stall (`codex.stall_timeout_ms`) and a `loop stop` are decided outside the runner, which cancels the runner's flow, and a cancelled flow can emit nothing more: before bl-108 the usage of the `result` lines already read was lost, so the attempt row and its ledger line held null tokens and no cost (11 stalled attempts of 2026-11-15 spent $35.73, $34.96 of it recorded as nothing). Now a stalled or stopped turn that read at least one `result` line with usage records it exactly as a timed out turn does since bl-37: on the attempt row, the turn's ledger line (a stopped turn has none, as before), `loop status`, `loop trace` and every spend budget. With no such line it records no usage, as before. The session keeps that total for its later turns however the turn ended. A turn woken by a background task now emits one `usage` event per `result` line; each replaces the last, so the turn counts once, and a budget passed by the first segment cuts the turn at the next event instead of at its end. The contract is unchanged: [`RunnerEvent.Usage`](#usage-event-and-token-accounting-extension-of-135-m1-05) was already absolute and allowed several per turn. Tests: the `bl-108` tests of `plugins/runner-claude-code/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/claudecode/ClaudeCodeRunnerTest.kt` (the event right after its line, a cancel that keeps it, the next turn adding to it, no usage before any `result` line) and, end to end with a fake `claude`, `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ClaudeCodeStalledUsageTest.kt` (a stall, a `loop stop` through the installed launcher, a stall with no `result` line, a per Attempt dollar budget).

### Token thresholds (extension, m1-38)

A threshold reports, it never stops. Every `budgets.token_threshold_every` tokens of the Attempt's total (the m1-08 measure, cache reads included), Loop records one `token_threshold` event per step crossed: 10M, 20M, 30M and so on for the default of 10,000,000. `0` turns thresholds off.

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `tokens` | The Attempt's total when the step was crossed. |
| `threshold` | The step crossed. |
| `cost_usd` | The spend so far, the `total_cost_usd` of the latest real usage report; absent before any report gave one. Cache reads are most of the tokens but cost a tenth as much, so the spend is the better gauge. |
| `estimated` | `true` when the total came from the mid turn estimate; absent for a real usage report. |

The event carries the Issue and the Attempt ids. A usage report that jumps several steps records each step once, in order (9M to 31M records 10M, 20M and 30M). A step is never recorded twice for one Attempt: Loop keeps the highest step recorded, so a real total that comes in below an earlier estimate and climbs again records nothing new, and when an Attempt starts Loop reads the highest step the store already holds for its id, so a restart does not record it again. One report records at most 100 steps; the rest come with the next report. Each event is logged as `level=info event=token_threshold outcome=reached` with the same fields.

`loop trace` prints each event in the Attempt's timeline as `token_threshold  threshold=<step> tokens=<total> estimated=<true|false> cost_usd=<spend>` (m1-41). `estimated=false` stands for the absent key of a real usage report, and `cost_usd` is left out when the event has none, never printed as 0, since Loop keeps no price table.

- **Hook:** `hooks.on_token_threshold`, an optional shell command, runs on the host in the workspace once per event, like the other hooks, with `hooks.timeout_ms` and the Attempt's hook environment plus `LOOP_ISSUE` (the Issue identifier), `LOOP_ATTEMPT`, `LOOP_TOKENS`, `LOOP_THRESHOLD` and `LOOP_COST_USD` (empty while no cost is known). Its exit code and output are recorded as `hook_finished` with `hook` = `on_token_threshold`. A failing or timed out hook is logged at WARN and never fails or stops the Attempt. It runs inside the turn's event loop, so the runner's events wait for it, for at most `hooks.timeout_ms`.
- **Mid turn estimate:** Claude Code reports usage only in the `result` line at the end of a turn, so a long turn could cross 10M with nothing said until it ends. For thresholds only, the Claude Code runner emits a usage estimate after each main session `assistant` line that carries usage: the real totals of the earlier turns plus the sum of this turn's per message usage, one entry per message id (Claude Code writes one line per content block, a later line of a message replaces the earlier one). Subagent lines (with a `parent_tool_use_id`) and lines without a message id are left out. Since bl-71 the `result` line's tokens count the subagents, so on a turn that delegates the estimate can be well below the real total that replaces it. The `result` line's usage replaces the estimate at turn end. The estimate is not recorded as a `usage` event, never meets the ceiling and never parks anything.
- **Ceiling:** `budgets.per_attempt_tokens` stays as an opt in ceiling with no default, checked only on real usage reports, the per Attempt token budget since m2-20. See [the retry cap](#5-retry-cap-retriesmax_attempts) and [Spend budgets](#spend-budgets-extension-m2-20).
- **Out of scope:** the M2 spend budgets in dollars (story 84, landed in m2-20); applying a capped turn's queued requests (m1-37), still needed when a ceiling is set; the human readable log (m1-39), which prints the threshold lines.

### Spend budgets (extension, m2-20)

Story 84 and PLAN.md 6.8: spend budgets per Attempt, per Issue and per rolling 24 hours, in tokens and in dollars, read from the ledger. Every budget is opt in, with no default, for the reason m1-38 gave for tokens: nobody can know in advance what an Issue needs, and a default would park normal work.

| Key | Measures |
|---|---|
| `budgets.per_attempt_tokens` | The Attempt's tokens, the m1-08 total (input, output, cache read and cache creation). The token ceiling of m1-08, folded in. |
| `budgets.per_attempt_usd` | The Attempt's dollars, the latest `total_cost_usd` the runner reported. |
| `budgets.per_issue_tokens`, `budgets.per_issue_usd` | Every Attempt of the Issue: the ledger rows of its finished Attempts in the store, plus the running one. |
| `budgets.per_day_tokens`, `budgets.per_day_usd` | Every Attempt of the Project in the last 24 hours, rolling: the ledger rows of the Project whose Attempt finished after now minus 24 hours, plus every Attempt running in this process. |

- **Counting:** tokens and dollars are those of the ledger (m2-19): latest absolute totals, never summed twice. With Claude Code both include the subagents: `total_cost_usd` always did, the tokens since bl-71, see [Claude Code subagent spend](#claude-code-subagent-spend-extension-bl-71). A runner that reports no cost spends nothing against a dollar budget, since Loop keeps no price table. Since m3-21 `loop run` warns at startup when its runner reports no cost and the workflow sets a dollar budget, see [Codex runner usage, rate limits and cost](#codex-runner-usage-rate-limits-and-cost-m3-21). An Attempt running in this process is counted from memory until its row is written, then from its row, never both. The store keeps the rows, so the Issue and day budgets count the Attempts of earlier processes after any restart, with or without a Run id. One Loop process per Project (ADR 0001) means the running Attempts of the Project are all in this process. The machine wide daily budget across Projects is story 85, see [Machine limits](#machine-limits-extension-m3-16).
- **When:** on every real usage report, never on the mid turn estimate, and once before the first turn, so an Issue or a day already past its budget parks without spending a turn. With Claude Code usage arrives once per turn, so a turn can overshoot a budget before it is checked, as with the token ceiling; accepted at triage.
- **Warning:** when a budget's spend reaches 80 percent of its limit, Loop records `budget_warning` with `budget` (the key without `budgets.`), `used` and `limit`, logs `level=warn event=budget_warning outcome=reached` with the same fields, writes a human log line and runs `hooks.on_budget_warning`. Once per budget and Attempt: a later Attempt of the same Issue warns again when it starts above 80 percent. A report that jumps past 100 percent warns too, then breaches.
- **Hook:** `hooks.on_budget_warning`, an optional shell command, runs on the host in the workspace like `hooks.on_token_threshold`, with `hooks.timeout_ms` and the Attempt's hook environment plus `LOOP_ISSUE`, `LOOP_ATTEMPT`, `LOOP_BUDGET`, `LOOP_BUDGET_USED` and `LOOP_BUDGET_LIMIT`. Recorded as `hook_finished` with `hook` = `on_budget_warning`; a failing hook is logged and never fails or stops the Attempt.
- **Breach:** a budget is passed when its spend is greater than its limit, so exactly the limit is allowed (the m1-08 rule). The first budget passed records `budget_reached` (same data), logged at WARN, the turn is cut like the token ceiling's (`capped` = `true`), and the Attempt ends as a failure with the reason `budget`. The Issue is Parked with the reason `budget`; `issue_parked` carries `budget`, `used` and `limit`. Like the token ceiling it is not a failure: no `max_attempts` slot, no retry, and the counters reset. `loop trace` prints `budget=<key> used=<used>/<limit>` on the three events, `loop status` prints `reason=budget`, and `loop resume` or a tracker change unparks it.
- **After a park:** an Issue parked on its Issue budget parks again at its next Attempt's start while its spend stays above the limit: raise the budget before `loop resume`. An Issue parked on a day budget can be resumed once the window has room again.
- **Values:** tokens are printed as integers, dollars as plain decimals without trailing zeros (`10`, `8.5`).
- **One reading (bl-84):** the Attempt and the auto merge gate (m2-28) read the spend through one reader, `SpendReading` beside `BudgetMeter`: per scope, the Issue and the day, the ledger part and the live part apart, only the scopes whose budget is set. It reads the running Attempts before the ledger and leaves out the rows of those still running, so an Attempt whose row is written before it leaves the running set is counted once.
- **Read failure:** a ledger read that fails is logged as `event=budget outcome=failed` and the budgets keep the spend read last (none before the first read); it never fails the Attempt. The gate instead falls back, see [conditional auto merge](#conditional-auto-merge-and-merge-risk-extension-m2-28).
- **Known limit:** an Attempt killed without closing (SIGKILL, power loss) writes no ledger row, so its spend is not counted after the restart. Its turns are in the ledger file, `<LOOP_HOME>/ledger/<project_id>.jsonl`, but the budgets read the store.
- **Tests:** `BudgetMeterTest` (80 percent once per budget, the limit rule), `SpendReadingTest` (the ledger and live parts, counted once, the scopes asked, a failed read), `OrchestratorBudgetTest` (dollars, the Issue rows, the rolling day across restarts with a controlled clock, the running Attempts, the start check, the hook, a failed read), `OrchestratorTokenCapTest` (the per Attempt token budget), `SqliteLedgerTest` (the rows since an instant), `HumanLogTest`, `TraceCommandTest` and `ClaudeCodeRunCommandTest` (a dollar budget park end to end).

### Machine limits (extension, m3-16)

Story 85 and ADR 0001, decided on 2026-11-14 (carrying m2-21). Several `loop run` processes on one machine, one per Project, stay within one machine wide concurrency cap and one machine wide daily budget, coordinated through transactions in the shared store, never through a host process.

**Where.** The limits live in a machine level config, `<LOOP_HOME>/config.yaml`, never in a Project `WORKFLOW.md`, since no Project owns the machine:

```yaml
machine:
  max_concurrent_agents: 3      # agents and merge checks at once, every Project together
  per_day_usd: 25               # dollars of the last 24 hours, every Project together
  per_day_tokens: 50000000      # tokens of the last 24 hours, every Project together
```

- **Opt in:** no file means no machine limit and no slot row, Loop behaves as before m3-16. A key left out is no limit for it.
- **Read every tick,** with the preflight, so an edit counts from the next tick without a restart. `loop run` also reads it at startup.
- **Values:** `max_concurrent_agents` and `per_day_tokens` are positive integers, `per_day_usd` a positive number of dollars. A malformed file fails the preflight with `invalid_config`, the message naming the file and the key, for example `<LOOP_HOME>/config.yaml: machine.max_concurrent_agents must be a positive integer, got 0`, and no dispatch runs until it is fixed; at startup `loop run` exits with that message. An unknown key under `machine:` fails the same way, so a typo never lifts a limit silently; other top level sections are ignored. A malformed machine config is not a rejected `WORKFLOW.md`: no `config_reload_failed` is recorded and a valid `WORKFLOW.md` edit is still applied.

**Machine slots.** With `max_concurrent_agents` set, each launch the slot plan (bl-86, see [Merge check and merge](#merge-check-and-merge-extension-m2-26)) proposes claims a machine slot before it starts, so the Project's own `agent.max_concurrent_agents` and per state caps decide first and the machine slot adds no capacity rule to the plan: one row of the `machine_slots` table (schema version 7) written in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction that first reads every slot of the machine, so of two processes wanting the last slot exactly one gets it. The row names its Project, a slot id, the Run id and the pid of the `loop run` process, and is deleted when the Attempt ends, whatever its end.

- **No slot:** the claim fails the plan's launch, so a dispatch that cannot claim one is skipped with the reason `no_machine_slot` (`candidate_skipped`), and a due retry is requeued with the error `no available machine slots`, like the orchestrator slot rule. A store failure on the claim is a skip too, logged as `event=machine_slot outcome=failed`: the cap holds even when the store does not answer. A failed release is logged the same way and retried on the next tick, before its preflight, so a malformed `config.yaml` does not keep it held (bl-101).
- **Runner hold:** while the [runner unavailable hold](#runner-unavailable-hold-extension-bl-39) waits for its probe, the launches after the probe wait with `runner_unavailable` before they claim a machine slot, and the probe starts only once its own machine slot is held; a refused claim leaves the probe to the next launch or tick. Since bl-101 the hold is read again after each claim, which can wait on the store: when an Attempt that ended meanwhile started the hold, the slot just claimed is released and the launch waits with `runner_unavailable`, like the ones after it.
- **Cancelled launch (bl-101):** a claim cancelled midway releases its slot, since the store may have written it before the cancel, and an Attempt launched on a tick cancelled since its claim starts anyway, runs nothing and releases its slot and its claim, like a merge check's job (bl-180).
- **Skip order (bl-101):** the skips of a tick are recorded in slot order, the `runner_unavailable` ones of the launches among the `no_available_slots` ones of the plan.
- **The merge check takes a slot** (m2-26) like an Attempt, claimed once step one of the slot plan lets it start and released when the check ends; with none free it waits for a later tick. Since bl-173 a delivery's merge phase, which needs no agent slot, claims a machine slot only when its approval is the auto policy's own, since only the auto merge gate runs a model session, the merge risk reviewer; a merge phase approved by a human claims none.
- **Liveness, no heartbeat:** only slots whose Project's run file names a live process count. A slot counts while that run file names the slot's Run id and pid, and the process is alive and started when the run file says (the m1-09 guard against pid reuse). A crashed run's slots stop counting at once and are deleted by the next claim; a run file Loop cannot read counts, so a half written one never lifts the cap. Since bl-101 a claim reads each run file once, with no wait for a second read, since it reads under the store's write lock, and logs `level=warn event=machine_slot outcome=failed project=<id>` once for an unreadable one, again only after a claim read it. A `loop run` never reads its own run file for this: closing a second descriptor of a file drops the process's POSIX lock on it, and `loop stop` would then take the live run for a stale one. It counts its own Project's slots when they are of its own run, the only live run of that Project.
- **Not a count of claims:** claims are held through waits and queued retries, so the slot is its own row, and the `claims` table gains no state column.

**Machine day budget.** With `per_day_usd` or `per_day_tokens` set, each tick sums what the Attempts of every Project that finished in the last 24 hours spent (their ledger rows in the store) and what the Attempts running in this process spent so far, the live part read before the ledger and the rows of those still running left out, like the [one reading](#spend-budgets-extension-m2-20) of bl-84. Once the sum is above a limit (exactly the limit is allowed, like the Project budgets), every new dispatch of every Project is skipped with the reason `machine_budget`, and a due retry is requeued with the error `machine_budget`. It logs `level=warn event=machine_budget outcome=reached` with `budget`, `used` and `limit` once, and `outcome=released` when it lifts.

- **Lifts by itself** once the 24 hour window has room, with no human step. It parks nothing and cuts no running Attempt; a merge check is not held by it.
- **Accepted overshoot:** a ledger row is written when its Attempt ends, so the live spend of other processes' running Attempts is not in the store. Several processes can each dispatch on a sum that leaves their neighbours' running Attempts out, and the machine can end the day above its budget. Accepted at triage.
- **`per_day_tokens` undercounted** Claude Code subagents before bl-71. Rows written before it keep their main session tokens until they leave the window, see [Claude Code subagent spend](#claude-code-subagent-spend-extension-bl-71).
- **`per_day_usd` is blind** to a runner that reports no cost, the command runner among them: Loop keeps no price table, so such Attempts spend nothing against it.
- **Read failure:** a failed read is logged as `event=machine_budget outcome=failed` and the budget keeps the sum read last. Before a first read succeeded it holds every new dispatch with `machine_budget` (bl-101), so it fails closed like the slot claim.
- **No index on `finished_at`:** the read scans the `attempts` table, one row per finished Attempt, which stays cheap at the sizes one machine reaches. An index needs a schema version, and each one makes an older build refuse the store (see [self hosting](self-hosting.md#known-limits)), so it waits for the next schema change (bl-101).

**`loop status`.** With a machine config, `loop status` prints two lines after the run line: `Machine slots 1 of 2 in use` (or `Machine slots 1 in use, no cap`), counting the live slots of every Project, and `Machine day spend 1200 tokens of 50000000, 0.5 usd of 25`, the finished Attempts of every Project in the last 24 hours. A malformed config adds `Machine config fails, no dispatch until it is fixed: <message>`. Without a machine config it prints no machine line.

**Out of scope (backlog):** an 80 percent hook for the machine budget and a per state machine cap.

**Tests:** `MachineConfigTest` (keys, values, unknown keys, no file), `SqliteMachineSlotsTest` (the cap, dead slots, two writers racing for the last slot, the migration, every Project's rows), `MachineLimitsOrchestratorTest` (the slot before the launch and its release, the skip, two Projects under one cap, the day budget in dollars and tokens across Projects, no park, no cut, the lift, the preflight failure, the requeued retry, and since bl-101 the release retried under a malformed config, the cancelled launch, the failed first budget read, the hold started during a claim), `MachineGateTest` (a cancelled claim), `MergeCheckOrchestratorTest` (the merge check slot), `MachineSlotLivenessTest` (a dead process, a reused pid, an earlier run), `MachineLimitsCommandTest` (two `loop run` processes under a cap of one, the startup failure) and `StatusCommandTest` (the machine lines).

### Runner error categories (extension of 10.6, m1-05)

Runner failures carry a runner neutral category, named after a 10.6 category where one exists. The reason is `<category>` or `<category>: <detail>`, and the orchestrator records it as the turn's failure. It falls back to `exit_code=N` only when a runner gives no reason.

| Category | When |
|---|---|
| `agent_not_found` | The agent executable cannot be found or started (the spec's `codex_not_found`). The reason is `agent_not_found: <command>: <message>`. |
| `invalid_workspace_cwd` | The workspace path is not a directory. |
| `response_timeout` | No `init` event within `read_timeout_ms`. |
| `turn_timeout` | No stdout line for `turn_timeout_ms`. The process group is killed. |
| `port_exit` | The process exited without a `result`. The reason is `port_exit: exit code N, no result line`. |
| `turn_failed` | Claude Code reported an error `result`. The subtype is in the event data. With `is_error` true the reason is `turn_failed: <first line of Claude's result text>`, falling back to `turn_failed: <subtype>`. An error `result` whose only permission denials are deny-listed commands is also `turn_failed: <subtype>`. |
| `turn_input_required` | The turn ended after a denial that is not a policy denial, a tool or command that is not deny-listed (see the user input requests row). |

A canceled turn keeps the orchestrator's existing cancel path.

Since m1-23 the command runner uses the same categories. A spawn failure is `agent_not_found: <command>: <message>`. A nonzero exit is `turn_failed: exit code N`, because the exit code is that runner's result protocol, so a nonzero code is an error result and not a `port_exit`. `exit_code=N` remains only as the orchestrator fallback for a runner that gives no reason.

### Agent environment (extension, m1-05, m1-23 and bl-01)

The Claude Code runner and the command runner share one policy. The agent gets the host environment minus these names, always:

- every `$VAR` name referenced in `WORKFLOW.md`: the `$VAR` references the loader expands, plus any front matter value, at any depth, that is exactly `$NAME`;
- `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, so Claude Code runs on the user's own Claude Code login, never on API billing. M1 has no opt in for API billing.

The names of `agent.env` are then added (see [`agent.env`](#agentenv-extension-bl-01) below), and the Attempt's own variables on top of them: `PATH` with the `loop` launcher folder first, the `LOOP_*` ids and `LOOP_TOOL_CONFIG` (see [Attempt directory and tool config snapshot](#attempt-directory-and-tool-config-snapshot-extension-m1-23)).

The one exception: `HOME`, `PATH`, `USER`, `LOGNAME`, `SHELL`, `TMPDIR`, `LANG`, `TERM` and every `LC_*` name are kept even when `WORKFLOW.md` references them. Paths are not secrets, and the agent needs them for its login, the Keychain, temp files and the locale. There is no exception for `XDG_*` or `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, no value heuristic and no startup warning.

Stripping every reference is possible because `loop tool` no longer reads `WORKFLOW.md` inside the agent: it reads the Attempt's tool config snapshot, and its tracker writes are applied host side (see [Host side tracker writes](#host-side-tracker-writes-extension-m1-23)). A stripped name is not by that a [secret](#secret-redaction-extension-of-153-and-131-m1-11): a path such as `workspace.root` is stripped from the agent environment and still not redacted.

The removed name set is computed for each Attempt from the workflow it starts with, so a [workflow reload](#workflow-reload-clarification-of-62-m1-15) that adds a `$VAR` keeps it out of the next Attempt's env (m1-15).

Hooks are unchanged: they get the host environment plus the `LOOP_*` variables, because SPEC 15.4 trusts them.

#### `agent.env` (extension, bl-01)

`agent.env` is an optional map in the `agent` section of `WORKFLOW.md`. Each entry adds one variable to the agent environment of every Attempt, for both runners:

```yaml
agent:
  env:
    GRADLE_OPTS: "-Dorg.gradle.daemon.registry.base={workspace}/.scratch/gradle-daemons"
```

- **Literal values:** a value is used as written. The one placeholder, `{workspace}`, is replaced by the Attempt's workspace path, every time it appears, when the runner builds the env of a turn. Nothing else is expanded: `$HOME/x` or `{other}` reach the agent as written.
- **No host values:** a value that is exactly `$NAME` is a config error at load time (`agent.env.TOKEN must be a literal value, $NAME would read the host variable NAME`), set or not. So `agent.env` can never carry a host variable, a secret included, to the agent, and the policy above, every `$VAR` stripped, still holds.
- **Loop's own names win:** a key Loop sets itself, `PATH` or any `LOOP_*` name such as `LOOP_TOOL_CONFIG`, is a config error (`agent.env.PATH is set by Loop and cannot be overridden`). So are `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, which the agent never gets (`agent.env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is never passed to the agent`).
- **Shape:** `agent.env` must be a map, each key an environment variable name (`[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*`) and each value a string. A number or a boolean is kept as its text, an empty string is a valid value, a list, a map or a missing value is a config error. Every error is `invalid_config` and fails preflight like any other.
- **Precedence:** an `agent.env` name replaces the host value of the same name, and is added even when `WORKFLOW.md` references that name elsewhere, since its value is literal.
- **Hooks:** hooks do not get `agent.env`.
- **When it applies:** an Attempt keeps the `agent.env` of the workflow it started with, like the rest of its runner settings.

Core knows nothing of what the variables mean. Loop's own `WORKFLOW.md` uses it to give each Attempt its own Gradle daemon pool, see [`self-hosting.md`](self-hosting.md#gradle-daemons-per-attempt).

### Secret redaction (extension of 15.3 and 13.1, m1-11)

Loop replaces every secret with a marker before it writes anything. A secret is one of:

- the host environment value of a value that is exactly `$NAME`, at any depth under `tracker.provider`, other than the `dir` path key. This is the same credential rule that decides which values the [tool config snapshot](#attempt-directory-and-tool-config-snapshot-extension-m1-23) omits;
- the values of `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`;
- a value matching a token pattern: GitHub (`ghp_`, `gho_`, `ghu_`, `ghs_`, `ghr_` and `github_pat_`), Anthropic (`sk-ant-`) and OpenAI (`sk-` and `sk-proj-`).

Path values, such as `workspace.root` and `tracker.provider.dir`, are not secrets and stay visible.

| Marker | When |
|---|---|
| `[redacted:NAME]` | A known value, named after its variable, for example `[redacted:LOOP_TRACKER_TOKEN]`. Two names with one value use the first name in sort order. |
| `[redacted:github-token]` | A GitHub token pattern hit. |
| `[redacted:anthropic-key]` | An Anthropic key pattern hit. |
| `[redacted:openai-key]` | An OpenAI key pattern hit. |
| `[redacted]` | Redaction itself failed. The whole input is replaced. |

A marker never holds any part of the value. Values under 4 characters are not redacted, because they would match too much text. `loop run` warns once per name, for example `loop run: warning: $LOOP_TRACKER_TOKEN is under 4 characters, so Loop does not redact it.` The warning is checked at startup, and on each [workflow reload](#workflow-reload-clarification-of-62-m1-15) for a short reference the reload adds, logged as `level=warn event=workflow_reload` (m1-15).

Token patterns also match right after a JSON escape such as `\n` in raw stream-json lines. A known value containing `"` or `\` is also matched in its JSON escaped form.

One reusable `Redactor` in `plugin-api` serves every write point, and m1-22's attempt file writer calls it too:

- the store, for `record` and parked issues;
- the operator log;
- the human log (m1-39), each line as a whole;
- the orchestrator, before each truncation, so no fragment of a value survives a cut: runner output (the 2000 character preview of the event, while the Attempt file gets the whole redacted line, m2-18), hook command (200 characters) and log reason (200 characters);
- hook output. When the tail is truncated, its partial first line is dropped and `hook_finished` carries `output_truncated=true`;
- CLI warnings and failure lines, internal errors included.

Redaction happens before writing, never on read. The one exception is a queued comment, which sits in its request file until Loop applies it (see [Host side tracker writes](#host-side-tracker-writes-extension-m1-23)). The redactor never throws (Symphony 17.6): on an internal failure it returns `[redacted]`, never the raw input.

The known set grows during a run and never forgets a value. `loop tool` runs in the agent environment, where no credential is reachable, and holds none, so its redactor uses the token patterns only. Loop redacts a comment again, with the full redactor, when it applies it host side.

The store takes one Project's redactor. A process serving several Projects would need the union of their secrets or a redactor per orchestrator as a decorator ([ADR 0001](adr/0001-one-process-per-project-shared-machine-store.md)).

One output is not routed through the redactor: the launcher's warn lines, which print only pids and durations.

### Attempt directory and tool config snapshot (extension, m1-23)

Each Attempt gets a directory outside its workspace: `<LOOP_HOME>/attempts/<project_id>/<attempt_id>/`. The orchestrator creates it with mode 0700 before the `before_run` hook, so hooks and the agent see a valid `LOOP_TOOL_CONFIG`, and deletes it when the Attempt ends, without following symlinks. The parent folder is shared with the `<attempt_id>.jsonl.gz` Attempt files (m1-13, see below), which do not collide with the directories.

The directory holds `tool-config.json`, the tool config snapshot. Its fields:

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `version` | Snapshot format, `1`. A `loop tool` that reads another version exits 4. |
| `project_id` | The Project's id. |
| `workflow_dir` | The folder of `WORKFLOW.md`, used to open the store and derive the Project. |
| `tracker_kind` | The `tracker.kind` value. |
| `defined_states` | The tracker's defined states, checked by `loop tool move`. |
| `tool_output_tokens` | Since m3-14, `context.tool_output_tokens`, which `loop tool mcp` trims its replies to. Optional: a snapshot without it reads with the default, 2000. See [Prompt slots filled and tool outputs trimmed](#prompt-slots-filled-and-tool-outputs-trimmed-extension-m3-14). |
| `provider` | The non credential `tracker.provider` fields, with `dir` resolved to an absolute path. |

A credential is a value exactly `$NAME` at any depth under `tracker.provider`, other than the `dir` path key. It is omitted, never replaced with a placeholder. A literal token written in `WORKFLOW.md` is copied as is: it is already readable in the checkout, and SPEC 10.5 says literal credentials should not be used when isolation matters.

Loop sets `LOOP_TOOL_CONFIG` to the snapshot's absolute path. `loop tool` reads only the snapshot and the `LOOP_*` ids, never `WORKFLOW.md`, and builds no tracker. Writing the snapshot from the effective workflow at the start of each Attempt meets SPEC 10.5's "bound to one session snapshot": a workflow reload applies to the next Attempt.

### Attempt file and `loop show` (extension, m1-13)

Each Attempt also gets an Attempt file beside its directory, never inside it: `<LOOP_HOME>/attempts/<project_id>/<attempt_id>.jsonl.gz`. It is kept when the Attempt ends, and the start of run sweep deletes directories only. One json object per line, in the shape m1-22's triage set: `type`, `seq` (from 1, per file), `ts`, then the fields of the type. Every append writes one complete gzip member, so the file is a series of concatenated members and a record is whole on disk once written. The text is redacted with the run's redactor before it is written, never on read. The file is created with mode 0600 and never opened through a symlink. There is no store schema change. Retention is set by `observability.retention_days` (m2-18, see [Attempt file retention](#attempt-file-retention-extension-m2-18)).

Three types exist:

- `prompt`: `{"type":"prompt","seq":1,"ts":"...","turn":1,"text":"..."}`, the turn 1 rendered prompt, appended right after the render, before the `BuildingPrompt` phase is recorded. Continuation turns get guidance, not a rendered prompt, and are not recorded. A failed write records `prompt_record_failed` with a redacted `reason` and logs a warning; it never fails the Attempt.
- `runner_output` (m2-18, carried from m1-22): `{"type":"runner_output","seq":2,"ts":"...","stream":"stdout","line":"..."}`, one per runner line, the whole line after redaction, appended before its event is recorded. The `runner_output` event in the store keeps a preview: `stream`, `line` (the first 2000 characters of the redacted line), `chars` (the length of the whole redacted line), `truncated` = `true` (present only when the preview is cut, left out otherwise) and `record_seq` (the `seq` of the record, present only when it was written). The first failed write of an Attempt records `runner_output_record_failed` with a redacted `reason` and logs a warning, `event=runner_output_record outcome=failed`; a later failure of the same Attempt only leaves `record_seq` out, so a full disk cannot flood the store. It never fails the Attempt.
- `tool_output` (m3-14): `{"type":"tool_output","seq":9,"ts":"...","tool":"move","text":"..."}`, the full text of a reply of Loop's MCP server that reached the agent trimmed to `context.tool_output_tokens`, redacted. `loop tool mcp` saves it in the `tool-outputs` folder of the attempt directory, and the orchestrator, the one writer of the file, appends it at the next drain or when the Attempt ends, then deletes the saved file. See [Prompt slots filled and tool outputs trimmed](#prompt-slots-filled-and-tool-outputs-trimmed-extension-m3-14).

A reader skips a type it does not know, and a `runner_output` record of a stream it does not know.

A cut or unreadable record never makes the whole file unreadable (m2-18). A kill or a full disk in the middle of an append leaves a cut gzip member at the end, a later append can follow it, and `loop show` on a live Attempt can read a member being written. The reader decodes the file member by member and checks each member's CRC32 and size. A member that is cut or does not check out is skipped, and reading goes on at the next gzip header after it. A whole member whose text is not a record of a known shape is skipped too. Each skipped part is counted, and the records around it read as usual. A writer that opens an existing file takes the highest `seq` it reads plus 1, so a skipped record never makes a `seq` repeat. A file that does not start with a gzip header is not an Attempt file and is still a failed read; an empty file has no records.

`loop show <attempt>` takes the full Attempt id or a unique prefix, such as the short id `loop trace` prints, and one view per call:

- `--prompt` prints the text of the first `prompt` record, as written. An Attempt with no record (recorded before m1-13, or its write failed) prints `No prompt recorded for this attempt.`
- `--events` prints every store event of the Attempt, one json object per line, in recording order. A `runner_output` event with `truncated` prints the whole line of the record its `record_seq` names, without `truncated` (m2-18). When that record is gone, pruned, never written, cut or unreadable, it prints as stored, with `truncated`, so the flag always says whether the printed line is cut. The Attempt file is read only when an event was cut.
- `--cost` prints the latest absolute `usage` totals, against the token ceiling when one is set, and the spend, never a sum.
- `--evidence [--check <n>]` prints one evidence file of the Attempt (m2-05, see [Evidence and verdicts](#evidence-and-verdicts-extension-m2-05)).

An unknown or ambiguous id exits 2, a workflow that does not load exits 1, an unreadable store or Attempt file exits 3. An Attempt file with cut or unreadable parts is not unreadable: the view prints what reads, exits 0, and says on stderr how many parts were left out, `loop show: the attempt file at <file> has 1 cut or unreadable part, ...`, ending with `left out.` for `--prompt` and `a cut runner line whose record is missing prints its stored preview.` for `--events`. The Attempt must belong to the workflow's Project.

### Attempt file retention (extension, m2-18)

Plan 10.3 keeps raw streams for 90 days by default, and the ledger and verdicts forever. `observability.retention_days` (a positive integer, default 90) sets that age in days, in `WORKFLOW.md`. The plan's other `observability` keys are not read: `LOOP_HOME` sets the store folder ([Machine level folder](#machine-level-folder-and-loop_home-extension-m1-02)), and OTLP export is not built.

`loop run` prunes at startup, right after the [Attempt directory sweep](#host-side-tracker-writes-extension-m1-23), once the run lock is held, so no live Attempt of the Project writes a file:

- **What goes:** every regular file named `*.jsonl.gz` directly in `<LOOP_HOME>/attempts/<project_id>/` whose last modification is more than `retention_days` days before now. A file is last modified by its last append, so an Attempt file ages from its Attempt's last runner line.
- **What stays:** evidence files (`<attempt_id>.evidence-<n>.log`), because a verdict names its evidence and verdicts are kept forever; Attempt directories, which the sweep owns; other Projects' folders, which their own `loop run` prunes ([ADR 0001](adr/0001-one-process-per-project-shared-machine-store.md)); the store, whose events, ledger and verdicts are never pruned. A symlink is neither followed nor deleted.
- **When:** at startup only. A run that lasts longer than the retention prunes on its next start. A workflow reload changes nothing until then.
- **Log:** a prune that deletes something logs `level=info event=attempt_retention outcome=completed pruned=<n> retention_days=<days>`; nothing to delete logs nothing. A file that cannot be deleted logs one `level=warn event=attempt_retention outcome=failed` with the count and the first reason, and the run goes on.

After a prune, `loop show --prompt` prints `No prompt recorded for this attempt.` and `loop show --events` prints the stored previews.

### Context config and prompt slot sizes (extension, m1-17)

The `context:` section of the plan (8.2, 8.4) is parsed and validated from m1-17, with the defaults above, and nothing enforces it yet: occupancy limits, rotations, tool output trimming and the prompt budget arrive in M3. `context.windows` adds to the windows Loop ships, or overrides one: `anthropic/claude-opus-5-5: 1000000`, the one value the plan gives, and since m4-02 `openai/gpt-6.1-sol: 475000`, the pinned Codex model.

Each rendered prompt records the size of every slot of plan 8.5, in characters, in the data of its `BuildingPrompt` phase event, after `prompt_chars`, in priority order: `slot_template_chars`, `slot_issue_chars`, `slot_verify_findings_chars`, `slot_session_brief_chars`, `slot_lessons_chars`, `slot_context_sources_chars`. Nothing is cut, and the prompt sent to the agent is the one rendered before m1-17, byte for byte. Since m3-14 a prompt over its limit is cut, and the sizes are those of the prompt sent, see [Prompt slots filled and tool outputs trimmed](#prompt-slots-filled-and-tool-outputs-trimmed-extension-m3-14).

- **Measure:** the template renders as one piece in M1, so the issue slot is the difference between the rendered prompt and the same template rendered with the Issue's fields emptied (strings empty, lists empty, nullable fields null). A field used twice counts twice, and text the template shows only for a present field counts in the issue slot. The template slot is the rest, the `attempt` variable included, so the slots add up to `prompt_chars`. Since m2-06 the verify findings slot is the difference between the rendered prompt and the same template rendered without `loop.verify_findings`, and the issue slot is measured on that second render. Every render keeps `attempt` and `loop.workspace_reused` (bl-19), so what they show counts in the template slot. Since m3-10 the session brief slot is the difference between the rendered prompt and the same template rendered without `loop.session_brief`, and the findings slot is measured on that render (see [Session brief](#session-brief-extension-m3-10)). The two later slots are 0 until their milestones fill them.
- **Characters, not tokens:** token counting with the model's tokenizer is part of the M3 prompt budget. Characters are exact and match `prompt_chars`. Since m3-13 each slot has its size in tokens too, see [Prompt tokens and prompt limit](#prompt-tokens-and-prompt-limit-extension-m3-13).
- **No measure:** when the emptied render fails (an index lookup such as `issue.labels[0]` on an empty list is a strict miss), the event keeps `prompt_chars` alone and Loop logs a warning with `event=prompt_slots outcome=failed`. The Attempt goes on with its prompt.
- **`loop trace`:** the `BuildingPrompt` line prints `prompt_chars=3601 template=3425 issue=176 verify_findings=0 session_brief=0 lessons=0 context_sources=0`. An event recorded before m1-17 prints `prompt_chars` alone.

### Host side tracker writes (extension, m1-23)

`loop tool move` and `loop tool comment` hold no credentials and write nothing to the tracker, for every tracker kind, the `files` tracker included. Each one validates its request (the state is defined, the Attempt is live) and queues it as one JSON file in the Attempt directory's `requests/` folder: a temp file in the same folder, then an atomic move to `%019d-<pid>-<random>.json` (the epoch milliseconds on 19 digits, so name order is time order). Invalid arguments are refused at once with a nonzero exit, and nothing is queued. On success the agent reads, for a move, `Queued a move of <identifier> to "<state>". Loop applies this in the tracker when this turn ends. Do not run it again.`, and for a comment, `Queued a comment on <identifier> (<n> characters). Loop applies this in the tracker when this turn ends. Do not run it again.`

After every turn whose process group is dead, Loop applies the queue in name order with its own tracker adapter, so credentials stay host side, and deletes each file right after applying it. It then re-reads the Issue, so the continuation decision and m1-27's no progress check see the moved state, and a host side move counts as progress. A crash between apply and delete can apply a request twice: a harmless repeated move, or a duplicate comment. This is accepted. One failed request records `tool_request_failed` and the rest still apply. A request file that cannot be decoded records `tool_request_failed` with `request` = `unknown` and the reason `not a valid tool request`. The decoder's message is not used, because it would quote the file, which may hold comment text.

A queued comment sits in its request file, readable only by the user (0700 folder), with only the token patterns applied, until the drain. There Loop redacts it with the full redactor before it writes it to the tracker or the store. This is the one exception to "redaction happens before writing" (see [Secret redaction](#secret-redaction-extension-of-153-and-131-m1-11)). In Loop mode, verification plugs in at this drain point, see [Loop mode](#loop-mode-extension-m2-03).

Only a turn that completes normally drains. On `loop stop`, a reconciliation cancel or a restart, queued requests are dropped unapplied with `tool_request_dropped`, so an agent's move never overrides a human's. `loop run` sweeps the Attempt directories a previous run left behind at startup, after the store opens under the run lock, so no live Attempt of the Project owns one. It takes directories only (m1-22's files share the folder) and follows no links. Each queued request is recorded as `tool_request_dropped`, with the Issue and Run of that Attempt's `attempt_started` event, then the directory is deleted. Each swept directory logs a warning, `attempt_directory outcome=completed dropped=N`.

Edge cases:

- A `loop tool` call from the `after_run` hook, which also sees `LOOP_TOOL_CONFIG`, runs after the last drain, so its request is dropped with `tool_request_dropped`.
- A process that escaped the process group and queues a request between the final drop and the directory delete can lose it without an event. The window is tiny, and this is accepted.
- A request queue that cannot be read records `tool_request_failed` with `request` = `unknown` and the reason `cannot read the request queue: <sentence>`.
- An entry of the queue that is not a regular file, or a `requests` folder that is a symlink, is never followed. It is reported unreadable, and only the entry itself is removed.

| Event | Data | When |
|---|---|---|
| `issue_move_requested` | `to` | `loop tool move`, or the `move` MCP tool (m2-17), queued a move. |
| `issue_comment_requested` | `chars` | `loop tool comment`, or the `comment` MCP tool (m2-17), queued a comment. |
| `issue_moved` | `from`, `to`, `by` = `host` | Loop applied a move. |
| `issue_commented` | `chars`, `by` = `host` | Loop applied a comment. |
| `tool_request_failed` | `request`, `to` or `chars`, `reason` | Loop could not apply a request. |
| `tool_request_dropped` | `request`, `to` or `chars` | A request was dropped unapplied. |

Since m2-07 a failed request is also reported to the agent: the next turn of the same Attempt names it in its continuation guidance, "Loop could not apply your last request (move to <state>): <reason>. Run it again if it is still needed.", with "(comment)" for a comment, "(move to review)" for the failed move of a `done` result file (m2-15) and "(unknown request)" for an undecodable request or an unreadable queue. The event's `to` and `reason` are redacted, the reason cut to 200 characters, also for an unreadable request file, whose reason can hold the file name; the guidance line also puts both on one line and cuts the state to 64 characters (see the continuation guidance row of the decisions table). A drain with no next turn (the last turn of the Attempt, or a runner without a session) reports it only as the event, and turn 1 of the next Attempt is the rendered prompt, unchanged. Since m3-14 the notices of one drain together are trimmed to `context.tool_output_tokens`, each staying whole in its event, see [Prompt slots filled and tool outputs trimmed](#prompt-slots-filled-and-tool-outputs-trimmed-extension-m3-14).

### Machine level folder and `LOOP_HOME` (extension, m1-02)

The store is `store.db` in the machine level folder, `~/.loop/` by default ([ADR 0001](adr/0001-one-process-per-project-shared-machine-store.md)). The `LOOP_HOME` environment variable overrides the folder. Loop refuses to start when `LOOP_HOME` is the Project's folder or inside it, so a store can never end up in a repository.

The folder also holds `install/`, the default destination of `./gradlew installLoop` since m1-12: the installed Loop build that runs Loop's own issues, outside every repository. `-PloopInstallDir` picks another path. See [`self-hosting.md`](self-hosting.md).

### Loop version per Attempt (extension, m1-12)

Every `attempt_started` event, first and retry Attempts alike, stores the version of the running Loop as `loop_version` in its data: the 12 character hex git SHA of the build, with `-dirty` when tracked files had uncommitted changes at build time, or `unknown` when the build had no git. The value comes from `build-info.properties` in the installed distribution. There is no store schema change, since `data` already holds string pairs. `loop trace` shows it in the Attempt header as `loop <version>`, and `loop --version` prints it. Attempts recorded before m1-12 have no `loop_version`. See [`self-hosting.md`](self-hosting.md#version).

### Internal errors and `LOOP_DEBUG` (extension, m1-26)

An internal error is an unexpected `Exception` that no command handled. The CLI never lets the JVM print it, because the JVM would write the message and stack trace unredacted. Instead it prints one line on stderr and exits 1:

```
loop <command>: internal error: <redacted sentence>
```

- `Exception` is caught. `Error` (out of memory, stack overflow) is not, and the JVM handles it as before. `CliktError` is unchanged: usage and handled failures keep their own messages and exit codes. A `CancellationException` that reaches the command level counts as an internal error.
- In `loop run`, the error goes through the operator log as `level=error event=run outcome=failed reason="internal error: ..."`, and the process exits 1. The plain stderr line is used only before the log exists, for example when startup fails early.
- In `loop run`, a default uncaught exception handler covers background threads and coroutines. It logs the same line, cancels the run and lets the process exit 1. Without it, an exception on a background thread would print unredacted and leave the run going.
  - An `Error` on a background thread goes to the previous default handler, or, when there is none, is printed the way the JVM prints it.
  - An exception after the first is logged as a redacted error line, and the first failure decides the exit.
  - The handler is restored when the run returns, so a thread that outlives the run falls back to the JVM default.
- The fallback in `main` uses a redactor built from the host environment: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, the tracker token when the workflow file can be read (it reads `LOOP_WORKFLOW` first, then `./WORKFLOW.md`), and the token patterns. Inside an Attempt, when `LOOP_TOOL_CONFIG` is set, it reads no workflow: the Anthropic keys and the token patterns only. It never learns the whole environment, because the fallback cannot know which variables are secrets.
- `LOOP_DEBUG=1` prints the stack trace after the line, redacted as a whole. The value must be exactly `1`. Any other value, or unset, drops the trace. It is an environment variable, not a flag, so that `loop tool move` inside an Attempt can use it too.

### `loop run --once` (extension of 16.2 and 17.7, m1-02)

`loop run [path-to-WORKFLOW.md]` is the daemon of the spec: one tick at once, then one every `polling.interval_ms`, until SIGINT or SIGTERM stops it cleanly. `--once` runs a single tick, waits for the Attempts it started and exits. It serves tests and operators. In both modes the exit code is nonzero only for a startup or host failure, never for the outcome of an Attempt. Since m1-34 the daemon stopped by `loop stop` (reason `stop_requested`) or by an operator's SIGTERM or SIGINT (reason `signal`) exits 0 once it unwound within the 10 s shutdown grace, keeps the signal code (143 for SIGTERM, 130 for SIGINT) when the grace runs out, and exits 1 when an internal error canceled it (reason `internal_error`). `--once` is unchanged and keeps the signal code.

### Human log and `loop log` (extension, m1-39)

`loop run` writes two logs. The `key=value` operator log on stderr (plan U3) is unchanged, byte for byte. The [human log](../CONTEXT.md) is added next to it: plain sentences a person can follow, one file per Project, `<LOOP_HOME>/logs/<project_id>.log`, appended across runs. The project id is the store's (first 16 hex of the SHA-256 of the real path of `WORKFLOW.md`). The folder is created with mode 0700, the file with mode 0600, never through a symlink.

A line is `HH:mm:ss  <identifier>  <sentence>` in local time, or `HH:mm:ss  <sentence>` for a run level line. Sentence case, no em or en dash, tokens as `7.2M`, money as `$3.38`. A workspace prints relative to the workflow's folder, a file the agent touched relative to its workspace, each in full when it is outside.

```
17:02:11  run started for /home/me/project/WORKFLOW.md
17:02:11  1 issue eligible (m1-13)
17:02:12  m1-13  attempt started in workspaces/m1-13
17:02:14  m1-13  turn 1 of 20 started
17:03:40  m1-13  read CONTEXT.md, docs/spec-decisions.md and 6 other files
17:05:02  m1-13  edited cli/src/main/kotlin/loop/cli/ShowCommand.kt
17:09:30  m1-13  ran ./gradlew build (exit 0)
17:15:05  m1-13  turn 1 finished, 7.2M tokens, $3.38
17:15:06  m1-13  moved to review by the agent's request
17:15:06  m1-13  attempt succeeded, the issue is in review
17:15:36  nothing to do, waiting for eligible issues
```

What gets a line:

- **Run:** start (with the workflow path) and end. The end says why: `run ended`, `run stopped by loop stop`, `run stopped by a signal` or `run stopped by an internal error`, with the number of canceled attempts.
- **Polls:** no line per poll. `N issues eligible (ids)` when a poll finds work and the line differs from the last one, or `N issues eligible, waiting for a free slot (ids)` while running attempts fill `agent.max_concurrent_agents` (m1-40), `nothing to do, waiting for eligible issues` when the run turns idle (nothing eligible, nothing running). The first failing preflight and the next passing one get a line each, and so do a reload of `WORKFLOW.md` and a rejected edit (m1-15).
- **Issues:** a skip with its reason in words (`skipped, blocked by m1-12 (in_progress)`), a move and who asked (`moved to review by the agent's request`), an agent comment, a park with its reason (`parked after 3 failed attempts, until loop resume or a tracker change`), an unpark.
- **Attempts:** start, with the workspace once it is prepared, and end with the outcome and why (`attempt failed, the agent exited with code 1`). A first dispatch is an `attempt`, a retry is `retry N` and a continuation `continuation N`, N being the spec attempt of `retry_scheduled`. A failure retry gets `retry 2 in 20s` and a dropped one `retry dropped, <reason>`. A continuation is scheduled after every normal exit (16.6) and mostly dropped at the next poll, so it gets a line only when it starts.
- **Turns:** start (`turn 1 of 20 started`) and end with the attempt's tokens and spend of the latest `usage` (`turn 1 finished, 7.2M tokens, $3.38`, or `turn 1 failed, <reason>`).
- **Others:** a failed or timed out hook with the last line of its output, a token threshold (`passed 10M tokens, $4.12 so far`, `about` for an estimate), a budget warning (`used $8.50 of $10.00, 80 percent of its per_attempt_usd budget`) and a budget passed (m2-20), an agent request Loop could not apply or dropped.
- **Agent activity**, from the runner lines, Claude Code's stream-json only: files read, grouped while they come in a row (`read A, B and 6 other files`), files edited (`Edit`, `MultiEdit`, `NotebookEdit`) or written (`Write`), and commands run (`Bash`) with their result once its `tool_result` arrives: `(exit 0)` when `is_error` is false, `(error)` when it is true (m1-40). Loop cannot know what a command was meant to prove, and `./gradlew test | tee out.txt` exits with the code of `tee`, so the line states the exit, never that the command passed. A command whose result never comes is written without one when the turn ends. A command shows its first line followed by `(+N lines)` when more lines follow (a heredoc reads `python3 - <<'EOF' (+12 lines)`). A leading `cd <workspace> && ` is dropped and the Attempt's workspace, from its `PreparingWorkspace` phase, is written `.` everywhere in the command. Both happen before the 120 character cut. Other tools, the agent's own text and the lines of other runners give nothing. The orchestrator hands the human log each redacted runner line in full, because the stored `runner_output` is cut at 2000 characters and a long tool result would lose its `is_error`.

Every line goes through the run's redactor before it is written, like the operator log. A failed write never fails the run or an Attempt: the first one is reported once on stderr as `level=warn event=human_log outcome=failed path=... reason=...`, later ones are dropped silently. The file has no rotation yet.

`loop log [path-to-WORKFLOW.md]` prints the last 50 lines, or `No human log yet for this project, loop run writes it to <path>.` when there is none. `--follow` then prints every line appended, until interrupted. A workflow that does not load exits 1. Colors, a TUI, and changes to `loop trace` or `loop status` are out of scope.

### `loop status` runtime snapshot (extension of 13.3 and 13.4, m1-14)

`loop status [path-to-WORKFLOW.md] [--full-ids]` prints what the Project's run is doing right now: one line per running, retrying, waiting and Parked Issue, with its Attempt. It reads only the store and the run file, never the memory of the run, so it works from any terminal while `loop run` works in another process (ADR 0001).

```
Project ec213d764331bfa8   /path/to/WORKFLOW.md
Run 45e4b849  pid 57294

running   s-run    attempt 1  8d5830ca  started=18:56:53 turns=1
retrying  s-retry  attempt 1  c5c2ee1f  kind=error retry=1 due=18:57:03 error="turn_failed: exit code 1"
waiting   s-check  attempt 2  29974f64  reason=verify since=2026-11-12T14:07:26Z
parked    s-park   attempt 1  c1000000  reason=max_attempts state=todo since=2026-11-11T09:30:00Z
```

How each part is found:

- **The live run:** the run file `<LOOP_HOME>/runs/<projectId>.json` whose pid is alive and started at the recorded instant. `loop status` reads it without taking its lock and never removes it, so it cannot make a starting `loop run` see a busy run file. A run file whose process is gone means `No run in progress.`, and the file is left for `loop stop` or the next `loop run`.
- **Running:** an Attempt of the live run with an `attempt_started` and no `attempt_finished`. `turns` counts its `turn_started` events (13.3 `turn_count`), `tokens` is the `attempt_tokens` of its latest `usage` event, left out before the first.
- **Retrying:** the latest `retry_scheduled` of an Issue in the live run, until a later `attempt_started` (the retry fired), `retry_dropped` or `issue_parked`. The fields are those of the event: `kind` (`error` or `continuation`), `retry` (the spec's retry `attempt`, not the trace number), `due` and `error`. An Issue that is running is not also listed as retrying.
- **Waiting (m2-04):** every record of the `waiting_issues` table of the Project, with its reason (`verify` while the checks of a submitted result run, `approval` while a verified result waits for `loop approve`, m2-24) and since when. A waiting Issue is listed once, as waiting, even while the Attempt whose checks run is still open, and it shows without a live run too, since the wait survives a restart.
- **Parked:** every record of the `parked_issues` table of the Project, with its reason, the tracker state it was parked in and when.
- **The Attempt column:** the Attempt as `loop trace` numbers it (its rank among the Issue's Attempts across every run) and its short id, so a line leads straight to the trace. A Parked Issue shows the Attempt that parked it, a waiting Issue the Attempt that started the wait, else each its latest. `no attempt` when none was recorded.

Only the live run's events count for running and retrying: run ids are new for every `loop run` process, a retry queued by an earlier process shows once the live run restored it (its `retry_scheduled` with `restored=true`, m2-02), and an Attempt a dead process left open is not running (the next `loop run` closes it as `abandoned`, m1-36). Without a live run, waiting and Parked Issues still print. With nothing to show, the line is `Nothing running, retrying, waiting or parked.` A workflow that does not load exits 1; a run file or a store that cannot be read exits 3.

**Not shown:** the 13.3 `codex_totals`, aggregate `seconds_running` and `rate_limits`, and the tracker issue URL. The snapshot is optional in the spec, and these need data the store does not hold yet. M2's `Waiting` is shown since m2-04.

### No tracker write on dispatch (clarification of 8, m1-02)

Loop does not move an Issue to `in_progress` when it dispatches it. The claim is decided in memory and kept in the store (m2-02), and the tracker is read only. The one write Loop makes on its own at dispatch time is the Loop mode [readiness bounce](#readiness-check-extension-m2-11) of an Issue it does not dispatch. An agent that wants the tracker to show the work calls `loop tool move in_progress` itself, as it calls `loop tool move review` at the end. Since m1-23 Loop applies these moves host side when the turn ends (see [Host side tracker writes](#host-side-tracker-writes-extension-m1-23)).

### Hook environment variables (extension, m1-04)

Hooks are never rendered as templates. Loop passes `LOOP_ISSUE_ID`, `LOOP_ISSUE_IDENTIFIER`, `LOOP_WORKSPACE_KEY`, since m4-12 `LOOP_TRACKER_ID` (see [Namespaced issue ids](#namespaced-issue-ids-extension-m4-12)) and, since m4-18 in Loop mode, `LOOP_KIND`, the name of the issue's kind (see [Kind map and matcher](#kind-map-and-matcher-extension-m4-18)), as environment variables, so a hook can, for example, create a git worktree on the right branch. They are added on top of the host environment (see the hook shell row in [implementation defined behaviors](#implementation-defined-behaviors)). A hook run outside an Attempt, such as `before_remove` from `removeWorkspace`, still receives `LOOP_ISSUE_ID`, `LOOP_ISSUE_IDENTIFIER`, `LOOP_WORKSPACE_KEY`, `LOOP_WORKFLOW` and, in Loop mode, `LOOP_KIND`. Since bl-59 the hooks of an Attempt of a kind with `delivery.pull_request` also get `LOOP_BASE_REF`, the base commit Loop fetched right before them, see [Base fetch before every attempt](#base-fetch-before-every-attempt-extension-bl-59); a hook outside an Attempt never gets it. Loop's own worktree hooks are documented in [`workspaces.md`](workspaces.md).

### Continuation turns (extension of 7.1 and 16.5, m1-06)

**Turn loop.** One Attempt runs up to `agent.max_turns` turns on one agent session (thread). Turn 1 gets the full rendered prompt, turns 2 to n get the continuation guidance (see the implementation defined table). After each completed turn the Attempt refreshes the Issue by id. A refresh error fails the Attempt with reason `issue_state_refresh_error`, a counted failure (answer 5), and the `attempt_finished` event records the tracker's error detail in a separate `error` field. The Attempt ends normally when the Issue is not found, is terminal, is not active, is no longer eligible, or when the turn number reaches `agent.max_turns`. The phases `LaunchingAgentProcess`, `InitializingSession` and `StreamingTurn` are recorded once, on turn 1, and `Finishing` once at the end. `before_run` runs once per Attempt, and the session is stopped before `after_run`.

**Exit reasons.** The `attempt_finished` event and the Succeeded phase carry `exit_reason` and `turns`. `exit_reason` is one of `not_found`, `terminal`, `not_active`, `not_eligible`, `max_turns` or `no_continuation` (the runner offered no session, so the Attempt ran one turn). Since m4-03 it can also be `max_wake_turns`, and a wake turn does not count against `agent.max_turns`, see [Wake turns](#wake-turns-extension-m4-03).

**Events.** New event kinds `turn_started`, `turn_completed` and `turn_failed`, with data `turn`, `max_turns`, `thread_id`, `turn_id`, plus `reason` on `turn_failed`. `turn_completed` and `turn_failed` are the spec's names (10.4). `turn_started` is a Loop extension. `session_started` is emitted once per thread, with `session_id` `<thread_id>-<turn_id>`.

**Runner API.** `AgentRunner.openSession(ctx): AgentSession?` returns null when the runner has no continuation. `AgentSession.runTurn(input, turn)` and `AgentSession.stop()` are the object form of the `continueTurn(session, guidance)` and `stop(session)` of [PLAN.md section 6.3](PLAN.md). Turns 2 to n emit `RunnerEvent.TurnStarted`. Since bl-132 the orchestrator stops a session with `AgentSession.stop(usage)`, named `stop(report)` since m4-04: it ends the session like `stop()` and gives the callback what the session read while it stopped, `RunnerEvent.Usage` reports absolute for the session and at most one `RunnerEvent.StopGraceExpired`, last, before a failed stop throws. Its default calls `stop()` and reports nothing; see [Usage at the stop](#usage-at-the-stop-extension-m4-04). `AgentRunnerContract` checks a runner that overrides it (bl-152). Since m4-46 `AgentRunner.resumeSession(ctx, runtimeSessionId): AgentSession?` opens a session that continues the runtime session of an earlier attempt, or returns null, its default, when the runner cannot; see [Resume after a network hold](#resume-after-a-network-hold-extension-m4-46). The command runner is described in [Command runner continuation](#command-runner-continuation-extension-m1-06).

**Claude Code runner.** Since m3-07 the turns of an Attempt share one long lived `claude -p --input-format stream-json` process, see [Claude Code long lived process](#claude-code-long-lived-process-extension-of-103-m3-07). From m1-06 to m3-07 turns 2 to n ran `claude -p --resume <runtime session id>`, one process per turn. The newest `init` session id wins and the `result` line's `session_id` is the fallback. When a new process is needed and no id is known the turn fails with `missing_runtime_session_id`.

**Continuation retry (16.6, 17.4).** Every normal exit schedules attempt 1 after 1000 ms, with `retry_scheduled` data `kind=continuation` and no `error`, logged at INFO. Failure retries carry `kind=error`. A continuation also carries `no_progress` and `max_continuations` (answer 5). The retry entry and the failure counter are cleared first, so a failure of the continued Attempt is attempt 2 after 20 s; the no progress count is kept. The template variable `attempt` is 1 on a continuation dispatch.

**Visible consequences.**
- A handoff straight to a terminal state now removes the workspace about 1 s later, through the retry timer's terminal path (8.5).
- A normal exit that does not change the Issue's state or labels re-dispatches about 1 s later, each Attempt running up to `max_turns` turns, until the continuation cap (`retries.max_continuations`, default 3, answer 5) parks the Issue with the reason `max_continuations`.

### Loop mode (extension, m2-03)

PLAN 6.0. A `kinds:` key in `WORKFLOW.md` switches the workflow to Loop mode. Without it Loop is a plain Symphony implementation and nothing on this page changes: the conformance suite runs without it.

```yaml
kinds:
  code:
    verify:
      checks:
        - { type: command, run: "./gradlew --no-daemon build", timeout_ms: 1800000 }
```

**Config.** Until m4-18 the only kind was `code`, matching every Issue; since then `kinds:` is a map of named kinds, see [Kind map and matcher](#kind-map-and-matcher-extension-m4-18). A `command` check has a non blank `run` and an optional positive `timeout_ms`, one hour by default; m2-12 adds the other [core check types](#core-check-types-extension-m2-12) and m2-09 adds `type: acceptance_commands` (see [Acceptance commands](#acceptance-commands-extension-m2-09)). Each of these is `invalid_config` and fails preflight: `kinds: ~` or `kinds: {}`, kinds with no `code` kind, a missing `verify` or `checks` key, a `checks` that is not a list, a check `type` that is not a core type, a blank `run`, a `timeout_ms` that is not a positive integer. An empty or misspelled key can therefore never switch verification off silently. An explicit `checks: []` is valid: with no check nothing proves the work, so no move is applied, and since m2-08 the verdict is `unverified` and the Issue is parked (see [Unverified park and rerun](#unverified-park-and-rerun-extension-m2-08)).

**The intercept point** is the host side drain of queued tool requests ([Host side tracker writes](#host-side-tracker-writes-extension-m1-23)). `loop tool move` and its message are unchanged, so the same prompt works in both modes. When the drain reaches a queued move whose target is not one of `tracker.active_states`, the move is a submitted result: a move to the handoff state (`review`) or to a terminal state, and any other state except `needs_info`, so no unverified way out is left. States are compared trimmed and lowercased. A move to `needs_info` or to an active state, and every comment, applies as in Symphony mode, except a move to an active state that would change the issue's kind, refused since m4-18 (see [Kind map and matcher](#kind-map-and-matcher-extension-m4-18)).

- Deviation from story 66, which also names `needs_info` a handoff state: a question is not a result, so it is never verified.
- Loop records `result_submitted` (`to`), then runs the kind's checks in order in the Attempt's workspace, and stops at the first failure. Since m2-08 a check that fails runs once more before it counts as a failure (see [Unverified park and rerun](#unverified-park-and-rerun-extension-m2-08)).
- When every check passes, Loop reads the Issue again and applies the move the agent asked for itself, recording `issue_moved` with `by` = `host` and `verified` = `true`. If the Issue is no longer active, a human changed it during the checks and the human's change wins: nothing moves, and `tool_request_failed` gives the reason `the issue is no longer active, it is in <state>`. An Issue already in the target state is left alone.
- When a check fails, times out or cannot start, the move is not applied and the Issue stays in its active state. No `tool_request_failed` is recorded: the check events are the record.
- A second submitted move in the same drain reuses the first result, so the checks run once per drain. Requests keep their name order, so a comment queued before or after the move still applies.
- The Attempt verifies the `kinds:` of the workflow it started with. An Attempt that started before `kinds:` was added applies its move unverified.

**After a failed check** the next turn's guidance carries the findings, and an Attempt whose turns run out on a refutation fails with the reason `refuted` (m2-06, see [Verify findings](#verify-findings-extension-m2-06)). Each later submitted move reruns the full check list.

**Running a check.** `sh -lc <run>` in the workspace, in its own process group, like a hook: the group is stopped when the shell exits, at the timeout, or on a cancel. Stall detection only watches runner events, so `timeout_ms` is the only limit on a check. Its env is the [agent environment](#agent-environment-extension-m1-05-m1-23-and-bl-01) policy, the host env minus every `$VAR` the workflow references and minus `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, plus `LOOP_ISSUE_ID`, `LOOP_ISSUE_IDENTIFIER`, `LOOP_WORKSPACE_KEY`, `LOOP_PROJECT_ID`, `LOOP_RUN_ID` and `LOOP_ATTEMPT_ID`. A check runs code the agent wrote, so it gets no secret the agent does not have, and no `LOOP_TOOL_CONFIG`, launcher or `agent.env`: it cannot queue tool requests, and it never shares the agent's per Attempt Gradle daemons. `PATH` is the host's.

**Cancels.** With a session the drain runs inside the Attempt's turns, and in Loop mode a runner without a session drains there too, before the close path. So `loop stop` and reconciliation stop a running check: its group is stopped, `check_finished` records `canceled` = `true`, and the move is recorded as `tool_request_dropped`. Since m2-04 a `loop stop` keeps the Issue `Waiting(verify)`, so the next start runs the checks again and applies the move; reconciliation ends the wait (see [Waiting](#waiting-claim-state-extension-m2-04)).

| Event | Data | When |
|---|---|---|
| `result_submitted` | `to` | A queued move was taken as the agent's result. |
| `check_started` | `check` (index), `command` | A check starts. |
| `check_finished` | `check`, `command`, `exit_code` (absent when the check did not exit), `duration_ms`, `timed_out`, `reason` (could not start), or `canceled` = `true` | A check ended. |

The command is redacted, then cut to 200 characters. `loop trace` prints `result_submitted to=review` and `check_finished check=0 command="..." exit=1 duration=2.1s`, with `timed_out` or `canceled` when set, and `issue_moved ... by host after checks` for a verified move. The human log reads `submitted its result, a move to review`, `check ./gradlew test failed with exit code 1 in 42s` and `moved to review after its checks passed`.

Not in m2-03: the `verdict` event and evidence (m2-05), the `Waiting` claim state while a check runs (m2-04), findings as guidance ([m2-06](#verify-findings-extension-m2-06)), the `unverified` park and the rerun of a failing check (m2-08), other check types ([m2-12](#core-check-types-extension-m2-12)) and delivery ([m2-24](#delivery-policy-and-approval-extension-m2-24): since then the verified move is applied at once only under `delivery: { policy: auto }`, and the default `human` waits for `loop approve` first).

### Waiting claim state (extension, m2-04)

PLAN 6.6 and story 76. `Waiting(reason)` is a Loop claim state on top of the spec's: a waiting Issue is not dispatched, whatever its tracker state, and its queued retry, a continuation included, does not fire until the wait ends. The reasons so far are `verify` and, since m2-24, `approval` (see [Delivery policy and approval](#delivery-policy-and-approval-extension-m2-24)); `ask` comes later.

**When an Issue waits for `verify`.** In Loop mode, when the drain takes a queued move as a submitted result ([Loop mode](#loop-mode-extension-m2-03)) and the kind has at least one check, the Issue waits from `result_submitted` until the move is applied or the checks failed. The wait is held in memory and written to the `waiting_issues` table with the Attempt, the Run, the time and the target state `to`. The Attempt's own claim already keeps a running Issue from a second dispatch; the wait is what still holds after the process that ran the checks is gone. With no check, no workspace or a second move in the same drain, nothing waits.

**How a wait ends.**

| End | Wait | Next |
|---|---|---|
| Every check passed and the move is applied, or a human change wins | Ended | The Issue is out of the active states, or stays active as the human left it |
| A check failed, timed out or could not start | Ended | The usual continuation of m2-03 |
| Reconciliation stops the Attempt during a check | Ended | The human change wins (m1-16) |
| `loop stop`, a signal, `kill -9` or a crash during a check | Kept in the store | The next start runs the checks again |

**At startup.** The first tick of a new process reads every row back after the stored retry queue and before it dispatches anything; a store that cannot be read logs `level=error event=waiting outcome=failed` and dispatches nothing until it can. For each `verify` wait:

- When the Issue is no longer among the active candidates (a human moved it, or it is gone), the workflow has no `kinds:` any more, the wait has no target state or the workspace is gone, the wait ends at once, logged `event=waiting outcome=released` with the reason, and the Issue is dispatched by the usual paths, in the same tick.
- Otherwise the Issue keeps waiting and the kind's checks of the current workflow run again in its workspace, with no agent, no Attempt and no hook, logged `event=waiting outcome=resumed`. The events go to the new Run with the Attempt id of the wait: `result_submitted` with `restored` = `true`, then the check events, then `issue_moved` with `verified` = `true` when every check passed and the Issue is still active, or `tool_request_failed` with the reason when the move could not apply. A failed check ends the wait, and the Issue is dispatched as a candidate on the next tick: the abandoned Attempt is not a failure (m1-36). `loop run --once` waits for these checks in its one tick.
- A wait for another reason keeps its Issue waiting.

A `loop stop` during a rerun keeps the wait again. The checks of a `kill -9` process keep running in their own process group until they end; the rerun does not wait for them.

**Store writes.** A failed write or delete of a row logs `level=warn event=waiting outcome=failed` and changes nothing else: the wait in memory decides for the process. A row a failed delete left is read back by the next start, where the rules above end it or check again, never dispatching twice.
