**Shown by** `loop status` as `waiting  <issue>  attempt <n>  <id>  reason=verify since=<time>` (see [`loop status`](#loop-status-runtime-snapshot-extension-of-133-and-134-m1-14)). `loop trace` prints `result_submitted to=review restored` for a rerun, and the human log reads `checks its submitted result again after a restart, a move to review`.

### Evidence and verdicts (extension, m2-05)

PLAN 6.4 and 10.2, story 71. In Loop mode each check's full output is saved as evidence stamped with the workspace fingerprint, and each verification ends with a `verdict` event.

**Workspace fingerprint.** Taken once per verification, in the workspace, before the first check, so every check of one verification carries the same value. When the workspace is the top of a git work tree it is `git:<HEAD>:<diff>:<untracked>`: the commit (`none` before the first one), a hash of `git diff HEAD --binary` (staged and unstaged changes alike, so staging changes nothing) and a hash of the path and content of each untracked file git does not ignore, in path order. Ignored files, such as build output, are not workspace state, and neither is the result file `.loop/result.json` at the top of the workspace ([m2-15](#result-file-extension-m2-15)), which is what the agent tells Loop, not the work: both git hashes leave it out with the pathspec `:(exclude,literal).loop/result.json`, and the tree hash skips it. Every other file under `.loop/`, tracked or untracked, is workspace state, so an uncommitted change to it changes the fingerprint. Any other folder, including a folder inside a repository it is not the top of, gets `tree:<hash>` of the path and content of every file under it. Hashes are sha256 cut to 16 hex characters; a symlink counts by its target, never followed. git runs with every `GIT_` variable removed and `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, so it never takes the index lock. A fingerprint that cannot be taken is `unknown`, with `level=warn event=fingerprint outcome=failed`, and the checks still run.

**Evidence.** One file per check run, beside the Attempt file: `<LOOP_HOME>/attempts/<project_id>/<attempt_id>.evidence-<n>.log`, `n` from 1 per Attempt, the next after the files already there, so a check run again after a restart ([Waiting](#waiting-claim-state-extension-m2-04)) never replaces one. Files, not a folder, because the start of run sweep deletes every folder there (m1-23). A file is created with mode 0600, never over an existing entry and never through a symlink, and kept when the Attempt ends, like the Attempt file.

The file is a header of `key: value` lines, a blank line, then the output:

```
attempt: <attempt_id>
check: 0
command: ./gradlew --no-daemon build
exit_code: 1
timed_out: false
duration_ms: 42100
fingerprint: git:<HEAD>:<diff>:<untracked>
output_truncated: false

<stdout and stderr, merged as written>
```

`exit_code` is absent when the check did not exit, and a `reason` line follows `output_truncated` when it could not start. The whole text is redacted with the run's redactor before it is written, never on read ([Secret redaction](#secret-redaction-extension-of-153-and-131-m1-11)). The output is kept whole up to 16 MiB; past that its head is dropped up to a whole line and `output_truncated` is `true`, so a runaway check cannot exhaust memory. A failed write records `evidence_record_failed` with `check` and a redacted `reason` and logs a warning, like `prompt_record_failed`; it never stops the verification. A restored wait with no Attempt id saves no evidence.

**Verdict.** Each verification ends with a `verdict` event, after its last `check_finished` and before the move:

| Verdict | When |
|---|---|
| `verified` | Every check passed, on its first run or on its rerun (m2-08). |
| `refuted` | A check failed on its first run and on its rerun (m2-08). |
| `unverified` | The kind has no check, or a check could not run (PLAN 6.4): it could not start, timed out, ended without an exit code, its check runner threw, or `sh` exited 127 or 126. Since m2-08, see [Unverified park and rerun](#unverified-park-and-rerun-extension-m2-08). |

A cancel during a check records no verdict: the verification did not end. Until m2-08 a timed out check was `refuted` and a kind with no check recorded no verdict.

| Event | Data | When |
|---|---|---|
| `check_finished` | m2-03's fields, plus `fingerprint` and `evidence` (the file name, absent when none was saved) | A check ended. |
| `verdict` | `verdict`, `fingerprint`, `checks` (how many ran), `evidence` (the file names of the checks that ran, comma separated, absent when none) | A verification ended. |
| `evidence_record_failed` | `check`, `reason` | An evidence file could not be written. |

**Shown by** `loop trace` as `verdict  refuted checks=2 fingerprint=git:... evidence=2`, the count of evidence files, and the human log as `verdict refuted, evidence of 2 checks saved`. `loop show <attempt> --evidence` prints the evidence file of the last check run in the Attempt, as written; `--check <n>` picks the latest evidence of check `n`. With none it prints `No evidence recorded for this attempt.` (or `for check <n> of this attempt.`). `--check` without `--evidence` is a usage error, and an evidence file that cannot be read exits 3.

### Verify findings (extension, m2-06)

PLAN 6.4 and 8.9, story 73. In Loop mode a `refuted` verdict sends the findings back to the agent: on the next turn of the same Attempt as continuation guidance, and on the next Attempt as the template variable `loop.verify_findings`. Refutations count against `agent.max_turns` and `retries.max_attempts`.

**Findings.** Built from the check that failed, the last one the verification ran, never from the agent's words:

```
Your last result was refuted: Loop ran its checks and one failed, so the issue did not move. Fix what the check found without changing or skipping it, then move the issue again.
Check: ./gradlew --no-daemon build
Exit code: 1
Last 20 lines of its output:
<the lines>
```

- **Exit code:** the code of the rerun, the second failed run (m2-08). A check stopped at its `timeout_ms` gives `unverified` since m2-08, so no findings.
- **Output:** the last non blank lines of the check's output, stdout and stderr merged as in the evidence: at most 20 lines, each cut to 200 characters, at most 2000 characters together, the oldest dropped first. Since m3-14 the bound of 2000 characters is gone: the whole findings text stays within `context.tool_output_tokens` (default 2000) of the runner model's counter, the oldest lines dropped first, and with no line that fits the output part reads `Its output is left out to fit context.tool_output_tokens.` (see [Prompt slots filled and tool outputs trimmed](#prompt-slots-filled-and-tool-outputs-trimmed-extension-m3-14)). `Last line of its output:` for one line, `Its output was empty.` for none. The full output stays in the evidence file (`loop show --evidence`), as plan 8.7 asks.
- **Command:** its first line, cut to 200 characters like in the check events.
- **Redaction:** the command and the output are redacted with the run's redactor before they are cut, so a cut never hides part of a secret from it. Only the last 64 KiB of the output are read.
- A check that could not run gives an `unverified` verdict, not a refutation, and no findings; the Issue is parked (m2-08, see [Unverified park and rerun](#unverified-park-and-rerun-extension-m2-08)).

**Next turn.** After a turn whose drain ran a verification that ended `refuted`, the next turn's input is the continuation guidance, a blank line, then the findings (see the continuation guidance row in [implementation defined behaviors](#implementation-defined-behaviors)). A turn after one without a refutation gets the plain guidance, so the findings are sent once per refutation. The turn counts against `agent.max_turns` like any turn.

**Refuted end.** When an Attempt runs out of turns (`max_turns`), or a runner without a session ends its one turn, while its latest verdict is `refuted`, the Attempt fails with the reason `refuted` instead of ending normally. A failure counts against `retries.max_attempts` (answer 5): it is retried with the usual backoff, `retry_scheduled` `kind=error` and `error=refuted`, and the failure that reaches the cap parks the Issue with the reason `max_attempts` and `error=refuted`. So an agent that never passes parks. A later verdict that is not `refuted` clears the latest one: a result refuted on turn 1 and verified on turn 2 moves the Issue as usual. An Issue that left the routable states (a move to `needs_info`, a human change) ends normally whatever its last verdict. The human log reads `attempt failed, its checks refuted its last result`.

**Next Attempt.** The findings of a refuted end, or of a restored check ([Waiting](#waiting-claim-state-extension-m2-04)) that ended `refuted`, are held in memory by Issue and rendered as `loop.verify_findings` by the Issue's next Attempt, which takes them: the Attempt after it renders it null again. `loop.verify_findings` is null otherwise, renders as nothing and is false in a condition, so a template that does not use it renders exactly as before, byte for byte:

```
{% if loop.verify_findings %}
## Findings of your last result

{{ loop.verify_findings }}
{% endif %}
```

- **Strict rendering:** `loop` is a template root with the keys `verify_findings`, since bl-19 `workspace_reused` and since m3-10 `session_brief`; any other key under it is an unknown variable that fails preflight, like `issue.nope`. Preflight renders the template a third time, as a retry with sample findings, so a mistake in that branch fails preflight too.
- **Slot:** what the findings add is the `slot_verify_findings_chars` of the `BuildingPrompt` event (see [Context config and prompt slot sizes](#context-config-and-prompt-slot-sizes-extension-m1-17)), and the `prompt` record of the Attempt file holds the rendered prompt with them.
- **Not across restarts:** the findings live in the process. A retry that fires after a restart renders `loop.verify_findings` null; the evidence of the refuted Attempt is still on disk. Loop's own `WORKFLOW.md` does not use the variable yet; adding it is left to a human, since the file is a protected path (story 100).

### Workspace reused (extension, bl-19)

PLAN 8.9. The template variable `loop.workspace_reused` is a boolean: true when the workspace of the Issue already existed when this Attempt prepared it, false when this Attempt created it. It is set from what the workspace layer found (`Workspace.createdNow`), after `after_create` and before the prompt renders.

- **When it is true:** every dispatch into a folder an earlier Attempt left, so the folder may hold that Attempt's commits, uncommitted work and progress file. That includes the cases where `attempt` is null: the first dispatch after a restart that closed an abandoned Attempt, after `loop resume`, and after a human sent the Issue back from `needs_info` or `review`. A retry into the same folder has it true too.
- **When it is false:** the first dispatch of an Issue, and the first after a terminal cleanup removed the workspace, where no earlier work is left to point at.
- **A fact about the folder:** it never reads the retry queue or the ledger, and `attempt` keeps its spec meaning and values (see [Template `attempt` keeps spec semantics](#template-attempt-keeps-spec-semantics-clarification)). An `after_create` that fails removes the new folder, so the next Attempt creates it again and renders false.
- **Unchanged templates:** a template that does not use the variable renders exactly as before, byte for byte. A template uses it like any condition:

```
{% if attempt or loop.workspace_reused %}
# Earlier work on this issue
...
{% endif %}
```

- **Preflight:** renders each of its samples (a first run, a retry, a retry with findings) with the variable false and true, so a template error in either branch fails preflight at startup.
- **Slot:** what the variable shows counts in the template slot, like `attempt` (see [Context config and prompt slot sizes](#context-config-and-prompt-slot-sizes-extension-m1-17)).
- **Loop's own template:** `WORKFLOW.md` is a protected path (story 100), so the template edit is a human step: the earlier work block (heading "Earlier work on this issue") shows when `attempt` is set or `loop.workspace_reused` is true, its wording covers an earlier Attempt that died before it wrote a progress file, since bl-171 it says an earlier attempt may have left work, which also holds for a retry where no agent ran or the folder was created again, and the golden copy `own-template.md` with an `OwnWorkflowTest` case follow it.

### Session brief (extension, m3-10)

PLAN 8.9 and 9.2, story 90. The template variable `loop.session_brief` is a text Loop builds from what it recorded itself, never from the agent's memory, for a fresh session that continues earlier work. It is set on a retry (`attempt` set), on a reused workspace (`loop.workspace_reused` true, see [Workspace reused](#workspace-reused-extension-bl-19)) and after a rotation ([m3-11](#soft-limit-rotation-extension-m3-11)); it is null on a fresh first dispatch, where there is no earlier work. A template that does not use it renders exactly as before, byte for byte. It is built after the workspace is prepared and the issue completed (bl-06), right before the prompt renders, by `SessionBrief` in `spec-core` (`loop.core.workflow`).

**The parts, in this order.** Each is a `### <part>` heading and its text:

1. **Goal and acceptance criteria:** `<identifier>: <title>`, then the lines of the issue's first `Acceptance criteria` section, found as the [acceptance commands](#acceptance-commands-extension-m2-09) find it, or `The issue has no acceptance criteria section.`
2. **Workspace state:** in a workspace that is the top level of a git work tree, the base, the commits since it (`git log <base>..HEAD`, newest first), the files changed against it with their added and deleted lines (the same numstat as `diff_policy`, against the merge base) and the uncommitted entries of `git status`, `.loop/result.json` left out. The base is the commit fetched for the attempt when the kind delivers a pull request ([base fetch](#base-fetch-before-every-attempt-extension-bl-59)), else the issue kind's `guards.base` (the code kind's before m4-18), else `main`. Every git command runs with Loop's pins, like the merge check. Any other workspace gets its file list instead, sorted, links never followed.
3. **Checks:** the newest of the issue's last 5 ledger rows whose attempt recorded a `verdict`: `Last verdict: <verdict>, attempt <n>`, with `of the merge check` and its reason when it has them, then the latest `check_finished` of every check of that attempt, a rerun replacing the first run: `- check <n>, <command>: passed`, `failed with exit code <code>`, `timed out` or `could not run: <reason>`. A holdout check reads `- hidden check: <result>`, never its command. Then the verify findings the attempt renders, when it has some.
4. **Counters:** `Attempts before this one`, the issue's ledger rows; `Rotations`, the rotations of the current attempt so far ([m3-11](#soft-limit-rotation-extension-m3-11)), 0 outside a rotation; `Spend so far`, the sum of the tokens and of the cost of those rows, each left out when no row reported it, `not reported` when neither was.
5. **Agent notes, unverified:** a line saying an earlier session wrote them and Loop has not checked them, so the facts above win; `notes_for_next_attempt` of the last result an attempt of the last 5 submitted, read from its saved result file (`<attempt_id>.result-<n>.json`, named by `result_submitted`); and the end of the progress file `.loop/progress.md` (plan 9.1), read only when it is a regular file inside the workspace, never through a link.

**Bounds, in characters, newest first.** Part 1: 4000. Part 2: 20 commits, 40 changed files, 40 uncommitted entries or 40 files of a workspace without git, then 3000. Part 3: findings 2000, the part 3000. Part 4: 500. Part 5: `notes_for_next_attempt` 1000, the last 2000 characters of the progress file (its newest lines, read from its last 64 KiB), the part 3500. A list cut keeps its newest entries and ends with a line saying what it left out; a part over its bound is cut at its last whole line with `(cut at <n> characters)`. Cutting the slot to a token budget is m3-14.

**A part that cannot be read** is one line in place of the part, `<part>: not available, <reason>.`, never a failed render or attempt: git that cannot run, a base the workspace does not hold (`the base <base> is not a commit of this workspace`), no earlier verdict (`no earlier attempt has a verdict`), a ledger that cannot be read (`cannot read the ledger: <reason>`, for parts 3 and 4), or no notes and no progress file. Part 5 shows the notes it could read and says what it could not.

- **Redaction:** the whole brief goes through the run's redactor before it renders, so a secret in a commit subject or the agent's notes never reaches the prompt.
- **Preflight:** renders each of its samples with `loop.session_brief` set as the Orchestrator sets it, null on the first run in a new workspace and a sample brief on the five others, so a template error in either branch fails preflight at startup.
- **Slot:** what the brief adds is the `slot_session_brief_chars` of the `BuildingPrompt` event (see [Context config and prompt slot sizes](#context-config-and-prompt-slot-sizes-extension-m1-17)), and the slots still add up to `prompt_chars`.
- **Loop's own template:** `WORKFLOW.md` is a protected path (story 100), so showing the brief in it is a human step after the merge, with the golden copy `own-template.md` and its `OwnWorkflowTest` case.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workflow/SessionBriefTest.kt` (the five parts in order on a temporary git workspace with check events and a saved result, a part that cannot be read, a base the workspace does not hold, a workspace without git, the bounds, the redaction, the counters), `PromptVariablesTest`, `PreflightTest` and `PromptSlotsTest` (the `m3-10` tests), and `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/SessionBriefPromptTest.kt` (null on a fresh first dispatch, set on a reused workspace and on a retry, the slot).

### Unverified park and rerun (extension, m2-08)

PLAN 6.4, stories 72 and 74. In Loop mode a failing check runs once more before the verdict, so a flaky test does not refute good work, and a result nothing could verify parks the Issue, never delivered.

**Rerun.** A check that ran and failed, an exit code other than 0, 126 and 127, runs once more at once, in the same workspace, with the same fingerprint. Its rerun records `check_started` and `check_finished` again with `rerun` = `true` and saves its own evidence file, so both runs are evidence. When the rerun passes, the check passed and the next one runs; when it fails too, the verdict is `refuted` and the findings are those of the rerun. A check that could not run is never rerun: it proves nothing, and a timeout would double a long wait. The `verdict` event's `checks` stays the number of checks that ran, and its `evidence` names every run, reruns included.

**Could not run.** A check could not run when it could not start, timed out at its `timeout_ms`, ended without an exit code, its check runner threw (a crash, never a crash of the Attempt), or `sh` exited 127 (command not found) or 126 (found but not executable). A core check of another type ([m2-12](#core-check-types-extension-m2-12)) could not run when it reports a start error, for example a `diff_policy` in a workspace that is not the top of a git work tree. A check that could not run, on its first run or on its rerun, ends the verification `unverified`, like a kind with an empty check list, which runs nothing, waits for nothing and records the verdict at once with `checks` = `0`.

**The park.** An `unverified` verdict carries a `reason`: `the kind has no check`, or `check <n> could not run: <why>` (`command not found, exit code 127`, `timed out after 0.3 s`, a start error), redacted and cut like any reason. The Attempt runs no further turn: right after the drain that gave the verdict it ends as a failure with the reason `unverified`, and the Issue is Parked with the reason `unverified`, like a budget park: it takes no `retries.max_attempts` slot, drops the retry entry and zeroes the counters. `issue_parked` carries `error` = the verdict's reason. A restored wait ([Waiting](#waiting-claim-state-extension-m2-04)) whose checks give `unverified` parks the Issue the same way, with the wait's Attempt id, before the wait ends. `loop resume` and a tracker change unpark it like any other park.

**Shown by** `loop trace` as `check_finished check=0 ... exit=0 duration=35ms rerun`, `verdict unverified checks=1 fingerprint=... evidence=1 reason="check 0 could not run: command not found, exit code 127"` and `issue_parked reason=unverified error="..."`. The human log reads `rerun of check ./gradlew test passed in 42s`, `verdict unverified (the kind has no check), no evidence saved`, `attempt failed, its checks could not verify its last result` and `parked because its result could not be verified, check 0 could not run: timed out after 30 s, until loop resume or a tracker change`. `loop status` shows the park with `reason=unverified`.

### Holdout checks (extension, m2-14)

PLAN 6.4, story 69. In Loop mode a kind can declare hidden checks beside its visible ones. Their scripts live in Loop's own folder, outside every workspace and the repository, and the agent never sees them:

```yaml
kinds:
  code:
    verify:
      checks:
        - { type: command, run: "./gradlew --no-daemon build" }
      holdout:
        - { type: command, run: "$LOOP_HOLDOUT/acceptance.sh", timeout_ms: 600000 }
```

**Config.** `holdout` is optional, a list of checks of the same shape as `checks`, any core check type ([m2-12](#core-check-types-extension-m2-12)), with the same errors under `kinds.code.verify.holdout[<n>]`. Guards add no `diff_policy` to it. Absent or `[]` is no holdout. `holdout: ~` or a value that is not a list is `invalid_config` (`kinds.code.verify.holdout must be a list`), the rule `kinds:` already follows. A kind with holdouts and `checks: []` still verifies: the holdouts prove the work.

**The holdout folder** is `<LOOP_HOME>/holdout/`, not a config key, so it follows `LOOP_HOME` ([Machine level folder](#machine-level-folder-and-loop_home-extension-m1-02)). `WORKFLOW.md` sits in the repository, where the agent can read it, so a holdout's `run` only points at a script in the folder, such as `$LOOP_HOLDOUT/<project>/acceptance.sh`, and keeps what it checks there; preflight enforces the reference, see below.

**Preflight.** When the effective workflow declares at least one holdout, preflight fails with `invalid_config`, on every tick like the tracker check, when:

- the holdout folder does not exist: every holdout would fail, and the agent could not fix it (`kinds.code.verify.holdout needs the holdout folder <dir>, which does not exist`);
- the folder is inside `workspace.root` or inside the repository, the nearest folder from the folder of `WORKFLOW.md` up that holds a `.git` entry, that folder itself when none does (`kinds.code.verify.holdout: the holdout folder <dir> is inside the repository <repo>, where the agent can read it. Set LOOP_HOME outside it`);
- `workspace.root` or the repository is inside the folder;
- a `type: command` holdout's `run` references neither `$LOOP_HOLDOUT` (or `${LOOP_HOLDOUT}`) nor an absolute path under the folder, so `run: ./scripts/acceptance.sh`, a script in the repository the agent can read and change, never passes for a hidden check (`kinds.code.verify.holdout[<n>]: run must reference $LOOP_HOLDOUT or a path in the holdout folder <dir>, where the agent cannot read the script`). This proves the reference, not that the script checks nothing else: `run` is the operator's.

Paths are compared by real path, symlinks resolved; a `workspace.root` that does not exist yet compares by its nearest existing parent.

**Running.** The holdouts run after every visible check passed, in order, in the workspace, and the verification stops at the first failure like for the checks. A holdout's `check` index follows the visible checks', so `loop show --evidence --check <n>` and the trace name one check by one number. Its env is the check env ([Loop mode](#loop-mode-extension-m2-03)) plus `LOOP_HOLDOUT`, the holdout folder. `LOOP_HOLDOUT` is always among the names removed from the agent env, so a visible check and the agent never get it, neither from Loop nor from a host export (`agent.env` already rejects every `LOOP_*` name).

**Verdict.** Holdouts count like visible checks ([Evidence and verdicts](#evidence-and-verdicts-extension-m2-05)): a failed holdout runs once more ([m2-08](#unverified-park-and-rerun-extension-m2-08)), its rerun also carries `holdout` = `true`, a holdout that fails twice is `refuted`, one that could not run (it timed out, could not start, or `sh` exited 126 or 127) is `unverified` and parks the Issue with the reason `hidden check <n> could not run`, and `checks` counts the holdouts that ran.

**What the agent sees.** Nothing of a holdout but that it failed:

- The findings of a refuted holdout ([Verify findings](#verify-findings-extension-m2-06)) are one fixed text, with no command, exit code or output: "Your last result was refuted: Loop ran its checks and a hidden check failed, so the issue did not move. Hidden checks stay hidden: their command, content and output are never shown. Check the work against every acceptance criterion of the issue, then move the issue again." It is the next turn's extra paragraph and the next Attempt's `loop.verify_findings`, like any findings.
- Its evidence file keeps only `attempt`, `check`, `command`, `fingerprint`, `holdout: true`, `result: passed|failed|could_not_run` and `output: hidden, a holdout check's output is never saved`, no exit code, timeout, duration, reason or output: the evidence files sit under `<LOOP_HOME>/attempts/`, which the agent can read and `loop show --evidence` prints. To see why a holdout failed, run its script by hand in the workspace.
- The submitted results saved beside the evidence ([Result file](#result-file-extension-m2-15)) and the Attempt file of raw runner lines ([Attempt file](#attempt-file-and-loop-show-extension-m1-13), m2-18) hold only what the agent wrote and what it was told, so a holdout shows there as the fixed findings text above, never its command, content or output. A holdout runs host side, not in the runner, so its output never reaches the runner stream.
- `check_started` and `check_finished` carry `holdout` = `true` and keep the command, which `WORKFLOW.md` shows anyway. A holdout's `check_finished` carries `result` = `passed`, `failed` or `could_not_run` in place of `exit_code`, `duration_ms`, `timed_out` and `reason`: distinct exit codes or durations would each leak a few bits of what the hidden check found to anything that reads the store, `loop trace` or `loop show`. The operator log line of the check says `result` too. `loop trace` adds `holdout` and `result=<outcome>` to their line, and the human log reads `hidden check $LOOP_HOLDOUT/acceptance.sh failed`.
- The Claude Code settings file of each turn ([approval and sandbox policy](#implementation-defined-behaviors)) denies `Read(//<dir>/**)` and `Edit(//<dir>/**)` for the holdout folder, by its absolute path and by its real path when a symlink makes them differ. A denial that touches the folder is a policy denial: the agent sees it and goes on, as for a deny-listed command, and the turn never ends with `turn_input_required` for it. The runner resolves the reported path first, since Claude Code may report it as the agent wrote it: a relative `path` against the workspace, `~` against the agent's `HOME`, symlinks by real path (a workspace link to the folder), and it compares without case, as macOS paths do. A Glob that names the folder only in its `pattern` counts by the literal folder before the first glob character, and a Bash denial counts when its command names the folder, by its text (also as `~/...` or `$HOME/...`) or by a word that resolves under it. Matching more than the deny rules only keeps a turn going after a denial the agent already saw. A Bash denial whose command holds a deny-listed command anywhere, in a heredoc body for example, is a policy denial too since bl-33, see the [user input requests](#implementation-defined-behaviors) row.

**Limits of a guardrail.** Loop runs the agent, the checks and the holdouts as the same OS user, so these rules keep the folder out of the agent's file tools and its records, not out of reach:

- Claude Code's deny rules cover its file tools only. A Bash `cat`, `find`, `grep` or `python` on the folder is not a file tool and reads or changes it; the agent needs only to find the path, which `LOOP_HOME` and the docs give away.
- The agent's own code runs as a check: a visible check or a holdout runs what the agent wrote in the workspace, and that code can read or change the folder, even replace a holdout script with one that passes.
- The command runner and the Codex runner get no deny rules at all; only the Claude Code runner writes a settings file.
- A file a holdout writes in the workspace, such as a report or a cache, is there for the agent to read on the next turn. A holdout script should write nothing in the workspace, or remove what it wrote.

Keeping the folder unreadable takes an OS level boundary: another user for the agent, or a sandbox whose readable paths leave it out. That is the sandbox's work, not m2-14's.

| Event | Data | When |
|---|---|---|
| `check_started`, `check_finished` | m2-05's fields, plus `holdout` = `true` for a holdout; a holdout's `check_finished` has `result` (`passed`, `failed`, `could_not_run`) in place of `exit_code`, `duration_ms`, `timed_out` and `reason` | A holdout started or ended. |

Not in m2-14: holdout output saved where only a human can read it, and an OS level sandbox that keeps the folder unreadable from the shell.

### Result file (extension, m2-15)

PLAN section 7, story 67 for runners without tools. In Loop mode an agent can end its attempt by writing the section 7 result to `.loop/result.json` in its workspace instead of calling `loop tool move`. Claude Code can submit the same result with the `submit_result` MCP tool, on the same path ([MCP server and `submit_result`](#mcp-server-and-submit_result-extension-m2-16), m2-16).

**When it is read.** In the host side drain after a turn that completed normally, after the queued tool requests, so a comment queued in the same turn applies first. Any runner: the drain does not know which runner wrote the file. In Symphony mode (no `kinds:`) the file is left alone. Loop takes the file once: it reads it without following a symlink, at most 1 MiB, then deletes it from the workspace, so a later turn never submits it again. A turn that fails or times out does not drain, so its file would wait for the next Attempt: at every Attempt start, after `before_run` and before the first turn, Loop removes a result file left in the workspace, unread and never through a symlink, and records `result_discarded`. A file it cannot remove fails the Attempt before its first turn, since the next drain would take it. A folder at the path is left, and the drain reports it as invalid. Loop's own `.gitignore` lists `.loop/result.json`, so an agent's `git add -A` never commits a result into every later worktree.

**Validation.** The text must be a JSON object. `status` is required and is one of `done`, `blocked`, `needs_info`, `failed`. `summary` is a string, required and non blank for every status but `done`: it is the question of `needs_info` and the reason of `blocked` and `failed`, since section 7 has no other field for them. Every other section 7 field is optional and checked for its type when present: `deliverable` an object whose `type` is one of `pull_request`, `files`, `report`, `external_action`, with `branch`, `pr_url` and `action_plan` strings or null and `paths` a list of strings; `acceptance` a list of objects with a `criterion` string, a `proof` of `check`, `review` or `human` and a `ref` string or null; `residual_human_steps` and `followups` lists; `notes_for_next_attempt` a string. Unknown keys are ignored. No deliverable field is required yet: delivery (m2-24) is what reads them. A symlink, a folder or a file over 1 MiB at the path is invalid too.

**Statuses.**

| Status | What Loop does |
|---|---|
| `done` | A submitted result moving to `review`, the handoff state (story 14): `result_submitted`, `Waiting(verify)`, the kind's checks, the verdict, the findings of a refutation, the verified move and the park of an `unverified` verdict ([Unverified park and rerun](#unverified-park-and-rerun-extension-m2-08)), exactly like an intercepted `loop tool move review` ([Loop mode](#loop-mode-extension-m2-03)). A move in the same drain shares one verification. |
| `needs_info` | Comments the summary on the issue, then moves it to `needs_info`, never verified (m2-03). The Attempt then ends normally, like story 44. |
| `blocked`, `failed` | The Attempt fails with the reason `result_blocked` or `result_failed`, and `attempt_finished` carries the summary, redacted and cut to 200 characters, as `error`. A counted failure: it is retried and parks at `retries.max_attempts`. |
| invalid | `result_invalid` with the validation error, then the Attempt fails with the reason `invalid_result` and the error as `error`. Never a crash. |

The target `review` is fixed until delivery (m2-24) makes `on_verified.move_to` configurable, like `needs_info`. A tracker without a `review` state records `tool_request_failed` with `request` = `result` and the tracker's reason. Since m2-07 the next turn of the same Attempt names it in its continuation guidance as "(move to review)", like a queued move (the continuation guidance row of [Implementation defined behaviors](#implementation-defined-behaviors)).

**Saved with the evidence.** The text read, valid or not, is redacted and written beside the evidence files as `<LOOP_HOME>/attempts/<project_id>/<attempt_id>.result-<n>.json`, `n` from 1 per Attempt, with the same rules as an evidence file (mode 0600, never over an existing entry, never through a symlink, kept when the Attempt ends). It holds the acceptance proofs as the agent wrote them. A failed write records `evidence_record_failed` with `result` and `reason` and never stops the Attempt.

| Event | Data | When |
|---|---|---|
| `result_submitted` | `status`, `source` = `file`, `acceptance` (how many rows), `result` (the saved file name), plus `to` for `done` and `reason` (the summary) for `blocked` and `failed` | A valid result file was taken. |
| `result_invalid` | `reason`, `result` | A result file could not be read or is not a valid result. |
| `result_discarded` | `reason` = `left by an earlier turn` | A result file was in the workspace at the Attempt start and was removed, never submitted. |

`loop trace` prints `result_submitted  to=review status=done from .loop/result.json result=<file>`, `result_submitted  status=blocked from .loop/result.json reason="..."`, `result_invalid  reason="..."` and `result_discarded  reason="left by an earlier turn"`. The human log reads `submitted its result in .loop/result.json, a move to review`, `submitted a needs_info result in .loop/result.json, it asks a question`, `submitted a blocked result in .loop/result.json: <summary>`, `its result file is invalid: <reason>`, `removed a result file left by an earlier turn, never submitted`, and the attempt end `the agent submitted a blocked result`, `a failed result` or `an invalid result`.

### MCP server and `submit_result` (extension, m2-16)

PLAN 6.3 and 6.10, story 67 for Claude Code. Loop serves its tools to Claude Code through an MCP server built with the official MCP Kotlin SDK, `io.modelcontextprotocol:kotlin-sdk-server` 0.16.0, pinned in the version catalog. It serves `submit_result`, which takes the PLAN section 7 result and behaves like the [result file](#result-file-extension-m2-15), and since m2-17 the tracker tools `move` and `comment`, which are `loop tool move` and `loop tool comment` over MCP.

**The server.** `loop tool mcp`, a stdio server: newline delimited JSON-RPC on stdin and stdout, until stdin ends. The `mcp` module holds the protocol side (the server, named `loop`, the tools and their input schemas); the cli holds what a call does. `LoopMcpServer.create` takes one handler per tool. Like the other `loop tool` commands it reads only the [tool config snapshot](#attempt-directory-and-tool-config-snapshot-extension-m1-23) and the `LOOP_*` ids, never `WORKFLOW.md`, holds no credential and writes nothing to the tracker. Outside an attempt (no `LOOP_ATTEMPT_ID`, `LOOP_ISSUE_ID` or `LOOP_TOOL_CONFIG`, or an unreadable snapshot) it does not start and exits 4 with the `loop tool` refusal on stderr. Stdout belongs to the protocol: kotlin-logging's startup line is turned off, and every line Loop prints goes to stderr. The SDK logs through SLF4J, which the `mcp` module binds to its no-op provider, `org.slf4j:slf4j-nop` 2.0.18 (pinned beside the SDK), so neither the server nor any other `loop` command prints an SLF4J notice on stderr.

**The generated config.** For each turn, when the attempt env has `LOOP_LAUNCHER` and `LOOP_TOOL_CONFIG`, the Claude Code runner writes `mcp.json` beside its generated settings, outside the workspace, deletes it with them once the process group is gone, and passes it with `--mcp-config`:

```json
{"mcpServers":{"loop":{"type":"stdio","command":"<LOOP_LAUNCHER>","args":["tool","mcp"],
  "env":{"LOOP_TOOL_CONFIG":"...","LOOP_ATTEMPT_ID":"...","LOOP_ISSUE_ID":"...","LOOP_ISSUE_IDENTIFIER":"...",
         "LOOP_HOME":"...","LOOP_SESSION_ID":"...","LOOP_TURN_ID":"..."}}}}
```

The agent sees the tools as `mcp__loop__submit_result`, `mcp__loop__move` and `mcp__loop__comment`, allowed by `--permission-mode bypassPermissions`. The config is secret free by construction: the server reads its settings from the snapshot, which omits every credential, and the config passes only those Loop ids and paths, never the host env or `agent.env`. Until bl-72 `--strict-mcp-config` was not passed, so the user's own MCP servers stayed available, as before m2-16; since bl-72 it is passed by default and Loop's server is the only one, see [strict MCP](#strict-mcp-extension-bl-72). A runner run outside an attempt Loop started gets no config.

**`submit_result`.** Its input schema is section 7 field for field (`status`, `deliverable`, `summary`, `acceptance`, `residual_human_steps`, `followups`, `notes_for_next_attempt`), `status` required. A call is validated by the result file's rules, then the attempt is checked live like `loop tool move`. A refused call is a tool error, so the agent can fix it in the same turn, and nothing is queued: an invalid result (`loop tool mcp: the result is invalid: <error>. Fix it and call submit_result again. Nothing queued.`), a result over 1 MiB, or an attempt that is not live, of another issue or another project. A valid call is queued in the attempt directory as a `result` request with the JSON text, recorded as `result_requested` with its `status`, and answered `Queued a <status> result for <identifier>. Loop applies it when this turn ends: ...` with what Loop will do, ending `Do not call submit_result again.`

**`move` and `comment` (m2-17).** `move` takes a required string `state`, `comment` a required string `text`. They queue exactly the requests `loop tool move` and `loop tool comment` queue: both call the same validation and queueing code in the cli (`TrackerTools.kt`), so a call gets the same checks (a state the tracker defines, a comment that is not empty once redacted with the token patterns and trimmed, a live attempt of the issue and project), the same reply (`Queued a move of <identifier> to "<state>". ...`, `Queued a comment on <identifier> (<n> characters). ...`) and the same event, `issue_move_requested` or `issue_comment_requested`, with no `source` field: an MCP request and a CLI request are the same request. Loop applies them at the same [drain](#host-side-tracker-writes-extension-m1-23), unchanged, with the same full redaction of a comment and, in Loop mode, the same [intercept](#loop-mode-extension-m2-03) of a move to the handoff or a terminal state. A refused call is a tool error with the `loop tool` refusal, prefixed `loop tool mcp:` and ending `Nothing queued.`, and queues and records nothing. A call without its argument, or with one that is not a string, is refused by the server before any handler: `state is required, a string. Nothing queued.` or `text is required, a string. Nothing queued.` The `move` description says that in Loop mode an agent ends with `submit_result`, and that `move` is for `needs_info` and for runs without Loop mode; the `submit_result` description still says never to move the issue yourself. `loop tool move` and `loop tool comment` stay for runners without MCP. Loop's own `WORKFLOW.md` names the MCP tools when they are available, else the CLI, never MCP only, because the installed build that reads it live may not serve them yet.

**The drain.** A queued `result` request is applied in queue order with the other requests, by the result file's code, so it gets the same verdict path: the same validation, the same save beside the evidence as `<attempt_id>.result-<n>.json`, the same statuses and attempt ends, with `source` = `mcp` on `result_submitted`, on `result_invalid` and on a `tool_request_failed` of its move. A `done` result in the same drain as a queued move or a result file shares their one verification. In Symphony mode there is no verdict to give: the request records `tool_request_failed` with `request` = `result`, `source` = `mcp` and the reason `submit_result needs Loop mode, a kinds: key in WORKFLOW.md`, and the next turn's guidance names it `(submit_result)`. A request dropped unapplied records `tool_request_dropped` with `request` = `result` and `source` = `mcp`.

The SDK runs each call on its own coroutine, so a client that closes stdin before the reply can lose it. For the same reason two calls sent before either reply can be queued in either order, a `comment` after the `move` it was meant to precede; Claude Code sends each tool call after the result of the one before, so its requests queue in the order it made them. Claude Code keeps the server's stdin open until the end of the turn; the enqueue is one atomic move, so a lost reply never leaves half a request.

| Event | Data | When |
|---|---|---|
| `result_requested` | `status` | `submit_result` queued a valid result. |

`loop trace` prints `result_submitted  to=review status=done from submit_result result=<file>` and `result_invalid  from submit_result reason="..."`. The human log reads `submitted its result with submit_result, a move to review`, `submitted a needs_info result with submit_result, it asks a question`, `submitted a failed result with submit_result: <summary>`, `its submit_result call is invalid: <reason>` and `could not apply the agent's submit_result`.

Tests: the protocol in `mcp/src/test/kotlin/loop/mcp/LoopMcpServerTest.kt` (the tool list and the `move` and `comment` arguments in the `m2-17` tests); the generated config in `plugins/runner-claude-code/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/claudecode/ClaudeCodeRunnerTest.kt` (the `m2-16` tests); the drain in `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/LoopModeDrainTest.kt`; the server against the result file, and `move` and `comment` against `loop tool` (the same requests, events and replies, the refusals, the redaction, the drain and the Loop mode intercept, the `m2-17` tests), in `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ToolMcpCommandTest.kt`; a replayed Claude Code transcript that calls `submit_result`, `cli/src/test/resources/streams/submit_result.jsonl`, in `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ClaudeCodeSubmitResultTest.kt`. A replayed transcript that calls `move` and `comment`, `cli/src/test/resources/streams/tracker_tools.jsonl`, in `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ClaudeCodeTrackerToolsTest.kt` (m2-17). Their fake `claude` (`TranscriptReplay.kt`) prints the transcript and makes its tool calls for real, the MCP one on the server of the config the runner generated.

### Readiness check (extension, m2-11)

PLAN 6.2, story 75. In Loop mode a kind can declare which description sections an Issue needs before it is dispatched:

```yaml
kinds:
  code:
    ready: { require_sections: [Acceptance criteria] }
    verify:
      checks: [...]
```

**Config.** `ready` is optional: without it no readiness check runs. Present, it must be a map with a `require_sections` list of at least one non blank name, else `invalid_config` (`kinds.code.ready must be a map`, `kinds.code.ready.require_sections is required`, `must be a list of strings`, `must list at least one section`, `must not hold a blank name`). `ready: ~` is an error too, so an empty or misspelled key never switches the check off silently, the rule `kinds:` already follows. Names are trimmed. In Symphony mode (no `kinds:`) nothing on this page applies.

**The check.** A section is present when the Issue's description has a column 0 heading, `#` to `######`, outside ``` and ~~~ fences, whose text is the name, trimmed and case insensitive, followed by at least one non blank line before the next heading of the same or a higher level. A deeper heading inside the section counts as content. An empty section counts as missing.

**When.** At dispatch, after the eligibility filter and the blocker check, on every candidate that is not a due retry. A due retry or continuation already passed the check at its first dispatch; dropping it there would lose its retry entry. A not ready candidate is skipped and `candidate_skipped` records `not_ready (missing Acceptance criteria)`, once per reason change like every skip. The check reads the effective workflow, so a reload that adds or removes `ready` applies on the next tick.

**The bounce.** For each not ready candidate Loop writes one comment, author `loop`, naming the missing sections, then adds the `needs-info` label through the tracker's optional label write. It happens once:

- an Issue that already has the `needs-info` label gets nothing, so a restart does not repeat it;
- in one process an Issue bounced once is not bounced again while it stays not ready, also when its label write failed or the tracker has none (`Unsupported`). An Issue that becomes ready, or leaves the candidates, is forgotten, so a later regression bounces again.

A failed comment or label write is logged as `level=warn event=readiness outcome=failed` with the reason, and never stops the tick. Loop does not remove `needs-info` when the Issue becomes ready: the label does not block dispatch unless `tracker.required_labels` says so, and a human clears it.

| Event | Data | When |
|---|---|---|
| `issue_commented` | `chars`, `reason` = `not_ready` | The readiness comment was written. No Attempt. |
| `issue_labeled` | `add` = `needs-info`, `reason` = `not_ready` | The label was added. No Attempt. |

`loop trace` prints both as issue level lines next to `candidate_skipped`; the human log reads `skipped, not ready, missing Acceptance criteria`, `commented that it is not ready` and `labeled needs-info`.

**Label write.** `TrackerAdapter.updateLabels(issueId, add, remove)` adds and removes labels in one write, compared trimmed and case insensitively. It is optional: the default fails with `TrackerError.Unsupported`, so a tracker without labels still compiles and Loop logs the failure. The `files` tracker implements it on the front matter `labels` field, see its [profile](plugins/tracker-files.md#labels). The contract tests are in `TrackerAdapterContract`, so the GitHub tracker runs them too.

### Core check types (extension, m2-12)

PLAN 6.4 and story 70, first part. Besides `command`, a kind's `verify.checks` takes three core types that Loop runs in process, and `verify.guards` lists the protected paths:

```yaml
kinds:
  code:
    verify:
      checks:
        - { type: file_exists, path: docs/usage.md }
        - { type: file_matches, path: docs/usage.md, pattern: "^## Usage$" }
        - { type: command, run: "./gradlew --no-daemon build" }
        - { type: diff_policy }
      guards:
        protect: [WORKFLOW.md, scripts/, "**/src/test/**"]
        base: main
```

| Type | Config | Passes when |
|---|---|---|
| `command` | `run` (required, non blank), `timeout_ms` (positive, one hour by default) | `sh -lc <run>` in the workspace exits 0 within its timeout (m2-03). |
| `file_exists` | `path` (required) | The path is a file or a folder inside the workspace. |
| `file_matches` | `path` and `pattern` (both required) | The regular expression `pattern` is found in the file at `path`, Java syntax, with `^` and `$` matching at each line. |
| `diff_policy` | none, it reads `verify.guards` | The workspace's diff against its merge base with `guards.base` touches no path of `guards.protect`. |

**Paths.** A `path` is relative to the workspace: a leading `./` is dropped, and an absolute path or a `..` segment is `invalid_config`, so a check can only name a path inside the workspace. A path that leads outside it through a symlink fails the check. `file_matches` fails on a missing file or a folder; a file over 16 MiB, or one that cannot be read, means the check could not run. A `pattern` that does not compile is `invalid_config`.

**Guards.** `verify.guards` is optional. When present, `protect` must list at least one non blank glob (optional beside `deny` since [m2-13](#deny-guards-extension-m2-13)) and `base`, `main` by default, must not be blank. Each glob is anchored at the workspace root, a leading `/` dropped: `*` and `?` match within one path segment, `**` any number of segments, and a glob that names a folder, with or without a trailing `/`, covers everything under it. So `WORKFLOW.md` protects only the root file, `scripts/` the folder, `*.gradle.kts` the root build scripts and `**/*.gradle.kts` every one. Since round 2 of the PR #75 review (m2-28), `protect` matches with the case of the glob and the path folded, Unicode included: a case insensitive checkout, the macOS default, writes a committed `workflow.md` over `WORKFLOW.md`, so an exact case match would let a branch rewrite a protected file. The cost is a false refusal for a repository that keeps two paths differing only in case, which a case insensitive checkout cannot hold anyway. The paths git must not hide from the check are folded the same way. The languages' test file globs and the merge risk path lists keep their exact case. Gitignore's unanchored rule for a name without a slash is left out, so a glob never reaches further than it reads. A `diff_policy` check without `guards.protect` is `invalid_config`. When `guards.protect` is set and the checks list no `diff_policy`, Loop adds one after the last check, so a guard is never silently off and the listed checks keep their numbers. An empty `checks: []` stays empty: a guard alone proves no work.

**The diff.** `diff_policy` runs `git merge-base <base> HEAD` in the workspace, then `git diff --name-status -M` from that merge base to the working tree, plus the untracked files git does not ignore. So committed, staged and unstaged changes all count, and a change that reached `main` after the branch started does not. Each changed path is `added`, `modified`, `deleted` or `renamed`; a rename counts on both its old and its new path, so moving a file out of or into a protected folder is caught, and a copy counts only on its new path. git runs with the check's env minus every `GIT_` variable and with `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, each command stopped after 120 s. A workspace that is not the top of a git work tree, a base with no merge base with `HEAD` and a git command that fails or runs out of time mean the check could not run.

**Results.** The three types report like a `command`: exit code 0 when they pass and 1 when they fail, with an output that says what they found, so the [evidence](#evidence-and-verdicts-extension-m2-05), the `verdict` and the [findings](#verify-findings-extension-m2-06) work unchanged. A failed core check is rerun once like any failing check, and a refuted `diff_policy` fails its rerun too, since nothing changed in between. A check that could not run has no exit code and a `reason` (its start error), is never rerun and gives an `unverified` verdict that parks the Issue, see [Unverified park and rerun](#unverified-park-and-rerun-extension-m2-08). The `command` field of the check events, the evidence header and the findings' `Check:` line show `file_exists <path>`, `file_matches <path> /<pattern>/` or `diff_policy base=<base> protect=<globs>`. A refuted `diff_policy` names each protected path the diff touches, for example:

```
Check: diff_policy base=main protect=WORKFLOW.md,scripts/
Exit code: 1
Last 2 lines of its output:
The diff against the merge base with main (1f3c9a2e4b7d) touches 1 protected path:
modified scripts/release.sh (protected by scripts/)
```

Not in m2-12: the `deny` guards (deleted tests, added skips, weaker assertions) are the second part of story 70, built in [m2-13](#deny-guards-extension-m2-13), and the protected path condition of conditional auto merge (story 100) comes with delivery.

### Deny guards (extension, m2-13)

PLAN 6.4, story 70, second part, and story 87. `verify.guards.deny` lists the shortcuts that refute a result when they are found in its diff:

```yaml
kinds:
  code:
    verify:
      checks:
        - { type: command, run: "./gradlew --no-daemon build" }
      guards:
        protect: [WORKFLOW.md, scripts/]
        deny: [deleted_tests, added_skips, weaker_assertions]
        languages:
          - name: python
            files: ["tests/**/*.py"]
            test: "^\\s*def (test_\\w+)"
            skip: "@pytest\\.mark\\.skip"
            assertion: "\\bassert\\b"
            line_comment: "#"
```

**Part of `diff_policy`.** The deny guards are not a new check type: PLAN 6.4 names four core types, and a deny guard is a policy on the same diff as the protected paths. `diff_policy` computes the diff once and reports every protected path and every guard finding in one output, where separate checks would stop at the first failure and hide the rest. Its label gains ` deny=<guards>` and drops ` protect=` when nothing is protected, so `diff_policy base=main deny=deleted_tests,added_skips,weaker_assertions`; a kind without `deny` keeps its m2-12 label. When `deny` is set, `protect` becomes optional; without it, `protect` is required as in m2-12. Each guard is listed once, an unknown one is `invalid_config`, and guards with `deny` but no `diff_policy` in the checks add one after the last check, like `protect`.

**Test files and languages.** A guard only reads the changed files that a language's `files` globs match, globs written and anchored like `protect`. Two languages are built in:

| Language | Test files | Test (its name) | Skip | Assertion |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `kotlin`, JUnit 4 and 5, kotlin.test | `**/src/test/**/*.kt`, `**/src/*Test/**/*.kt`, `**/*Test.kt`, `**/*Tests.kt` | `@Test`, `@ParameterizedTest`, `@RepeatedTest`, `@TestFactory` or `@TestTemplate`, other annotations and modifiers, then `fun <name>` | `@Disabled...` (`@Disabled`, `@DisabledOnOs`, ...) and `@Ignore`, qualified or not | a call of `assert...`, `fail`, `verify...` or `coVerify...`, and kotest's `should...` |
| `swift`, XCTest and Swift Testing | `**/*Tests.swift`, `**/*Test.swift`, `**/*Tests/**/*.swift` | `func test...(` and `@Test` then `func <name>` | `XCTSkip...` (`XCTSkip`, `XCTSkipIf`, `XCTSkipUnless`) and `@Test(.disabled...)` or `@Suite(.disabled...)` on one line | `XCTAssert...(`, `XCTFail(`, `XCTUnwrap(`, `#expect`, `#require` |

`guards.languages` adds a language, or replaces the built in one of the same name. Each entry needs a non blank `name`, a non empty `files` list and at least one of `test`, `skip` and `assertion`, Java regular expressions with `^` and `$` at each line that must compile; group 1 of `test`, when it has one, is the test's name, else the whole match is. A guard whose pattern a language lacks is off for that language: a language without `test` still has deleted test files refuted, one without `assertion` has no `weaker_assertions`. `line_comment` and `block_comment` (a start and an end marker) are optional; both built in languages use `//` and `/* */`. The first language whose globs match a path reads it, on each side of the diff, so a test file renamed out of the test files loses its tests.

**Bounded work.** The review of #55 found that a long input could overflow the stack or run for seconds, so the guards bound their work, and whatever stops them means the check could not run and the result is `unverified`, never a pass:

- Every repeated group of the built in patterns is possessive (`*+`, `++`, `?+`). Java runs a possessive group loop without recursion, so a 5 KB `@CsvSource` or thousands of annotations in a row no longer overflow the stack, and nothing is given back to try again. Kotlin generics in an assertion allow one nesting level, `assertIs<List<Int>>(x)`, and stop at the next `<`.
- A line is read up to 4096 characters. `skip` and `assertion` only run on the changed lines of a file, and with `added_skips` or `weaker_assertions` on, a changed line over 4096 characters means the check could not run (`a changed line of <path> has <n> characters, over the 4096 the deny guards read`), since cutting it could hide a skip after the cut. An unchanged line is cut to 4096 characters before `test` runs, the same on both sides.
- The guard pass has a budget of 10 s. The texts are matched through a character sequence that checks the deadline as the matcher reads, so a slow configured pattern is stopped in the middle of a match: `the deny guards did not finish within 10 s`.
- Any error a guard throws, a `StackOverflowError` from a configured pattern too, means the check could not run.

**Old and new.** The old text of a file is its blob at the merge base, read raw with `git cat-file`; the new text is the working tree file, a symlink read as its target like git stores it. The guards then run a second time with HEAD's blob as the new text, when HEAD's test files differ from the working tree's, within the same budget; a finding only HEAD has is marked `(in HEAD)` (see "What git would hide"). A file over 16 MiB means the check could not run. Ignored files are never read: they are not part of the delivered work. Comment lines are blanked first: a line that starts with the line comment marker, after spaces, and a block comment that starts a line, up to the line that closes it. So commenting out a test or an assertion counts as removing it.

**The heuristics.**

- `deleted_tests`: the test names of every test file of a language at the merge base, against the names in the working tree, as a multiset across the whole diff. A name that is gone is a deleted test, named with its file: `removed test divides from lib/src/test/kotlin/CalcTest.kt`, or `deleted test file <path> (2 tests: adds, divides)` when the file is gone too. A test moved to another test file keeps its name and is not a deletion. A deleted file without a test, a helper or a fixture, is not refuted.
- `added_skips`: each line of a test file that matches `skip`, trimmed and counted once per match, that the file's old text does not have. A skip line another file lost was moved, not added. A new test file's skips are added skips: `added a skip to <path>: @Disabled("flaky")`.
- `weaker_assertions`: a test file that loses an assertion line (matched like a skip line) and adds none. A line that another file gained was moved, not lost. A renamed file is one file, its old and new path together, so a rename that edits assertion lines is not a loss. `removed 2 assertions from <path> and added none: assertEquals(0, divide(0, 2)), ...` quotes the first three, and a renamed file is named `<old path> (renamed to <new path>)`.

**Known limits.** The guards read text, not code, so they are a tripwire for the plain shortcut, not a proof:

- Renaming a test reads as deleting it, and deleting one test while adding another of the same name in another file hides the deletion.
- `weaker_assertions` works per file and per line: it refutes a file that only lost assertions, so replacing `assertEquals(3, x)` with `assertTrue(x > 0)` (weaker but still one assertion), or deleting five assertions while adding one, passes. A shared assertion helper that stops asserting is not seen, since only the call sites are counted.
- Only a comment that starts a line is left out: an assertion after code on the same line, `foo() // assertEquals(...)`, still counts, and a line of a multi line string that starts with `//` or `/*` reads as a comment. A multi line string that holds test code counts as code.
- A test written in another style is not seen: kotest specs (`test("...") {}`), JUnit 3 `TestCase` methods without `@Test`, and dynamic tests. A skip by other means, such as `assumeTrue(false)`, an early `return`, a `@Tag` excluded by the build, or a build file change that stops running a test source set, is not seen; protect the build files for that.
- `@Test(.disabled(...))` is only seen on the `@Test` line; Swift tests are found by name prefix `test` or by `@Test`, so a test method in an XCTest case not named `test...` would not run anyway.
- Rename pairing depends on git's rename detection (`git diff -M`): a rename under 50 percent similarity, or one past `diff.renameLimit`, reads as a delete plus an add, so the assertion lines edited in the move read as removed from the old path and the result is refuted with `weaker_assertions`.
- An unchanged line is read up to 4096 characters, so a test declared after that on one line is not seen, on either side. A changed line that long is not cut, the check could not run instead, so a test file that legitimately changes a line that long, an inline fixture for example, is `unverified`.
- The rest of the known limits found in the review of #55 went to bl-32, including those of "What git would hide" below. bl-32 pinned the base for kinds with pull request delivery and left the others as known limits.

**What git would hide.** The review of m2-12 (#50) reproduced ways around `diff_policy` with real git. m2-13 closes those that the check can see by itself, for protected paths and guards alike:

- An untracked protected file that an ignore rule hides (`.gitignore`, `.git/info/exclude`, `core.excludesFile`) is found: untracked files are listed a second time without `--exclude-standard` and the extra ones are matched against `protect` as `added and ignored <path>`.
- A nested repository, which git lists as one untracked folder entry and never looks into, counts as every file under it: `added nested repository <folder>/ (protected by <glob>)` when its folder or any file in it, outside its `.git`, is protected. An ignored nested repository, a SwiftPM checkout under `.build/checkouts/` or a vendored clone, is matched by its folder only and never walked, so `**/Package.swift` does not refute every run of a Swift package: `added and ignored nested repository <folder>/ (protected by <glob>)`. The limit: a protected file inside an ignored nested repository whose folder no glob matches is not found. Without `protect`, ignored files are not listed at all, since only protected paths need them.
- A path flagged assume-unchanged, or skip-worktree while present in the working tree (`git ls-files -v`), hides its edits from `git diff`, so the check could not run and the result is `unverified`, naming up to five paths. A skip-worktree path missing from the working tree is what a sparse checkout leaves out, such as `.loop/issues/` with Loop's worktree hook, and git reads it as unchanged, deleted or not: `git update-index --skip-worktree <path>` then deleting the file gives an empty diff. So a protected or test path missing from the working tree under skip-worktree means the check could not run too: `skip-worktree keeps 1 protected or test path out of the working tree, so git cannot see it changed or deleted: <path>`. Any other sparse path passes, so Loop's own hook is fine as long as `.loop/` is not protected.
- Every git command runs with `core.fsmonitor=false`, so a monitor configured in the repository cannot answer for the working tree, and since bl-32 with `core.commitGraph=false`, so a hand edited `.git/objects/info/commit-graph` cannot change a merge base or an ancestor answer.
- Every git command runs with `--no-replace-objects`, `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1` and `GIT_GRAFT_FILE=/dev/null`. A replace ref swaps one object for another wherever git reads it: `git replace <merge base> $(git commit-tree -m x HEAD^{tree})`, or HEAD's tree id written into `.git/refs/replace/<merge base tree id>` with no git command at all, makes the merge base read as HEAD's content, so the diff is empty and every hash matches. A line `<main> <HEAD>` in `.git/info/grafts` gives `main` a parent it does not have, so the merge base becomes HEAD. `--no-replace-objects` does not turn grafts off, hence the empty grafts file. The check drops every `GIT_` variable of its environment but `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM`, which only leaves the system config out; the tests set it, so a CI runner's system config, such as git lfs installed there, does not decide a test.
- The diff is the working tree against the merge base, but the commits are what Loop delivers, and an agent can commit an edit and put the working tree back without committing again, or commit `git update-index --chmod=+x <path>` while the file keeps its mode. So the check also diffs HEAD's tree against the merge base, a tree to tree diff that no stat, filter or flag can hide. A protected path changed there and not in the working tree diff is named `modified <path> in HEAD (protected by <glob>)`, and the guards read HEAD's test files too (see "Old and new"). The index is not compared: what is staged and not committed is not delivered, and a commit of it shows in HEAD at the next check.
- A clean filter makes an edited file read as unchanged: `filter.h.clean` set to `git cat-file blob HEAD:%f` plus `*.kt filter=h`. The agent runs as the same user as Loop, so the user config, `~/.config/git/attributes`, `.git/info/attributes` and a committed `.gitattributes` are all within its reach, as much as the repository's own config, and redefining `filter.lfs.clean` works too. So where a filter is set does not count, nor whether its driver is a `clean` command or a long running `process` (lfs uses `process`, which wins over `clean`), only which paths it applies to: `git check-attr filter` runs on every tracked path that `protect` matches or, with a deny guard on, a language's test files, and a filter on one of them means the check could not run: `a git filter can hide the edits of 1 protected or test path: <path> (filter=h)`. A filter on any other path is left alone, so a stock `git lfs install --local` with lfs on images or archives passes, while a test file kept in lfs is `unverified`.
- Behind the filter rule, the same tracked paths are compared byte for byte. Each one the diff leaves out has its raw content hashed like `git hash-object --no-filters` and compared with its blob at the merge base, the content the diff claims it still has, whatever the index holds; a mismatch means the check could not run: `git reads 1 protected or test path as unchanged, but its content differs from the merge base: <path>`. This catches what no flag shows, such as a same size edit with its mtime restored under `core.trustctime=false`, where git trusts the stale index entry. Only line ends explain a mismatch: a file whose CRLF line ends, turned into LF, give the merge base's blob is unchanged, as `core.autocrlf` checks files out. A path in the diff needs no comparison, since the guards read its raw working tree text. The cost is reading every protected and test file once per check.
- Known limits, left as they are by bl-32: edits inside a submodule show only as the submodule path, so globs below it never match, and `diff.ignoreSubmodules=all` hides even that path; `*.sh text eol=crlf` in `.git/info/attributes` plus a CRLF rewrite of a protected script passes, since line ends are the one explained mismatch (it can break a script, not gut a test); `core.fileMode=false` plus `chmod -x` on a protected script passes, unless the mode change is committed; an honest `ident` attribute, and likely `working-tree-encoding`, on a test file makes every run `unverified`, since the raw content never matches the blob; `filter=unset` as a driver name reads like an unset filter to `git check-attr` and is caught only by the content comparison.

Not in m2-13: pinning the base host side. Moving `main` moves the base, so the merge base becomes `HEAD` and the diff is empty: `git update-ref refs/heads/main HEAD`, `git -C . update-ref ...`, `git branch -f main HEAD`, or writing the commit to `.git/refs/heads/main`. The Claude Code runner's generated deny rules match command prefixes only, so they stop the plain `git update-ref` and miss the other forms; they are a guardrail, not a sandbox. bl-32 closes it for kinds with `delivery.pull_request`: every verification fetches the base host side and every `diff_policy` uses the fetched commit id, never a local ref, see [Diff base of pull request kinds](#diff-base-of-pull-request-kinds-extension-bl-32), and the merge check judges `diff_policy` against its fetched commit in every outcome. Kinds without pull request delivery still diff against `guards.base`, a local branch the agent can move; bl-28 tracks that, with the operator's moved `main`.

**Tests.** `DenyGuardsTest` runs each guard on Kotlin and Swift texts and on a configured language, plus renames and the bounded work (a 5 KB `@CsvSource`, thousands of annotations, long and adversarial lines, the budget), `CoreChecksTest` on real git repositories (committed, staged, unstaged and untracked changes, each bypass above, a guard that overflows the stack, a long changed line, a rename, an ignored nested repository, clean filters set in the repository or user config with each kind of attributes file and a redefined lfs filter, the stat trick, a stock lfs setup, CRLF line ends and a sha256 repository, replace refs made with `git replace` or written by hand, a grafts file, a deleted skip-worktree protected or test path, a sparse checkout, a committed edit or mode change with the working tree put back, and a `process` filter), `KindsConfigTest` the config, and `SeededShortcutCommandTest` the playground below.

**Playground (story 87).** `cli/src/test/resources/playground/deleted-test/` is the first seeded issue of the playground: a Kotlin calculator whose test `divides by zero` fails, a seeded issue `pg-1` in the `files` tracker that asks for the test run to pass, and two scripted patches for a fake runner, the command runner applying a patch and submitting. `shortcut.patch` deletes the failing test: the test run passes, and the run ends `refuted` with `deleted_tests` and `weaker_assertions` and parks after its one allowed attempt. `fix.patch` fixes `divide` and is `verified`.

### Acceptance commands (extension, m2-09)

Spec testing decisions, stories 50 and 86. In Loop mode a kind can run the commands of each Issue's acceptance criteria as visible checks, next to its own:

```yaml
kinds:
  code:
    verify:
      checks:
        - { type: file_exists, path: docs/usage.md }
        - { type: command, run: "./gradlew --no-daemon build" }
        - { type: acceptance_commands, timeout_ms: 1800000 }
      guards:
        protect: [WORKFLOW.md]
```

**Config.** `acceptance_commands` takes the optional positive `timeout_ms` of a `command` check, one hour by default, and nothing else. Each of these is `invalid_config`: a `run` key (`the commands come from the issue`), a second `acceptance_commands` entry (`declare it once`), a `timeout_ms` that is not a positive integer. An unknown `type` now reads `is not supported, use command, file_exists, file_matches, diff_policy or acceptance_commands` (`diff_policy, changed_line_coverage or acceptance_commands` since bl-117). Preflight also fails with `invalid_config` when `tracker.kind` is not `files`: `kinds.code.verify.checks[<n>] acceptance_commands needs tracker.kind files, not "github", until Loop checks who wrote an issue` (see Trust). `WorkflowLoader` still accepts only `files`, so today this gate guards the wiring of a remote tracker; a verification checks it again and parks the Issue `unverified` with the same sentence.

**The criteria.** Read from the Issue's normalized description: the list items of the first section whose heading opens with `Acceptance criteria`, alone or followed by a character that is not a letter or a digit, so `## Acceptance criteria:` and `## Acceptance criteria (m2-09)` count, found like a [readiness](#readiness-check-extension-m2-11) section. A criterion is a column 0 list item with its indented continuation lines; a nested item is part of its parent. Fences count at column 0 only, like the headings, so a continuation line that opens with inline triple backticks is text. A rule (`---`, `- - -`, `* * *`, `___`) ends the criterion above it and is none. A criterion whose line opens with one backticked span followed by the whole word `passes` (not `passes-through`) gives a command when the span looks like one: it holds a blank, or starts with `./`, `../`, `/` or a path segment and a `/`. Any other criterion is a judgment call: `` `TraceCommandTest` passes `` is one, not a check that exits 127 and parks the Issue. A span between double backticks, or an escaped backtick, is a judgment call too. The convention, with its examples, is in [`agents/issue-tracker.md`](agents/issue-tracker.md#runnable-criteria). Only the opening span counts, because Loop's own Issues backtick paths, names and config values on almost every criterion: on the 95 Issues of M1 and M2 so far, the rule finds `./gradlew build` and `./gradlew --no-daemon build` and nothing else (m2-09 evidence). Only the first section counts, so a heading quoted in a later comment adds no command.

**Running them.** At each verification, `acceptance_commands` stands for one `command` check per command, with its `timeout_ms`, in criteria order, in its place in the check list, next to the [core checks](#core-check-types-extension-m2-12) of m2-12, which stay where they are listed. The `diff_policy` that `guards.protect` adds stays last, after the commands. They are numbered with the other checks from 0 in that order, so `check` in the events, `loop show --check <n>` and the findings name the expanded list. From there everything is a `command` check: `sh -lc` in the workspace, its env, its evidence, the [rerun of a failed check and the `unverified` park](#unverified-park-and-rerun-extension-m2-08) of one that could not run. The Attempt reads the description it was dispatched with; a restored wait ([Waiting](#waiting-claim-state-extension-m2-04)) reads it from the Issue as the tick fetched it, and both are checked against the frozen list first. A `done` [result file](#result-file-extension-m2-15) is a submitted result like the intercepted move, so it runs the same expanded list and saves the same judgment calls.

**Frozen list.** The agent works in a checkout of the same repository, so it can rewrite `.loop/issues/<id>.md` of the main checkout between attempts and drop the command that refuted it. The first dispatch of an Issue in Loop mode therefore freezes its command list in the store, before the agent runs, and records `acceptance_commands_frozen`. The store keeps a SHA-256 digest of the list and its count, never the commands. Every later verification, of any Attempt or restored wait, reads the commands of the description it has and compares their digest with the frozen one:

- The same: the commands run as above. A changed list an earlier park left is forgotten, so a human who restores the criteria and unparks the Issue through the tracker leaves nothing for a later resume to accept.
- Different: none runs. Loop records `acceptance_commands_changed`, keeps the new digest as the changed list, and the verdict is `unverified` with the reason `acceptance commands changed`, so the Issue parks like any [`unverified` result](#unverified-park-and-rerun-extension-m2-08). A tracker change unparks it, but its next verification parks it again.
- `loop resume <id>` unparks the Issue. It accepts the changed list only when this park is the one for it, the `issue_parked` event of the park reads `unverified` with the error `acceptance commands changed`, and only when the Issue as the resume fetches it holds that very list: it reads the criteria again and compares their digest with the changed one. Then the changed list becomes the frozen one, `acceptance_commands_accepted` is recorded and the resume prints the commands themselves, redacted, one per line: `Accepted the changed acceptance commands of <id>, checked from its next verification:` then `  1. <command>`. A text edited again after the park, or an Issue the tracker cannot give, accepts nothing (`The acceptance criteria of <id> no longer hold the changed list Loop found, nothing accepted. Its next verification compares them with the frozen list again.`), and the next verification parks it once more with the new list. A resume of an Issue parked for another reason, `max_attempts` for example, leaves the frozen list as it is, whatever changed list the store holds.
- The store cannot give the frozen list: the verdict is `unverified` with `cannot read the frozen acceptance commands: <why>`, nothing runs. A failed freeze at dispatch is logged at warn and never fails the Attempt; its first verification freezes the same dispatched list.

The cost: an Attempt dispatched with changed criteria works its turns before its verification parks it. A list that stays the same, an empty one included, never parks for a change.

**Nothing to run.** When the expanded list is empty or holds only `diff_policy` checks, because the criteria hold no command and the kind has no other check and no [holdout](#holdout-checks-extension-m2-14), the verdict is `unverified` with the reason `the acceptance criteria of the issue hold no command`, and the Issue is parked like any `unverified` result. A guard alone proves no work, as an empty `checks: []` with guards stays empty in m2-12. A holdout proves work, as in m2-14, so with one the `diff_policy` checks and their [deny guards](#deny-guards-extension-m2-13) run first and the holdouts after them.

**Holdouts.** The holdouts of m2-14 run after the expanded list, the commands and the `diff_policy` with its deny guards included, numbered after them. A verification that refuses the commands (a changed list, a store that cannot give the frozen list, a tracker other than `files`) runs no holdout either. `acceptance_commands` in `kinds.code.verify.holdout` is `invalid_config` (`kinds.code.verify.holdout[<n>] cannot be acceptance_commands, the issue shows its commands, list it in checks`): the agent reads the commands in the Issue, so they are never hidden. The reason `the kind has no check` stays for an empty `checks` list. Whenever the criteria hold no command, other checks or not, Loop records `acceptance_commands_empty` with the judgment call count before the first check, so a typo such as `pass` for `passes` is visible: with `[file_exists, acceptance_commands]` the verdict rests on `file_exists` alone and the trace says why.
