**Judgment calls.** A criterion without a command is never a passed check. When the kind declares `acceptance_commands` and the Issue has such criteria, Loop saves them, before the first check, as one evidence file of their own, numbered with the check runs of the Attempt, then records `judgment_calls`; the verdict carries their count. They do not change the verdict, which rests on the checks only: a human reads them when the work is handed off. A restored wait with no Attempt id saves no file and still records the event. A failed write records `evidence_record_failed` with no `check` and never stops the verification. A verification that refused the commands saves no judgment calls.

```
attempt: <attempt_id>
judgment_calls: 2
fingerprint: git:<HEAD>:<diff>:<untracked>

- The convention is stated in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
- A human reads the copy.
```

| Event | Data | When |
|---|---|---|
| `acceptance_commands_frozen` | `count` | The first dispatch, or the first verification when the dispatch could not write it, froze the Issue's command list. |
| `acceptance_commands_changed` | `frozen` (the frozen count), `count` (the count read now) | A verification found commands that differ from the frozen list; nothing runs. |
| `acceptance_commands_accepted` | `count` | `loop resume` made the changed list the frozen one. |
| `acceptance_commands_empty` | `judgment_calls` | Before the first check of a verification whose criteria hold no command. |
| `judgment_calls` | `count`, `evidence` (the file name, absent when none was saved) | Before the first check of a verification whose criteria hold a judgment call. |
| `verdict` | m2-05's fields, plus `judgment_calls` (the count, absent when none) | A verification ended. |

**Trust.** The commands come from the Issue text and run on the host with the env of a check, so a kind that declares `acceptance_commands` trusts whoever can write the acceptance criteria of a dispatchable Issue as it trusts `WORKFLOW.md`. The agent can write them: the `files` tracker reads the main checkout, and an agent can edit `.loop/issues/<id>.md` there through its workspace path, so `loop tool comment` is not its only way in. The frozen list makes such an edit park the Issue instead of running, for the Issue's own later attempts and restored waits. It does not cover an edit made before the first dispatch, by another Issue's agent for example: a human reviews the agent's diff, and the protected paths of a `diff_policy` guard can name `.loop/issues/`. A remote tracker is refused outright: anyone who can edit a GitHub issue body after it is labeled could run `sh -lc` on the host, so until Loop checks who wrote or edited an Issue, `acceptance_commands` needs the `files` tracker. Secrets in a command or a judgment call are redacted like any text Loop writes: in the events, the evidence files and the human log, never in the command that runs.

**Shown by** `loop trace` as `acceptance_commands_frozen  count=2`, `acceptance_commands_changed  frozen=2 count=1`, `acceptance_commands_accepted  count=1`, `acceptance_commands_empty  judgment_calls=2`, `judgment_calls  count=2 evidence=<file>` and `verdict  verified checks=3 ... judgment_calls=2`. The human log reads `acceptance commands frozen, 2 commands`, `acceptance criteria hold 1 command where 2 commands were frozen at the first dispatch, none runs until loop resume accepts them`, `acceptance criteria hold no command, 2 judgment calls instead`, `loop resume accepted the changed acceptance commands, 1 command`, `2 acceptance criteria are judgment calls, listed in the evidence for a human` and `verdict verified, evidence of 3 checks saved, 2 judgment calls left to a human`. `loop show --evidence` prints check runs only, `--check <n>` names the expanded list; the judgment calls file is named on the trace line.

### GitHub tracker, read side (extension, m2-22)

Story 80, first part. The `github` tracker reads the issues of one repository through the GitHub REST API; its [adapter profile](plugins/tracker-github.md) is the reference. The decisions that read the spec:

- **States:** GitHub's own `open` (active) and `closed` (terminal) are the defined states and the profile defaults of 5.3.1. Handoff states are label changes, written in m2-23 (plan 6.6).
- **Dispatch identity:** `id` is the issue number as text, the key of `GET /issues/{number}`. `identifier` is `<repo name>-<number>`, such as `example-app-454`, in the shape of the spec's `ABC-123`: unique in the repository scope, and safe in shell hooks, branch names and workspace keys, where `#454` is not. GitHub's distinct ids go to `native_ref` (17.3 item 9), which is why `Issue` gains an optional `nativeRef` map of non secret strings, null for the `files` tracker.
- **Priority:** GitHub has none, so a `priority:<n>` label gives it, the lowest `n` when there are several. The label is read into `priority` and not repeated in `labels`, the way a front matter key is not a label in the `files` tracker. An issue without one has a null priority and sorts after the prioritized ones, then by `created_at`, which GitHub does give.
- **Scope:** the repository plus optional scope labels (`tracker.provider.labels`), filtered by GitHub in a list and checked again by id, so an issue that loses a scope label is no longer visible (11.1). Pull requests, which the issues API returns too, are never issues. `tracker.required_labels` stays the orchestrator's filter (11.1), so a list returns issues without the routing label too, and reconciliation sees them.
- **Blockers:** none in m2-22: `blocked_by` was empty and every issue dispatchable rather than an invented rule (11.3). Since bl-158 the adapter reads GitHub's native issue dependencies, one request per candidate that has a blocker, and only a blocker closed as `completed` resolves, failing closed on any blocker it cannot read, see [blockers](plugins/tracker-github.md#blockers). No `Blocked by` line is read from the body, which is text people edit. Since bl-157 an issue whose author is outside `tracker.provider.trusted_associations` is not dispatchable, with the reason `untrusted_author (<ASSOCIATION>)`, see [trusted authors](plugins/tracker-github.md#trusted-authors).
- **Pagination:** the `rel="next"` link is followed as given (GitHub pages with cursors). The call is atomic. A link to another origin, a repeated link, more than 100 pages, or an issue on two pages fails it with `tracker_pagination`; an issue on two pages means the list moved and may have skipped one.
- **Rate limits:** a 429, or a 403 with `x-ratelimit-remaining: 0` or `retry-after`, fails the call with `tracker_rate_limited`. No request is sent before GitHub's retry time, also after a success that spent the last request; calls in between fail at once. The adapter never sleeps in a call: the orchestrator's next tick is the retry (11.4).
- **Error categories:** `TrackerError` gains `Status`, `RateLimited` and `Pagination` for `tracker_status`, `tracker_rate_limited` and `tracker_pagination`; configuration errors are the adapter's `GitHubConfigError` with `invalid_tracker_config` and `missing_tracker_secret`.
- **Secret key:** `tracker.provider.token` is the adapter's documented secret key and the only key that reads `$NAME` (17.1 item 10). An unset or empty variable is `missing_tracker_secret`, naming the variable, never a value (5.3.1).
- **Contract:** the adapter runs `TrackerAdapterContract`. The contract gains two seams for it: `writesLabels`, false until m2-23, which skips the label write tests (reported as skipped), and `wellFormedId`, an id of the tracker's shape for the unreachable refresh, since the adapter omits an id that is not an issue number without a request. Tests use real GitHub responses recorded from the public `octocat/Hello-World` repository, served by a fake transport with no network (`plugins/tracker-github/src/test/resources/github/recorded/`).

Not in m2-22: writes and the token kept out of the agent (m2-23), and the host wiring. `WorkflowLoader` still accepts only `tracker.kind: files`, so `loop run` cannot use the adapter yet; the profile lists this under [known gaps](plugins/tracker-github.md#known-gaps).

### GitHub tracker, host wiring (extension, bl-23)

`tracker.kind: github` selects the GitHub tracker in `WORKFLOW.md` and `loop run`. The decisions:

- **Where the plugin plugs in:** spec-core never depends on a tracker plugin (plan 12). `WorkflowLoader` takes the tracker kinds the host registers, each a `TrackerKind` with its profile default states and a reader for `tracker.provider`. The cli registers `github`, whose reader is `GitHubTrackerSettings.parse`, and keeps the result on `WorkflowFile.trackerSettings`. `files` stays built in. Since m3-05 both are tracker plugins selected by kind, see [tracker plugins](#tracker-plugins-extension-m3-05).
- **Error categories:** a reader's error with a spec 11.4 category is `WorkflowError.TrackerConfig`, and preflight reports the category as its code, `invalid_tracker_config` or `missing_tracker_secret`, the way it maps the other workflow errors. Messages name keys and variables, never a value.
- **Default states:** with no `tracker.active_states` or `tracker.terminal_states`, a `github` workflow gets `open` and `closed` (5.3.1 profile defaults), not the `files` defaults.
- **State check:** preflight checks the configured states against `GitHubTracker.DEFINED_STATES`, without building an adapter every tick.
- **Token policy:** the token is a `$NAME` under `tracker.provider`, so the m1-23 policy for referenced variables applies unchanged: removed from the agent env, left out of the tool config snapshot, redacted.
- **Reload:** the adapter is built once per run. A reload that changes `tracker.kind`, or the GitHub settings, warns that Loop keeps reading the old tracker until `loop run` is restarted, naming the repository, never the token.
- **From bl-22:** a 404 or 410 on an issue is gone only when `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}` succeeds, asked once per call; a 301 or a failing repository request is a tracker error, so a renamed repository or a token that lost access never cancels running Attempts. A token with whitespace or a control character inside is `invalid_tracker_config` at load, and the message never quotes it.
- **End to end test:** `GitHubRunCommandTest` runs the installed launcher, so it cannot inject an in process transport. It serves the recorded responses from a loopback server set as `api_url`, with no network and no test seam in production code.

### Answers reach the agent through the description (extension, bl-06)

Story 45 says the next attempt resumes with the human's answer. The answer is a comment, and the worktree leaves `.loop/issues/` out, so the agent sees it only through the prompt. The template stays the only source of the prompt (story 48): Loop injects nothing and adds no default prompt. Continuation guidance is not the way either: an answer arrives on a new Attempt, whose turn 1 gets the full prompt.

- **Startup warning.** When the prompt template never uses `issue.description`, `loop run` warns at startup: `loop run: warning: the prompt template never uses issue.description, so the agent will not see the issue body or the answers under ## Comments.` A use is any lookup of `issue.description` or `issue["description"]` in the parse tree, branches not taken included, or `issue` whole; text in `raw` and `comment` blocks is not, nor is a key read through an `assign` of `issue`. The warning never stops the run: preflight passes and dispatch goes on. A workflow reload does not check it again (m1-15).
- **Loop's own template** renders `{{ issue.description }}`, pinned by `OwnWorkflowTest`.
- **Completing the issue.** `TrackerAdapter` gains an optional `completeIssue`, whose default returns the issue unchanged: the `files` tracker's comments are already part of the body. The orchestrator calls it once per dispatch, just before the prompt is rendered, never on a poll or a turn, and renders the prompt and builds the attempt context from the issue it returns. A failure starts no agent: the attempt fails with `tracker error: <message>` and is retried with the normal error backoff. Since bl-132 it is called again at each [rotation](#soft-limit-rotation-extension-m3-11), on the issue read by id after the old session's `stop(usage)`, before the rotated prompt is rendered; a failure there starts the new session anyway, with the issue the turn end read (bl-152). The `github` tracker appends the issue's comments under `## Comments`, one request, newest last, in the `files` tracker's shape; see its [profile](plugins/tracker-github.md#comments).
- **Slot cutting (M2):** when the issue slot is cut, the newest comments under `## Comments` must survive.

### Ledger (extension, m2-19)

PLAN 6.8, story 83. Loop measures itself: one ledger line per turn, in a file, and one row per finished Attempt, in the store, so `loop report` can compare trackers, kinds, runners, models and templates and count what was delivered with no human touch.

**Turn lines.** When a turn ends, completed or failed, Loop appends one JSON object on one line to `<LOOP_HOME>/ledger/<project_id>.jsonl`, created like the human log (folder mode 0700, file mode 0600, never through a symlink); a canceled turn has no line. A failed write warns once, `level=warn event=ledger outcome=failed`, and never stops the Attempt. String values are redacted before the encoding. Every key is always there:

| Key | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `ts`, `project_id`, `run_id`, `issue_id`, `issue_identifier`, `attempt_id`, `session_id`, `turn_id` | When the turn ended and the ids of 13.2. |
| `turn`, `outcome` | The turn number, `completed` or `failed`. |
| `tracker`, `kind`, `runner`, `model` | The id of the issue's tracker since m4-13 (`tracker.id`, `default` when unset, or the `id` of an extra tracker), `tracker.kind` before it, the issue kind (the kind the issue matched in Loop mode since m4-18, `code` before it, null in Symphony mode), `runner.kind`, and the model the runtime reported for the turn's session, as the `context.windows` key `<provider>/<model>`, else the runner's configured model as written (null when it has none: the command runner, or Claude Code without `model`). Since bl-147, see [the model in the ledger](#the-model-in-the-ledger-extension-bl-147). |
| `template_version`, `config_version` | SHA-256 of the prompt template, so templates compare across config edits, and of `WORKFLOW.md` (null when not read from a file). |
| `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`, `tokens` | This turn's share: the latest absolute totals at its end minus those at the end of the turn before, each at least 0. Null when the turn reported no usage. |
| `attempt_tokens` | The Attempt's absolute total so far, cache reads included, like the per Attempt token budget. Null before any report. |
| `cost_usd`, `attempt_cost_usd` | This turn's share of `total_cost_usd` and the latest one; null when none was reported. Loop keeps no price table. A reported cost below the cost at the turn start gives a share of 0 and warns once per Attempt, `level=warn event=ledger outcome=completed reason="decreasing_cost: ..."` (bl-37): Claude Code restores the session's cost on `--resume`, and a version that stopped would make the Attempt's cost its last invocation's only. |
| `wall_ms` | The turn's wall time. |
| `thread_id`, `peak_tokens`, `window`, `peak_fraction` | Since m3-09: the turn's agent session and its peak occupancy so far, null when it has no report, see [peak occupancy](#peak-occupancy-and-runners-without-occupancy-extension-m3-09). |

The rule of [token accounting](#usage-event-and-token-accounting-extension-of-135-m1-05) holds: a report replaces the last one, so two reports in one turn count once, and the shares of an Attempt's turns add up to its total. A Claude Code turn woken by a background task carries the sum of all its `result` lines (bl-25), so its line and the row hold the whole turn.

A turn that ends with no usage report, by a timeout (`response_timeout`, `turn_timeout`), a stall or a `port_exit`, has null tokens and cost on its line, as now: the runner reported nothing, and a Claude Code invocation killed before its `result` line never reports what it used, so those tokens count nowhere. That gap is bl-64 (taken over from bl-31). A Claude Code turn that times out after at least one `result` line (a background wake, then silence) reports the sum of those lines as one `usage` event, the session total, before its `TurnFailed`, and the session keeps that total for its later turns (bl-37), so its line, the row and the budgets count that spend. Since bl-108 that event follows each `result` line as it is read, so a turn ended by a stall or cancelled by `loop stop` after such a line counts it the same way, on its line (a stall) and its row (both); a stalled turn with no `result` line still has null tokens and cost.

**Attempt rows.** When an Attempt records `attempt_finished`, whatever its outcome, Loop writes its row to the `attempts` table (schema version 6; m2-09 took version 5 for `acceptance_commands`, and a store already at 5 is migrated to 6, which adds `attempts`): the ids, the setup keys above, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `wall_ms`, `outcome` (`succeeded`, `failed` or `canceled`), `reason`, `turns`, `issue_state` (as last read), the latest token totals and `cost_usd`, `checks` (how many checks its verifications ran, the `checks` of its `verdict` events summed), `verdict` (its latest), `guards`, `attempt_number` (1 based per issue) and `human_touches`. A measure nobody could report is null, never zero: tokens and cost without a usage report, `model` without one reported or configured, `kind` in Symphony mode, `checks` and `verdict` when no result was submitted, and `guards`: the guards run inside `diff_policy` (m2-12, m2-13) and count among its `checks`, and no verdict reports them apart yet. A second row for the same Attempt id is ignored. An Attempt that never finished, abandoned by a dead process (m1-36), has no row. A failed write warns like a turn line.

**Human touches.** A `human_touch` event, issue level with `touch` = `resume`, `approve` or `move`, is recorded for each moment a human acted (PLAN glossary):

| Touch | Recorded when |
|---|---|
| `resume` | `loop resume` unparks the issue. |
| `approve` | `loop approve` approves a delivery ([m2-24](#delivery-policy-and-approval-extension-m2-24)). |
| `move` | A tracker change unparks the issue (`from_state`, `to_state`); reconciliation stops its Attempt (`to_state`, absent when the issue is gone), unless the state is the one the Attempt itself moved it to; a human change wins over a verified move (`to_state`) or ends a `Waiting(approval)` by moving the issue out of the active states (m2-24, no `to_state`: the poll only sees that it left them); or an Attempt starts while the issue's last row left it outside the active states, so a human moved it back, for example from review to rework or after an answer to `needs_info` (`from_state`, `to_state`). |

The store counts them in the statement that writes the row: the `human_touch` events of the issue recorded after the last one an earlier row of the issue counted. Each touch therefore counts once, in the next row of its issue, across processes and restarts. A move Loop cannot see, such as a human closing an issue that waits in review, counts nowhere.

`loop trace` prints `human_touch` with its data like any event; the human log does not repeat it, since the unpark, the stop and the move each have their own line.

### Delivery policy and approval (extension, m2-24)

PLAN 6.5 and 6.6, stories 77 and 78. In Loop mode a kind's `delivery.policy` decides what happens to a `verified` result: `human`, the default, waits for `loop approve`; `auto` delivers at once.

```yaml
kinds:
  code:
    delivery: { policy: human }
    verify:
      checks: [...]
```

**Config.** `delivery` is optional; absent, or without `policy`, it is `human` (story 77, PLAN "default human for each new project"). Present, it must be a map (`kinds.code.delivery must be a map`, `delivery: ~` included) and `policy` must be `human` or `auto`, trimmed and case insensitive (`kinds.code.delivery.policy must be human or auto, got "humna"`), so a typo never switches the approval off. `auto` is the m2-03 behavior: the verified move is applied at once. Since m2-28 `auto` needs the opt in `delivery.auto_merge.enabled: true`, and with `pull_request.merge` it merges only on the [auto merge conditions and a low merge risk band](#conditional-auto-merge-and-merge-risk-extension-m2-28).

**Delivery on the files tracker** is the move to the handoff state the agent asked for, `to` of its submitted result, whether it came as an intercepted move, a `done` result file (m2-15) or `submit_result` (m2-16). With `delivery.pull_request`, a pull request comes first ([m2-25](#pull-request-delivery-extension-m2-25)). bl-02 already syncs the branch with main on every dispatch.

**When a result waits for approval.** When a verification under `human` ends `verified`, in an Attempt's drain or in the rerun of a restored `Waiting(verify)` (m2-04), Loop reads the Issue again: one no longer active is left alone, as for a move (`tool_request_failed` gives the reason, and a human touch is recorded unless the Attempt moved it there itself). Otherwise:

- Loop posts the evidence summary on the Issue, author `loop`, redacted, and records `issue_commented` with `reason` = `evidence_summary`. A failed comment logs `level=warn event=delivery outcome=failed` and the wait still starts: the approval is what protects.
- The `Waiting(verify)` becomes `Waiting(approval)`, in memory and in the `waiting_issues` row, with the Attempt, the Run, the time, the target state `to` and the verified `fingerprint`. Loop records `approval_requested` (`to`, `policy`, `fingerprint`) and logs `event=delivery outcome=started`.
- The Attempt runs no further turn: it ends normally right after that drain with the exit reason `waiting_approval`. Its retry entry is dropped and its counters zeroed, like progress: no continuation is queued behind the wait.

The evidence summary is built from the verification, never from the agent's words:

```
Loop verified the result of attempt 0f3c9a2e: every check passed.

Fingerprint: git:<HEAD>:<diff>:<untracked>
Checks:
- check 0, ./gradlew --no-daemon build: passed with exit code 0 in 42.1s, evidence <attempt_id>.evidence-1.log
- hidden check 1: passed

The delivery policy is human: m2-24 moves to review once an operator approves it.
`loop show <attempt_id> --evidence --check <n>` prints the evidence of check n.
```

The summary addresses the operator and gives no `loop approve` command: it stays on the Issue, where a rework Attempt reads it in its prompt, and approving is the operator's. A command shows its first line, cut to 200 characters. A check that passed on its rerun (m2-08) reads `passed on its rerun`. A holdout check (m2-14) reads `hidden check <n>: passed`, with no command, exit code, duration or evidence. A restored wait with no Attempt id posts no summary.

**`loop approve <issue> [--workflow WORKFLOW.md]`** approves the delivery from the terminal. The Issue is looked up like `loop resume`: in the tracker first, then by id or identifier among the waiting Issues. In one store transaction it marks the wait approved, `approved_by` (the `USER` of the environment, else `LOGNAME`, else `human`) and `approved_at` in the row's data. Then it records `approval_given` with the wait's Attempt id, `by` and `to`, the state the Issue ends in: the wait's own, or `on_delivered.move_to` under `pull_request.merge`, since the merged Issue never stops at the handoff state (m4-29). It then records a `human_touch` with `touch` = `approve`, counted by the [ledger](#ledger-extension-m2-19). It prints `Approved m2-24, waiting since <time>. Loop moves it to review on its next tick.` It delivers nothing itself.

| Case | Exit | Message |
|---|---|---|
| Approved | 0 | `Approved <issue>, waiting since <time>. Loop moves it to <to> on its next tick.` |
| Already approved | 0 | `<issue> is already approved, Loop moves it to <to> on its next tick. Nothing changed.` |
| Approved, under `pull_request.merge` (m4-29) | 0 | `Approved <issue>, waiting since <time>. Loop delivers its pull request on its next tick, runs the merge check and merges it, then moves it to <move_to>.` |
| Already approved, under `pull_request.merge` (m4-29) | 0 | `<issue> is already approved, Loop delivers its pull request on its next tick, runs the merge check and merges it, then moves it to <move_to>. Nothing changed.` |
| Workflow does not load | 1 | the load error |
| Unknown issue | 2 | `loop approve: no issue "<issue>" in <tracker>.`, where `<tracker>` is `the tracker at <dir>` or `the github tracker of <owner>/<repo>` (m4-29) |
| Store cannot be read or written | 3 | the store error |
| No wait | 4 | `loop approve: <issue> is not waiting for approval. Nothing changed.` |
| A wait for its checks | 4 | `loop approve: <issue> is waiting for verify, not for approval. Nothing changed.` |
| Inside an Attempt | 5 | `loop approve: refused inside a Loop attempt, only an operator approves a delivery. Nothing changed.` |

**Only an operator approves or resumes.** `loop approve` and `loop resume` refuse to run inside an Attempt: when `LOOP_ATTEMPT_ID` is in their environment, even empty, they exit 5 before they read the workflow, the tracker or the store, and change nothing. `loop resume` prints `loop resume: refused inside a Loop attempt, only an operator resumes a parked issue. Nothing changed.` The variable is `LOOP_ATTEMPT_ID` because every Attempt env holds it, the agent's (with the launcher on `PATH`) and its checks', an operator shell never does, and `loop tool` already requires it. It stops the paths the deny rules of the generated Claude Code settings miss, since those match literal command prefixes only: `"$LOOP_LAUNCHER" approve`, `env loop approve`, `sh -c "..."`, `command loop`, a relative path or a symlink. The deny rules stay as a first line. Neither is a sandbox: an agent that unsets the variable on purpose still gets through, which is why the summary on the Issue names no command.

**Delivery.** Every tick reads the approval waits of the store after the stored waits are back (m2-04), so an approval from another terminal is seen like a `loop resume` (m1-07), and one given while no Loop runs is delivered by the next start:

- An approved wait whose Issue is still among the active candidates is delivered: Loop moves the Issue to `to` and records `issue_moved` with `by` = `host`, `verified` = `true`, `approved` = `true` and `approved_by`, with the wait's Attempt id, logs `event=delivery outcome=completed`, and ends the wait. The Issue is not dispatched in that tick. A failed move logs `level=warn event=delivery outcome=failed` and keeps the wait, so the next tick tries again. While the Attempt that asked for the approval is still closing, delivery waits for a later tick.
- A wait whose Issue is not among the active candidates is read by id, since the by-state read skips a file it cannot parse. When that read succeeds and shows the Issue out of the active states (a human moved it) or gone, the wait ends at once, logged `event=waiting outcome=released`, and records a `human_touch` with `touch` = `move`: the human change wins, and a later `loop approve` exits 4. When the read fails, a malformed or half saved issue file included (the by-id read fails closed on it), the wait is kept, approved or not, and logged `level=warn event=waiting outcome=failed` with `cannot read the issue, it keeps waiting`. The next tick reads it again and delivers it once the file parses.
- Any other approval wait keeps its Issue waiting: it is never dispatched, whatever its tracker state, and survives every restart.

A store that cannot be read logs `level=warn event=waiting outcome=failed` and changes nothing for that tick.

| Event | Data | When |
|---|---|---|
| `approval_requested` | `to`, `policy`, `fingerprint` | A verified result waits for approval. |
| `approval_given` | `by`, `to` | `loop approve` approved it. Recorded by the cli, with the wait's Attempt id and no Run, followed by its `human_touch`. |
| `issue_moved` | m2-03's fields, plus `approved` = `true` and `approved_by` | An approved result was delivered. |

**Shown by** `loop status` as a waiting line with `reason=approval`, `loop trace` as `approval_requested to=review policy=human fingerprint=...`, `approval_given by=<name> to=review` and `issue_moved ... after checks after approval`, and the human log as `commented the evidence summary of its verified result`, `waits for approval to move to review, the delivery policy is human, approve it with loop approve` and `moved to review after its checks passed, approved by <name>`.

Not in m2-24: the pull request ([m2-25](#pull-request-delivery-extension-m2-25)) and its merge check (m2-26), `on_verified` and `on_delivered` moves (PLAN 6.5), conditional auto merge (story 100) and approvals through the terminal channel (M4).

### Pull request delivery (extension, m2-25)

Story 79, first part. When a kind sets `delivery.pull_request`, delivering a `verified` result pushes the verified commit to `loop/<workspace key>` and opens or updates a pull request with an evidence summary, host side with Loop's token, before the move to the handoff state. Agents still cannot push: their generated settings deny `git push` and `gh` as before. m2-26 adds the merge check and the merge; this stops at an open or updated pull request.

```yaml
kinds:
  code:
    delivery:
      policy: human
      pull_request:
        repo: acme/widgets       # owner/name
        token: $LOOP_PR_TOKEN    # a $NAME reference, never the token
        base: main               # default main
        remote: origin           # default origin, checked by the preflight only
        api_url: https://api.github.com  # default the tracker's, https only
    verify:
      checks: [...]
```

**Gate.** Pull request delivery happens only when `delivery.pull_request` is present. Without it, delivery stays the m2-24 move, so Loop's own self hosting run opens no pull request when it adopts `kinds:`. Loop mode only: Symphony mode has no Loop delivery.

**Config.** `pull_request` must be a map (`pull_request: ~` is an error). `repo` is `owner/name` and defaults to `tracker.provider.repo`; `token` must be exactly `$NAME` and defaults to the tracker's token. Both are required on the `files` tracker. `base` (default `main`), `remote` (default `origin`) and `api_url` (default the tracker's `api_url`, else `https://api.github.com`, for GitHub Enterprise and tests) are optional. Only the variable's name is kept in the config, never the token. The preflight, at startup and every tick, refuses a missing `repo` or `token` on `files` (`kinds.code.delivery.pull_request.repo is required with the files tracker`), an unset or blank variable (`kinds.code.delivery.pull_request.token uses $NAME, which is not set`), a remote the project repository does not have (`kinds.code.delivery.pull_request.remote is "origin", which is not a remote of the repository at <dir>`) and an `api_url` that is not `https` (`kinds.code.delivery.pull_request.api_url must be an https address, http is allowed only for a loopback host, got "<value>"`). The github tracker's `api_url` is held to the same rule. Plain `http` to a loopback host (`localhost`, `127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`) is the escape for the fake servers of the tests: nothing sent there leaves the machine. The push never reads `remote` (see the push below); the key stays for the preflight decided in triage.

**Policies.** `human` and `auto` call one delivery. Under `human` it runs on the tick that sees the approved `Waiting(approval)`, from the facts the wait holds. Under `auto` it runs in the drain, right after the verdict. Neither merges.

**Tree restored after the checks.** With `delivery.pull_request`, when the fingerprint taken before the checks is a clean commit, Loop puts the work tree back to that commit after the checks, whatever the verdict: tracked files a check rewrote are restored from `HEAD` (staged and unstaged), and untracked files a check created are deleted, with the folders their deletion left empty. Ignored files, build output for example, a nested repository and the result file stay. So a check that regenerates a tracked file (on the pilot app, `fastlane test` rewrites `Config/Version.xcconfig`) never refutes the next verification for it. A tree that was not clean before the checks is left as it is: what the agent left is the agent's, and the dirty tree rule below refutes it. A restore that fails is a warning in the log, `event=restore outcome=failed`. The git processes of the fingerprint and of the restore run with only `PATH`, `HOME` and the locale of the host, and `core.fsmonitor` off, so a command the work tree's config names never sees a token or an Anthropic key.

**Dirty tree refuted at verification.** With `delivery.pull_request`, a verification whose checks all passed but whose `git:` fingerprint has a diff or untracked part that is not the hash of nothing ends `refuted`, not `verified`: a pull request carries only commits. The finding names the uncommitted changes and the untracked files as they were when the fingerprint was taken, before any check could regenerate one, at most 10 of each, each name cut at 100 characters and redacted. It reaches the next turn and the next attempt through the refutation path of m2-06, so the agent commits what belongs to the work and restores or removes what a build regenerates (on the pilot app, `fastlane test` rewrites the tracked `Config/Version.xcconfig`). No approval is requested and no `delivery_failed` follows. The `verdict` event carries `uncommitted`, the number of files. Without `delivery.pull_request` such a tree is `verified` as before. A `tree:` fingerprint is not refuted here: the fingerprint step of the delivery refuses it.

**Order.** The steps are separate, each idempotent, so a crash before the move replays without a second pull request:

1. The fingerprint check. The commit is taken from the stored fingerprint `git:<HEAD>:<diff>:<untracked>`, and the delivery is refused when the diff or untracked part is not the hash of nothing. Since the dirty tree is refuted at verification, this guards a `tree:` fingerprint, a work tree with no commit, or a wait written before.
2. The body is built and redacted, and refused over 65,536 characters.
3. The lookup: `GET /repos/<repo>/pulls?head=<owner>:loop/<key>&base=<base>&state=all`, kept to the pull requests whose `head.ref` and `base.ref` match. The open one wins, else the latest merged, else the latest closed. A pull request already merged with its head at the verified commit counts as delivered: no push, no new pull request, no update, `pull_request_found` is recorded and the comment and the move still run, so a replay after the merge of m2-26 opens nothing. A closed one, or one merged at another commit, is history: a new pull request is opened.
4. The push of a commit of the project repository (the folder of `WORKFLOW.md`, as the worktree hooks see it): the local branch `loop/<key>` must be at the verified commit, then `<sha>:refs/heads/loop/<key>` is pushed to the explicit URL `https://<host>/<owner>/<repo>.git`, from the host of `api_url` and `repo`, never a remote name, and never forced. A rejection (not a fast forward) is permanent: the hooks never reset the branch and rebase is denied to the agent. The project's `.git/config` is shared with every worktree and the agent can write it, so the push never reads it: it runs in a temporary bare repository that borrows the project's objects through `objects/info/alternates`, with no config file of the project, the user or the system. Only Loop's settings on the command line apply: only `https` allowed (`protocol.allow=never`, `protocol.https.allow=always`), empty `core.askPass` and `core.sshCommand`, empty `http.proxy`, `http.sslVerify=true`, `push.followTags=false`, no hooks. Before pushing, git's resolved push URL must be the URL itself, so no `url.*.insteadOf` can redirect it. Command line pins alone would not do: a URL scoped key of the repository, such as `http.<url>.proxy` or `http.<url>.sslVerify`, wins over a generic one. Details in the [adapter profile](plugins/tracker-github.md#pull-request-delivery).
5. The pull request: a PATCH of the title and body when the lookup found an open one, a POST otherwise. A 422 "already exists" triggers one more lookup, and the open one found is patched.
6. A comment on the issue, author `loop`: `Loop opened pull request #<n> for this issue: <url>` (or `updated`, or `found ... already merged`). The URL is kept in the wait, so a replay after a failed move does not comment twice.
7. The move to the handoff state, then the wait ends. It is the last step and optional: `PullRequestDelivery.deliver` takes a `Handoff`, and without one it stops after the comment, so m2-26 can wait as `Waiting(merge)` instead.

**Token to git.** Through a credential helper that reads an environment variable set only for the push, the git process and what it starts for the push, with `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and any other helper cleared first. The helper answers only when git asks for the configured GitHub host over `https`: the host of `api_url`, `api.github.com` read as `github.com`. For any other host or protocol it answers nothing, so git finds no credential. Never in argv, a URL or a git config file. Every git process Loop runs for delivery (the push, the branch tip `rev-parse`, `git init` and `git remote` of the temporary repository, the preflight's `git remote get-url`) gets a minimal environment, `PATH`, `HOME` and the locale (`LANG`, `LANGUAGE`, `LC_*`), plus the token variable for the push alone: the token under its configured `$NAME`, the Anthropic keys and the host's `GIT_` variables never reach git. The delivery token is a secret for redaction (`secretValues`), and as a `$NAME` of `WORKFLOW.md` it is removed from the agent env. The tool config snapshot holds no `kinds` setting.

**Title and body.** The title is `<identifier>: <issue title>`. The body is built from the verification's facts, never from the agent's result file or words:

```
Loop verified the result of attempt 0f3c9a2e for t-1: every check passed.

Verdict: verified
Fingerprint: git:<HEAD>:e3b0c44298fc1c14:e3b0c44298fc1c14
Checks:
- check 0, ./gradlew --no-daemon build: passed with exit code 0 in 42.1s, evidence <attempt_id>.evidence-1.log
- hidden check 1: passed
Judgment calls left to a human: 0

Closes #26
```

The last line is `Closes #<number>` when the issue comes from the `github` tracker on the same repository, `Refs <identifier>` otherwise. The approval wait stores the rendered check lines (`checks`) and the number of judgment calls (`judgment_calls`) beside `to` and `fingerprint`, so a delivery after a restart builds the full body.

**Failures.** A network error, a 5xx answer or a rate limit hold keeps the wait approved and the next tick tries again; it records no event and logs `level=warn event=delivery outcome=failed`. A 401, 403, 404, any other 422, a rejected push, a branch not at the verified commit, a fingerprint that is not a clean commit, or a body over the limit records `delivery_failed`, clears the approval and waits for a new `loop approve`. A move refused for good after the pull request opened does the same; a move that fails for a while is tried again at the next tick, with no second pull request or comment. Under `auto`, a failure that can be tried again leaves a `Waiting(approval)` already approved (`approved_by` = `auto`), and a permanent one falls back to the `Waiting(approval)` of `human`, evidence summary included. Before m2-25 a failed approved move retried every tick forever; that stays so without `pull_request`.

| Event | Data | When |
|---|---|---|
| `branch_pushed` | `sha`, `branch` | The verified commit is on the remote branch. |
| `pull_request_opened` | `number`, `url` | No open pull request had this branch as head, one was opened. |
| `pull_request_updated` | `number`, `url` | The open pull request of this branch got the new title and body. |
| `pull_request_found` | `number`, `url`, `state` = `merged` | A pull request of this branch was already merged at the verified commit, nothing was pushed or opened. |
| `issue_commented` | `chars`, `by` = `host`, `reason` = `pull_request` | The URL was commented on the issue. |
| `issue_moved` | `from`, `to`, `by` = `host`, `verified` = `true`, and under `human` `approved` and `approved_by` | The last step moved the issue to its handoff state. |
| `delivery_failed` | `step` (`fingerprint`, `body`, `pull_request` for the lookup and the open or update, `push`, `comment`, `move`), `reason` | A step was refused for good. |

**Shown by** `loop trace` as `branch_pushed sha=... branch=loop/t-1`, `pull_request_opened number=1 url=...`, `pull_request_found number=1 state=merged url=...` and `delivery_failed step=push reason=...`, and the human log as `pushed 0123456789ab to loop/t-1`, `opened pull request #1, <url>`, `found pull request #1 already merged, <url>`, `verdict refuted (2 files are not committed), ...` and `could not be delivered, push: <reason>. It waits for a new approval with loop approve`.

**Modules.** `DeliveryExecutor` in `plugin-api` (`push`, `findPullRequest`, `openPullRequest`, `updatePullRequest`, failures as `DeliveryError.Rejected` or `DeliveryError.Unavailable`), since spec-core cannot see plugins. `PullRequestDelivery` in spec-core orders the steps, comments and moves through the tracker, records the events and classifies failures. `GitHubDelivery`, `GitPush` and `GitHubPullRequests` live in `plugins/tracker-github`, where the private request code and rate limit hold of the tracker became `GitHubClient`, shared by both. `cli/` builds the executor from the workflow and the host env. See the [adapter profile](plugins/tracker-github.md).

Not in m2-25: the merge check and the merge (m2-26), the manual run on the playground (bl-49), and a rate limit hold shared by the tracker and the delivery (bl-50).

### Merge check and merge (extension, m2-26)

Story 79, second part. After an approved pull request delivery (m2-25), Loop fetches the current base, merges it into the branch in the issue's own workspace, reruns the kind's checks on the result and merges the pull request with a sha guard. m2-26 merged only with `ci: none`; since m3-15 `ci: actions` waits for GitHub Actions between the check and the merge, see [CI wait](#ci-wait-before-the-merge-extension-m3-15).

```yaml
kinds:
  code:
    delivery:
      policy: human
      pull_request:
        repo: acme/widgets
        token: $LOOP_PR_TOKEN
        merge:
          method: merge          # merge (default) or squash, never rebase
          ci: none               # required, none or actions (m3-15)
      on_delivered:
        move_to: done            # required with merge, a terminal state
```

**Gate and config.** Without `merge`, delivery is m2-25's, unchanged. `merge` must be a map. `method` is `merge` or `squash`: a rebase rewrites the commits, so what merges would no longer be what was checked (`kinds.code.delivery.pull_request.merge.method must be merge or squash, got "rebase"`). `ci` is required, so nobody merges without CI by leaving it out (`... merge.ci is required, none or actions`), and it is `none` or, since m3-15, `actions` (`... merge.ci must be none or actions, got "travis"`). `delivery.on_delivered.move_to` is required with `merge` and must be a terminal state of the tracker (`kinds.code.delivery.on_delivered.move_to is "review", which is not a terminal state, use one of done, canceled`). `merge` under `policy: auto` was refused until m2-28, which owns the auto merge conditions, see [Conditional auto merge and merge risk](#conditional-auto-merge-and-merge-risk-extension-m2-28). These are workflow errors, so the preflight refuses them at startup and every tick, and a reload that introduces one is rejected.

**Approval.** `loop approve` under `policy: human` approves the merge too: once approved, Loop merges on a passing merge check with no second approval.

**Handover from m2-25.** With `merge`, an approved delivery pushes, opens or updates the pull request and comments, then stops: `PullRequestDelivery.deliver` runs without a `Handoff`. Nothing is moved and the issue stays active. The approval row is replaced by a `Waiting(merge)` row, as approval replaced verify.

**Wait state.** `WaitReason.Merge`, shown as `Waiting(merge)` and `reason=merge` by `loop status`. Beside every key of the approval wait, its approval included, it stores `pull_request_number`, `phase` (`check`, `merge` or, since m3-15, `ci`, with `ci_started_at` and `ci_waited_ms`), `base_sha` (in phase `check`, the base being merged, written before the merge), `verified_fingerprint` (the fingerprint the result was verified on), `merged_head`, `round`, after one refusal because a branch moved, `merge_retry`, and, since bl-174, `candidate_head` (the merge commit of phase `check`) with `candidate_pushed` = `true` once it was pushed, and, since bl-181, `merge_called` = `true`, stored right before Loop calls the merge, so a restart resumes in the same phase with no second pull request, no second merge and no lost approval. An older binary keeps an unknown reason waiting, and `loop approve` on a merge wait exits 4, as on any wait that is not an approval.

**Slot.** Since bl-173 the merge check holds an agent slot and the merge phase does not. Phase `check`, which builds in the workspace, counts against `agent.max_concurrent_agents` like an attempt and asks `SlotPlan.mergeCheckFits`. Phase `merge` (the push, the head wait, the base read, the issue read, the auto merge gate, the merge) is network work plus at most one read only reviewer session: it holds no agent slot and asks no slot plan, so a head whose CI turned green merges at the next tick even when attempts hold every agent slot. Both run in the background, not inline in the tick, and `loop run --once` waits for them.

The phase a background run starts in decides its slot. A run started in phase `check` keeps its slot through the merge that follows it in the same run under `ci: none`, and through a new round on a moved base. A run started in phase `merge`, after a green CI wait or a merge tried again, holds none, and when the base moved it stops at the new round's phase `check`, which a later tick starts on a free slot.

So `agent.max_concurrent_agents` caps the agent attempts and the merge checks, not the auto merge reviewer. The reviewer's model session runs in phase `merge`, which asks for no agent slot of its own, so it is bounded only by the machine slot of m3-16, when a machine cap is set, and by the delivery queue below, whose head alone advances. The machine slot is claimed for phase `check` and for a merge phase approved by the auto policy, whose gate runs the merge risk reviewer; a merge phase approved by a human claims none.

The machine slot and the running check are taken before the background job is created, so the job starts atomically (bl-180): on a scope cancelled between the claim and the start, its body still runs, stops at once, and its `finally` releases the machine slot and ends the running check, instead of leaving the slot row until the pid liveness rule clears it.

Tests, the `bl-173` tests: in `CiWaitOrchestratorTest.kt`, `a green CI wait merges at the next tick while attempts hold every agent slot (bl-173)` and `a moved base at the merge starts a new round of the check and of the CI wait`, now with the check at the next tick; in `MergeCheckOrchestratorTest.kt`, the human and the auto merge phase under a full machine cap; in `WaitsTest.kt`, a running merge phase that counts as no merge check. The `bl-92` tests, the case seen on Loop's own run where a green CI wait queued behind two attempts dispatched during its CI wait: `bl-92, two slots taken by attempts dispatched during the CI wait, the green wait merges at the next tick` in `CiWaitOrchestratorTest.kt` and `bl-92, a merge wait in phase ci holds no slot, so two attempts fill both slots and the plan is full when CI turns green` in `SlotPlanTest.kt`. The `bl-180` tests, in `MergeCheckOrchestratorTest.kt`: `a merge check under ci none keeps its agent slot through the merge phase that follows it (bl-180)`, `a merge tried again at the next tick starts in phase merge and holds no agent slot (bl-180)` and `a merge check whose job starts on a cancelled scope leaves no machine slot and no running check behind (bl-180)`.

**Delivery queue (bl-172).** The whole delivery runs one at a time per project, not only the merge check, so a merge never makes another delivery's check stale. The merge waits form a queue with no stored lock state: the head is the merge wait with the oldest `since`, ties broken by issue id, sorted explicitly (`MergeQueue`), since the waits read gives no order. `since` is set when the merge wait starts and survives a new round (`again()`); a refusal or a fallback ends the merge wait, so a later approval starts a new one at the back.

Only the head advances, its merge check, its CI poll (`Merges.advance`) and its merge; the merge waits behind it stay in phase `check`, do nothing and take no slot. A wait whose issue left the active states is settled wherever it stands and leaves the queue; a head whose check runs, whose attempt runs or whose issue cannot be read holds the queue for that tick.

At startup, merge waits behind the head found in phase `ci` or `merge`, which a Loop that delivered several at once may have left, go back to phase `check` at their round, as `again()` does, each recording `merge_requeued` (data `phase`, the phase it was found in, `round` and `head_issue`, the identifier of the head; the human log reads `waits behind bl-2 in the delivery queue, back to the merge check of round 1 from phase ci`). `loop status` shows each merge wait's place, `queue=1` for the head. The queue is per project: two projects delivering to the same repository and base still invalidate each other.

Tests, in `MergeCheckOrchestratorTest.kt`: `two approved deliveries go one at a time, the second starts its merge check only after the first merged, one round each`, `a head that falls back lets the next one start, and its new approval goes behind it`, `merge waits with the same since go in issue id order, whatever the store's order` and `an older merge wait goes first, whatever its issue id`; in `CiWaitOrchestratorTest.kt`: `a merge wait behind the head is never polled while the head waits for its CI` and `at startup merge waits behind the head found in phase ci or merge go back to phase check at their round, with merge_requeued`; in `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/`: `RuntimeSnapshotTest.kt` and `StatusCommandTest.kt`, the `bl-172` tests.

**Slot plan (bl-86).** Who gets a slot is decided by two pure steps of `SlotPlan` sharing one capacity rule: running attempts and running merge checks count against `agent.max_concurrent_agents`, a running merge check being phase `check` work only since bl-173 (`SlotUse.mergeChecks`), a delivery's merge phase asking neither step and counting in neither, `agent.max_concurrent_agents_by_state` caps attempt launches only, and at most one merge check runs at a time. Step one decides the merge check slot where the tick always did, before the parked read and the due retries, so a merge check takes the last free slot ahead of them and still starts in a tick whose parked read fails. Step two fills the attempt slots in the order of [due retries before new candidates](#due-retries-before-new-candidates-deviation-from-82-and-166-bl-34) and returns the launches, the due retries to requeue and the candidates to skip. A launch is a proposal the orchestrator may still refuse when it claims it. Since bl-172 only the head of the delivery queue, the merge wait with the oldest `since`, ties broken by issue id, asks step one for a slot, so the order of the waits read (bl-82 finding 1) no longer matters. Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/SlotPlanTest.kt` and, in `MergeCheckOrchestratorTest.kt`, `a tick whose parked read fails still starts the waiting merge check and dispatches nothing (bl-86)`.

**Lookup first.** Every run of the merge check looks the pull request up first. Merged at the stored head, or in phase `check` at the stored `candidate_head` (bl-174), it is delivered (the move below), so a restart never merges twice. Merged at another commit, closed by hand, or missing, the merge is refused for good. A workflow reloaded without `merge` turns the wait back into an approved `Waiting(approval)`, and m2-25 delivers it.

**Phase `check`.**

1. The base is fetched host side, never from local `main` or a remote name. The fetch is a token bearing git process, so it copies the push's isolation: a temporary bare repository borrowing the project's objects, Loop's pins, the minimal environment, the credential helper scoped to the host, the explicit `https://<host>/<owner>/<repo>.git`. The objects land in the project's object store by file copy, and `refs/loop/base/<base>` is written by a git process that has no token. The fetched sha is the proof; no ref name is trusted after the copy. Details in the [adapter profile](plugins/tracker-github.md#pull-request-delivery). `base_fetched` is recorded.
2. The workspace fingerprint must still be the stored one, a clean commit V. Otherwise the merge is refused for good, with two exceptions for a stop (a crash, Ctrl-C, a cancellation) that came in the middle of an earlier check: a merge left in progress at V is aborted, and a workspace at a clean merge commit whose first parent is V and whose second parent is the stored `base_sha` is Loop's own merge commit M, pushed early unless the wait says it was (step 5), then rechecked as it is (step 6) against that base with no second merge and no fetch. Both are looked at before the fetch, so no later refusal can leave the approval at V while the branch is at M. The work tree is restored first, in case a check was writing when the stop came.
3. `merge_check_started`. When the fetched base is already an ancestor of V, nothing is merged and only the kind's `diff_policy` checks, visible and hidden, run again, each at its number in the full list, through the same `Verification` with the fetched base sha as their base and every event marked `merge_check` = `true` (bl-32): the verdict the approval rests on may have read a base the agent moved. No other check runs again, no acceptance command gate applies and no judgment call is saved; a kind with no `diff_policy` runs nothing. When they pass, outcome `up_to_date`, the merged head is V. A failure, refuted or could not run, is handled like a failing recheck (below), outcome `refuted` with no `head`, and the branch is unchanged.
4. Otherwise the fetched sha is written to the wait as `base_sha` first, the intent a stopped check is resumed from, then `git merge --no-ff --no-edit --no-verify -m "<identifier>: merge <base> at <sha> for the merge check" <sha>` runs in the workspace, author and committer `Loop <loop@localhost>`. When the wait cannot be written, nothing is merged and the next tick tries again. A merge that stops with paths `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=U` lists is a conflict, aborted: outcome `conflict`, the workspace stays at V. Any other failure of `git merge` (a lock file, an untracked file in the way, a bad object) is not a conflict: what it started is aborted, the workspace stays at V, and the merge is refused for good with git's error, `delivery_failed` with step `merge_check`, no conflict comment and no new dispatch.
5. On a merge commit M, since bl-174, M is stored in the wait as `candidate_head`, then pushed unforced to `loop/<key>` (`branch_pushed`) and `candidate_pushed` = `true` stored, so the GitHub Actions runs of M start while the checks run and a round costs the longer of the two instead of their sum. M is stored first, so a lookup after a stop knows a pull request merged at it. A push that fails, or a wait that cannot be written, is not fatal: it is logged, a failed push is recorded since bl-181 as `branch_push_failed` with `early` = `true`, the check goes on, and the push after a passing check pushes M as before. Nothing else changes in phase `check`: the tick leaves the running check's wait alone and the CI wait starts only once the check passed. The local result decides first, so a conflict or a failing recheck is handled as below, whatever CI says. `again()`, a refusal and a new approval drop both keys. A restart between the early push and the end of the check resumes on M with no second merge commit and no second `branch_pushed`, tested by `bl-174, a restart between the early push and the end of the check resumes on M with no second merge commit` in `MergeCheckOrchestratorTest.kt`.
6. On a merge commit M, every check, holdout and guard runs again through the same `Verification` as an attempt's, with the same env policy and the m2-08 rerun rule, but `diff_policy` takes the fetched base sha as its base instead of `guards.base`, so other people's commits on the base never count as the attempt's diff. Every event of the rerun carries `merge_check` = `true`, so its `verdict` is not read as a new verification, and the evidence files of the first run stay (evidence files are numbered and never replaced). The m2-25 tree restore applies after the checks; a tree still not clean at M after it fails the check as refuted. Outcome `merged` or `refuted`.
7. A passing check makes the merged head the delivered commit everywhere: the local branch is at it (the merge was made there), the wait's `fingerprint` becomes `git:<M>:<empty>:<empty>` with V kept as `verified_fingerprint`, and the phase becomes `merge`.

**Phase `merge`.** The merged head is pushed unforced to `loop/<key>` through the existing push (`branch_pushed` when it is not V and was not pushed early in phase `check`), so a later replay is a fast forward and the lookup finds the pull request at M. GitHub shows a just pushed head a moment late, and a merge sent before is refused, so Loop reads the pull request until its head is M, at most 10 reads one second apart; still another head after them, the merge goes on and the sha guard decides. Then the base tip is read once more through the API (`GET /repos/<repo>/git/ref/heads/<base>`). When it moved since the check, the check runs again, at most 3 rounds, then `delivery_failed`. Since bl-176 a move only in listed paths costs no round: with `kinds.code.delivery.pull_request.merge.recheck_skip_paths` set, a list of globs anchored at the workspace root like `guards.protect`, matched case sensitive, empty by default (a value that is not a list of strings or a blank glob is a workflow error the preflight refuses), Loop fetches the new tip host side as phase `check` does (`base_fetched`) and merges with no new round only when the base the check merged (`base_sha`) is an ancestor of the fetched tip, every file of `git diff --name-only --no-renames <base_sha> <tip>` matches a glob, and none of those files is in the pull request's diff, `git diff --name-only --no-renames <tip>...<merged head>`. Otherwise, or when the fetch or a git read fails, a new round starts as before. A skip records `merge_round_skipped`, leaves `base_sha` as it was, and the auto merge gate still runs on the merged head; the base read after the gate lets the same tip through again with no second fetch. Docs are never a good candidate, since tests read them: Loop's own `WORKFLOW.md` lists `.loop/issues/**` only. Tests, the `bl-176` tests: in `MergeCheckOrchestratorTest.kt`, a move only in listed paths, a listed path the pull request also changed, a tip that does not descend from `base_sha` and a move outside the list; in `AutoMergeOrchestratorTest.kt`, a move while the reviewer runs; in `MergeConfigTest.kt`, the default, a blank glob and a value that is not a list. Then the issue is read once more: one a human moved out of the active states during the check is not merged, the wait ends with a `human_touch` (`touch` = `move`) and the pull request stays open. Otherwise the wait stores `merge_called` = `true` (bl-181), then `PUT /repos/<repo>/pulls/<n>/merge` with `sha` = the merged head and the configured method; `pull_request_merged` is recorded. A wait that cannot be written is not merged, the next tick tries again. The small windows between those reads and the merge stay: a commit landing on the base in it is merged untested, since GitHub's sha guard only covers the pull request's head, and a human move in it does not stop the merge.

**After the merge.** The issue moves to `on_delivered.move_to` (`issue_moved` with `merged` = `true`), unless it is there already, the wait ends, and the workspace is removed; the `before_remove` hook keeps the local branch. Loop never deletes the remote branch. A move that fails keeps the wait, and the next tick finds the merged pull request and tries the move again. On GitHub, `Closes #n` may have closed the issue as Loop merged: the move to `closed` is skipped or a harmless PATCH. A PATCH that lands while GitHub closes the issue is answered 422 `This issue was closed by another request` (bl-68, reproduced on the playground); the tracker then reads the issue once and counts the move done when it shows `closed`, so the merge logs no failed delivery. On the `files` tracker the pull request says `Refs <identifier>` and Loop moves the issue file; issue files stay local, uncommitted writes.

**Failures.**

- Conflict: the merge is aborted and the workspace stays at V. A comment, author `loop`, names the pull request, the base sha and tells the next attempt to merge `refs/loop/base/<base>` (`git merge` is not denied to the agent). The wait ends, so the issue dispatches again, and the findings reach the next attempt. The comment is what survives a restart.
- Failing recheck: the same, but the merge commit stays on the local branch, so the next attempt starts already synced. Since bl-174 it was pushed before the checks ran, so the remote branch and the pull request show it too; the wait still ends refuted, never at the CI verdict.
- A `diff_policy` failure on a base already in the branch (bl-32): the same as a failing recheck, with nothing merged. The comment says the base at its sha is already in the branch and `diff_policy` failed against it, and the findings for the next attempt lead with that before the check's own findings.
- Since bl-181 none of these three sends back a pull request a human merged while the check ran: the pull request is read first, see [CI wait](#ci-wait-before-the-merge-extension-m3-15).
- A 405 "Base branch was modified" or a 409 "Head branch was modified" from the merge says a branch moved under it, which GitHub's delayed head update can cause: the merge wait is kept with `merge_retry` = `true` and the next tick tries again (the push, the head wait, the base read, the merge). The same refusal once more counts as permanent, below.
- A timeout, 401, 403, any other 405 or 409, a pull request closed by hand, a workspace not at the stored fingerprint, a `git merge` failure that is not a conflict, or 3 rounds used: `delivery_failed` with the step (`fetch`, `merge_check`, `merge`, `push`, `pull_request`) and the reason, a comment on the issue, the pull request left open, and the row goes back to an unapproved `Waiting(approval)` holding the fingerprint the wait holds, the merged one after a passing check, so a new approval replays cleanly. A 403 to the merge names the missing permissions.
- A network error, a 5xx answer or a rate limit hold keeps the merge wait, and the next tick tries again.

**Human changes.** The sync of merge waits looks the pull request up first for an issue that left the active states. Merged at the stored head, or since bl-174 at its pushed `candidate_head` in phase `check`, is Loop's own merge when the wait holds `merge_called` (bl-181): the move, no `human_touch`. Without it, whatever the phase, it is a human's merge: the move plus a `human_touch` (`touch` = `merge`), never a merge at another commit. Until bl-181 the phase decided, so a human merge in phase `ci`, or in phase `merge` before Loop's call, passed for Loop's own. The lookup before each run of the merge check reads it the same way. Anything else is an issue a human moved out of the active states: the wait ends with a `human_touch`, as for approval.

**The next attempt's diff.** After a conflict or a failing recheck the branch holds, or soon holds, commits of the base that local `main` lacks. Since bl-32 every ordinary verification of a kind that delivers a pull request bases `diff_policy` on a base commit it fetched itself, see [Diff base of pull request kinds](#diff-base-of-pull-request-kinds-extension-bl-32), so a protected path someone else committed on the base never refutes the agent. No verification reads `refs/loop/base/<base>` to choose a diff base: the ref is as writable by the agent as `guards.base`. It stays what the next attempt merges after a conflict.

**Git isolation in the workspace (part of bl-55).** Every git command Loop runs in a workspace, the merge check's and the fingerprint and tree restore's of m2-25, goes through `WorkspaceGit`: `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c core.fsmonitor=false -c commit.gpgsign=false -c core.commitGraph=false` (the last since bl-32), then after `-C` `-c merge.verifySignatures=false -c gpg.program= -c gpg.ssh.program= -c gpg.x509.program= -c gc.auto=0 -c maintenance.auto=false --no-replace-objects`, so a signed base tip (GitHub signs its merges) never runs a gpg program an agent configured, no automatic gc or maintenance starts in the background, and replace refs change nothing; only `PATH`, `HOME` and the locale plus `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, no token, and a 5 minute timeout after which git and what it started are stopped. Filters and merge drivers that `.gitattributes` names and a config defines can still run as Loop's user, without the token; bl-55 and M5 own that cure.

**Token permissions.** Contents write (push, fetch, merge), pull requests write, issues write, and since m3-15 Actions read, for the CI wait and the `ci` condition of an auto merge, its runs listing and, since m4-07, the jobs of a failed run. The preflight does not probe them, a fine grained token cannot be asked. Listed in the [adapter profile](plugins/tracker-github.md#pull-request-delivery).

| Event | Data | When |
|---|---|---|
| `base_fetched` | `sha`, `base` | The tip of the base was fetched and written to `refs/loop/base/<base>`, by the merge check, since bl-32 by a verification of a pull request kind before its `diff_policy` checks, and since bl-59 by every attempt of a pull request kind before its hooks. |
| `base_fetch_failed` | `base`, `reason` (redacted), `sha` when an earlier fetch left `refs/loop/base/<base>` | The fetch before an attempt's hooks failed; the hooks get `sha` as `LOOP_BASE_REF`, or no `LOOP_BASE_REF` without it (bl-59). |
| `diff_base_unpinned` | `reason` (redacted) | A verification of a pull request kind could not fetch the base, so its `diff_policy` checks used `guards.base` (bl-32). |
| `merge_check_started` | `base_sha`, `round`, `resumed` = `true` when it resumes a stopped check's merge commit | The merge check started in the workspace. |
| `merge_check_finished` | `outcome` (`up_to_date`, `merged`, `conflict`, `refuted`), `base_sha`, `round`, `head` when merged | The merge check ended. |
| `merge_round_skipped` | `base_sha`, `tip`, `files` (comma separated, at most 20) | The base moved after the check only in `recheck_skip_paths`, outside the pull request's diff, so the merge goes on with no new round (bl-176). |
| `pull_request_merged` | `number`, `sha` (the merged head), `method`, `merge_sha` | GitHub merged the pull request. |
| `issue_commented` | `chars`, `by` = `host`, `reason` = `merge_check` | A conflict, a failing recheck or a refused merge was commented. |

**Shown by** `loop trace` as `base_fetched base=main sha=...`, `merge_check_started base_sha=... round=1`, `merge_check_finished outcome=merged round=1 head=...`, `pull_request_merged number=1 sha=... method=merge`, `merge_round_skipped base_sha=... tip=... files=.loop/issues/bl-1.md`, and a rerun's `verdict` with `merge_check`; and by the human log as `fetched main at 0123456789ab`, `merge check started against 0123456789ab, round 1`, `merge check passed, merged the base into 0123456789ab and every check passed again`, `merge check failed, merging 0123456789ab conflicts, the issue goes back to its agent`, `merge check failed, diff_policy failed against the base 0123456789ab already in the branch, the issue goes back to its agent`, `merged pull request #1 at 0123456789ab with merge`, `the base moved to fedcba987654 only in recheck_skip_paths, merges with no new round: .loop/issues/bl-1.md` and `verdict verified of the merge check, ...`; `diff_base_unpinned` shows as `diff_base_unpinned reason="..."` and `could not fetch the base, diff_policy uses guards.base: ...`; `base_fetch_failed` shows as `base_fetch_failed base=main sha=... reason="..."` and `could not fetch main before the attempt, the hooks start from the last fetched main at 0123456789ab: ...` (or `no earlier fetch, the hooks get no LOOP_BASE_REF`), and as a warn line `event=base_fetch_failed` of the run log. No token appears in an event, a comment, the wait or the log.

**Modules.** `DeliveryExecutor` gains `fetchBase`, `baseTip` and `mergePullRequest`, refused by default so existing executors keep compiling. `MergeCheck` in spec-core holds the names, texts and wait data; the orchestrator runs the phases. `GitPush.fetch` and `GitHubPullRequests` in `plugins/tracker-github` implement them. `WorkspaceGit` in spec-core runs every workspace git command.

**Self hosting.** Since m3-01, Loop's own `WORKFLOW.md` delivers with `merge` under conditional auto merge (m2-28), so the merge check runs on every verified Loop issue. See [`self-hosting.md`](self-hosting.md#loop-mode-on-loops-own-backlog).

Not in m2-26: the CI wait (m3-15, carrying m2-30), new branches starting from the fetched base (bl-59), CI commit statuses (bl-60), the playground merge run (bl-53), and disarming `.gitattributes` filters and merge drivers (bl-55, M5).

### Conditional auto merge and merge risk (extension, m2-28)

Story 100. `delivery: { policy: auto }` on a `code` kind with `pull_request.merge` merges a verified pull request with no approval only when every hard condition holds and the merge risk band is low with no dimension at its worst verdict. Otherwise it falls back to `policy: human`: the issue waits as `Waiting(approval)` and the comment says why. Off unless the project opts in.

```yaml
kinds:
  code:
    delivery:
      policy: auto
      auto_merge:
        enabled: true            # the opt in, required for policy auto
        protected_paths: [...]   # default below
        max_diff_lines: 400      # added plus deleted outside tests and docs, default 400
        doc_paths: [...]         # default below, since bl-96
      risk:
        reviewer: claude-sonnet-5-5   # required with policy auto and merge
        sensitive_paths: [...]   # default below
        one_way_paths: [...]     # default **/migrations/**
        timeout_ms: 600000       # default 10 minutes
      pull_request:
        repo: acme/widgets
        token: $LOOP_PR_TOKEN
        merge: { ci: none }
      on_delivered: { move_to: done }
```

**Opt in.** Every `policy: auto` on a `code` kind needs `kinds.code.delivery.auto_merge.enabled: true`, since a `WORKFLOW.md` is one project. Without it the workflow fails to load, so the preflight refuses it at startup and every tick: `kinds.code.delivery.policy is auto, which needs the opt in kinds.code.delivery.auto_merge.enabled: true`. `enabled` must be `true` or `false`; `auto_merge` and `risk` must be maps; path lists (`protected_paths`, `doc_paths`, `sensitive_paths`, `one_way_paths`) hold no blank glob, globs are anchored at the workspace root like `guards.protect`; `max_diff_lines` and `timeout_ms` are positive; `reviewer` is not blank. With `merge` under `auto`, `risk.reviewer` is required (`kinds.code.delivery.risk.reviewer is required with policy auto and kinds.code.delivery.pull_request.merge`). Without `pull_request.merge`, an opted in `auto` keeps its m2-24 and m2-25 behavior, the move or the pull request at once: nothing merges, so no condition applies.

**Flow.** Under `auto` with `merge`, the drain does not move the issue or post the evidence summary: it writes a `Waiting(approval)` already approved with `approved_by` = `auto`. The next tick delivers the pull request (m2-25), the merge wait starts and the merge check runs (m2-26), then, under `ci: actions`, the CI wait (m3-15). In the merge phase, after the push, the head wait, the base tip read and the issue read, and right before the merge, the auto merge gate runs for a wait whose `approved_by` is `auto`. The review can take up to `risk.timeout_ms`, and a tick leaves a running merge check alone, so once the gate passes the issue is read again: one a human moved out of the active states during the review is not merged, the wait ends with a `human_touch` (`touch` = `move`) and the pull request stays open. Then the workflow file must not hold a rejected change, and the effective workflow, as a reload during the review left it, must still allow an auto merge, `policy: auto` with `pull_request.merge`; otherwise nothing merges and it falls back to an unapproved `Waiting(approval)`, `auto_merge_fallback` and the comment naming the reason (`the workflow no longer allows an auto merge, kinds.code.delivery.policy is human`) with the merge risk as assessed. A rejected change counts because the file on disk is then not the effective workflow, so the operator's intent is unknown, and because turning only `auto_merge.enabled` off under `policy: auto` is itself a change the loader rejects, which leaves the effective workflow allowing the merge (round 3 of the PR #75 review). Its reason names the preflight's code and message (`the workflow no longer allows an auto merge, its last change was rejected, invalid_config: kinds.code.delivery.policy is auto, which needs the opt in kinds.code.delivery.auto_merge.enabled: true`). A rejection from before the review counts the same, until a passing preflight clears it. The merge's sha guard covers only the head, so last the base tip is read again: a base that moved schedules the merge check again, a new round counted against the same limit of rounds, and the new merged head goes through the gate again. A wait an operator approved, after a fallback for example, merges with no gate.

**Hard conditions**, each a veto, all read and all named in the comment when several fail:

| Condition | Holds when |
|---|---|
| `verified` | The delivered attempt recorded a `verdict` `verified` outside the merge check, at the verified fingerprint, and the wait's check lines hold a `hidden check` line for every holdout of the kind. |
| `merge_check` | The delivered fingerprint is the clean fingerprint of the merged head, and the pull request's head, as the provider last showed it, is the merged head. Since bl-96 a pull request that shows no head fails it too. |
| `ci` | Read last, after the merge risk review, so the runs of a head pushed a moment before had time to register. What green means follows the kind's `ci` (m4-05). Under `ci: actions`, GitHub reports the pull request `mergeable` with the state `clean` (or `has_hooks`); `unstable` (a status or check not green), `dirty`, `blocked`, `behind`, `draft`, an unknown state or a read that fails all fail it. Then, since m3-15, the Actions runs of the merged head go through the verdict function of the [CI wait](#ci-wait-before-the-merge-extension-m3-15), never `GET /commits/<sha>/check-runs`, so a fine grained token with Actions read and no Checks permission passes it (GitHub grants `checks: read` only to GitHub Apps and classic tokens). Since m4-07 the jobs of its failed runs are read too, at most 10, so a run GitHub did not start fails it with its own reason (`GitHub did not start the Actions runs on the merged head <sha>, so the code was not judged: ...`), never as a red run; a jobs read that fails fails it, closed. A red or pending verdict fails it, and a head with no run unless `ci_no_run` is `pass`. A listing that cannot be read fails, a 403 or 404 naming "Actions: read". The CI wait already waited for the runs. Under `ci: none`, green means the merge check passed, which the gate proved before it reads `ci` (bl-146): no Actions run is read, of the merged head or the base tip, and no workflow file, so a result merges on its own while Actions is down or refuses its jobs. GitHub must still report the pull request `mergeable` with the state `clean`, `has_hooks` or `unstable`: `unstable` is failing checks GitHub reports, such as runs Actions refused for billing (bl-98), which `ci: none` does not wait for. `dirty`, `blocked`, `behind`, `draft`, an unknown state, `mergeable: false` or a read that fails still fail it, since they are not CI. Under both values `mergeable` is read up to 5 times, 2 seconds apart, while GitHub computes it. Commit statuses of an external CI count only through `mergeable_state` (bl-60), so under `ci: none` a failing external status no longer stops an auto merge either. Tests, the `m4-05` tests: in `AutoMergeTest.kt`, a table of runs and one of mergeable states under `ci: none`; in `AutoMergeOrchestratorTest.kt`, a `ci: none` result with no Actions run on the merged head that auto merges with no runs request sent, and an `unstable` one. |
| `no_lost_tests` | The kind's `diff_policy` checks, which the merge check reruns against the fetched base (bl-32), carry the three deny guards `deleted_tests`, `added_skips` and `weaker_assertions`. A finding refutes the merge check before the gate; without the guards nothing was looked for. |
| `protected_paths` | No file of the diff matches `auto_merge.protected_paths`, compared with the case folded like `guards.protect`, so `workflow.md`, `app/Agents.md`, `.Claude/settings.json` or `Gradle/` fail it too. Default: `WORKFLOW.md`, `scripts/`, `.github/`, `**/*.gradle`, `**/*.gradle.kts`, `**/gradle.properties`, `**/gradlew`, `**/gradlew.bat`, `build-logic/`, `gradle/`, `.loop/`, `docs/adr/`, `CONTEXT.md`, and what steers an agent or the reviewer or changes how git diffs: `**/.claude/`, `**/CLAUDE.md`, `**/AGENTS.md`, `.mcp.json`, `**/.gitattributes`, and since bl-96 `**/CLAUDE.local.md` and `**/.gitmodules`. |
| `diff_size` | The diff changes at most `auto_merge.max_diff_lines` lines in files that are neither a test nor a doc (bl-96), and git counted the lines of every file, tests and docs included. A test is a file of the guards' language globs (`guards.languages`, the built in Kotlin and Swift ones by default), as the merge risk facts read them; a doc is a file of `auto_merge.doc_paths`, default `**/*.md`, `**/*.mdx`, `**/*.markdown`, `**/*.adoc`, `**/*.rst` and `docs/`. Both match with their exact case, so a case variant counts. The reason gives both counts: `the diff changes 1200 lines outside tests and docs, over the limit of 1000, 3995 lines in all`. |
| `diff_base_pinned` | The delivered attempt recorded no `diff_base_unpinned` (bl-32, bl-67). |

The diff is `git diff --numstat --no-renames --no-ext-diff --no-textconv` of the merged head against its merge base with the base the merge check stored, so commits of the base never count.
