No attribute hides lines: numstat prints `-` for a binary file and for one a `-diff` or `binary` attribute marks, from a committed `.gitattributes`, the `info/attributes` the worktree shares with the project, or a `core.attributesFile` (turned off with `-c core.attributesFile=/dev/null`), and numstat ignores `--text`; so each such file is counted again from its own `--text` patch, its `+` and `-` lines after the first hunk header. A file whose patch cannot be read either, or for which git prints no patch, stays uncounted and fails `diff_size`. A real binary file counts its lines the same way. The diff passes `--ignore-submodules=none`, so a `diff.ignoreSubmodules` in the shared git config hides no gitlink change, which counts as one line; paths are kept as git prints them, a leading newline included (bl-96). Events of the attempt that cannot be read fail `verified` and `diff_base_pinned`. Since bl-96 the reviewer runs even when a hard condition fails, as long as the diff can be read, so the fallback comment carries a band for the operator who reviews the result; the spend budgets still apply to it. CI is read only when every hard condition holds.

**Merge risk assessment**, adapted from Warp's `merge-risk` skill. Five dimensions, three verdicts each, best first, worth 1, 2 and 3 points:

| Dimension | Verdicts | Weight | Reads |
|---|---|---|---|
| `alignment` | `aligned`, `partial`, `misaligned` | 1 | The diff matches the issue and its acceptance criteria, no drive by change. |
| `impact` | `low`, `medium`, `high` | 2 | Scope and blast radius. |
| `verification` | `strong`, `partial`, `weak` | 2 | Behavior proven, edge cases tested. |
| `rollback_cost` | `two_way`, `costly`, `one_way` | 1.5 | A revert undoes it, or not. |
| `error_detection` | `loud`, `partial`, `silent`, or `n/a` scored 1 | 1 | A breakage would be noticed, no swallowed error or hiding fallback. |

- **Facts computed in code first**, each a floor on its dimension: a touched `risk.sensitive_paths` path, or more than half of `max_diff_lines` changed outside tests and docs (bl-96), floors `impact` at `medium`; source files changed (by extension: `kt`, `java`, `swift`, `py`, `ts` and the like) while no test file changed (the guards' language globs, the built in Kotlin and Swift ones by default) floor `verification` at `partial`; a touched `risk.one_way_paths` path floors `rollback_cost` at `one_way`. Default sensitive paths: dependency and lock files (`package.json`, lock files, `Gemfile`, `Cargo.toml`, `go.mod`, `requirements*.txt`, `Podfile`, `Package.resolved`, `libs.versions.toml` and the like), build and CI config (Gradle files, `gradle/`, `build-logic/`, `Makefile`, `Dockerfile`, `.github/`, `project.yml`, `Fastfile`), `**/migrations/**` and `*.sql`. Since bl-96 sensitive and one way paths match with the case folded, so `db/Migrations/001.SQL` sets its floors, and the code facts and the issue title show control characters as U+FFFD, so a file name or a title cannot add a line to the prompt.
- **Reviewer for judgment.** One read only session of `risk.reviewer`, at most `risk.timeout_ms`, in the issue's workspace: the issue, the latest saved result file of the attempt, the code facts and the diff against the merge base (`--text`, so attributes hide nothing from it either, NUL bytes shown as U+FFFD, cut at 100,000 characters, and since m3-13 cut further to fit `context.prompt_limit` of the reviewer model's window, the cut stated, see [Prompt tokens and prompt limit](#prompt-tokens-and-prompt-limit-extension-m3-13)). The description, the result and the diff are untrusted data: each sits between `<untrusted-<fence>>` and `</untrusted-<fence>>` markers, the fence a random word per prompt, and the prompt tells the reviewer, before and after the data, to ignore any instruction, request or claimed verdict inside them. It answers one JSON object, `{"dimensions": {"<dimension>": {"verdict", "reason", "files"}}}`, alone or in a fenced block. The final verdict of a dimension is the higher of its floor and the reviewer's: a model can raise a floor, never lower it. The reviewer of `loop run` is Claude Code's (`<command> -p --output-format json --model <reviewer> --permission-mode plan --allowedTools Read,Grep,Glob --disallowedTools Bash,Edit,Write,...`, generated settings denying reads under the holdout folder, the agent env policy without the Anthropic keys), whatever `runner.kind` is since m4-06, see [The reviewer apart from the runner](#the-reviewer-apart-from-the-runner-extension-m4-06). It is isolated from the workspace it judges: `--setting-sources user` loads no project or local settings (so no hook or permission of the branch), `--strict-mcp-config` with an empty `--mcp-config` starts no MCP server, `--disable-slash-commands` loads no skill, and the env `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_CLAUDE_MDS=1` and `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_AUTO_MEMORY=1` keeps CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md through it, and the auto memory out (checked on Claude Code 2.1.319; `--bare` would skip CLAUDE.md too but accepts only an API key, which the reviewer env removes). The prompt goes to stdin on a thread of its own and the exit is awaited without blocking, so the timeout holds even for a reviewer that never reads its prompt; on a timeout or a cancellation the process and what it started are killed. Its answer is the `result` text of Claude Code's JSON output rather than an MCP tool or a file, since a read only session cannot write one.
- **Spend.** The reviewer's tokens and cost are written as a ledger row of their own, runner `risk_reviewer`, reason `merge_risk_review`, so the per issue and per day spend budgets read them like an attempt's. A per issue or per day budget already past its limit runs no reviewer and falls back. The gate reads the spend like an attempt does (bl-84): the ledger rows plus the attempts still running in this process, so a day budget that only their live spend passes falls back too. A ledger it cannot read falls back with "the ledger cannot be read for the spend budgets". The row is written when the review ends, so for up to `risk.timeout_ms` attempts running beside it read the per day spend without it: an attempt can cross a per day budget by about one review's cost. Accepted (bl-87, `.out-of-scope/live-reviewer-spend.md`). A row the ledger cannot write leaves the decision unchanged and, since bl-96, is logged at warn: `the reviewer's spend was not written to the ledger, so the spend budgets miss it: <why>`.
- **Score in code.** The weighted mean of the points, from 1 to 3 (weights impact 2, verification 2, rollback cost 1.5, alignment 1, error detection 1). Bands: `low` below 1.5, `medium` from 1.5 to below 2.25, `high` from 2.25.
- **Auto merge rule.** Every hard condition holds, the band is `low`, and no dimension is at its worst verdict. A one way door with every other dimension at best scores 1.40, low, and still falls back. Since bl-137 a diff cut to fit the reviewer's prompt floors the band at `medium`, so it always falls back, see [A cut reviewer diff floors the band](#a-cut-reviewer-diff-floors-the-band-extension-bl-137).
- **Fail closed.** A reviewer timeout, a crash (it could not start, exited in error, threw, printed no result, or since bl-96 printed more than 1 MiB on stdout, of which only the first MiB is kept in memory), an answer that is not valid JSON with `dimensions`, a dimension with no verdict or a verdict that is not one of its own, a host with no reviewer: each falls back naming the reason.

**Fallback.** `auto_merge_fallback` is recorded with `conditions` (the failed ones), `band` when the risk was assessed and `reason`; a comment, author `loop`, reason `auto_merge_fallback`, names each failed condition with its reason, then `Merge risk: band <band>, score <score>` with each dimension's verdict, the floor that set it and the reviewer's reason, or `Merge risk: not assessed, <why>`, also when a hard condition failed (bl-96); and the wait goes back to an unapproved `Waiting(approval)` holding the merged fingerprint, the pull request left open. It is not a `delivery_failed`. An operator's `loop approve` then merges after a new merge check, with no gate. A workflow reloaded away from `auto` falls back the same way.

| Event | Data | When |
|---|---|---|
| `merge_risk_assessed` | `alignment`, `impact`, `verification`, `rollback_cost`, `error_detection` (the final verdicts), `floors` (`<dimension>:<verdict>` by comma, empty when none), `score`, `band`, `reviewer`, since m3-13 `prompt_tokens`, and since bl-137 `diff_cut` (`true` or `false`) and `files_left_out`. A cut floors the band, not the score, so a cut review can record `score` 1.00 with `band` medium: read the band, never recompute it from the score. | The reviewer answered and the score was computed, whether the hard conditions held or not (bl-96). |
| `auto_merge_fallback` | `conditions`, `band`, `reason` | An auto merge fell back to the human policy. |
| `issue_commented` | `chars`, `by` = `host`, `reason` = `auto_merge_fallback` | The fallback comment. |

**Shown by** `loop trace` as `merge_risk_assessed band=low score=1.27 alignment=aligned impact=low verification=partial rollback_cost=two_way error_detection=loud floors=verification:partial reviewer=...` and `auto_merge_fallback conditions=ci band=... reason="..."`, and by the human log as `merge risk band low, score 1.27: alignment aligned, ..., floors verification:partial`, `did not merge on its own, <reason>. It waits for approval with loop approve` and `commented why it did not merge on its own`. A merge without a human records no `human_touch`, so the attempt's ledger row counts zero human touches.

**Modules.** `AutoMerge` (the conditions and the comment) and `MergeRisk` (dimensions, floors, parsing, score, band, prompt) in `spec-core/delivery`; `AutoMergeGate` in `spec-core/orchestrator` runs them; `RiskReviewer`, `Mergeability` (`DeliveryExecutor.mergeability`) and, since m3-15, `ActionsRuns` (`DeliveryExecutor.actionsRuns`), both refused by default, in `plugin-api`; `ClaudeCodeRiskReviewer` in `plugins/runner-claude-code`; GitHub's `mergeable` and Actions runs reads in `GitHubPullRequests`. The m2-28 check runs reader (`CheckRun`, `DeliveryExecutor.checkRuns`, `GitHubPullRequests.checkRuns`) and its tests were removed by hand after m3-15 merged: a fine grained token cannot read check runs, and an agent removing them is refuted by `deleted_tests`. The orchestrator reads an attempt's events through `attemptEvents`, which fails by default, so no auto merge passes without the store.

Not in m2-28: the GitHub Actions wait (m3-15, carrying m2-30), branch protection and a worker token that cannot merge (M5), and a reviewer for runners other than Claude Code (m4-06).

### CI wait before the merge (extension, m3-15)

Story 79, CI part, carrying m2-30. With `ci: actions`, a passing merge check (m2-26) does not merge at once: Loop pushes the merged head, waits for the GitHub Actions runs of that commit and merges only when CI allows it. `ci: none` keeps m2-26's merge right after the check.

```yaml
kinds:
  code:
    delivery:
      pull_request:
        merge:
          ci: actions                # none or actions
          ci_timeout_ms: 2700000     # 45 minutes by default
          ci_no_run: fail            # fail (default) or pass
          ci_no_run_grace_ms: 300000 # 5 minutes by default
```

**Config.** `ci_timeout_ms` and `ci_no_run_grace_ms` are positive integers (`kinds.code.delivery.pull_request.merge.ci_timeout_ms must be a positive integer, got 0`), the grace below the timeout, defaults included (`... merge.ci_no_run_grace_ms (600000) must be below kinds.code.delivery.pull_request.merge.ci_timeout_ms (600000)`), and `ci_no_run` is `fail` or `pass`, trimmed and case insensitive (`... merge.ci_no_run must be fail or pass, got "wait"`). Under `ci: none` no `ci_*` key is allowed (`... merge.ci_timeout_ms, ci_no_run need ci: actions, ci is none`), so a key that would change nothing is never read as a wait. These are workflow errors, refused by the preflight at startup and every tick; no network probe checks the token.

**Order.** Check, ci, merge. Since bl-174 the merge commit of the check is pushed as soon as it exists, before the checks run (step 5 of phase `check`), so the Actions runs of M start while the local checks run and the first poll often finds them finished. The polling still starts only in phase `ci`, once the local check passed, and no CI verdict is applied while the fingerprint is still V. `build.yml`'s concurrency group cancels the run of an earlier push of the same branch, so pushing M cancels the run of V, and a new round's M cancels the stale M, which removes most of the double CI run of bl-91. The wait is a `Waiting(merge)` in phase `ci`: after the passing check, the merged head is pushed unforced (`branch_pushed` when it is not the verified commit and was not pushed early), the wait stores `phase` = `ci`, `ci_started_at` (the push) and `ci_waited_ms` = 0, and `ci_waiting` is recorded. The merge phase that follows pushes again, a no op, and records no second `branch_pushed`. Neither does the push that starts the CI wait after an early push, tested by `bl-174, after the early push of the merge commit phase ci records no second branch_pushed` in `CiWaitOrchestratorTest.kt`. A base that moved at the merge starts a new round: the check again, then the CI wait again on the new merged head. Every new round and every new approval starts the wait anew, and a refusal drops the CI keys and the candidate keys with the other merge keys. The phase and its keys are kept in the store, so a restart resumes the wait; an older binary reads phase `ci` as `check` and runs the check again.

**Low findings of the bl-174 review (bl-181).** Three changes around the early push and a pull request a human merges while Loop waits:

1. A failed early push is recorded. When the early push of the merged head M fails, Loop logs a warning and records `branch_push_failed` (`issue`, `head`, `reason`, `early` = `true`). Nothing else changes: the merge check goes on, and the push that starts the CI wait still fails loudly, `delivery_failed` with step `push`, when the remote refuses it. Tested by `bl-181, a failed early push records branch_push_failed and the check goes on` in `MergeCheckOrchestratorTest.kt`.
2. A merge Loop did not make is a human merge. The merge phase stores `merge_called` = `true` in the wait right before it calls the merge; `again()`, a refusal and a new merge wait drop it, so it holds only for this round. A pull request found merged at the stored head or at the pushed candidate head records a `human_touch` (`touch` = `merge`) exactly when the wait does not hold the key, on every path that finds it: the lookup at the start of a run, the merge phase after the CI wait, the sync of an issue that left the active states, a restart, and the read of fix 3. Loop's own merge never records one, even when its move failed and a later tick finds the pull request merged. Before, the phase decided, so a human merge of M in phase `ci`, or in phase `merge` before Loop's call, passed for Loop's own. A wait an older binary left in phase `merge` after its own merge call, with no key, now reads as a human merge, a rare case of one human touch too many. Tested by `bl-181, a pull request a human merged in phase ci records a human touch, Loop merges nothing` and `bl-181, Loop's own merge after the CI wait records no human touch` in `CiWaitOrchestratorTest.kt`, and `bl-181, a pull request a human merged in phase merge before Loop's merge call records a human touch` and `bl-181, Loop's own merge records no human touch, even when a later tick finds it after a failed move` in `MergeCheckOrchestratorTest.kt`.
3. A merged pull request is never dispatched again. Before a conflict, a refuted recheck or a `diff_policy` failure sends the issue back to its agent, Loop reads the pull request. Merged at the stored head or at the candidate head, the wait ends as a merged delivery, the move with the human touch of fix 2, with no refusal comment and no findings, and the issue is not dispatched again; `merge_check_finished` still says the check refuted. Merged at another commit, the merge is refused as at the start of a run, `delivery_failed` and an unapproved `Waiting(approval)`, still with no dispatch. A read that fails is logged and the refusal goes on as before. Tested by `bl-181, a pull request a human merged before a refuted recheck ends the wait as merged and is not dispatched again` in `MergeCheckOrchestratorTest.kt`.

The other findings of that review were closed: an unverified M left on the pull request after a refuted recheck is never merged by Loop and has the tree of a GitHub merge of the verified commit; a second `branch_pushed` after a crash between the early push and `candidate_pushed` only doubles the event, the push is idempotent; `ci_started_at` set at phase `ci` rather than at the early push only makes the timeout more conservative; a history rewrite that fails its unforced push after a refuted recheck is forbidden by `WORKFLOW.md` and fails loudly.

**Polling.** One poll per tick per waiting issue, at most one every 30 seconds, inline in the tick, with no agent slot and no workspace: `GET /repos/<repo>/actions/runs?head_sha=<merged head>&per_page=100`. Never check runs, since a queued run has no check run yet and would read as no run, and never branch protection, which answers 403 on the free plan. A `pull_request` run reports the branch head as its `head_sha` and its `pull_requests` can be empty, so runs are matched by sha only. The first poll comes 30 seconds after the push. Since m4-07 a poll whose listing holds failed kept runs also reads their jobs, `GET /repos/<repo>/actions/runs/<id>/jobs?per_page=100`, for at most 10 failed runs, since the listing does not show steps; a poll with no failed run reads none.

**Verdict**, a pure function (`CiWait.verdict`). Runs are grouped by `workflow_id` and `event`, the highest `run_number` kept; a rerun raises `run_attempt` of the same run and the listing shows its latest attempt. A `total_count` above the runs listed is pending, never judged on a part. No run at all is no run. A kept run completed with any conclusion but `success`, `skipped` or `neutral`, an unknown one or none included, has failed. Since m4-07 a failed run whose jobs were read, all on their page, at least one, and every one with zero steps, is not started: GitHub refused to start it, for billing or limits, and judged no code (bl-98). When every failed run is not started the verdict is not started; otherwise it is red, naming the other failed runs, fail closed. A failed run whose jobs were not read, past the 10 of a poll, stays red, and so does a failed run with no job at all, such as a workflow file GitHub cannot read. Both come even while others still run, since the head can no longer turn green. A kept run not completed is pending. Otherwise green. There is no allow list of workflows.

**Decision**, on every answered poll, in this order: the verdict first, green merges, red fails and not started fails with its own outcome, `not_started`, never read as no run, so `ci_no_run: pass` never merges work nobody built; then the timeout, only while runs are pending; then the no run rule, only with zero runs once the grace is over, `pass` merging and `fail` failing. The timeout and the grace count `ci_waited_ms`, the time between answered polls only: an unreachable provider, a 5xx answer or a rate limit hold keeps the wait, records nothing, writes nothing and never counts, and the time Loop was down never counts either, since the next answered poll starts the count again from itself.

**Failures.** Red CI, a run not started, a failing no run rule and a timeout record `ci_finished`, then `delivery_failed` with step `ci` and the reason, and comment as `loop`, reason `ci`, naming the failed runs (at most 10, each line redacted and cut at 200 characters, with its URL); the pull request is left open and the row goes back to an unapproved `Waiting(approval)` holding the merged fingerprint. No dispatch, since the agent cannot read CI logs, and no automatic rerun, which needs Actions write and costs minutes: a human reruns on GitHub, then `loop approve`, which merges after a new merge check and a new CI wait. A 403 or 404 on the listing, or since m4-07 on the jobs read, is `delivery_failed` naming "Actions: read", with no `ci_finished`; a jobs read that can pass with time keeps the wait like the listing. A run not started (m4-07) fails the same way mechanically, but its reason and comment say that GitHub did not start the Actions runs, for billing or limits, so the code was not judged: the comment lists them under `Runs not started:` and asks to check the Actions billing and limits of the repository, rerun the runs on GitHub, then `loop approve`. `not_started` is its own outcome wherever it is read, never folded into red: it does not pause the delivery queue, and no reaction may fire on it as on broken code (m4-35's `ci_failed` first). Under `ci: none` no run is read (m4-05), so nothing changes there. Tests, the `m4-07` tests: in `CiWaitTest.kt`, the not started table, the jobs read only for failed runs at most 10 per poll, the outcome kept apart from red and from no run, and the comment; in `CiWaitOrchestratorTest.kt`, the recorded run 43779352010 and its jobs (`plugins/tracker-github/src/test/resources/github/recorded/`, recorded in m4-00) ending the wait `not_started`, the same under `ci_no_run: pass`, a 403 on the jobs read, a jobs read that can pass with time, and a green poll that reads no jobs; in `GitHubPullRequestsTest.kt`, the jobs reader on the same recordings.

**Queue pause (bl-175).** Under the delivery queue of bl-172 an Actions outage would make each queued delivery wait its own `ci_timeout_ms` in turn, then fall back, one after another overnight. So when the head's CI wait ends with `ci_finished` `outcome` = `timeout`, it falls back as above, then the queue of its project pauses: no merge wait starts its merge check, and the waits behind stay in phase `check`. A red run, a failing no run rule and any other refusal do not pause it. `loop approve` of any issue of the project, given at or after the pause (its `approved_at`), resumes it in the tick that reads the approval, before the merge waits run; an auto approval (`approved_by` = `auto`) does not, nor an approval from before the pause still waiting for its delivery. The pause is held in memory only, like the runner hold (bl-39), so a restart ends it: at its first tick, the new run reads the latest `delivery_queue_paused` an earlier run left with no `delivery_queue_resumed` after it (`DeliveryQueuePaused.openIn` over the store) and records the resume. Both events are recorded on the issue whose CI timed out, so its trace pairs them. `loop status` prints one line while the live run keeps the queue paused, `paused    delivery queue  issue=bl-2 reason=ci_timeout since=2026-11-21T14:35:06Z  resume with loop approve or a restart`. Tests, in `CiWaitOrchestratorTest.kt`: `a CI timeout at the head pauses the queue, the next merge wait does not start its merge check`, `a red CI at the head does not pause the queue`, `loop approve of an issue of the project resumes a paused queue` and `a restart resumes the queue a previous run left paused`; in `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/`: `RuntimeSnapshotTest.kt`, `StatusCommandTest.kt` and `TraceCommandTest.kt`, the `bl-175` tests, and `HumanLogTest.kt` in `spec-core`.

**Early conflict check (bl-178).** Under the delivery queue of bl-172 a queued delivery that conflicts with the base would wait its turn, then spend a whole merge round only to find the conflict. So every tick, each merge wait behind the head of the queue, its issue active, is checked against the base tip the merge waits fetched last: the head's merge check fetch, or the round skip fetch of bl-176. No new fetch is made. "After a merge lands" is the next head's merge check fetch, so the waits behind it are checked against the base the merge made. The check is `git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only --no-messages -z <tip> <head>` (git 2.38 or later), `<head>` the committed head of the wait's fingerprint, the commit the merge check would merge into, run in the issue's own workspace through `WorkspaceGit` with the pins and the env of the merge check's `git merge`. It writes objects only, never the work tree, the index or a ref, and it holds no slot, no lock and no machine slot. Exit 0 is clean and changes nothing. Exit 1 with a tree id first is a conflict naming its paths: Loop records `merge_conflict_early`, comments the m2-26 conflict comment naming the paths and the tip, hands the conflict findings to the next attempt and ends the wait, so the issue goes back to its agent and leaves the queue; its next delivery queues anew at the back. Any other answer, a missing object (also exit 1, but with no tree id), a git too old for `--write-tree`, a conflict that names no path, a timeout or a workspace Loop cannot locate, is a failed call: Loop logs a warning, records `early_check_failed` and changes nothing, and the merge check finds the conflict in its turn. A clean or conflicting answer is given at most once per wait per head and base tip, so a wait whose head and tip did not change is not checked again; a failed call does not count as checked, so the next tick tries again (bl-182). The record is in memory, so a restart checks each wait once more. As for the merge check, a pull request a human merged meanwhile is read before the issue goes back: merged at the stored head, the wait ends as a merged delivery with a human touch (bl-181), and the issue is not dispatched again, not even in the tick that read it active before the early check ended its wait. When a wait ends, by any path, its CI poll and its early check are dropped from memory: the paths of the merge waits drop them as the wait ends, and every tick drops those of an issue that no longer holds a merge wait (bl-182). Early checks keep running while the delivery queue is paused (bl-175): no merge check starts, but a conflict found early sends the issue back to its agent, which saves a round once the queue resumes. The head of the queue is never checked early, and the merge check itself is unchanged. Tests, the `bl-178` tests: in `MergeCheckOrchestratorTest.kt`, a queued wait that conflicts goes back to its agent with the conflict comment before its turn, a clean one stays queued, the head is not checked early, a failing call leaves the wait queued, and an unchanged head and tip are not checked twice; in `WorkspaceGitTest.kt`, a clean merge and a conflict that leave the work tree, the index and the refs as they were, and a missing commit that is an error, not a conflict; `HumanLogTest.kt` and `TraceCommandTest.kt`. The `bl-182` tests: in `MergeCheckOrchestratorTest.kt`, a failing call tried again at each tick with `early_check_failed`, a queued wait whose pull request a human merged that ends as merged with a human touch and is not dispatched again, a wait that ends with no CI poll and no early check left in memory, a base tip from the round skip of bl-176, and a queue three deep where only the conflicting wait goes back; in `CiWaitOrchestratorTest.kt`, a paused queue that still refuses a conflicting queued wait early; `HumanLogTest.kt` and `TraceCommandTest.kt`.

**Rate limit.** `loop run` keeps one delivery executor per repository, API URL, token and base for the life of the run (`DeliveryExecutors` in the CLI), so the rate limit hold of its GitHub client lasts across ticks and no HTTP client is built per poll. Since m4-48 the hold is shared with the GitHub trackers, see [One rate limit hold per GitHub origin and token](#one-rate-limit-hold-per-github-origin-and-token-extension-m4-48).

**Workflow changes.** A wait in phase `ci` whose workflow no longer merges goes back to an approved `Waiting(approval)`, as in m2-26; one whose workflow is now `ci: none` goes on to the merge.

**Auto merge.** Under `policy: auto` with `ci: actions`, the auto merge gate runs in the merge phase, after the CI wait, and its `ci` condition reads the Actions runs again through the same verdict, so a red, pending or missing CI at that read falls back to `Waiting(approval)` naming it, see [conditional auto merge](#conditional-auto-merge-and-merge-risk-extension-m2-28). Under `ci: none` the condition reads no run since m4-05: green there means the merge check passed.

| Event | Data | When |
|---|---|---|
| `branch_push_failed` | `issue`, `head`, `reason`, `early` = `true` | bl-181: the early push of the merge commit failed, the merge check goes on. |
| `ci_waiting` | `sha` (the merged head) | The merged head was pushed and the merge waits for its Actions runs. |
| `ci_finished` | `outcome` (`green`, `red`, `no_run_pass`, `no_run_fail`, `timeout`, and since m4-07 `not_started`), `runs` (the ids of the kept runs, by comma) | The CI wait ended on an answered poll. |
| `delivery_failed` | `step` = `ci`, `reason` | CI refused the merge, or the listing was refused for good. |
| `issue_commented` | `chars`, `by` = `host`, `reason` = `ci` | The comment naming the failed runs. |
| `delivery_queue_paused` | `issue` (the identifier of the head), `reason` = `ci_timeout` | bl-175: the head's CI wait timed out and fell back, the queue pauses. |
| `delivery_queue_resumed` | `by` (`approve` or `restart`) | bl-175: `loop approve` of any issue of the project, or a restart, ended the pause. |
| `merge_conflict_early` | `issue` (the identifier of the queued issue), `base` (the base tip, a commit), `paths` (the conflicted paths, comma separated, at most 20) | bl-178: the early conflict check of a merge wait behind the head found a conflict, the issue goes back to its agent. |
| `early_check_failed` | `issue` (the identifier of the queued issue), `head` (the committed head checked), `base` (the base tip, a commit), `reason` | bl-182: the early conflict check of a merge wait behind the head failed, the wait stays queued and the next tick tries again. |

**Shown by** `loop trace` as `ci_waiting  sha=...` and `ci_finished  outcome=red runs=30433642,30433650`, and by the human log as `waits for the Actions runs of 0123456789ab before the merge`, `CI passed, every Actions run of the merged head is green`, `CI failed, an Actions run of the merged head did not pass, the pull request stays open`, `CI passed, the merged head has no Actions run and ci_no_run is pass`, `CI failed, the merged head has no Actions run and ci_no_run is fail`, `CI timed out, Actions runs of the merged head are still pending` and `commented why CI did not let it merge`. Since m4-07 `loop trace` shows `ci_finished  outcome=not_started runs=43779352010`, and the human log `CI not started, GitHub did not start the Actions runs of the merged head, the code was not judged`. Since bl-181 `loop trace` shows `branch_push_failed  issue=bl-2 head=0123456789abcdef early=true reason="..."` and the human log `could not push the merge commit 0123456789ab before the checks, it is pushed after them: <reason>`. Since bl-175 `loop trace` shows `delivery_queue_paused  issue=bl-2 reason=ci_timeout` and `delivery_queue_resumed  by=approve`, and the human log writes run level lines, `delivery queue paused, the CI of bl-2 timed out, no merge check starts until loop approve or a restart`, `delivery queue resumed by loop approve` and `delivery queue resumed by a restart`. Since bl-178 `loop trace` shows `merge_conflict_early  issue=bl-3 base=0123456789abcdef paths=src/main/kotlin/App.kt`, and the human log writes `merging the base at 0123456789ab conflicts in src/main/kotlin/App.kt, found while queued, the issue goes back to its agent before its turn`. Since bl-182 `loop trace` shows `early_check_failed  issue=bl-3 head=fedcba9876543210 base=0123456789abcdef reason="..."`, and the human log writes `the early conflict check of fedcba987654 against the base at 0123456789ab failed, it is tried again at the next tick: <reason>`.

**Modules.** `CiWait` and `CiVerdict` in `spec-core/delivery` (the verdict, the decision and the texts); the orchestrator starts and polls the wait; `DeliveryExecutor.actionsRuns` in `plugin-api`, refused by default; `GitHubPullRequests.actionsRuns` in `plugins/tracker-github`. The tests use Actions answers written after GitHub's documented shape (`actions-runs`, `actions-forbidden` in the recorded responses), since the org's Actions minutes are spent until December 1.

Not in m3-15: sending a red CI back to the agent with findings, an allow list of workflows, the double CI run on the verified commit and on the merged head (backlog), and commit statuses (bl-60).

### Runner unavailable hold (extension, bl-39)

Run c519fe87 (2026-11-13) lost two issues to a Cloudflare block of the model API: every turn failed with `Failed to authenticate. API Error: 403`, each failure took a `max_attempts` slot after the usual 10 s to 1 m 20 s backoff, all five attempts went in about three minutes, each cost about 40K tokens to rebuild the session, and both issues parked with `max_attempts` although nothing was wrong with them. A failure of the runner's access to its model is about the runner, not the issue, and it hits every issue at once. The decisions, validated in m2-29:

- **A typed signal on `turn_failed`.** `RunnerEvent.TurnFailed` gains a nullable `category`, whose one value is `runner_unavailable` (`TurnFailed.Category.RunnerUnavailable`), and an optional `retryAfter`, the wait the runtime asked for. Both default to null, so every runner that sets neither keeps today's behavior. It is a `plugin-api` change made before the Codex runner copies the contract. The orchestrator reads only the category, never the reason text.
- **Recognized inside the runner, on error results only.** The Claude Code runner sets the category only on a `result` line with `is_error` set, from its text: `API Error: 401`, `403`, `429` or `529`, `Failed to authenticate`, `authentication_error`, `Invalid API key`, `Please run /login`, an expired token, login, session or credentials, `rate_limit_error` or a rate limit, `usage limit reached`, and `overloaded`. The same words in the agent's final answer, a tool output or any other stream line never match, and an ordinary error result (`error_max_turns`, `error_during_execution`) keeps a null category. Claude Code gives no typed retry time, so `retryAfter` comes from a `retry-after: <seconds>` in the error text when there is one. Each case has a test from a recorded result line, the 403 one from run c519fe87. Since m4-08 a lost network is recognized too, see [network loss](#network-loss-extension-m4-08).
- **No `max_attempts` slot.** The attempt ends as a failure, its turn's `turn_failed` carries `category` = `runner_unavailable` (and `retry_after_ms`), and its issue is requeued for the hold's probe: `retry_scheduled` `kind=error` with the next spec `attempt`, `due_at` the probe time, the failure as `error` and the failure counter unchanged.
- **A run level hold.** The first such failure starts the hold: no attempt and no retry is dispatched until its probe time, 1 minute after the failure. The probe is the next due retry, the first in slot order once the time comes, with no special cheap call; while it runs nothing else is dispatched, and the other due retries are requeued with the error `runner_unavailable`, keeping their first due time (like a slot requeue). A probe that fails the same way starts the next hold period, twice the last, at most 15 minutes, or the runtime's `retryAfter` when that is longer. The hold waits indefinitely: no issue is ever parked by it. A probe that ends any other way without a successful turn (an ordinary failure, a stop) makes the next probe due at once, without doubling.
- **Running attempts are left alone.** One that fails `runner_unavailable` before the probe time falls into the current period, with no new event; once the probe time passed, its failure starts the next period.
- **The lift.** Any successful turn, of any issue, lifts the hold and resets its backoff, so the next hold starts again at 1 minute.
- **An issue that hits it 3 times in a row while other issues have successful turns** counts as an ordinary failure from that third hit on: no hold, a `max_attempts` slot as today, until the issue ends any other way. The failure is then about the issue.
- **In memory only.** The hold is not stored: a restart forgets it, and the next such failure starts a new hold at 1 minute. Its requeued retries are stored like any retry (m2-02).

| Event | Data | When |
|---|---|---|
| `turn_failed` | the turn data, `reason`, and `category` = `runner_unavailable` with an optional `retry_after_ms` | The runner marked the failure as its own. |
| `runner_hold_started` | `reason`, `hold` (the period, 1 for the first), `delay_ms`, `probe_at`, optional `retry_after_ms` | A hold period began, recorded once per period with the issue and attempt whose failure began it. |
| `runner_hold_lifted` | `holds` (the periods it lasted), `held_ms` | A successful turn lifted the hold, recorded with the issue and attempt of that turn. |

**Shown by** `loop trace`, in the attempt's timeline, as `turn_failed  number=1/10 turn=turn-1 category=runner_unavailable reason="..."`, `runner_hold_started  hold=1 delay=1m probe=13:49:41 reason="..."` and `runner_hold_lifted  holds=1 held="1m 9s"`; by `loop status`, while the live run is held, as one line above the issues, `held      runner unavailable  hold=1 next_probe=10:05:50 reason="..."`; by the human log as one run level line per period, `runner unavailable, hold 1, no dispatch until the next probe in 1m at 10:05:50 UTC: <reason>`, and `runner available again after 3m, dispatch resumes`; and by the operator log as `event=runner_hold outcome=started` (with `hold`, `delay_ms`, `probe_at` and the reason) and `outcome=released`.

**Modules.** `RunnerEvent.TurnFailed` in `plugin-api`; `RunnerAccess` in the Claude Code runner's `StreamJsonMapper.kt`; `RunnerHold` (the periods, the probe and the streaks), the dispatch gate in `Orchestrator`, and `RetryCoordinator.requeueUnavailable` with `RetryQueue.onUnavailable` in `spec-core/orchestrator`; `RunnerHoldStarted` and `RunnerHoldLifted` in `spec-core/events`; the `held` line in the CLI's `RuntimeSnapshot` and `StatusCommand`.

### Diff base of pull request kinds (extension, bl-32)

`diff_policy` diffs the workspace against its merge base with its base. With `guards.base`, a local branch name resolved when the check runs, an agent that moves the branch to its own head (`git -C . update-ref refs/heads/main HEAD`, allowed even while `main` is checked out elsewhere) empties the diff: protected paths and the deny guards see nothing. Workspaces are git worktrees sharing the project's refs, so nothing local is trusted.

- **Every verification fetches.** For a kind with `delivery.pull_request`, a verification with a `diff_policy` to run, in an attempt or restored from a `Waiting(verify)` row, first fetches the base host side through the delivery executor's `fetchBase()`, the merge check's fetch, and records `base_fetched`. Every `diff_policy` of that verification, visible and hidden, takes the returned commit id as its base, so its label reads `diff_policy base=<sha> ...`. The commit id reaches the check as a value, never by reading a ref back. Its merge base with the branch is always a real base commit, so nothing needs pinning at workspace creation and nothing needs advancing: base commits the agent merged never count as its diff, and moving a local ref changes nothing.
- **A failed fetch** keeps `guards.base` and records `diff_base_unpinned` with the redacted reason. The verdict is the checks' alone: the fetch failure never changes it.
- **No fetch** for a kind without `delivery.pull_request`, nor for a verification with no `diff_policy` to run. Those kinds diff against `guards.base` as before, a known limit tracked by bl-28; they can never auto merge.
- **The merge check** passes its own fetched commit to its rerun on a merge commit, and on outcome `up_to_date` runs the `diff_policy` checks against it (step 3 of the merge check), so no pull request merges on a `diff_policy` verdict that never read a trusted base.
- **The commit graph is off.** Every git command of `diff_policy` and of `WorkspaceGit` runs with `core.commitGraph=false`, since the design relies on merge base and ancestor answers being true, and the commit graph file is writable by the agent.

Nothing is stored: the commit id flows in memory, with no table and no schema change.

### Base fetch before every attempt (extension, bl-59)

With pull request delivery Loop merges on GitHub, and nothing moves the main checkout's local `main`, so a branch cut from it starts without the merges since the last pull. On 2026-11-15 (run 284454f4) bl-85 was dispatched seconds after its blockers bl-84 and bl-86 merged on GitHub, branched from the stale `main` without them, verified, and lost the attempt to a merge check conflict; m3-16 and bl-39 lost one each in the same hour. Decided in triage on 2026-11-15:

- **Every attempt fetches.** For a kind with `delivery.pull_request`, every Attempt, the first and every retry or continuation, first fetches the base host side through the delivery executor's `fetchBase()`, the same isolated fetch with the token as the merge check's (step 1 of the merge check), and records `base_fetched`. It runs after the attempt directory is created and before `after_create` and `before_run`. A retry reuses its branch, but its prompt merges the base too, so it needs a fresh one as much as a new workspace does. A merge a human made on GitHub is covered the same way.
- **`LOOP_BASE_REF`.** The fetched commit id reaches every hook of the Attempt (`after_create`, `before_run`, `after_run` and the threshold hooks) as `LOOP_BASE_REF`. Loop's `worktree-after-create.sh` already cuts a new branch from it, see [`workspaces.md`](workspaces.md#branch). The agent's own environment does not get it.
- **The prompt names `refs/loop/base/<base>`.** The fetch writes `refs/loop/base/<base>` in the project repository at the same commit, so that is the ref a prompt tells the agent to sync with (`git merge-base --is-ancestor refs/loop/base/main HEAD`, else `git merge refs/loop/base/main`), as the merge check's send back text already does. The ref is not trusted to choose a diff base, see [Diff base of pull request kinds](#diff-base-of-pull-request-kinds-extension-bl-32); as a start point and a merge source it is what Loop fetched.
- **No local branch moves.** Loop never moves the operator's local `main` or any other branch: the fetch writes only `refs/loop/base/<base>`.
- **A failed fetch never holds the dispatch.** It records `base_fetch_failed` with the redacted reason and logs a warning, then the Attempt goes on. When an earlier fetch left `refs/loop/base/<base>`, read in the project repository through `WorkspaceGit`, the hooks get that commit as `LOOP_BASE_REF` and the event carries it as `sha`; with no such ref, `LOOP_BASE_REF` is unset and the hook's own default applies (local `main` for Loop's scripts).
- **No pull request delivery, no change.** A kind without `delivery.pull_request`, and a workflow without `kinds:`, fetches nothing and sets no `LOOP_BASE_REF`; new branches start from the local `main` and the operator pulls after each merge, as m1-42 and bl-02 decided.

A known gap stays: when the fetch of a verification fails (`diff_base_unpinned`), `diff_policy` falls back to `guards.base`, the local `main`, and base commits the agent merged from a newer `refs/loop/base/<base>` count as its diff.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/BaseFetchOrchestratorTest.kt`, and in `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/PullRequestDeliveryCommandTest.kt` the two `bl-59` tests, end to end with the real worktree hooks and a real fetch from a loopback git server.

### First real delivery on the pilot app (m2-27, bl-52)

The M2 exit on a real project, 2026-11-14. The pilot app's `WORKFLOW.md` is tracked at the root of its repository; bl-52 records its setup and m2-27 the run. The issue was worked by attempt 8962fa9e (build a35d222, `claude-opus-5-5`) in one turn of 2771 s, verified, approved with `loop approve`, and merged by Loop with `ci: none`. What it taught:

- **Silence.** A cold `xcodebuild`, a simulator slot wait or a working subagent prints nothing for many minutes, and the stall timer restarts only on a stdout line, so the default `codex.stall_timeout_ms` of 5 minutes would have stopped the turn. The pilot app sets 75 minutes and a 90 minute `turn_timeout_ms`.
- **The check reads the workspace.** The check runs `scripts/loop-check.sh` and every script it calls from the agent's own worktree, so `guards.protect` lists them, with the CI skip list, the shared schemes, `project.yml` and the skills and agents. An issue that needs a protected path ends in `needs_info`. The wrapper runs `xcodegen generate` and refuses a diff, since a new test file left out of the tracked project compiles into nothing and the suite still passes.
- **Subagents inherit the session.** Probed with the runner's argv: a subagent gets the `permissions.deny` rules of `--settings` (Bash and file rules) and the session's MCP tools, Loop's `submit_result`, `move` and `comment` included. The prompt is what keeps them with the main session. Without `--strict-mcp-config` the agent and its subagents also get every MCP server of the operator, under `bypassPermissions`; the pilot app passed it through a wrapper command until bl-72 made it the runner's default ([strict MCP](#strict-mcp-extension-bl-72)).
- **Tokens.** Loop recorded 8,844,163 tokens and $11.02; the transcripts of the session and its 8 subagents add up to 21,878,566. Token budgets and thresholds undercounted a delegating skill, so the pilot app uses dollar budgets. bl-71 found that `total_cost_usd` already covered the subagents and made the tokens count them, see [Claude Code subagent spend](#claude-code-subagent-spend-extension-bl-71).
- **The token.** Kept in the Keychain and set only in the `loop run` process, so no agent shell sources it. `loop status`, `trace` and `log` then need it too (bl-70).
- **Base.** The worktree hooks start a branch from the main checkout's local `main`, which lagged behind (bl-59); the pilot app's hooks fetch `origin main` first and pass `LOOP_BASE_REF=origin/main`. Since bl-59 Loop fetches the base itself and sets `LOOP_BASE_REF`, see [Base fetch before every attempt](#base-fetch-before-every-attempt-extension-bl-59).
- **The merge check on an unmoved base** was `up_to_date`: only `diff_policy` reran, as bl-32 specifies, so the unit suite ran once, at verification. The suite reruns on the merged head only when the base moved.
- **Writes on the issue:** the evidence summary at the approval request (801 characters), the pull request comment, and the close by `Closes #<number>`; no `issue_moved` was needed and no 422 came (compare bl-68). Labels Loop does not own, `in-progress` here, stay for a human.

### GitHub tracker, write side (extension, m2-23)

Stories 80 and 81. The `github` tracker writes: moves, comments and label writes; its [adapter profile](plugins/tracker-github.md#states) is the reference. Until now `loop tool move review` was refused on GitHub, whose only states are `open` and `closed`. The decisions:

- **Label states:** the tracker defines `review` and `needs_info` as handoff states made of labels, beside `open` and `closed`, so the tool move accepts them; since bl-46 preflight refuses them in `tracker.active_states` and `tracker.terminal_states`. `tracker.provider.handoff_labels` sets the label of each, by default `review: needs-review` and `needs_info: needs-info`, the labels of m2-11 and the playground. A move to one adds its label and removes every `tracker.required_labels` label, the required labels of the effective workflow at the time of the move. It is refused, with nothing sent, when there is no required label or the handoff label is one of them, since the issue would never leave the active set; preflight refuses both cases too, the first since [bl-46](#github-tracker-handoff-preflight-extension-bl-46). Issues keep GitHub's state when read: a handed off issue reads `open`.
- **Order and partial failure:** the handoff label is added first, then the required labels are removed. Both calls are idempotent, and a removal answered 404 counts as success. A failure after the add leaves the handoff label on with the routing label still there; the move fails, the issue stays eligible, and the next move attempt repeats both calls.
- **The orchestrator sees its own move as a handoff:** a move the drain applied to a handoff state, a defined state neither active nor terminal, counts as a handoff whatever the issue reads after it, since after a label move it is still `open` and a read right after the write may not show the labels yet. The attempt runs no continuation turn and ends with `not_active`, its workspace is kept, its retry entry is dropped with the counters reset, and no continuation retry is scheduled. A reconciliation stop of the attempt after its own handoff is `not_active` too, with no `human_touch`. This holds for the `files` tracker as well, where the only change is that the continuation retry after a handoff move is no longer scheduled: its timer used to drop it.
- **Moves to `open` and `closed`:** a `PATCH` of the issue state. Agents never move to them (Loop's prompt forbids it), but the tracker supports them.
- **Return path:** a human sends an issue back by restoring `ready-for-agent`. Removing `needs-review` is the human's job too.
- **Comments:** posted as given, already redacted. GitHub's limit is 65,536 characters, counted as code points: a longer comment is refused with `Rejected` and a clear message, never cut. After a comment write the tracker adds one to the comment count it holds for bl-06, so the next dispatch reads the new comment.
- **Errors and rate limit:** `TrackerError` gains `Rejected`, a write the tracker refuses before sending it. A 403, 404 or 422 on a write is `Status` and is recorded as `tool_request_failed`. A write is never retried inside a call, and writes respect the rate limit hold of the read side; a write answered with a rate limit holds the reads.
- **The token:** only a `$NAME` reference keeps it out of the agent's reach: removed from the agent env, left out of the tool config snapshot, redacted from events and traces, as before. A literal token in `WORKFLOW.md` is out of scope.
- **Contract:** `writesLabels` is true for the GitHub tracker, and the label write tests of `TrackerAdapterContract` run against simulated label endpoints. The write responses are written by hand after GitHub's documented shapes, since no test writes to a real repository.

Not in m2-23: the real run against the playground repository, which needs `LOOP_PLAYGROUND_GITHUB_TOKEN` that no agent sees (bl-40, by a human).

### GitHub tracker, handoff preflight (extension, bl-46)

With the defaults, a `github` workflow could start and never hand off: `tracker.required_labels` defaults to empty and the tracker refuses every handoff move without a required label, so the refusal came at the drain, after the work, and the attempt ran to `max_turns` and its continuations until the issue parked. The decisions:

- **Empty required labels:** preflight fails with `invalid_config` on a `github` tracker whose `tracker.required_labels` is empty once blank labels are dropped, naming the key and saying every move to `review` or `needs_info` would be refused. The `files` tracker is unchanged: its handoff is a state, not a label change.
- **Handoff state listed as active or terminal:** preflight fails with `invalid_config` when `review` or `needs_info` is in `tracker.active_states` or `tracker.terminal_states` on a `github` tracker. A list reads issues by GitHub's state only, so such a state matches no issue, and the orchestrator counts a move only to a state neither active nor terminal as a handoff, so the worker would go on.
- **Where:** both are part of the tracker check of the preflight (`trackerProblem`), run at startup and on every tick. A reload that would cause either is rejected like any invalid edit ([workflow reload](#workflow-reload-clarification-of-62-m1-15)): `config_reload_failed`, dispatch skipped, the last good workflow kept, so a reload can no longer empty the required labels of a running attempt.
- **Order:** an unknown state first, then a handoff state listed as active or terminal, then empty required labels, then a handoff label that is also required (m2-23). The message is the first problem found.

### Runner plugins (extension, m3-04)

Story 43, runner half, and plan 12 (in process plugins). `runner.kind` selects a runner plugin instead of a `when` in the host, so a new runner, the Codex runner first, is a module that registers itself. The decisions:

- **The interface:** `RunnerProvider` in `plugin-api`: its `kind`, its `section` under `runners` (the kind unless it says otherwise, `codex` for `codex-app-server`), `validate(config)`, which reads the section and throws `RunnerConfigException` naming the key, and `create(config, env, holdoutDir)`, which builds the `AgentRunner`. `config` is the section as written, string, list, map or null values, an empty map when the section is absent. `env` is the agent env policy the host built before, `AgentEnvPolicy` (the host env, the removed names and `agent.env`, see [agent environment](#agent-environment-extension-m1-05-m1-23-and-bl-01)). A runner reports its configured model through `AgentRunner.model`, which the ledger records (`model`, see [ledger](#ledger-extension-m2-19)), since spec-core no longer reads a runner section.
- **Discovery:** Java's `ServiceLoader`. The `command` and `claude-code` runners register through a `META-INF/services/loop.api.RunnerProvider` resource in their modules, `CommandRunnerProvider` and `ClaudeCodeRunnerProvider`, found on the classpath. Each plugin parses its own section: `runners.command.command` in `plugins/runner-command`, the [Claude Code keys](#claude-code-runner-configuration-keys-extension-m1-05) in `plugins/runner-claude-code`. The declared profile (keys, defaults, secrets, limits) and its startup check came in m3-05, see [plugin profiles](#plugin-profiles-extension-m3-05).
- **The plugin folder:** besides the classpath, every `*.jar` file directly in `<LOOP_HOME>/plugins/` is loaded with its own class loader, whose parent is Loop's, so a plugin shares `plugin-api` and the libraries Loop ships (Kotlin, coroutines). A JAR counts only for the providers its own class loader defines. An absent folder holds no plugin. The folder is read once per process, when a command first loads `WORKFLOW.md`; a new JAR needs a restart.
- **Order:** the kinds found are listed classpath first, in `ServiceLoader` order, then the JARs' by file name.
- **Where it is checked:** spec-core never depends on a runner plugin. `WorkflowLoader` takes a `RunnerCheck`, the host's plugins, and asks it on every load, so at startup, in the preflight of every tick and on every reload: the kind must have a provider, which must accept its section. A loader built without one (spec-core and conformance tests) accepts any kind and reads no section. `WorkflowFile.runner` is `RunnerSettings(kind, sections)`, the kind and the `runners` map as written.
- **Errors:** an unknown `runner.kind` fails preflight with `invalid_config`, naming the kind and the kinds found: `runner.kind "nope" is not supported, use command, claude-code or codex-app-server` (`no runner plugin was found` when there is none). A section its provider refuses fails the same way with the provider's sentence, for example `runners.command.command is required`; a section that is not a map gives `runners.<section> must be a map`. Two providers of one kind fail startup naming both: `two runner plugins provide runner.kind "command": loop.runner.command.CommandRunnerProvider from the classpath and com.example.OtherProvider from <LOOP_HOME>/plugins/other.jar`. A JAR whose services entry names a class that cannot load fails startup naming the JAR. As every load failure, a reload that causes one is rejected and the last good workflow kept.
- **Not moved:** the merge risk reviewer stays wired to Claude Code (`riskReviewerFor`), since m4-06 for every `runner.kind`, and so does the Claude Code machine check (`runnerPreflight`, the uid and managed settings). m3-05 added the profile check of the section, not a plugin hook for machine checks.

Tests: `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/RunnerPluginsTest.kt` (selection by kind through `runnerFor`, the classpath providers, a provider in a JAR built at test time under `<LOOP_HOME>/plugins/`, selected in process and through the installed launcher, the unknown kind, a refused section and two providers of one kind), with `PluginJars.kt`, which compiles the provider from Java source so only the JAR defines it; `CommandRunnerProviderTest` and `ClaudeCodeRunnerProviderTest` in their plugin modules; the loader seam in `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workflow/WorkflowFileTest.kt`; the runner block of a workflow loaded through the host, the tests that read `RunnerSettings` until m3-04, in `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/RunnerWorkflowTest.kt`.

### Tracker plugins (extension, m3-05)

Story 43, tracker half, and plan 12. `tracker.kind` selects a tracker plugin found the way runner plugins are (m3-04), instead of the host's `TRACKER_KINDS` map and a `when` in `trackerFor`. The decisions:

- **The interface:** `TrackerProvider` in `plugin-api`: its `kind`, its `profile`, its profile default states (`defaultActiveStates`, `defaultTerminalStates`, spec 5.3.1), its `definedStates`, known without building the tracker, `parse(provider, env)`, the settings reader, which throws `TrackerConfigException` with a spec 11.4 category, and `create(settings, context)`, which builds the `TrackerAdapter`. `TrackerContext` holds what the host gives every tracker: the resolved `tracker.provider.dir`, the lock folder, the malformed and warning callbacks and the effective `tracker.required_labels`. It replaces spec-core's `TrackerKind` (bl-23).
- **Discovery:** the `files` and `github` trackers register through a `META-INF/services/loop.api.TrackerProvider` resource in their modules, `FilesTrackerProvider` and `GitHubTrackerProvider`. Like runners, providers are found on the classpath, then in each JAR of `<LOOP_HOME>/plugins/`. The host opens the plugin folder once, so the runner and tracker plugins of one JAR share its class loader. Two providers of one kind fail startup naming both: `two tracker plugins provide tracker.kind "files": loop.tracker.files.FilesTrackerProvider from the classpath and com.example.OtherProvider from <LOOP_HOME>/plugins/other.jar`.
- **Where it is checked:** `WorkflowLoader` takes a `TrackerCheck`, the host's tracker plugins, and asks it for the provider of `tracker.kind` on every load. An unknown kind fails preflight with `invalid_config` naming the kinds found, sorted: `tracker.kind "jira" is not supported, use files or github`. A loader built without one (spec-core and conformance tests, which see no tracker plugin) accepts only `files`, with its default states and no settings. `trackerFor` builds the tracker with the matching provider, and throws for a kind no provider answers to (bl-104), which a loaded workflow never has; the tracker check of the preflight reads the provider's `definedStates`. The github handoff checks (bl-46, m2-23) stay in the host.
- **Not changed:** `tracker.provider.dir` is still resolved by the loader, for every kind, and the categories and messages of the github settings reader are those of its [adapter profile](plugins/tracker-github.md#configuration).

Tests: `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/TrackerPluginsTest.kt` (selection by kind through `trackerFor`, the classpath providers, a provider in a JAR built at test time, selected in process and through the installed launcher, an unknown kind, two providers of one kind); `FilesTrackerProviderTest` and `GitHubTrackerProviderTest` in their plugin modules; the loader seam in `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workflow/TrackerKindTest.kt` (the bl-23 cases, ported to providers) and `TrackerProviderTest.kt` (`files` as a provider, the profile check). The `TrackerAdapterContract` tests of both trackers are unchanged.

### Plugin profiles (extension, m3-05)

Plan 12: "Each plugin has a profile (its config keys, defaults, secrets and limits)". Every runner and tracker plugin declares one, and Loop checks the section of each selected plugin against it at startup. The decisions:

- **The type:** `PluginProfile` in `plugin-api`: its `keys`, each a `ProfileKey` with its name, its `ProfileType` (`STRING`, `INTEGER`, `LIST`, `MAP`, and since bl-72 `BOOLEAN`, `true` or `false` in any case, bare or quoted) and whether it is required or its documented default; its `secrets`, the keys whose value may be a `$NAME` reference to the host env; its `limits`, the allowed range of integer keys. `RunnerProvider.profile` and `TrackerProvider.profile` declare it. `RunnerProvider.profile` defaults to `PluginProfile.UNDECLARED` (bl-104), which checks no key and warns of none, so a runner JAR built against m3-04 still loads instead of failing with an `AbstractMethodError`. The check, `PluginProfile.check(section, config, env)`, lives beside it, so a plugin can test its own profile.
- **What is checked:** the selected runner's section `runners.<section>` and the selected tracker's `tracker.provider`, never the section of a runner that is not selected. Keys are checked in profile order and the first problem fails: a required key absent, null or blank; a value of the wrong type (an integer may be written bare or quoted); a secret written `$NAME` whose variable is unset or blank; an integer outside its limits. A secret written as a literal is left to the plugin, as the github tracker accepts a literal token. A section that is not a map fails with `<section> must be a map`.
- **When:** in `WorkflowLoader`, on every load of `WORKFLOW.md`, so at startup, in the preflight of every tick and on every reload, before the plugin reads the section itself (`RunnerProvider.validate`, `TrackerProvider.parse`). A reload that fails it is rejected and the last good workflow kept.
- **Error codes:** a runner section fails with `invalid_config`, an unresolved runner secret with `missing_env_var`, the code of every other unset `$NAME`. A tracker section keeps the spec 11.4 categories its settings reader uses: `invalid_tracker_config`, and `missing_tracker_secret` for a secret key that is missing, of the wrong type or unresolved. The issue asked for `invalid_config`; the tracker categories win so `tracker.provider.repo is required` and `tracker.provider.token is required` keep the categories the github profile documents.
- **Messages:** each names the full key, `runners.claude-code.turn_timeout_ms must be between 1 and 86400000, got 0`, `tracker.provider.repo is required`, `runners.command.command must be a string`. An unresolved secret names the key and the variable, `tracker.provider.token uses $GITHUB_TOKEN, which is not set` (or `which is empty`), never a value. A type error prints no value; only an integer out of its limits is printed, and not for a key listed in `secrets` (bl-104), `<key> must be between 1 and 9999`.
- **Unknown keys:** a key the profile does not list stays allowed, for forward compatibility, with a warning: `runners.command.comand is not a key of the command runner, Loop ignores it`. The loader keeps them on `WorkflowFile.configWarnings`; `loop run` prints each at startup as `loop run: warning: <warning>.`, and a reload that adds one logs it once with the other reload warnings.
- **The built in profiles:** published in [`plugins/runner-command.md`](plugins/runner-command.md), [`plugins/runner-claude-code.md`](plugins/runner-claude-code.md), [`plugins/tracker-files.md`](plugins/tracker-files.md#profile) and [`plugins/tracker-github.md`](plugins/tracker-github.md#profile), each with a `## Profile` table that a test compares with the declared profile. The Claude Code timeouts gained a limit, 1 to 86400000 ms (one day); any positive integer was accepted before.

Tests: `plugin-api/src/test/kotlin/loop/api/PluginProfileTest.kt` (the check); `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/PluginProfilesTest.kt` (a missing required key, a wrong type, a value out of its limits and an unresolved secret each failing preflight on a built in plugin, the secret through the installed launcher with no value printed, and the startup warning of an unknown key); `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/PluginProfileDocsTest.kt` (each built in plugin's doc matches its profile); the loader in `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workflow/WorkflowFileTest.kt` and `TrackerProviderTest.kt`; the profiles in each plugin's provider test.

### Runner capabilities and the `AgentRunner` contract (extension, m3-06)

Story 97, runner half, and the contract part of stories 88 and 94, plan 6.3 and 8.3. The runner contract is fixed before the Codex runner copies it. The decisions:

- **Capabilities:** `RunnerCapabilities` in `plugin-api` (`AgentRunner.kt`), declared by every runner as `AgentRunner.capabilities`, with no default, so a runner cannot leave it out:

  | Capability | Meaning | `command` | `claude-code` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `continuation` | `openSession` returns a session for turns 2 to n; false means it returns null | true | true |
  | `loopTools` | how Loop's tools reach the agent: `mcp` (Loop's MCP server), `client_tools` (tools the runner serves to the runtime, the Codex app-server) or `files` (`loop tool` and the result file) | `files` | `mcp` |
  | `usage` | the runner emits `Usage`, absolute totals for the session | false | true |
  | `cost` | `Usage.totalCostUsd` is set; it needs `usage`, the constructor refuses a cost without it | false | true |
  | `occupancy` | the runner emits `ContextUsage`; false means Loop can enforce only the prompt limit and a turn cap (plan 8.3, m3-09) | false | true since m3-08 |
  | `sandbox` | the agent runs in an OS sandbox or a container; the deny rules of generated settings are a guardrail, not a sandbox (plan 11.1) | false | false |

  m3-09 reads `occupancy` for the [weaker guarantee line](#peak-occupancy-and-runners-without-occupancy-extension-m3-09). The runner profiles list them with the reason for each value, in [`plugins/runner-command.md`](plugins/runner-command.md#capabilities) and [`plugins/runner-claude-code.md`](plugins/runner-claude-code.md#capabilities-and-telemetry).
- **The `context_usage` event:** `RunnerEvent.ContextUsage(sessionId, turnId, occupancyTokens)`. The occupancy is the input size of the latest model request of the main session, cached tokens included (input, cache read and cache creation tokens of that one request), never a cumulative total: a new report replaces the last one and may be smaller. It covers the main session only; a subagent's window is its own and is not counted. Spend never reads it, spend reads `Usage`. Since m3-08 it also carries the `model` of the request, `provider/model`, and Claude Code emits it, see [Claude Code context occupancy](#claude-code-context-occupancy-extension-m3-08).
- **The contract:** `AgentRunnerContract` in `plugin-api/src/testFixtures/kotlin/loop/api/`, beside `TrackerAdapterContract`. A runner's test extends it and supplies runners driving fake agents: `succeedingRunner`, `failingRunner`, `hangingRunner` (an agent that starts a child process that never ends), and optionally `exitsWithoutResultRunner`, `inputRequiredRunner` and `unavailableRunner`, each null when the runtime has no such signal. `CommandRunnerContractTest` runs it with shell commands, `ClaudeCodeRunnerContractTest` with the fake `claude` scripts of the runner tests. The cases:

  | Case | What it checks |
  |---|---|
  | `SessionStarted comes first and its session id is the thread id and the turn id` | turn 1 opens with `SessionStarted`, session id `<thread_id>-<turn_id>` |
  | `a succeeding agent ends with exactly one terminal event, TurnCompleted, last` | one terminal event, the last |
  | `a failing agent ends with exactly one terminal event, TurnFailed, last` | one terminal event, the last |
  | `an agent that exits without a turn result ends with exactly one TurnFailed, last` | skipped when the runner has no turn result apart from the exit code (`command`) |
  | `an agent that asks for input ends with exactly one terminal event, TurnInputRequired, last` | skipped when the runtime has no such signal (`command`) |
  | `a runtime that cannot reach its model fails the turn with runner_unavailable` | bl-39; skipped when the runtime has no such signal (`command`) |
  | `a TurnFailed category is null or runner_unavailable` | every `TurnFailed` of every run the contract observes |
  | `every event after SessionStarted carries its session id and turn id` | ids of `Usage`, `UsageEstimate`, `ContextUsage` and the terminal events |
  | `openSession matches the declared continuation` | a session exactly when `continuation` is declared |
  | `resumeSession returns a session only with continuation` | m4-46: no session without `continuation`; the default, null, passes |
  | `a resumed session starts with SessionStarted and ends with exactly one terminal event` | m4-46: turn 1 of a session `resumeSession` gave, with the runtime session id of a first session; skipped when the runner keeps the default |
  | `a second turn keeps the thread, starts a new turn and stops the session` | turn 2 opens with `TurnStarted`, same thread, new turn id, one terminal event; runs when `continuation` is declared |
  | `Usage is reported exactly when the runner declares usage` | no `Usage` without `usage`; at least one in a succeeding run with it |
  | `a cost is reported exactly when the runner declares cost` | no cost without `cost`; a cost on the last `Usage` of a succeeding run with it |
  | `ContextUsage is reported exactly when the runner declares occupancy` | no `ContextUsage` without `occupancy`; at least one in a succeeding run with it, never negative |
  | `Usage values are absolute and never decrease within a session` | no token count (nor the cost) goes down from one `Usage` to the next, within a turn and across the two turns of a session |
  | `the agent runs in the workspace and sees the context env` | working directory and context env |
  | `cancelling the collection kills the agent process and its children` | the agent and every child it started are gone after cancel |

  The capability cases look at every run the runner's test supplies plus a two turn session of the succeeding runner when it opens one. `loopTools` and `sandbox` are declared, not checked: the contract has no runtime neutral way to observe them.
- **A wrong declaration fails:** `plugin-api/src/test/kotlin/loop/api/AgentRunnerContractCapabilitiesTest.kt` runs the capability cases against an honest in memory runner, which passes, and against deliberately wrong ones (a session without declared continuation and the reverse, undeclared usage, cost and occupancy and declared ones never reported, usage that goes down within a turn or between turns), each failing with the contract's own message.

Tests: `plugin-api/src/test/kotlin/loop/api/AgentRunnerContractCapabilitiesTest.kt`, `plugins/runner-command/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/command/CommandRunnerContractTest.kt`, `plugins/runner-claude-code/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/claudecode/ClaudeCodeRunnerContractTest.kt`.

### Read commands without secrets (extension, bl-70)

`loop status`, `loop trace`, `loop log` and `loop show` read the store, the run file and the logs, so they no longer need the secrets of `WORKFLOW.md`. An operator who keeps the tracker token in the Keychain and sets it only in the `loop run` process can run them from any shell, and never has to put a write token in a shell startup file. The decisions:

- **Which commands:** every command that opens its project for read only (`Access.READ`): `status`, `trace`, `log` and `show`. Every command that writes the store or the tracker (`run`, `approve`, `resume`, `stop`) still requires every secret and refuses at startup naming the key and the variable, as before. `loop approve` leads to a delivery, so it keeps the check.
- **How:** the loader of a read command is built with `deferSecrets`. A secret written `$NAME` whose variable is unset or blank, of the selected tracker's profile, of the selected runner's profile or `kinds.code.delivery.pull_request.token`, is named in `WorkflowFile.deferredSecrets` instead of failing the load. Every other check runs as usual: the profile check and the tracker plugin's own parse see a stand in for the missing value, so a key that is required, of the wrong type or out of its limits still fails the read command. The stand in never reaches a tracker: `trackerSettings` is null when the tracker's secret was deferred. A `$NAME` path such as `workspace.root` is not a secret and still fails.
- **The tracker:** `OpenedProject.trackerSecret` is the deferred secret of `tracker.provider`, and `OpenedProject.tracker()` throws a startup failure naming it, so a read command that needs the tracker still names the variable rather than sending a request without a token.
- **`loop trace` without the tracker:** with the tracker's secret unset it reads no tracker. It prints `loop trace: the tracker is not read, tracker.provider.token uses $NAME, which is not set. The issue is looked up by its id in the store.` on stderr, looks the issue up by its id in the store (the issue number for the github tracker), and heads the trace with `Issue <identifier>` from the recorded events, without the title. An id with no recorded attempt prints `No attempt recorded for this issue in project <id>.` and exits 0, since the store cannot tell an unknown issue from one never worked. With the secret set, `loop trace` reads the tracker as before.
- **Redaction:** nothing changes. The redactor learns the secrets the host env holds; an unset one has no value to learn.

Tests: `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ReadCommandsWithoutSecretTest.kt` (`loop status`, `loop trace` and `loop log` through the installed launcher on a github workflow whose token variable is unset, sending no request, `loop trace` still reading the tracker with the token set, and `loop approve` refusing on the same workflow, naming the variable); `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/OpenProjectTest.kt` (a project opened for read defers the token, one opened for write refuses it); `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workflow/DeferredSecretsTest.kt` (the loader: tracker, runner and delivery secrets deferred, the other checks kept, a path variable still failing).

### Strict MCP (extension, bl-72)

PLAN 11.1. An attempt runs under `--permission-mode bypassPermissions`, so every MCP tool the session loads is called with no prompt, by the agent and by every subagent it spawns (bl-52). Without `--strict-mcp-config`, Claude Code loads, beside Loop's server, every MCP server of the operator's user config, of the project (`.mcp.json` in the workspace, which the agent can write) and the operator's claude.ai connectors. Probed on Claude Code 2.1.319 with an empty `--mcp-config`: 24 servers without the flag, among them analytics, mail, payment and app store publishing tools that can act on real accounts, and none with it, the flag alone or with the config. The decisions:

- **The setting:** `runners.claude-code.strict_mcp`, a boolean of the runner's [profile](plugins/runner-claude-code.md#profile), `true` or `false` in any case, bare or quoted; any other value fails preflight with `invalid_config`, `runners.claude-code.strict_mcp must be a boolean`. `ProfileType.BOOLEAN` was added to `plugin-api` for it, since a runner section reaches its plugin with every scalar as a string.
- **The default is strict, `true`, in Loop mode and in Symphony mode.** The runner is given an attempt, not the mode, and the risk is the same in both: a tool that writes outside the workspace, called with no prompt, by an agent working an issue it did not choose. Strict is the safe choice, and turning it off is one line. With it each turn passes `--strict-mcp-config` right after `--permission-mode bypassPermissions`, followed by `--mcp-config` with Loop's server when the attempt has one; without that config (a runner run outside an attempt Loop started) the flag alone loads no server. A wrapper script such as the pilot app's `claude-strict.sh` is no longer needed.
- **`strict_mcp: false`** brings back the behavior from m2-16 to bl-72: Loop's server beside every other server Claude Code finds. It is for a workflow whose agents need the operator's servers and whose operator accepts them under `bypassPermissions`.
- **Not in this issue:** listing extra MCP servers in `WORKFLOW.md` to load under strict. A workflow that needs one server turns strict off for now.
- **The merge risk reviewer** is unchanged: it always passes `--strict-mcp-config` with an empty config, whatever the setting ([conditional auto merge](#conditional-auto-merge-and-merge-risk-extension-m2-28)).
- **The Codex runner** follows the same default, to be verified against the pinned Codex CLI in m3-02. Codex reads MCP servers from the `mcp_servers` table of `~/.codex/config.toml` (and from a project's `.codex/config.toml` in the versions that read one, m3-02 checks the pinned one), and the app-server would hand them to the agent like Claude Code does. Its section, `runners.codex`, gets the same key, `strict_mcp`, `true` by default: the runner starts the app-server so that no `mcp_servers` entry of the operator's or the project's config loads, while keeping the operator's Codex login, which rules out pointing `CODEX_HOME` at an empty folder. Loop's own tools reach a Codex agent as client tools (`loopTools` = `client_tools`), not through MCP, so strict leaves the Codex agent no MCP server at all. m3-02 checked the pinned CLI: neither `-c mcp_servers={}` nor the per thread config empties the table, a trusted project's `.codex/config.toml` adds servers, and a ChatGPT login adds `codex_apps`. The mechanisms that work are in [Codex CLI, app server schema and MCP loading](#codex-cli-app-server-schema-and-mcp-loading-m3-02). If none applies, the Codex runner refuses to start with `strict_mcp: true` rather than run with the operator's servers, and says so at startup. Built in m3-20 on the first mechanism, see [Codex runner approvals, user input, tool calls and guards](#codex-runner-approvals-user-input-tool-calls-and-guards-m3-20).

Tests: `plugins/runner-claude-code/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/claudecode/ClaudeCodeRunnerTest.kt` (the `bl-72` tests: strict by default on every process of a session, before Loop's config; the flag alone without a config; `strict_mcp: false` without it), `ClaudeCodeRunnerProviderTest.kt` (the profile key and its default, the parse, a refused value, the runner built with the setting), `plugin-api/src/test/kotlin/loop/api/PluginProfileTest.kt` (the boolean type), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/PluginProfilesTest.kt` (a value that is not a boolean fails preflight, `true`, `false` and `"false"` load with no warning) and `PluginProfileDocsTest.kt` (the profile table of the runner's doc).

### Claude Code long lived process (extension of 10.3, m3-07)

Symphony 10.3 keeps the agent process alive across continuation turns. Since m3-07 the Claude Code runner does, behind the same `AgentSession` interface, with the decisions triaged in m1-30 (2026-11-08):
