- **One process per Attempt.** Turn 1 spawns `claude -p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose` plus the usual flags (see [the configuration keys](#claude-code-runner-configuration-keys-extension-m1-05)), through the same process group launcher. Each turn writes one user message line, `{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":...}]}}`, and keeps stdin open. There is no switch: this replaces the process per turn of m1-06. `LaunchSpec.stdinLines` in `plugins/runner-process` keeps stdin open while its channel is.
- **Turn boundary.** The first `result` line after the input ends the turn and decides it, as `StreamJsonMapper.turnEvents` did at exit before. The process is not expected to exit. A completed turn reports exit code 0 and a failed one none, since the process still runs. Turns 2 to n emit `TurnStarted` at once; turn 1 still emits `SessionStarted` at `init`, with the runtime session id.
- **Silence rules.** During a turn, `read_timeout_ms` runs until the first stdout line of the turn (`response_timeout`) and `turn_timeout_ms` of stdout silence gives `turn_timeout`; both kill the process group. Between turns the process is idle by design and no timer runs on it: it lives until the next turn or `stop()`. Stall detection (m1-09) only watches turns in progress.
- **Lines written while idle.** What the process writes between turns, such as an agent woken by a background task, is read by the next turn: emitted as output right after its start event, its `result` lines adding their usage without ending that turn. A turn that finds a wake still running, an `init` or `assistant` line read while idle with no `result` line since, keeps reading it as idle lines until its `result` line, and only then writes its input, so the wake's `result` line cannot end the new turn while the real answer comes later. The wait follows the turn's silence rule: `turn_timeout_ms` with no stdout line kills the group and fails the turn with `turn_timeout`, its input never written; a process that exits during the wait died between turns, see the respawn below. A wake has no marker line before its `result` (the bl-25 probe shows `assistant` lines then a `result`), so a wake that has started but written no line yet when the input is written can still end that turn at its `result` line. A segment written after the last turn is lost to the trace and to the ledger when the session stops. Both are limits of a result line ending the turn, accepted as known limits and tracked in bl-114. Since bl-132 a `result` read at the stop counts in the attempt's spend. Since m4-03 a subagent's `assistant` line read while idle no longer marks a wake (bl-122), and a wake's `result` read after the input, whose `origin.kind` is `task-notification`, does not end the turn unless the turn was interrupted, which closes the first limit for the wakes Claude Code marks that way; the idle queue is bounded, see [Wake turns](#wake-turns-extension-m4-03).
- **Other lines.** The recording shows lines a process per turn did not print: a second `system` `init` line at the start of each later turn, with the same session id, and `rate_limit_event`, `system` `post_turn_summary` and `system` `task_summary` lines, one `task_summary` after the last `result` line, before the exit. A later `init` line starts no new session and only refreshes the stored runtime session id; the other lines are kept as runner output of the turn they come in, and a line after the last `result` is read while idle, so it belongs to no turn.
- **Respawn.** A process that exited between turns, or that a cancelled, timed out or failed turn killed, is not an Attempt failure by itself: the next turn spawns a new long lived process with `--resume <runtime session id>`, after a `loop: warning: ...` line on stderr, the runner's WARN, recorded as runner output. A process of an earlier turn that exits before writing any line of the new turn counts as died between turns, since its exit may not have been read when the input was written. A turn whose process exits after writing for it, before its `result` line, fails with `port_exit` as before. A rotation (m3-11) never respawns with `--resume`.
- **Usage.** Every `usage` event stays the absolute total of the session, so the token budget (m1-08, m2-20) works unchanged. The runner sums each `result` line's `usage` and keeps the latest `total_cost_usd`, the bl-25 rule. A real two turn recording of one `claude -p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose` process (Claude Code 2.1.319, haiku, `plugins/runner-claude-code/src/test/resources/streams/real_two_turns_one_process.jsonl`) pins it: each `result` line carries its own turn's usage, summed over that turn's API calls (cache creation 18,933 tokens, then 15,623), not a running total, while `total_cost_usd` and `num_turns` are the session's so far (0.00403, then 0.00791). The test `usage stays absolute for the session across the turns of one process` runs on that recording. The bl-108 rule holds: each `result` line with usage is followed at once by a `usage` event, the session total so far, whether the line ends the turn or was written while the process was idle and read at the start of the next turn, so that turn keeps the spend if a stall or a `loop stop` cancels it later. A turn's end emits no usage of its own. A `result` line followed by silence in one turn is now such an idle line, since a `result` line read during a turn ends it; the end to end stall tests of bl-108 see the `result` line complete turn 1 and the stall in turn 2.
- **Stop.** `stop()` closes stdin, gives the process 5 s to exit, then kills its group. `stop(usage)`, `stop(report)` since m4-04, does the same and reports the usage of each `result` line read meanwhile, a wake that ended while the process was idle or during the grace (bl-132), and, when the grace ends with a wake still running, the usage of its messages then `StopGraceExpired` (m4-04). It throws when the group cannot be stopped (the launcher reported members it could not kill, or a probe after the kill still finds some) or when the settings folder cannot be deleted. A cancelled, timed out or failed turn kills its process at once and probes the group: a group with members left is probed again by `stop()`, which throws when it still has some, so the common stop after a cancelled turn records `session_stop_failed` too. A launcher that failed while the process was idle counts as a process that did not exit, so the group probe decides; the settings folder is deleted in every case. `run()`, a single turn, stops its session before the terminal event and reports a stop problem as a `loop:` stderr line before it.
- **`session_stop_failed`.** The orchestrator's session stop catch records a `session_stop_failed` event (data: `reason`, the error) next to the WARN log, with the Attempt's ids. That catch is the only orchestrator change: the turn loop does not change.

Tests: `ClaudeCodeRunnerTest` (one process for every turn, a turn timeout and a response timeout on the live process, an idle process left alone past both timeouts, a respawn with `--resume` after the process died between turns or was killed by a cancelled turn, idle lines read by the next turn, a turn that waits for a running wake's `result` line before it writes its input, a wake that times out or whose process exits during the wait, the stop cases, a group left by a cancelled or timed out turn failing the stop, a launcher failure while idle, usage and the extra line types on the real two turn recording), `ClaudeCodeRunnerContractTest`, `ProcessGroupLauncherTest` (stdin kept open), `OrchestratorTurnsTest` (`session_stop_failed`), and `ClaudeCodeRunCommandTest` (a background wake between turns counts in the next turn, end to end).

### Claude Code context occupancy (extension, m3-08)

Story 88 for Claude Code, plan 8.3. The Claude Code runner maps the usage of each assistant message of the main session to a `context_usage` runner event (m3-06), declares `occupancy`, and the orchestrator records the session's occupancy at each turn end. Enforcement, the soft and hard limits and rotation, is m3-11 and m3-12. The decisions:

- **The mapping.** The occupancy of one assistant message is `input_tokens + cache_read_input_tokens + cache_creation_input_tokens` of its `message.usage`: the input size of that request, cached tokens included. Output tokens never count. On the real two turn recording of m3-07 (`real_two_turns_one_process.jsonl`) that gives 43,302 tokens for turn 1, then 43,351 and 43,455 for the two messages of turn 2, while the `result` lines report each turn's usage summed over its requests, which spend keeps reading.
- **One event per message.** Claude Code writes one `assistant` line per content block, each with the message's id and the same usage, so only the first line of a message id emits `ContextUsage`, for the whole session. A line without a message id counts on its own: occupancy replaces the last report and is never summed, so a repeat cannot inflate it. The event follows its line, after the line's `UsageEstimate`. A main session line the process wrote while idle, a wake read by the next turn (m3-07), counts in that turn.
- **Main session only, the subagent gap.** A line with a `parent_tool_use_id` is a subagent's and emits nothing. A subagent's window is its own, and Loop cannot see it yet: a subagent can fill its own window while the main session stays low, and no Loop limit applies to it. The gap is recorded here, not enforced. Spend is not affected: since bl-71 a subagent's tokens count from the `result` line's `model_usage_since_last_result`, and its cost was always in `total_cost_usd`, see [Claude Code subagent spend](#claude-code-subagent-spend-extension-bl-71).
- **The model and the window.** `ContextUsage.model` is the `context.windows` key of the request, `provider/model`. Claude Code's is `anthropic/` and the `model` of the newest `system` `init` line of the session, as written, kept across turns and respawns; null while no `init` line named one. The orchestrator looks the window up in `context.windows` (the defaults merged with the workflow's entries) with it.
- **A model with no window.** Its occupancy is still recorded in tokens, with no `window` and no `fraction`. The first report of such a model logs a warning naming it once per run, `event=context_window outcome=failed model=<model>` on the operator log, with the reason `no window for <model> in context.windows, so context occupancy is reported in tokens without a fraction. Add <model> under context.windows`; a runner that names no model warns once the same way. There is no startup warning: the configured `runners.claude-code.model` may be an alias such as `sonnet`, which never matches the model of the `init` line.
- **The `context_usage` trace event.** The orchestrator keeps the latest and the peak occupancy of the agent session, a new count for each session (each `SessionStarted`), and records one `context_usage` event per turn end, right before `turn_completed` or `turn_failed`, never one per message. Its data: `latest_tokens`, `peak_tokens`, `model` (absent when the runner named none), and, when the window is known, `window` and `fraction`, the latest over the window with 4 decimals. A turn with no report of its own repeats the session's latest and peak; a session with no report, as under a runner that does not declare `occupancy`, records none. `loop trace` prints it in the attempt's timeline, `context_usage  latest_tokens=120000 peak_tokens=300000 window=1000000 fraction=0.1200 model=anthropic/claude-opus-5-5`. The human log writes no line for it.

Tests: `plugins/runner-claude-code/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/claudecode/ClaudeCodeRunnerTest.kt` (the `m3-08` tests: the declared capability, the sum after each line, the real two turn recording, a subagent message, repeated lines of one message id, a wake read by the next turn, an `init` line with no model), `StreamJsonMapperTest.kt` (the `init` line's model), `ClaudeCodeRunnerContractTest` (the contract with occupancy declared), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/OrchestratorContextUsageTest.kt` (one event per turn end with latest, peak, window and fraction, a configured window, a turn with no report, a model with no window and a runner with no model warning once, no report recording nothing) and `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/TraceCommandTest.kt` (the trace line, with and without a window).

### Peak occupancy and runners without occupancy (extension, m3-09)

Stories 95 and 94, plan 8.3. The peak occupancy of each agent session goes into the ledger, so the limits can be tuned from data, and a runner that cannot report occupancy says so where the operator looks. The decisions:

- **A session is the agent thread.** The occupancy meter of m3-08 starts a new count at each `SessionStarted`, and a rotation (m3-11) will start a new thread, so a session's peak is keyed by its `thread_id`. The spec's `session_id`, `<thread_id>-<turn_id>`, changes with every turn and cannot key it. An attempt holds several sessions once rotation lands.
- **The ledger turn line.** Each turn line of `<LOOP_HOME>/ledger/<project_id>.jsonl` ([m2-19](#ledger-extension-m2-19)) gains four keys, always there like the others:

  | Key | Meaning |
  |---|---|
  | `thread_id` | The agent session of the turn, null when the turn never started one. |
  | `peak_tokens` | The session's peak occupancy at the end of the turn: the highest input size of its main session requests so far, cached tokens included. The last line of a session therefore holds the session's peak. |
  | `window` | The window `context.windows` gives the model of the session's reports, null when the model has none. |
  | `peak_fraction` | `peak_tokens` over `window`, rounded to 4 decimals, null without a window. |

  A session with no `context_usage` report, as every session of a runner that does not declare `occupancy`, has null `peak_tokens`, `window` and `peak_fraction`, never 0. A turn line written before m3-09 has none of the four keys; nothing in Loop reads the file back, and a reader takes a missing key as null. The attempt row in the store is unchanged, so the store schema stays at version 7: `loop show` and `loop status` read the `context_usage` events the store already holds, which carry the same latest and peak per session.
- **`loop show --cost`** ends, when the attempt recorded a session, with `Peak occupancy per session` and one line per session in the order its thread first started a turn: `Session 1       300000 tokens, 0.3000 of a 1000000 token window, thread <thread_id>`, `window unknown` in place of the fraction when the model had no window, and `not reported, thread <thread_id>` for a session with no report. The peak is the `peak_tokens` of the session's latest `context_usage`, found through the `turn_started` events of its thread.
- **`loop status`** adds to each running attempt the occupancy of its running session, the thread of its latest `turn_started`: `latest_tokens`, `peak_tokens`, and when the window is known `window`, `fraction` (latest over window) and `peak_fraction`, for example `running   t-1  attempt 1  a2000000  started=10:02:03 turns=2 latest_tokens=40000 peak_tokens=120000 window=1000000 fraction=0.0400 peak_fraction=0.1200`. A running session with no report yet prints none of them, never the numbers of an earlier session.
- **The weaker guarantee line.** A runner without `occupancy` (the `command` runner today) gets only the prompt limit (m3-13) and the turn cap (`agent.max_turns`), no soft or hard limit and no rotation. Loop says so in two places:
  - `loop run`, at startup, when the runner of the startup workflow declares no occupancy: one operator log line before `event=run outcome=started`, `level=warn event=context_occupancy outcome=failed run_id=<id> project_id=<id> runner=command reason="the command runner reports no context occupancy, so only the prompt limit and the turn cap (agent.max_turns) hold for it"`. It is a `key=value` line like every other log line on stderr.
  - `loop status`, under each running attempt whose runner declares no occupancy, indented to the issue column: `the runner reports no context occupancy, so only the prompt limit and the turn cap (agent.max_turns) hold for this attempt`. Each attempt builds its runner from the workflow it starts with (m1-15), so a reload can change the runner: `attempt_started` therefore records `occupancy`, `true` or `false`, from the capabilities of the attempt's own runner, and `loop status` reads it. An attempt recorded before m3-09 has no `occupancy` key and gets no line, since nothing says what its runner declared.
- **Out of scope.** `loop doctor` does not exist and stays out of M3. The limits themselves are m3-11 and m3-12, the prompt limit m3-13.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/OrchestratorLedgerTest.kt` (the `m3-09` tests: each turn line with its session's peak, window and fraction and a new session starting over, a model with no window, a runner without occupancy recording no peak), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/ledger/JsonlTurnLedgerTest.kt` (the four keys, nulls without a peak or a window, 4 decimals), `OrchestratorContextUsageTest.kt` (`occupancy` on `attempt_started`), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ShowCommandTest.kt` (the session peaks of `--cost`, a session with no report), `StatusCommandTest.kt` (the running session's latest and peak with the fractions, a running session with no report yet, a model with no window, the weaker guarantee line per attempt, an older store whose `attempt_started` has no `occupancy`, and the line read from another process while `loop run` works with the `command` runner), `RunCommandTest.kt` (the startup line with the `command` runner) and `ClaudeCodeRunCommandTest.kt` (no startup line with Claude Code, which declares occupancy).

### Soft limit rotation (extension, m3-11)

Story 89, the soft limit part, plan 8.2 and 8.4. A session whose occupancy reaches `context.soft_limit` of its window during a turn is ended at the end of that turn, and a new one starts in the same workspace with a [session brief](#session-brief-extension-m3-10). The hard limit, which interrupts a turn, came with [m3-12](#hard-limit-and-split-issue-extension-m3-12). The decisions:

- **The trigger.** Checked at the end of a turn that completed normally, after its tool requests are applied and the issue is read again, and only when the attempt would run a next turn: the issue is still eligible, the turn is below `agent.max_turns`, and no verdict, approval wait or result file ended the attempt. It fires when the latest `context_usage` report of the session (m3-08), never its peak, is at or above `soft_limit` times the window `context.windows` gives its model. A runner that does not declare `occupancy`, a session with no report, a report with no model and a model with no window never rotate: the trigger also requires the declared capability, so a runner that reports occupancy without declaring it is not trusted.
- **The rotation.** Loop calls `stop()` on the session, then opens a new one with `openSession` on the attempt's runner, never `resumeSession` (m4-46), in the same workspace and with the same environment. The new session runs its own turn 1: `AgentSession.runTurn` gets the turn's number within its session, so the Claude Code runner spawns `claude` without `--resume` and the runtime session starts empty, while the attempt's turn number goes on (`turn_started` of the new session reads `turn=<n+1>`). Its first input is the prompt template rendered again with `issue`, `attempt` and `loop.workspace_reused`, `loop.retry_kind` = `rotation` and `loop.session_brief` built now, whose counters read `Rotations: <n>`. Since bl-132 the `issue` of the rotated prompt and of its brief is read again by id after the stop, right before the render, and completed as at dispatch (bl-06), so the new session sees what changed during the attempt; a failed read or completion, or an issue the tracker no longer has, falls back to the issue the turn end read moments before (bl-152), and logs one warning, `event=rotation_issue outcome=failed` with the reason. `session_rotated` is recorded once the old session is stopped, even when the attempt ends before the new session's first turn: a template error, a `loop stop`, a reconciliation stop or an error during the read records it without the new session's ids, before `attempt_finished` (bl-132, bl-152). The new session knows nothing of the turn that just ended, so `loop.verify_findings` and the brief's findings are those of the attempt's latest verdict, whichever turn of the attempt gave it: its findings when it refuted the result, none when it did not (bl-132: a verified result whose move failed rotates with no findings, never those of an earlier attempt). Only a rotation before any verdict of the attempt carries the findings the attempt was dispatched with. The notices of the requests the last drain could not apply (m2-07, the continuation guidance row of [Implementation defined behaviors](#implementation-defined-behaviors)) follow the rendered prompt, one per line after a blank line, as they would follow the continuation guidance of the old session's next turn. That prompt is appended to the attempt file as a prompt record of its turn; `loop show --prompt` still prints the turn 1 prompt. `BuildingPrompt` and the once per attempt phases (`LaunchingAgentProcess`, `InitializingSession`, `StreamingTurn`) are not recorded again; the operator log writes `event=session outcome=rotated` with `rotation`, `max_rotations` and `fraction`.
- **A failed stop.** It records `session_stop_failed` (m3-07) and the operator warning as any stop failure, then ends the attempt as a failure with the reason `session_stop_failed` and the error as its detail: an error retry with the usual backoff, counted against `retries.max_attempts`. No second session starts beside one that may still live, and the session is not stopped a second time at the attempt's end.
- **Not a retry and not a failure.** A rotation takes no `retries.max_attempts` slot and no backoff, starts no new attempt and runs no hook. Turns keep counting across the sessions of an attempt against `agent.max_turns`.
- **Spend across sessions.** A runner reports usage absolute for its session, so a new session starts at 0. The orchestrator adds the last totals of the closed sessions of the attempt, tokens and cost, to every usage report and estimate of the new one, so the `usage` event, the token budget, the thresholds and the ledger keep reading the attempt's absolute totals, which never drop at a rotation. Since bl-132 a wake the old session was running when it stopped, or one that ended while it was idle, counts too: `AgentSession.stop(usage)`, `stop(report)` since m4-04, gives the usage reports of what the session read while it stopped, and the Claude Code runner adds each `result` line it reads then to the session's usage, as during a turn. The orchestrator records them as `usage` events before the session's totals are carried, they count in the next turn's ledger share, and a budget they go past ends the attempt with `budget` instead of the new session.
- **`session_rotated`.** Data: `reason` (`soft_limit`), `occupancy_tokens` and `window`, the latest report and its window, `fraction` (4 decimals), `rotation` (from 1), `max_rotations`, `old_thread_id` and `old_runtime_session_id` of the stopped session, and `new_thread_id` and `new_runtime_session_id` of the new one. It is recorded when the new session's first turn starts, before its `turn_started`, so it names the new thread; a first turn that ends before any start event records it at its end, without the new ids. The rotated prompt is rendered after the old session stopped and before the new one opens; since bl-132 a render failure records `session_rotated` at once, without the new ids, then ends the attempt with the template error as before, so the trace holds both. The ids are threads, as for the peak occupancy of [m3-09](#peak-occupancy-and-runners-without-occupancy-extension-m3-09), since the spec's `session_id` changes every turn; a runtime session id is absent when the runner gave none. `loop trace` prints `session_rotated  reason=soft_limit rotation=1/5 occupancy_tokens=250000 window=1000000 fraction=0.2500 from=<thread> to=<thread>` in the attempt's timeline, and the human log `started a new session at 25 percent of its window, rotation 1 of at most 5`.
- **The cap.** A rotation past `context.max_rotations` (5 by default, at least 1) starts none: the attempt ends and the issue parks with the reason `max_rotations`, like a budget park it takes no `max_attempts` slot. The `issue_parked` data carries `rotations` and `max_rotations` and the occupancy that asked for the refused rotation (`occupancy_tokens`, `window`, `fraction`); `loop trace` prints `reason=max_rotations rotations=5/5 fraction=0.3000` and `loop resume` unparks it like any park. Rotations without progress, the "split this issue" park of plan 8.4, came with [m3-12](#hard-limit-and-split-issue-extension-m3-12), which checks them before this cap.
- **Counted in the ledger and `loop show`.** Each ledger turn line gains `rotations`, the attempt's rotations at the end of the turn, so the last line of an attempt holds its count. The peak occupancy of a line, of `context_usage` and of `loop show` (m3-09) is per session, the intended figure since the 25 percent rule holds per session: each session gets a new meter, so a new session's peak starts from zero and the last line of each session holds its own peak (bl-132). The store's attempt row is unchanged, so the store schema stays at version 7, the m3-09 choice: `loop show --cost` reads the `session_rotated` events instead and ends its session block with `Rotations       <n>`, `<n> of at most <max>` once the attempt rotated.
- **`loop.retry_kind`.** A new variable under `loop.*` (plan 8.9) with the values Loop produces: `continuation` on the retry after a normal exit, `error` on the retry after a failure, `refuted` on the retry after a failure whose reason is a refutation (the retry's stored error is `refuted`, so it survives a restart), `rotation` in the first prompt of a rotated session, and null on a first dispatch. `reaction` comes with the reactions of plan 6.7. A template that does not use it renders exactly as before. Preflight renders a first run, each retry kind, and a rotation on a first dispatch and on a retry, each in a new and a reused workspace, with `loop.session_brief` set on every sample but the first run in a new workspace. The `PromptSlots` renders keep it like `attempt`, so what it shows counts in the template slot.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/OrchestratorRotationTest.kt` (a rotation into a new session in the same workspace with the brief, `session_rotated` and its place in the trace, a failed stop, turns counting toward `agent.max_turns` with no `max_attempts` slot and the ledger's `rotations`, the `max_rotations` park, a runner without occupancy and a model without a window never rotating, spend carried across sessions), `LoopModeDrainTest` (a rotation right after a refuted verdict carries its findings and the failed request notices), the `bl-132` tests of `OrchestratorRotationTest` (a wake's usage read at the stop in the spend and the next ledger line, a render failure after the stop still recording `session_rotated`, the issue as the tracker returns it at the rotation and the dispatch copy when that read fails, a retry whose latest verdict is verified rotating with no earlier findings, two refuted turns rotating with the second verdict's findings) and of `OrchestratorContextUsageTest` (each session's own peak), `OccupancyMeterTest`, `SessionSpendTest`, `RetryKindPromptTest`, `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workflow/PromptVariablesTest.kt`, `PreflightTest` and `SessionBriefTest` (the `m3-11` tests), `JsonlTurnLedgerTest` (`rotations`), `plugins/runner-claude-code/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/claudecode/ClaudeCodeRunnerTest.kt` (a new session spawns without `--resume`; bl-132: a wake running when the session stops reports the usage it read), and in `cli`: `ClaudeCodeRunCommandTest` (a rotation end to end with a fake `claude`: two processes in one workspace, neither with `--resume`, the rotation prompt, `session_rotated`, the ledger lines and `loop trace`), `RunCommandTest` (the `command` runner never rotates), `ShowCommandTest` and `TraceCommandTest`.

### Hard limit and split issue (extension, m3-12)

Story 89, the hard limit part, and story 91, plan 8.4 and 6.6. A session whose occupancy reaches `context.hard_limit` of its window during a turn is interrupted at once and rotated, and two rotations in a row without progress park the issue with `split_issue`, so a human splits it (from M6, triage proposes a split). The decisions:

- **The trigger.** Checked on every `context_usage` report during a turn (m3-08), not at its end: the session's latest report at or above `hard_limit` times the window `context.windows` gives its model. Like the soft limit, it needs a runner that declares `occupancy`, a model with a window, and a turn that runs on a session, since a runner without continuation cannot open a new one: its one turn is never interrupted.
- **The interrupt.** Loop asks the session to end the turn at once with `AgentSession.interrupt()`, a plugin API method that does nothing by default. The Claude Code runner writes a stream-json `control_request` of subtype `interrupt` to the process; Claude Code (seen with 2.1.319) answers at once with a `control_response` line and a `result` line of subtype `error_during_execution`, which carries the turn's usage (`model_usage_since_last_result`) and the session's `total_cost_usd`. Loop goes on recording the turn's events, so that usage counts in the ledger and the spend budgets, until the runner's terminal event, which ends the turn and is not recorded as a failure, or until a grace of 10 seconds, after which it cancels the runner's flow, which stops the agent's process group as on any cancel; the attempt goes on. Since bl-136 the grace is a deadline: it starts once, when Loop asks the session to interrupt, and the flow is cancelled 10 seconds later whatever the runtime writes in between, so a runtime that ignores the interrupt but keeps writing events is cut on time. The events it writes within the grace are still recorded. The grace of a turn cut by a spend budget (2 seconds) is unchanged and still restarts on each event. A wake the runner was reading when the interrupt came ends the same way, its usage reported, and the turn's input is never written; a turn cancelled while it reads a wake stops the process group, as any cancelled turn does. The turn is not a failure: it records `context_usage`, then `turn_completed` with `interrupted` = `hard_limit` (`loop trace` prints `interrupted=hard_limit`, the human log `turn <n> was interrupted at the hard limit`), and its ledger line carries `interrupted` = `hard_limit`, null on every other turn, with `outcome` = `completed`. It takes no `retries.max_attempts` slot. A runner that cannot interrupt a turn loses the usage it had not reported before the cancel; so far no runner but Claude Code declares `occupancy`.
- **The grace expiry (bl-136).** When the grace runs out and Loop cancels the flow, it records `interrupt_grace_expired` on the interrupted turn, before its `turn_completed`, so the lost usage is no longer silent. Data: `grace_ms` (10000), the session's latest occupancy as `occupancy_tokens`, with `window` and `fraction` (4 decimals) when `context.windows` knows the model, and the latest usage known then as `attempt_tokens` and `total_cost_usd`, absolute for the attempt as on `usage`; a field Loop does not know is absent. `loop trace` prints `interrupt_grace_expired grace_ms=10000 occupancy_tokens=300000 window=1000000 fraction=0.3000 attempt_tokens=9000 total_cost_usd=0.09` in the attempt's timeline; the human log writes no line for it. The operator log adds a warn line with `event=interrupt`, `outcome=failed` and `grace_ms=10000`. A runtime that ends its turn within the grace records none.
- **The rotation.** The turn then ends like a completed one: its queued tool requests are applied, a verdict, an approval wait or a result file ends the attempt as usual, the issue is read again, and a turn at `agent.max_turns` ends the attempt without rotating. Otherwise it rotates exactly as at the [soft limit](#soft-limit-rotation-extension-m3-11), with `session_rotated` `reason` = `hard_limit`: `stop()`, then a new session at its own turn 1, never `--resume`, the rotation prompt and brief, a failed stop recording `session_stop_failed` and starting no second session, counted against `context.max_rotations`. The operator log line `event=session outcome=rotated` gains `reason`. The human log reads `interrupted its turn at 26 percent of its window and started a new session, rotation 1 of at most 5`.
- **Progress between rotations.** Kept per attempt in memory, for every rotation, soft or hard. A rotation is with progress when the workspace's HEAD differs from the one at the previous rotation (at the attempt's first prompt for the first rotation), or when a verification of the attempt since the previous rotation failed fewer checks than the verification before it, which may come from an earlier session of the same attempt, or (bl-136) when the workspace's working tree changed since the previous rotation (since the attempt's first prompt for the first), so a healthy agent interrupted twice before its first commit does not park. The working tree is read as a fingerprint of file content only: the SHA-256 of the path of every entry `git status --porcelain=v1 -z --untracked-files=all --no-renames` lists, tracked changes and untracked files git does not ignore, each with what its file holds, so a second edit of a file already changed reads differently and an edit under the gitignored `.scratch/` does not count. Since bl-149 the status letters are left out and each path counts once, so `git add`, `git reset` or a stash and its pop with no content change read the same. A regular file reads as the SHA-256 of its content, or as its size and modified time when it is over 1 MiB or Loop cannot open it, so one unreadable file never fails the whole read and a change to it still shows. A symbolic link reads as the name of its target, a deleted file as nothing, and a nested repository or submodule as its HEAD and its own fingerprint, two levels down. It runs with `--no-optional-locks`, so the index is never rewritten, and writes nothing to the object store. The read is bounded and cancellable (bl-149): `git status` is read as it streams and more than 20000 entries fail it, the whole read of HEAD and the working tree runs on its own thread for at most 30 seconds, and a stop of the attempt ends the wait at once. Past the budget or on a stop, Loop stops the read's git and its hashing, and a thread stuck where nothing can stop it, such as the open of a FIFO swapped in for a file, is left behind, never awaited. A verification stops at its first failing check, so its failing checks are the checks it did not pass, the failing one and the ones it never ran after it, plus one when a verified work tree is refuted for uncommitted files; the first verification of an attempt compares with nothing. HEAD and the working tree are read with Loop's pinned git (`rev-parse`, `status`) only when the workspace is the top level of a git work tree, as the [session brief](#session-brief-extension-m3-10) does, each on its own. A workspace that is not one, or a failed read, gives no HEAD or no working tree and counts as progress: Loop never parks on a fact it did not read, and such a workspace is capped by `max_rotations` alone. Since bl-149 a failed read is logged on the operator log once per fact and attempt, `level=warn event=progress outcome=failed` with `fact` (`workspace`, `head` or `working_tree`) and the reason, so a workspace that never parks says why. A workspace that is not a git work tree logs nothing.
- **The park.** The second rotation in a row without progress starts no session, like the cap: the attempt ends with the reason `split_issue` and the issue parks with it, taking no `max_attempts` slot. The two are counted in one attempt only: a new attempt starts over. It is checked before `max_rotations`, so a refused rotation that is both reads `split_issue`, the more useful finding. `issue_parked` carries `without_progress` (2), `rotations` (made before the refused one), `limit` (`soft_limit` or `hard_limit`, which asked for it) and the occupancy (`occupancy_tokens`, `window`, `fraction`); `loop trace` prints `reason=split_issue without_progress=2 rotations=1 limit=hard_limit fraction=0.3000` and the human log `parked with the finding split this issue, after 2 session rotations in a row without progress`. The ledger row's `reason` is `split_issue`. `loop resume` unparks it like any park.
- **The comment.** Right after the park, Loop comments on the issue as `loop`: `Loop parked this issue with the finding: split this issue.`, then that 2 session rotations in a row came without progress, no new commit on the issue's branch, no change to its working tree (since bl-136) and no verification with fewer failing checks, so the issue looks too big for one agent session, and to split it and run `loop resume <identifier>` or change it in the tracker. It records `issue_commented` with `by` = `host` and `reason` = `split_issue` (human log: `commented that it should be split`); a failed comment is logged at warn and the issue stays parked.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/OrchestratorHardLimitTest.kt` (the interrupt and its `hard_limit` rotation, the usage and cost the runner reports after the interrupt in the ledger line, a runner that ignores the interrupt stopped after the grace, the `bl-136` tests: a runtime that keeps writing after the interrupt cancelled 10 seconds after the request and the `interrupt_grace_expired` it records, no `max_attempts` slot, a failed stop, a turn below the hard limit rotating at the soft limit, the `split_issue` park and its comment, a new commit resetting the count, a workspace without git never parking), `WorkspaceFactsTest` (the `bl-149` tests: a read that blocks past its budget, a cancel during a read, a plain folder, a HEAD git cannot read), `OrchestratorHardLimitTest` again (the `bl-149` test: a stall timeout shorter than the interrupt grace neither stalls the interrupted turn nor shortens the grace), `RotationProgressTest` (the `bl-136` tests: a changed working tree at the same HEAD resets the count, an unchanged tree and HEAD still counts, an unreadable tree counts as progress), `OrchestratorNoProgressTest` (the `bl-136` tests: a working tree changed between two rotations at the same HEAD does not park, a change under the gitignored `.scratch/` still parks; the `bl-149` tests: a readable HEAD with a working tree git cannot read counts as progress and is logged once, a HEAD git cannot read is logged once), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/verify/WorkspaceGitTest.kt` (the `bl-136` working tree fingerprint tests, and the `bl-149` tests: index only changes, deleted and staged files, symbolic links, an unreadable file, the byte cap, the entry cap, a nested repository, a cancelled read), `LoopModeDrainTest` (the `m3-12` tests: fewer failing checks is progress, the same failing checks parks), `OrchestratorRotationTest` (its occupancies now sit between the two limits), `JsonlTurnLedgerTest` (`interrupted`), `ClaudeCodeRunnerTest` (the `m3-12` tests: the interrupt request and the usage and cost of its result line, an interrupt while a wake is read, a turn cancelled while a wake is read stopping the process group), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/log/HumanLogTest.kt`, and in `cli`: `ClaudeCodeRunCommandTest` (a hard limit rotation end to end with a fake `claude` that ends its turn only when interrupted: the interrupt request, the usage and cost of its result line in the ledger line, two processes, neither with `--resume`, `turn_completed` with `interrupted`, the ledger line, `loop trace` and `loop log`; and two hard limit rotations in a workspace whose commit never moves, parked with `split_issue`, the comment in the issue file, `loop trace` and `loop log`) and `TraceCommandTest` (the `bl-136` test: the `interrupt_grace_expired` line).

### Prompt tokens and prompt limit (extension, m3-13)

Story 92, the budget part, and story 94, the prompt limit that holds for a runner without occupancy, plan 8.5. The rendered prompt is measured in tokens against `context.prompt_limit` of a window, for every runner and for the merge risk reviewer of [m2-28](#conditional-auto-merge-and-merge-risk-extension-m2-28). Nothing of an agent's prompt is cut: cutting slots is m3-14. The decisions:

- **The counter.** `TokenCounter` in `spec-core` (`loop.core.workflow.TokenCounter`), chosen per model by `TokenCounters.forModel`: the model's tokenizer when one is available offline, otherwise the conservative estimate, one token per 3 characters (UTF-16 units, as `prompt_chars` counts them), rounded up, method `estimate`. Real tokenizers average about 4 characters per token on English and code, so the estimate overcounts and a limit it holds also holds for the model. No tokenizer of the models Loop runs (Claude, Codex) is available offline on the JVM, so every count is the estimate today; the seam stays for one. The trace names the method beside every count, `token_counter=estimate`.
- **The window.** The limit is `prompt_limit` times a window of `context.windows` (the defaults merged with the workflow's entries), rounded down, computed in decimal so 0.05 of 1,000,000 is 50,000. The window is the entry of the model whose key is the model, else the smallest of the entries whose key ends with `/<model>`, so Claude Code's configured `claude-opus-5-5` finds `anthropic/claude-opus-5-5`. The model is the runner's configured model (`AgentRunner.model`) for an agent's prompt and `risk.reviewer` for the reviewer's. A runner with no known model, such as `command`, and a model no key names, such as the alias `sonnet` or a model missing from `context.windows`, use the smallest window in `context.windows`, so the limit is never looser than for any configured model. The model of the `init` line (m3-08) is not known before the session starts, so it is not used.
- **Slot sizes in tokens.** The `BuildingPrompt` phase event keeps the m1-17 data and adds, in this order: `prompt_tokens`, `slot_<name>_tokens` for each slot in priority order (`slot_template_tokens`, `slot_issue_tokens`, `slot_verify_findings_tokens`, `slot_session_brief_tokens`, `slot_lessons_tokens`, `slot_context_sources_tokens`), `prompt_limit_tokens`, `over_limit` (`true` or `false`) and `token_counter`. A token slot is measured like its character slot, as the difference of the token counts of the same renders, so the token slots add up to `prompt_tokens`. When the slots cannot be measured (m1-17's warning), the event keeps `prompt_chars`, `prompt_tokens`, the limit, `over_limit` and `token_counter`. `context.windows` cannot be emptied, so the limit is always known; without a window the limit keys would be left out.
- **`loop trace`.** The `BuildingPrompt` line prints the tokens after the characters: `prompt_chars=3601 template=3425 issue=176 verify_findings=0 session_brief=0 lessons=0 context_sources=0 prompt_tokens=1201 template_tokens=1142 issue_tokens=59 verify_findings_tokens=0 session_brief_tokens=0 lessons_tokens=0 context_sources_tokens=0 prompt_limit_tokens=50000 over_limit=false token_counter=estimate`. An event recorded before m3-13 prints none of them.
- **Preflight.** After the sample renders, `Preflight` renders the template with an empty issue (the m1-17 emptied fields), a first dispatch in a new workspace, no findings, no session brief and no retry kind, and counts it with the counter of the runner's model. Over the limit, the template alone can never fit, and the preflight fails with `invalid_config`: `the prompt template alone is 201 tokens, over the prompt limit of 200 tokens (context.prompt_limit 0.05 of a 4000 token window, estimate)`. A preflight failure blocks dispatch at startup and on every tick, as for any invalid config. `loop run` gives the preflight the model of the runner the workflow would build; a preflight with no model, such as the one the tests build with four arguments, takes the smallest window. A template that does not render with an empty issue (an index lookup such as `issue.labels[0]`) is not measured here, as m1-17 does not measure its slots.
- **At dispatch.** A prompt over the limit is recorded with `over_limit` = `true` on `BuildingPrompt` and the human log writes `the prompt is 302 tokens, over its limit of 200 tokens. It is sent whole` on the issue's line. Until m3-14 nothing is cut, and the attempt goes on with its prompt. Since m3-14 such a prompt is filled slot by slot and cut to fit, see [Prompt slots filled and tool outputs trimmed](#prompt-slots-filled-and-tool-outputs-trimmed-extension-m3-14).
- **The merge risk reviewer.** Its prompt (the issue, the submitted result, the code facts and the diff) is redacted as the reviewer reads it and counted with the counter of `risk.reviewer` against `prompt_limit` of its window. The diff, still read up to 100,000 characters, is shown whole when the prompt fits; otherwise the prompt keeps the longest prefix of the diff that fits, ending at a line end when such a prefix fits, and after the fenced diff, outside the untrusted markers, Loop states the cut: `The diff above is cut to fit the reviewer's prompt limit: 15234 of its characters are left out. It is the diff from the merge base <base> to the head <head>, the commit the workspace holds, so read the changed files there for the rest.` A diff read only up to its cap always shows the cut, with `more than <n>`. The heading reads `## Diff against the merge base <base> (cut)`. The reviewer runs with no Bash, so it reads the files of the workspace rather than running `git diff`. `merge_risk_assessed` records `prompt_tokens`, the tokens of the prompt the reviewer got, and `loop trace` prints it last. A prompt that cannot fit even with an empty diff runs no reviewer and falls back to `Waiting(approval)` as other reviewer failures do, the comment reading `Merge risk: not assessed, the reviewer's prompt is 2460 tokens even with an empty diff, over its prompt limit of 100 tokens (context.prompt_limit 0.05 of a 2000 token window for claude-sonnet-5-5).`. `ClaudeCodeRiskReviewer` is unchanged: the window comes from `risk.reviewer` through the lookup above. Since bl-137 a cut floors the band at `medium` and the prompt lists every changed file, see [A cut reviewer diff floors the band](#a-cut-reviewer-diff-floors-the-band-extension-bl-137).

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workflow/TokenCounterTest.kt` (the estimate and its method, the window of a key, of a model name, the smallest for no model or an alias, the decimal rounding, no window), `PromptSlotsTest.kt` (the `m3-13` tests: token slots that add up, the budget data and its order), `PreflightTest.kt` (the `m3-13` tests: a template alone over the limit, a template measured with no optional slot, the window of the runner's model), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/OrchestratorTest.kt` (the token data of `BuildingPrompt`, and with slots that cannot be measured), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/delivery/MergeRiskTest.kt` (the `m3-13` tests: a diff shown whole, a diff cut at a line end with the cut stated, a diff read up to its cap, a prompt that cannot fit), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/AutoMergeOrchestratorTest.kt` (the `m3-13` tests: `prompt_tokens` on `merge_risk_assessed`, a cut diff under a small window, the fallback) and `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/TraceCommandTest.kt` (the trace line, and with the `command` runner the smallest window and an over limit prompt recorded and logged).

### Prompt slots filled and tool outputs trimmed (extension, m3-14)

Stories 92 and 93, plan 8.5 and 8.7. A prompt over its limit (m3-13) is filled slot by slot in priority order, each slot cut by its rule, and what Loop's tools tell the model is trimmed to `context.tool_output_tokens`, the full text kept as evidence or in the Attempt file. The decisions:

- **When.** The attempt's first prompt and the first prompt of a rotated session (m3-11) are rendered as before, then counted against the prompt limit of the runner model's window (m3-13). Within the limit, the prompt is sent as rendered and nothing changes. Over it, `PromptFill` (`loop.core.workflow.PromptFill`) fills it again: each slot gets the room the slots before it left, measured like the token slots of m3-13, as what the slot adds to the render of the slots before it, so a slot later in the order is cut before an earlier one.
- **The slots and their rules.**

| Slot | Rule when over budget |
|---|---|
| Template and rules | Never cut. Preflight already fails when the template alone is over the limit (m3-13). |
| Issue | The description keeps its longest start that fits, cut at a line end when one fits, followed by a blank line and `(The description is cut here to fit the prompt limit: 6120 of its characters are left out. Read the full text <pointer>.)`, only that note when no start fits. The pointer is `at <url>` when the tracker gives the issue's web address (the GitHub tracker's `native_ref` `url`), else `in the issue file <path>` for the `files` tracker's file of the issue (`<id>.md` or `<id>-<slug>.md` in its folder), else `in the issue file of <identifier> under <folder>`, else `in issue <identifier> of the <kind> tracker`. The other fields are never cut. |
| Verify findings | The head stays: the lines up to the first one that ends with a colon among the first five (the lead, the check, the exit code and the line that opens the output), else the first line. Then one line summarizes the older lines left out, `(12 older lines of the findings are left out to fit the prompt limit.)`, then the newest lines that fit, the last ones. When not even the head and that line fit, the findings become `Your last result was refuted. Its findings are left out to fit the prompt limit: run the checks to see what failed.` |
| Session brief | Every part keeps its `### ` heading and, cut to the same share of its length as every other part, the largest share that fits, the lines that hold its newest facts. The goal, the workspace state and the counters list their facts first to last or newest first, so they keep their first lines, ended by `(cut to fit the prompt limit)`. The checks end with the findings of the last refutation and the agent's notes end with the progress file, newest last, so these two keep their first line (the last verdict, the warning that the notes are unverified), then `(older lines cut to fit the prompt limit)`, then their last lines. With no share that fits, each part keeps that frame alone. A part without a body, such as a part that could not be read, stays whole. |
| Lessons | Empty until M6: no `loop.lessons` yet, so the slot measures 0 and is never cut. |
| Context sources of the kind | Empty until m4-19. Since then the list of `loop.context_sources` keeps its first lines, one source each, then `(N more context sources are left out to fit the prompt limit.)`; with no room for that line the variable is null. See [Context sources and per kind templates](#context-sources-and-per-kind-templates-extension-m4-19). |

- **Still over.** A prompt whose slots are all at their smallest (the template, the issue's other fields, the smallest findings and brief) can still be over its limit. It is sent so, and `over_limit` says it.
- **`BuildingPrompt`.** `prompt_chars`, the `slot_<name>_chars`, `prompt_tokens` and the `slot_<name>_tokens` are those of the prompt sent, so the Attempt file's prompt record still matches `prompt_chars`, and `over_limit` says whether the prompt sent is over its limit. A prompt that was over its limit adds after `token_counter` the tokens each slot lost, in priority order: `cut_template_tokens`, `cut_issue_tokens`, `cut_verify_findings_tokens`, `cut_session_brief_tokens`, `cut_lessons_tokens`, `cut_context_sources_tokens`. The prompt as rendered was `prompt_tokens` plus their sum. A prompt within its limit records none, so its event is the one of m3-13. A rotation records no `BuildingPrompt`, as before: a rotated prompt that was over its limit adds to its `session_rotated` event, after the rotation's own data, `prompt_tokens` (of the prompt sent, the notices included), `prompt_limit_tokens`, `over_limit`, `token_counter` and the same `cut_<name>_tokens`. A rotated prompt within its limit adds none, so its event is the one of m3-11.
- **`loop trace`.** The `BuildingPrompt` line prints the cuts last, when present: `... prompt_limit_tokens=200 over_limit=false token_counter=estimate cut_template_tokens=0 cut_issue_tokens=1834 cut_verify_findings_tokens=0 cut_session_brief_tokens=0 cut_lessons_tokens=0 cut_context_sources_tokens=0`.
- **`loop trace` of a rotation.** The `session_rotated` line of a cut prompt adds, after the threads, `prompt_tokens=98 prompt_limit_tokens=100 over_limit=false` and the six `cut_<name>_tokens`.
- **The human log.** A cut prompt writes `the prompt was 2034 tokens, over its limit of 200 tokens, so Loop cut 1834 tokens: issue 1834` on the issue's line, naming each slot that lost tokens, with `. It is still 230 tokens, over its limit` when the prompt sent is still over. A rotation whose prompt was cut ends its line with `, its prompt cut by 60 tokens to fit its limit of 100 tokens: session_brief 60`, with the same `. It is still ...` when it is. A prompt over its limit whose fill cut nothing, a long title for example, writes `the prompt is 305 tokens, over its limit of 200 tokens, and no slot Loop may cut holds any of it. It is sent whole`.
- **Tool outputs, one trim.** `ToolOutputTrim` keeps the start of a text that is over `tool_output_tokens`, counted with the estimate, cut at a line end when one fits, and adds a line saying how much is left out and where the full text is: `(cut to fit context.tool_output_tokens: 152 of its characters are left out. The full text is in the attempt file.)`. Only that line when no start fits.
- **Verify findings.** The cap of `VerifyFindings` becomes `tool_output_tokens`, counted with the counter of the runner's model: the lead, the check and the exit code always show, then the newest output lines that fit, still at most 20 lines (the few lines of plan 8.7) of at most 200 characters each. The character bounds `OUTPUT_MAX` and `TEXT_MAX` are gone. The full check output stays in the evidence file, as before (m2-05).
- **Loop's MCP tools.** Every reply of `loop tool mcp`, the tracker tools' answers and the refusals of all three tools, the missing argument refusal included, goes through a `ReplyTrimmer` of `LoopMcpServer` before the agent reads it. `loop tool mcp` trims to the snapshot's `tool_output_tokens` and saves the full text of a trimmed reply, `{"tool","text"}`, in the `tool-outputs` folder of the attempt directory, written like a queued request. At every drain and when the Attempt ends, the orchestrator appends each saved reply to the Attempt file as a `tool_output` record, redacted, then deletes its file. The orchestrator stays the one writer of the Attempt file because a writer numbers its records from the highest `seq` it read once, so a second process appending would repeat a `seq`. A failed save is said on stderr and never fails the call. Today's replies are short, so the trim matters only for a small `tool_output_tokens`.
- **The failed tool request notice.** The notices of one drain (m2-07), sent after the continuation guidance or after the rotated session's prompt, are joined one per line and trimmed the same way, so the first notices stay whole, and the note reads `Loop records each request it could not apply, with its reason, as a tool_request_failed event.`, where each notice's request and reason stay whole.
- **The end to end rotation tests.** The m3-11 rotation of `ClaudeCodeRunCommandTest` and the m3-23 rotations of `CodexRotationRunTest` use a 1000 token window, whose default prompt limit of 50 tokens now cuts the rotated prompt's brief. Their workflows set `context.prompt_limit` to 0.20, the most the soft limit allows, so the brief fits whole and their assertions on it stay as they were.
- **A store opened by two processes at once.** Found while verifying m3-14: two `loop run` of a machine (m3-16) creating the shared store together could fail the second with `SQLITE_BUSY`, since switching a new store file to WAL takes a lock SQLite does not wait for on `busy_timeout`. `SqliteEventStore.open` now tries that switch again every 50 ms until the 5 second `busy_timeout` has passed, like any other lock wait. Test: `store/src/test/kotlin/loop/store/SqliteEventStoreTest.kt` (`opening a new store while another process holds its write lock waits for the lock instead of failing (m3-14)`), and `MachineLimitsCommandTest`, which failed on it about one run in five.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workflow/PromptFillTest.kt` (a prompt within its limit, the template never cut, the issue cut with its pointer, the findings head and newest lines with the older ones summarized, findings left out, the brief capped part by part, the lessons and context sources slots at 0, the cut data), `SessionBriefTest.kt` (the `m3-14` test: a brief built by `SessionBrief` and cut keeps the last verdict and the latest findings, the unverified warning and the newest progress, the goal's first lines), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/OrchestratorTest.kt` (the `m3-14` test: the prompt sent cut with the issue file pointer and the cuts on `BuildingPrompt`), `OrchestratorRotationTest.kt` (the `m3-14` test: a rotated prompt over a 100 token limit cut, its cuts on `session_rotated`), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/log/HumanLogTest.kt` (the `m3-14` test: the rotation's line with its cuts), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/TraceCommandTest.kt` (the `m3-14` tests: the trace line and the human log line, the `session_rotated` line with its cuts, and the `m3-13` test with nothing to cut), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/verify/VerifyFindingsTest.kt` and `LoopModeDrainTest.kt` (the `m3-14` tests: findings within `tool_output_tokens`, the evidence keeping the full output), `mcp/src/test/kotlin/loop/mcp/LoopMcpServerTest.kt`, `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ToolMcpCommandTest.kt`, `RunnerOutputRecordTest.kt` and `store/src/test/kotlin/loop/store/GzipAttemptRecordsTest.kt` (the `m3-14` tests: every reply trimmed, the full text saved, moved into the Attempt file as a `tool_output` record), `ToolRequestDrainTest.kt` (the `m3-14` test: the notices trimmed, each whole in its event) and `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ToolConfigSnapshotTest.kt` (the snapshot key and its default).

### A cut reviewer diff floors the band (extension, bl-137)

Found by the after the fact recheck of PR #107 (m3-13). m3-13 keeps a prefix of the diff in the reviewer's prompt, so the alphabetically first files survive and one long line can push the rest out. The reviewer has no git and, before bl-137, no list of the changed paths, so it could not tell what was cut, and a low band from a review of a cut diff merged on its own. The decisions:

- **The band floor.** When the reviewer's prompt holds a cut diff, cut to fit `context.prompt_limit` or read only up to its 100,000 character cap, the band is at least `medium`, whatever the score. The score stays the reviewer's and the floors', so a best review of a cut diff reads `band medium, score 1.00`, and a `high` band stays `high`. The auto merge rule needs `low`, so the pull request always waits for approval. The fallback reason names the cut: `the merge risk band is medium, an auto merge needs low, and at least medium since the reviewer's diff was cut to fit its prompt limit, 2 of 2 changed files left out in whole or in part`, and the comment adds `- band floor medium: <the same reason>` after the dimension lines. Only a successful review of a cut diff changes: every error path already fell back.
- **The changed files.** Whether or not the diff is cut, the prompt lists every changed file under `## Changed files`, before the diff, in the diff's order, one line each: `- <path>: +<added> -<deleted>`, or `lines not counted` for a file git could not count. The agent names the files, so the list sits between the untrusted markers, and a control character in a path shows as U+FFFD, so a path cannot add a line. A cut marks each file it left out in whole or in part with `, left out by the cut`, and the cut statement after the diff adds `The cut left out 2 of the 3 changed files, in whole or in part: each is marked "left out by the cut" in the list of changed files above.`
- **Which files the cut left out.** git prints the numstat and the patch in the same order with the same options (`--no-renames` and the rest), so the file at index i of the numstat is the patch section that starts at the i th `diff --git ` line, a line no patch line can start with. A file is shown whole when the next section starts at or before the cut, the last one only when the diff is shown whole. A file shown in part counts as left out. The marks make the prompt shorter as the kept prefix passes a file, so the prefix kept is the longest that fits only up to them.
- **The event.** `merge_risk_assessed` records `diff_cut`, `true` or `false`, and `files_left_out`, the count of files the cut left out in whole or in part, 0 when the diff was shown whole. `loop trace` prints them after `prompt_tokens`, `... prompt_tokens=2987 diff_cut=true files_left_out=2`, and the human log adds `, the reviewer's diff cut with 2 files left out` to the merge risk line when the diff was cut. An event recorded before bl-137 prints neither.
- **No limit.** A reviewer with no prompt limit and a diff read up to its cap kept the cut statement but dropped the cut from what the gate saw; it now keeps it, so the floor holds there too.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/delivery/MergeRiskTest.kt` (the `bl-137` tests: the band floor, the files a cut leaves out, the list of changed files whole and cut), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/log/HumanLogTest.kt` (the human log line, cut and whole), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/AutoMergeOrchestratorTest.kt` (the `bl-137` tests: a diff that must be cut waits for approval with the cut in the reason and the event, a diff that fits merges as before; the m3-13 cut test now falls back) and `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/TraceCommandTest.kt` (the trace line).

### Claude Code subagent spend (extension, bl-71)

PLAN 6.8. On the pilot app (bl-52) Loop recorded 8,844,163 tokens and $11.02 for a turn whose session and 8 subagents used 21,878,566 tokens, see [first real delivery on the pilot app](#first-real-delivery-on-the-pilot-app-m2-27-bl-52). Probed on Claude Code 2.1.319 with haiku: one turn that runs one general purpose subagent answering `pong`, then a `--resume` of that session answering `ok`. The scrubbed recordings are `plugins/runner-claude-code/src/test/resources/streams/real_subagent.jsonl` and `real_subagent_resumed.jsonl`. The findings and the decisions:

- **`cost_usd` covers subagents.** `total_cost_usd` is the sum of the `costUSD` of `modelUsage`, and `modelUsage` counts the subagent. Its 1,226 input, 331 output, 41,393 cache read and 38,365 cache creation tokens (19,520 of them the subagent's 5 minute writes), priced at haiku's list rates, give $0.00691103, the reported cost to the last digit. The result's `usage`, 4, 308, 41,393 and 18,845, prices to $0.00434. So `cost_usd`, the dollar figures of the ledger, `loop show --cost` and `loop status`, and every dollar budget have covered subagents all along. The rates come from the earlier real recording, whose one call of 2 input, 3 output, 24,367 cache read and 18,933 one hour cache write tokens cost $0.00403197: $0.10, $0.50, $0.01 and $0.20 per million tokens, and $0.125 for a 5 minute write.
- **`usage` does not.** The result's `usage` is the main session's only. Before bl-71 Loop recorded it, so every token figure left the subagents out: the attempt row, the ledger lines, the token thresholds and the token budgets `per_attempt_tokens`, `per_issue_tokens` and `per_day_tokens`.
- **Three sources in a `result` line.** `usage` is this segment's main session. `modelUsage` is the session's so far, every model and subagent included, and `--resume` restores it: 1,228 input tokens after the resume, 1,226 before. `model_usage_since_last_result` is this segment's, every model and subagent included: 2, 4, 30,452 and 40 after the resume, the new work only. In the earlier two turn recording, with no subagent, it equals each segment's `usage`.
- **Decision.** The runner reads a `result` line's tokens from `model_usage_since_last_result`, summed over its models, when it names at least one model, else from `usage` as before, for an older Claude Code. It is per segment like `usage`, so the summing rule of bl-25 and m3-07 stays as it is, and it does not depend on what Claude Code restores on `--resume`, which a killed process may not have saved. `modelUsage` would need the replace rule and that restore. The merge risk reviewer reads its tokens the same way.
- **What follows.** Every `usage` event, the attempt row, the ledger lines, `loop show --cost`, `loop status`, the token thresholds and every token budget count subagents. Ledger rows written before bl-71 keep their main session tokens, so an Issue or day token budget that reads them undercounts until they leave it.
- **Not changed.** The mid turn estimate counts main session `assistant` lines only, until the `result` line replaces it; in the probe Claude Code printed no `assistant` line of the foreground subagent anyway. Occupancy stays the main session's, see [context occupancy](#claude-code-context-occupancy-extension-m3-08).

Tests: the `bl-71` tests of `plugins/runner-claude-code/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/claudecode/StreamJsonMapperTest.kt` (the real recording with its subagent, the resumed segment rather than `modelUsage`, a sum over two models, `usage` without the field or with no model in it), `ClaudeCodeRunnerTest.kt` (the subagent in the turn's `usage` event, a resumed process adding its own segment), `ClaudeCodeRiskReviewerTest.kt` (every model's tokens) and, end to end with a fake `claude`, `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ClaudeCodeRunCommandTest.kt` (the attempt row, the ledger line and a per Attempt token budget that only the subagents pass).

### Changed line coverage (extension, bl-117)

PLAN 6.4. The deny guards refuse deleted tests, added skips and weaker assertions, but nothing asked for new tests: a result that added a code path with no test was verified as long as the existing tests passed. The `changed_line_coverage` check type asks for them. Wiring Kover into Loop's own build and declaring the check in its `WORKFLOW.md` is bl-121. The decisions:

- **Config.** `{ type: changed_line_coverage, reports: [<glob>, ...], min_percent: <0 to 100>, base: <ref> }`. `reports` is required and must list at least one glob, each relative to the workspace with no `..`, a leading `./` dropped. `min_percent` is required, a number from 0 to 100, decimals allowed. `base` is optional and defaults like the `diff_policy` base, to `guards.base`, else `main`. Each wrong shape is `invalid_config` naming the key. It can be a holdout too.
- **Changed lines.** `git diff -U0` of the working tree against the merge base with `base`, so committed, staged and unstaged changes count, plus every line of an untracked file git does not ignore. Renames count only their changed lines, deletions none. git runs like for `diff_policy`: no `GIT_` variable but `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM`, no replace refs, grafts, commit graph, external diff or textconv, explicit `a/` and `b/` prefixes, and a path git quotes is unquoted. The diff runs with `--text`: a `-diff` or `binary` attribute, in `.gitattributes` or the untracked `.git/info/attributes`, would otherwise make git print `Binary files differ` with no lines, so a source file's changes would never count and the check would pass (send back 1 of PR #94). Only a line feed ends a diff line, so a binary file's lone carriage return does not break the parse. A workspace that is not the top of a git work tree, or no merge base, means it could not run. The hardening of `diff_policy` against edits git cannot see (assume-unchanged, the `filter` attribute) is not repeated: the kind's `diff_policy` refuses those first. It does not refuse a `-diff` attribute, hence `--text`.
- **Pinned base.** A verification that pins `diff_policy` to a fetched base (bl-32), and the merge check's rerun, give `changed_line_coverage` the same commit, so base commits merged into the branch are never counted as the agent's lines. The fetch itself still happens only when a `diff_policy` is to run.
- **Reports.** JaCoCo XML, which Kover writes too, read with a SAX parser that never loads the external `report.dtd` nor any external entity. Each glob must find at least one regular file inside the workspace, symlinks and `.git` folders skipped: a report the build did not write must not drop its lines silently, so a glob that finds none means the check could not run, `no coverage report matches <glob>`. So does a report that is not well formed XML, or whose root is not `<report>`. The reports are merged: a line counts when one report lists it with missed or covered instructions, and is covered when one report covers it, so per module reports and an aggregated report add up.
- **Matching.** A source file is keyed by its package path and file name, `loop/core/verify/CoreChecks.kt`, and matches a changed file whose path is that key or ends with `/` and that key, the longest match winning. A changed file that matches none by path, and whose extension a report's source files use, is keyed like JaCoCo keys it, by the package its first `package` clause declares (none is the default package) and its file name, so a new file whose folder does not match its package counts, and fails when untested (send back 1 of PR #94). A changed file Loop cannot read means the check could not run. A file still in no report is named but does not count, not failed: a test file is in no main report, and JaCoCo leaves out a file with no instructions, an interface or declarations only, so failing it would refute every result that adds a test or a declaration. Two modules with the same package path and file name share one key, a limit of JaCoCo's format.
- **Verdict.** It passes when covered changed lines are at least `min_percent` percent of the countable ones, and when none counts, else it fails with exit code 1. Lines no report lists, declarations, comments and generated code, and changed files in no report never count; changed files in no report whose extension a report's source files use are named in the output.
- **Output.** On a failure, `Uncovered changed lines:`, then one line per file with its uncovered lines as ranges, at most 15 files and 10 ranges each, then the files in no report, then the summary last, `Changed line coverage against the merge base with <base> (<sha>): <covered> of <countable> changed lines covered, <rate>%, below the floor of <min>%. <n> coverage reports read.` The rate is rounded down to a tenth, so it never reads as the floor while under it. The summary comes last because the findings keep the tail of the output.
- **Trust.** The agent runs as the same user and could plant a report. Listing the exact paths the build writes, behind a `command` check that writes them, keeps a stale or planted file from counting.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/verify/ChangedLineCoverageTest.kt` (below the floor refuted with the uncovered lines, at or above verified, no countable line verified, unlisted lines and files ignored, a file matched by its declared package, a `-diff` attribute and a binary file in the diff, a missing, unparseable or foreign report could not run, multi module reports merged, committed, uncommitted and untracked lines, only lines since the merge base, the output cap, a quoted path, no git work tree or merge base), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workflow/KindsConfigTest.kt` (the parse, the base default and the config errors) and `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/MergeCheckOrchestratorTest.kt` (the merge check's fetched base).

### Codex CLI, app server schema and MCP loading (m3-02)

PLAN 14 and 20. The ground the Codex lane (m3-17 to m3-24) stands on, settled by hand on 2026-11-16 since it edits protected paths. The decisions:

- **The pinned CLI:** `@openai/codex` 0.167.0 from npm (`npm install -g @openai/codex@0.167.0`), the newest release older than the operator's seven day npm release age rule on that day (0.169.0 was three days old). `codex --version` prints `codex-cli 0.167.0`. The version is `codex-cli` in `gradle/libs.versions.toml`, the place Loop keeps it: the build reads it, the generated `loop.runner.codex.protocol.CodexCliVersion.PINNED` holds it for the runner, which refuses a different major version. m3-17 deferred that refusal, and bl-128 built it in the startup, see [Codex runner session startup](#codex-runner-session-startup-m3-18). The Claude Code runner pins Claude Code 2.1.330 and refuses another major at startup since bl-210, see [preflight for the Claude Code runner](#preflight-for-the-claude-code-runner-extension-m1-05).
- **The schema:** `codex app-server generate-json-schema --out <dir>` (stable methods only, without `--experimental`) writes one file per type and a bundle, `codex_app_server_protocol.schemas.json`, which holds them all: 83 definitions at the top (the v1 handshake, the JSON-RPC envelope, the request and notification unions) and 678 under `v2`. Only the bundle is committed, in `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/schema/`, with `codex-version` and `regenerate.sh`, which checks that the installed codex is the pinned one before regenerating. It needs no account: it ran before the login.
- **The types:** generated at build time, never committed, by a generator in the module's `generator` source set (task `generateCodexProtocol`) into `build/generated/codex-protocol`, packages `loop.runner.codex.protocol` and `loop.runner.codex.protocol.v2`. An object is a data class, a property outside `required` nullable with a null default; a `oneOf` tagged by a one value enum on a shared key (`type`, `method`) a sealed interface with that key as its class discriminator; a Rust enum serialized externally tagged (`"never"` or `{"granular": {...}}`) a sealed interface on `ExternallyTaggedSerializer`, with an `Unknown` variant where the schema has a catch all, as `CodexErrorInfo` does. Ten untagged unions stay `JsonElement`, the JSON-RPC envelope among them, pinned in a test so a schema update that adds one shows. The generator refuses a schema whose `codex-version` is not the pinned one. Read with `CodexJson.format`, which ignores unknown keys: the real `thread/start` response already carries `multiAgentMode`, which its own schema does not declare. Write a union through its base type, the only serializer that writes the tag.
- **The wire, checked on the real server:** one JSON object per line on stdin and stdout, with no `jsonrpc` member, `initialize` then the `initialized` notification, then `thread/start`. A recorded exchange, scrubbed of paths and ids, is the test fixture `src/test/resources/real/initialize_and_thread_start.jsonl`. No turn was started, so no model call was made.
- **Access:** a ChatGPT login (`codex login`, done by soule, Plus plan), stored in `$CODEX_HOME/auth.json`. Recorded in PLAN section 20. It unblocks m3-03, the enabled run of m3-24 and m3-28.

How the pinned CLI loads MCP servers, checked with a fake stdio MCP server, `codex app-server` over stdio, `thread/start` and the `mcpServer/startupStatus/updated` notifications. No account was needed for any of it except `codex_apps`: the checks ran in a temporary `CODEX_HOME` with no login, and `codex_apps` with soule's login.

- **User config:** every `[mcp_servers.<name>]` table of `$CODEX_HOME/config.toml` (default `~/.codex`) starts with each thread, at `thread/start`, `starting` then `ready`.
- **Project config:** a workspace's `.codex/config.toml` adds its servers only when the user config trusts that path, `[projects."<path>"] trust_level = "trusted"`. An untrusted project's file is ignored, with a warning on stderr. A Loop workspace is not trusted unless the operator trusts it, so an agent writing that file loads nothing.
- **`codex_apps`:** with a ChatGPT login, a built in server named `codex_apps` (ChatGPT apps and connectors, `https://chatgpt.com`) starts with each thread even when no `mcp_servers` table exists. It is the `apps` feature, stable and on by default.
- **What does not keep them out:** `-c mcp_servers={}` on the command line and `"config": {"mcp_servers": {}}` in `thread/start`. Both merge into the table, so every server still starts. `--disable plugins` does not either.
- **What does:** `-c mcp_servers.<name>.enabled=false` on `codex app-server`, or `"config": {"mcp_servers": {"<name>": {"enabled": false}}}` in `thread/start`, keeps that server from starting; it needs each name. `codex mcp list --json`, run in the workspace, gives the names of the user's and the trusted project's servers, with `enabled`. `--disable apps` on `codex app-server` removes `codex_apps`; `"config": {"features": {"apps": false}}` in `thread/start` keeps it from starting for that thread. An empty `CODEX_HOME` starts no server at all, but has no login: the login lives in `CODEX_HOME`, so a Loop owned home needs its own `CODEX_HOME=<dir> codex login`, once, by the operator.
- **What to check against:** `mcpServerStatus/list` answers from the process config, not from what a thread started: it lists a server that `enabled=false` kept from starting and misses a trusted project's server that started. The startup notifications are the signal; m3-20's test that a strict session loads no MCP server reads those.

So the [strict MCP](#strict-mcp-extension-bl-72) assumption for Codex is refuted: no single override empties the table. m3-20 builds strict on one of two mechanisms: disabling every name `codex mcp list --json` reports, plus `--disable apps`, which keeps the operator's login but trusts that list to be complete; or a Loop owned `CODEX_HOME`, which loads nothing Loop did not write, at the cost of a second login. m3-20 chose the first, with the names from `config/read` and `features.apps` off in `thread/start`, so the operator's login stays in use; the startup notifications of a real app server confirmed it, see [Codex runner approvals, user input, tool calls and guards](#codex-runner-approvals-user-input-tool-calls-and-guards-m3-20).

Tests: `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/protocol/ProtocolTypesTest.kt` (the real responses and notifications decode, a request writes its method, both forms of an externally tagged enum, a value variant, an unknown error kind kept, the pinned version), `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/schema/ProtocolGeneratorTest.kt` (the untyped places, a schema from another version refused, a tagged union, the names, defused comments).

### Codex runner session startup (m3-18)
