Story 42, spec 10.2 and 10.5, conformance 17.5 items 2 to 5. How `CodexAppServerRunner` starts a session on the pinned app server (`StartupHandshake` in `plugins/runner-codex-app-server`). The decisions:

- **Order:** `initialize` (request id 1), its answer, the `initialized` notification and `thread/start` (id 2), its answer, then `turn/start` (id 3) with the rendered prompt as one `text` input, and its answer. Each request is written only once the answer before it arrived, the order m3-02 checked on the real server. Notifications read meanwhile are passed over; a server request during startup is not answered yet, approvals and tool calls come with the turn handling.
- **Identity:** `clientInfo` is `{"name": "loop", "version": "<Loop's version>"}`, the version of the running build from the `build-info.properties` file `loop --version` reads, `unknown` when it cannot be read. Codex writes the name as the thread's `originator`. No `title`.
- **Capabilities:** `{"experimentalApi": false}`. Loop uses the stable protocol only, the one the committed schema holds (m3-02), so it never receives experimental methods or fields its types do not know. No notification is opted out and no attestation is asked for. Client tools are declared later, with 17.5 item 15. Since m3-22 `experimentalApi` is true when the thread declares Loop's tools, which the pinned app server refuses otherwise, see [Codex runner client tools](#codex-runner-client-tools-m3-22).
- **Approval policy: `never`,** on `thread/start` (`approvalPolicy`) and again on `turn/start`. An unattended run has nobody to answer an approval, and 10.5 forbids a run stalled on one. With `never` Codex never asks: it decides alone and the turn goes on. It is the posture of the Claude Code runner's `bypassPermissions` mode (see [implementation defined behaviors](#implementation-defined-behaviors)). Approval requests that still arrive are 17.5 item 10. Since m3-20 the policy is `untrusted`, so the runner can refuse the denied commands and the holdout folder, see [Codex runner approvals, user input, tool calls and guards](#codex-runner-approvals-user-input-tool-calls-and-guards-m3-20).
- **Sandbox: `danger-full-access`,** as `sandbox` on `thread/start` and `sandboxPolicy` `{"type": "dangerFullAccess"}` on `turn/start`. `workspace-write` would break ordinary work in Loop's workspaces: a git worktree keeps its git dir in the main repository, outside the workspace, so every commit would fail, and Gradle writes its caches and daemon under the home folder. `read-only` cannot do the work at all. So the Codex agent runs with the same reach as the Claude Code agent, which has no sandbox either, and the runner declares the `sandbox` capability false. What guards Loop's runs stays outside the agent: the checks of verification, the delivery policy and the `LOOP_ATTEMPT_ID` refusal of `loop approve` and `loop resume`. Codex has no deny rules like the Claude Code runner's settings file, so nothing in the runner keeps a Codex agent from `git push`, `gh`, a history rewrite or a read of the holdout folder yet; the approval handling (17.5 item 10) is where such a guard would go. So, after the merge risk review of pull request #100, the provider refuses to build the runner whenever the host gives it a holdout folder, which `loop run` always does: `loop run` with `codex-app-server` stops at startup with a configuration error naming m3-20, which lands the guards and lifts the refusal. Closing stdin after `turn/start` is not a limit to rely on. Built directly with no holdout folder, as in the module's tests, the runner starts. m3-20 landed the guards and lifted the refusal, see [Codex runner approvals, user input, tool calls and guards](#codex-runner-approvals-user-input-tool-calls-and-guards-m3-20).
- **Working directory:** the workspace's absolute path as `cwd` on `thread/start` and `turn/start`, as 10.2 asks wherever the protocol accepts one.
- **Ids:** the thread id is `thread.id` of the `thread/start` answer, the turn id `turn.id` of the `turn/start` answer, and `session_started` carries both with the session id `<thread_id>-<turn_id>`. It stays the first event of the run: output read before it is held and emitted right after it, as the Claude Code runner does. A run that ends before the turn started emits it with two UUIDs Loop minted, right before its terminal event.
- **A failed start:** a typed `StartupFailure`, its reason in the `response_error` category of 10.6. An error answer is `response_error: <method> failed with code <code>: <message>`; an answer to another request id `response_error: an answer to request <id> arrived while waiting for the <method> response`; an answer that does not decode into its method's response type `response_error: the <method> response does not match the pinned schema`. The runner stops reading and the process group is killed at once, so the exit code is null. A server that exits during the start fails with `port_exit: exit code <n>, before the <method> response`.
- **Version (bl-128):** the codex version is read from the `initialize` answer's `userAgent`, `<originator>/<version> (...)`, the only place the pinned protocol names it before the thread starts (`thread.cliVersion` comes with the `thread/start` answer, one request later). A major version other than the one of `CodexCliVersion.PINNED` fails the start with `response_error: codex <version> is not of the pinned major version <major> (codex-cli <pinned>)`, a `userAgent` with no readable version with `response_error: the initialize response names no codex version in its userAgent`, both before `initialized` is written, so the client never speaks the pinned protocol to a server of another major. Only the major is compared, as m3-02 decided, so a newer minor or patch release starts. The quoted version is bounded by the pattern that reads it, so the reason never carries more of what the server wrote.
- **Line size (bl-128):** the max line size of 10.1, 10 MB, holds in two places. The process group launcher reads each stdout and stderr line with at most 10 MB kept: a longer line is read to its line break and dropped, a `loop:` line on stderr naming its stream and size takes its place, and memory stays bounded. It is the launcher's default for every runner. `ProtocolFraming` also refuses a longer line as malformed, `line longer than 10 MB`, for a line that did not come through the launcher. A dropped protocol message is like any line that is not a protocol message: the turn goes on and the turn timeout bounds it.
- **After the start:** nothing more is written before the turn handling, so stdin is closed once the turn started and a real app server ends. Until the turn handling arrives every run still ends with `port_exit: exit code <n>, no turn result`. Since m3-19 stdin stays open and the turn ends at its `turn/completed`, see [Codex runner timeouts and continuation turns](#codex-runner-timeouts-and-continuation-turns-m3-19).

Not in m3-18: the read timeout of the startup requests (17.5 item 6), the turn handling and its result, approvals, user input and tool calls (items 7 and 10 to 16), a thread title with the issue's identifier, and continuation turns on the same thread.

Tests: `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/CodexAppServerRunnerTest.kt` (the `17_5 item 2` to `17_5 item 5` tests), `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/schema/PinnedSchemaTest.kt` (the schema check refuses a missing member, a value outside an enum, a wrong type, a union with no matching variant and a member the schema does not declare), `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/LoopVersionTest.kt`.

### Codex runner timeouts and continuation turns (m3-19)

Story 42, spec 10.2, 10.3 and 10.6, conformance 17.5 items 6 and 7. How `CodexAppServerRunner` ends a turn, enforces its two timeouts and runs the turns after the first. The decisions:

- **Where the timeouts live:** `runners.codex.read_timeout_ms` and `runners.codex.turn_timeout_ms`, beside `command`, the section m3-17 gave the runner, with the names the claude-code runner uses. The top level `codex` block, which the core still parses for `stall_timeout_ms`, is not read by the runner: two places for one setting would leave the reader guessing which wins.
- **Defaults:** the spec's (5.3.6), 5000 ms for the read timeout and 3600000 ms for the turn timeout, with the claude-code runner's limits, 1 to 86400000. Long silences are normal on real runs (see the silence lesson of [the first real delivery on the pilot app](#first-real-delivery-on-the-pilot-app-m2-27-bl-52)), so a project sets the turn timeout from its own work in `WORKFLOW.md`.
- **Read timeout:** runs from each request Loop writes, `initialize`, `thread/start` and every `turn/start`, to its answer. Notifications read meanwhile do not restart it. Past it the process group is killed and the turn fails with `response_timeout: no <method> response within <n> ms`.
- **Turn timeout:** runs once the turn started, the `turn/start` answer read, and restarts on every protocol message on stdout, a notification, a server request or an answer alike. Stderr lines and stdout lines that are not protocol messages do not restart it: they say nothing about the turn. Past it the group is killed and the turn fails with `turn_timeout: no protocol message for <n> ms`. Between turns the app server is idle and neither timer runs, as for the claude-code runner (m3-07).
- **Reasons:** the category, then a detail, as this runner's `port_exit` and `response_error` already are, so a trace says which request or how long. A killed app server has no exit code, so both report null.
- **Turn end:** the `turn/completed` notification of the current turn ends it, the protocol's completion signal of 10.3. A turn 2 needs turn 1 to end, so this minimal end comes with continuation: `completed` gives `turn_completed` with exit code 0, as the claude-code runner's long lived process does; `failed` gives `turn_failed` and `interrupted` `turn_cancelled`, each with `: <message>` when the turn carries an error. These two keep the app server, whose thread is still usable. A `turn/completed` that does not decode, or that says `inProgress`, fails with `response_error` and kills the group. A `turn/completed` for another turn id is passed over. Usage, approvals, user input and tool calls stay with the later issues of the lane.
- **Continuation:** `continuation` is declared true and `openSession` gives a session: one app server and one thread for every turn, stdin open across them. Turn 2 and later write `turn/start` with the next request id (4, 5 and so on), the thread id of the `thread/start` answer, the turn's input as the only `text` item, and the same approval policy, sandbox policy and `cwd` as turn 1, as 10.2 asks: continuation guidance on the same live thread, never the original prompt again. Its answer is read like the first turn's: an error, an answer out of order or one that does not decode fails with `response_error`. The turn emits `turn_started` with the thread id of turn 1 and the turn id of its answer; a turn that fails before its answer gets a turn id Loop minted. What the app server wrote between turns comes right after `turn_started`.
- **A gone app server:** an exit during a turn fails it at once with `port_exit: exit code <n>, no turn result`, or `before the turn/start response`, whatever the timeouts, so no turn stalls on a dead process. A later turn of the session fails at once, after its `turn_started`, with `port_exit: the app server of this session is gone, turn <n> cannot start on thread <id>`. No second app server is started and no `thread/resume` is tried: the orchestrator's retry starts a new attempt instead.
- **Stop:** `stop()`, and the end of `run()`, a session of one turn, close stdin, give the app server 5 s to exit and then kill its group. A cancelled turn kills it at once.
- **Controlled time:** the runner takes its dispatcher and its `TimeSource` as seams, so `CodexAppServerRunnerTimeoutTest` runs both timeouts on the virtual clock of `runTest`, with a scripted in memory server whose cancelled launch is the kill, and checks the exact time each turn failed at. The same cases run once more on a real process with short timeouts, to show the process gone.

Tests: `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/CodexAppServerRunnerTimeoutTest.kt`, `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/CodexAppServerRunnerTest.kt` (the `17_5 item 6` and `17_5 item 7` tests, `17_5 a continuation turn starts on the thread of the first turn, in the same app server, and keeps its thread id`, `17_5 the app server exiting mid turn fails the turn with port_exit at once, not at the turn timeout`, `17_5 the app server exiting mid continuation turn fails that turn, and a later turn fails at once on the gone server`, the `m3-19` tests), `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/CodexAppServerRunnerProviderTest.kt` (the `m3-19` profile tests).

### Codex runner approvals, user input, tool calls and guards (m3-20)

Story 42 and story 40, spec 10.5, conformance 17.5 items 10 to 12, and the guards Loop mode puts on the claude-code runner, given the same weight on Codex. How `CodexAppServerRunner` answers the requests the app server sends during a turn (`ServerRequestPolicy`), and how each guard holds on Codex (`DeniedCommands`, `HoldoutFolder` and the strict start of `StartupHandshake`, all in `plugins/runner-codex-app-server`). The decisions:

- **Approval policy: `untrusted`,** on `thread/start` and `turn/start`, in place of m3-18's `never`. Under `never` Codex asks nothing and runs everything, so no guard could refuse a command. Under `untrusted` the pinned Codex asks the client before every command it does not hold as known safe and before every file change, and runs its known safe read only commands (such as `cat`, `ls`, `rg`, `sed -n`, `head`, `git status` and `git diff`) without asking. The sandbox stays `danger-full-access`, for the reasons of m3-18. Every request is answered at once, by rule, so a turn never waits on a person: the run keeps the unattended posture of 10.5, and Loop asks no operator for anything.
- **Command approvals** (`item/commandExecution/requestApproval`): `decline` when its `kind` is set to anything but `command`, the schema's default, since input written to a running shell (`writeStdin`) or a human checkpoint (`humanCommand`) is not a command the rules can read; when it has no `command` text, since the parsed `commandActions` are for display only; when the command holds a denied command or names the holdout folder. Else `accept`. Both `command` and `commandActions` are optional in the pinned schema, so a request with neither would otherwise pass every rule unread. `decline` is the protocol's "denied, the agent goes on": the turn continues, as after a deny rule on the claude-code runner.
- **File change approvals** (`item/fileChange/requestApproval`): `decline` when a change of the item, by its path or the path it moves to, or the `grantRoot` it asks for, lies under the holdout folder, else `accept`. The request names only its item, so the runner reads the item's changes from its `item/started` notification and from `item/fileChange/patchUpdated`, which the protocol sends before the request. A request for an item whose changes the runner never saw is declined, since its paths are unknown.
- **The older forms,** `execCommandApproval` and `applyPatchApproval`, follow the same rules with `approved`, or `{"denied": {"rejection": "Loop refused this request: <reason>."}}`, the shape the pinned schema gives a denial. An empty command is denied.
- **Other approvals:** `item/permissions/requestApproval` is granted nothing (`{"permissions": {}}`), `mcpServer/elicitation/request` is declined.
- **User input** (`item/tool/requestUserInput`, 17.5 item 12): not answered. The turn ends at once with `turn_input_required` as its only terminal event, reason `item/tool/requestUserInput`, and the app server is stopped, as the claude-code runner fails a turn that needs input it cannot get (story 41): the attempt fails with the reason traced, is retried and eventually parked. A later turn of the session fails at once with `port_exit: the app server of this session is gone`, as after any kill (m3-19).
- **Dynamic tool calls** (`item/tool/call`, 17.5 item 11): Loop declares no dynamic tool yet (client tools are 17.5 item 15), so every call is unsupported and gets the protocol's tool failure result, `{"success": false, "contentItems": [{"type": "inputText", "text": "Loop serves no dynamic tool on this thread, so the tool <name> is not supported. Go on without it."}]}`, and the turn goes on, as 10.5 asks. Since m3-22 the thread declares Loop's tools in an attempt Loop started, and a call of one runs through `loop tool mcp`; the unsupported answer is for any other tool, see [Codex runner client tools](#codex-runner-client-tools-m3-22).
- **Any other server request,** such as `attestation/generate` or `account/chatgptAuthTokens/refresh`: a JSON-RPC error, code -32601, and the turn goes on.
- **Trace:** each answer adds one runner output line on stderr that never quotes the request: `loop: accepted the command approval request <id>`, `loop: declined the command approval request <id>: it runs a denied command (...)` or `...: it touches the holdout folder`, `loop: unsupported dynamic tool call, request <id>, answered with a failure result`, and so on. The holdout path is never written.
- **Every thread:** a request is answered by the same rules whatever thread or turn it names, so a subagent's requests are guarded like the agent's.
- **Launch by argv, not `bash -lc`** (deviation from 10.1, after the merge risk review of pull request #104). `runners.codex.command` is split into words as a shell would, with single quotes, double quotes and backslashes, nothing expanded, and run through the process group launcher with the agent env, as the claude-code runner runs its command. A login shell sources the user's profile after the [agent environment](#agent-environment-extension-m1-05-m1-23-and-bl-01) policy built the env, so a profile export would bring back a name the policy removed (`GH_TOKEN`, a tracker or Anthropic token, `LOOP_HOLDOUT`) for a Codex agent. A command written for a shell, with an unquoted operator, `$`, a backtick, a glob or a leading `~`, or with an unterminated quote, fails preflight with `invalid_config` naming `runners.codex.command`, so it never runs with literal words. The program is looked up on the agent env's `PATH`; one the launcher cannot run is `agent_not_found`.
- **The agent's commands without a login shell** (send back 2 of pull request #104). The launch fix covers the app server, but Codex runs each command of the agent in the user's shell, by default as a login shell (`/bin/zsh -lc '<command>'`), which sources the profile again one level below. Every `thread/start` now carries `"config": {"allow_login_shell": false, ...}`, with strict MCP on or off. The schema's `allowLoginShell` is a field of `ConfigRequirements`, the managed requirements, not a thread or turn parameter; the pinned binary has `allow_login_shell` as a top level key of `config.toml`, so it can be set as a thread config override or as `-c allow_login_shell=false` on the launch. Loop uses the thread config, since the launch command is the operator's `runners.codex.command`, which need not be a bare `codex`. Checked on codex 0.167.0 with no login and no real model: a fake Responses provider on localhost, set as the model provider of a temporary `CODEX_HOME`, answered the first model call with one `exec_command` call, in a `HOME` whose `.zprofile`, `.zlogin`, `.bash_profile` and `.profile` export `GH_TOKEN` and `LOOP_HOLDOUT`. By default the command ran as `/bin/zsh -lc` and printed both values from the profile. With the thread config, and also with the `-c` override, it ran as `/bin/zsh -c` and printed neither, with `shell_snapshot` (stable, on) left on. An explicit `"login": true` in the tool call got the error ``login shell is disabled by config; omit `login` or set it to false.`` and did not run. Not covered: `zsh -c` still reads `~/.zshenv`, as every zsh does, so a name exported there reaches the agent's commands when the user's shell is zsh; the agent env policy cannot remove it, and the operator should not export a secret there. A managed `requirements.toml` that forces `allowLoginShell` is the operator's own choice and is not checked.

The guards, how each holds on Codex and what it cannot cover:

- **`git push`, `gh` and history rewrites** (story 40). The denied commands are the claude-code runner's list, and since bl-135 `DeniedCommandsTest` fails when the two differ: `git push` and its force forms, `gh`, `git rebase`, the hard form of `git reset`, `git commit --amend`, `git filter-branch`, `git filter-repo`, `git reflog expire`, `git update-ref` and `git replace`, plus `loop approve` and `loop resume`, also through a launcher path (m2-24). A command is matched on its whole text, as Claude Code matches its deny rules: split into words at blanks, quotes and the shell's operators, a denied command matches as a run of whole words anywhere, so `cd x && git push`, `bash -lc 'gh pr create'` and a quoted script holding one are declined. They never run without a request: none of them is known safe to the pinned Codex, so under `untrusted` each comes as an approval request and is declined. Not covered, as on the claude-code runner: a command that dodges the words, such as a script file written first and run next. Since bl-135 a program by its path, git's own options, a backslash, a substitution as the git subcommand and a commit message are handled, and an execpolicy rule that allows a denied command stops the session before it starts, see [Codex guards after the m3-20 review](#codex-guards-after-the-m3-20-review-extension-bl-135).
- **Holdout read and edit deny** (m2-14). The holdout folder reaches the runner through plugin loading, as the `holdoutDir` of `RunnerProvider.create`, which `loop run` always gives, with no `Host.kt` edit; the provider's refusal of m3-18 is lifted. A command approval is declined when the command names the folder, by its text, from `~` or `$HOME`, by a relative word read from the request's `cwd`, or by the path of one of its parsed read actions; a file change approval when it touches the folder, as above. Paths are compared by normalized and real path, ignoring case. Not covered: a read by a command Codex holds as known safe, such as `cat`, `sed -n` or `rg` on the folder, runs without a request, so nothing can refuse it. This gap is wider than the claude-code runner's, whose deny rules cover its file tools and miss only a shell read: a Codex agent reads files through the shell. What the [holdout checks](#holdout-checks-extension-m2-14) say holds: it is a guardrail, not a sandbox, the agent runs as the same OS user, and what keeps a holdout check honest is that its output never reaches the agent.
- **Strict MCP** (bl-72). `runners.codex.strict_mcp`, a boolean of the profile, `true` by default as bl-72 decided for both runners; any other value fails preflight with `runners.codex.strict_mcp must be a boolean`. With it the startup sends `config/read` with the workspace as `cwd` between `initialized` and `thread/start`, and `thread/start` carries `"config": {"mcp_servers": {"<name>": {"enabled": false}, ...}, "features": {"apps": false}}`, one entry per server the answer lists. It is the first mechanism m3-02 found: no single override empties the table, but a server disabled by name never starts, and the `apps` feature off keeps `codex_apps` out, while the operator's login stays in use. Request ids become 1 (`initialize`), 2 (`config/read`), 3 (`thread/start`), 4 (`turn/start`), then 5 and on for continuation turns; without strict they stay 1, 2, 3, then 4 and on. A `config/read` error answer, or one that does not decode, fails the start with `response_error`, so a strict session never runs with the operator's servers. `strict_mcp: false` sends no `config/read`, and its `config` holds only `allow_login_shell: false`. Checked again on codex 0.167.0 in this issue, with a temporary `CODEX_HOME`, no login, no turn and no model call: `config/read` lists the user config's `mcp_servers` and, from a trusted workspace, the project's `.codex/config.toml` servers too; a thread started with them disabled and `apps` off started none, while a plain thread started both. The recorded `config/read` answer, scrubbed, is the fixture `src/test/resources/real/config_read.json`. Not covered: a server added to a config during the session, since each session reads the config once, at its start.
- **Observed on the pinned app server:** `thread/start` in a workspace inside a git repository wrote `[projects."<repository root>"] trust_level = "trusted"` into `$CODEX_HOME/config.toml`. So after the first thread a workspace is a trusted project, and its `.codex/config.toml`, which the agent can write, loads in later sessions. Strict MCP covers its MCP servers, since `config/read` lists them; nothing in Loop covers the rest of that project config. Since bl-135 a `.codex/rules/` file that allows a denied command stops the next session before it starts, see [Codex guards after the m3-20 review](#codex-guards-after-the-m3-20-review-extension-bl-135).

Not in m3-20: Loop's client tools (17.5 items 15 and 16), usage and rate limits (items 13 and 14), and a read deny that holds without an approval request, which would need a Codex sandbox that can deny reads under a folder. No model turn ran in this issue, so which commands the pinned Codex asks about under `untrusted`, and that a file change item starts before its approval request, come from the protocol's schema and documentation, not from a recorded session; the real integration profile (m3-24) checks them.

Tests: `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/CodexServerRequestsTest.kt` (approvals accepted and declined, `git push`, `gh`, history rewrites, `loop approve` and `loop resume`, holdout reads by text, relative path, home and read action, a holdout edit, a move, a patch update and a granted root under it, an unseen item, a command approval with no command or of another kind, the older forms and an empty legacy command, the unsupported dynamic tool call, user input, the other requests, strict MCP on, with no server, with a failing `config/read` and off, the `untrusted` policy, `m3-20 every thread start turns the login shell off, so a profile export never reaches an agent command`; every request and answer checked against the pinned schema), `CodexAppServerRunnerTimeoutTest.kt` (`17_5 item 12 m3-20 a user input request ends the turn at once with input_required as its only terminal event`, on virtual time), `CodexAppServerRunnerProviderTest.kt` (the `strict_mcp` key and its parse, the runner built with the holdout folder, the command split into argv words and a shell command refused), `CodexAppServerRunnerTest.kt` (`m3-20 a name the env policy removed stays out even when the login profile exports it`), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/RunnerPluginsTest.kt` (`m3-20 runner kind codex-app-server is built for Loop's runs, given the holdout folder, through plugin loading alone`), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/PluginProfileDocsTest.kt` (the profile table).

### Codex runner usage, rate limits and cost (m3-21)

Story 42, conformance 17.5 items 13 and 14, and the rate limit half of 17.6 item 4. How `CodexAppServerRunner` reads usage and rate limits from the pinned protocol (`Telemetry` in `plugins/runner-codex-app-server`), and what a runner with no cost means for the ledger and the budgets. The decisions:

- **One mapping per message kind:** each approval, user input, usage and rate limit signal of the pinned schema has its own mapping. The requests are m3-20's (`ServerRequestPolicy`): every server request kind of the schema has its own rule, and only `account/chatgptAuthTokens/refresh` and `attestation/generate`, which are none of these signals, get the method not found error. Usage is `thread/tokenUsage/updated` and rate limits `account/rateLimits/updated`, the only notifications of the schema about either. A test reads the kinds from the schema, so an update that adds one fails until the runner maps it.
- **Usage:** `thread/tokenUsage/updated` carries the thread's `total`, absolute for that thread so far, and `last`, the latest model response alone. The runner reads `total`. A session's usage is the sum of the latest `total` of every thread it saw, so a subagent's thread counts, the lesson of [Claude Code subagent spend](#claude-code-subagent-spend-extension-bl-71); a later total of a thread replaces its earlier one. An update that leaves the sum unchanged, such as a repeated identical one, emits nothing, so it counts once; the orchestrator's `TokenMeter` would count it once anyway. Each change emits one [`usage`](#usage-event-and-token-accounting-extension-of-135-m1-05) event at once, so a stalled, stopped or timed out turn has already reported what it spent, as bl-108 asks. Continuation turns run on one thread, whose total keeps growing, so turn 2's report replaces turn 1's, never adds to it.
- **Token counts:** the protocol's `inputTokens` holds the cached input, as the OpenAI usage it comes from does. So Loop's `input_tokens` is `inputTokens` minus `cachedInputTokens` and `cacheWriteInputTokens` (never below 0), `cache_read_input_tokens` is `cachedInputTokens` and `cache_creation_input_tokens` is `cacheWriteInputTokens` (0 when absent), and the four add up to the protocol's `totalTokens`. `outputTokens` already holds `reasoningOutputTokens`, which is not added again.
- **Rate limits:** a new runner event, `RunnerEvent.RateLimit` in `plugin-api`, recorded as `rate_limit` with `limit_id`, `limit_name`, `primary_used_percent`, `primary_window_minutes`, `primary_resets_at`, the same three for `secondary`, and `reached`, the runtime's word for a limit it says is reached (`rate_limit_reached`, or a workspace credit or usage limit). Each key is absent when Codex did not say. The schema calls the notification a sparse rolling update whose missing values do not clear earlier ones, so the runner keeps the last snapshot of each limit id and a value an update leaves out keeps the earlier one; `reached` is taken as each update gives it, so a reached limit clears. A snapshot that changes nothing emits nothing, which keeps the aggregation right across repeated updates (17.6 item 4). `resetsAt` is read as Unix seconds and recorded as an instant: the schema does not say its unit, the Codex source does; the real integration profile (m3-24) checks it on a recorded session. The event reports, it never stops: a turn Codex refuses past its limit fails on its own.
- **Trace:** `rate_limit` shows in the attempt's timeline as `rate_limit  limit=codex name=Codex primary=42%/300m primary_resets_at=2026-11-17T12:00:00Z secondary=7%/10080m reached=rate_limit_reached`, each part left out when its key is absent. It writes no human log line.
- **Unreadable telemetry:** a usage or rate limit notification that does not decode into its type of the pinned schema adds `loop: the <method> notification does not match the pinned schema, skipped` on stderr and is skipped. Telemetry never ends a turn.
- **Ids:** a usage or rate limit read before the turn's start event is emitted right after it with the turn's ids, as every event the runner holds.
- **Cost:** Codex reports no cost: no notification of the pinned schema gives a dollar figure for a turn. `RunnerEvent.Usage.totalCostUsd` is nullable for that, and the runner always leaves it null, with the capability `cost` false and `usage` true. So the `usage` event has no `total_cost_usd`, and the ledger's turn lines and attempt row record no cost, null, never 0: a 0 would say the attempt was free. Loop keeps no price table, so it estimates nothing.
- **Budgets:** a runner with no cost spends nothing against a dollar budget, as [spend budgets](#spend-budgets-extension-m2-20) already said: `budgets.per_attempt_usd`, `budgets.per_issue_usd` and `budgets.per_day_usd` never warn nor park its attempts, and the machine `per_day_usd` (m3-16) does not count them. So dollar budgets do not hold for the Codex runner. `loop run` says so once at startup when the workflow sets one: `loop run: warning: budgets.per_attempt_usd and budgets.per_day_usd are set, but the codex-app-server runner reports no cost, so dollar budgets do not hold for it: its attempts spend nothing against them. Token budgets still hold.` The warning reads the runner's declared `cost` capability, not its kind, so the command runner, which reports no cost either, gets it too. A reload that adds a dollar budget is not warned again. Token budgets and token thresholds still hold, from the usage above.

Not in m3-21: context occupancy for Codex (`last` gives it since [m3-23](#codex-runner-context-occupancy-and-rotation-m3-23)), Loop's client tools (17.5 items 15 and 16), and a recorded real session, which m3-24 brings.

Tests: `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/CodexTelemetryTest.kt` (against the fake server: `17_5 item 13 repeated identical usage updates count once and a later total replaces the earlier`, the token counts, no cost, a continuation turn, a subagent thread, telemetry read before the start, `17_5 item 13 a rate limit update maps to a RateLimit event with its windows, and a repeated identical one counts once`, a sparse update, unreadable notifications), `CodexSignalKindsTest.kt` (the `17_5 item 14` tests, one per kind: command approval, file change approval, the older exec and patch approvals, permissions, MCP elicitation, user input, token usage, rate limit, and the two that read the kinds from the pinned schema), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/OrchestratorRateLimitTest.kt` (`m3-21 a rate limit report is recorded as a rate_limit event of the attempt and its turn, and the turn goes on`), `OrchestratorLedgerTest.kt` (`m3-21 a session that never reports a cost has its tokens in the ledger and its cost empty, not zero`), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/TraceCommandTest.kt` (`m3-21 a rate limit shows its limit, its windows and their reset, and whether it is reached`), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/DollarBudgetWarningTest.kt` (the startup warning through `loop run --once`, the budgets it names, none for token budgets alone or a runner with a cost), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/EventDefinitionsTest.kt` (the `rate_limit` sample renders in the trace).

### Codex runner client tools (m3-22)

Story 42, conformance 17.5 items 15 and 16 (tools 1). How `CodexAppServerRunner` gives a Codex agent Loop's tools, `submit_result`, `move` and `comment`, as client tools of its thread (`LoopClientTools` and `LoopToolCall` in `plugins/runner-codex-app-server`), the `client_tools` way of the runner contract (m3-06). The decisions:

- **Declared on `thread/start`, with the experimental API.** The protocol's client tools are `dynamicTools` on `thread/start`, a list of `DynamicToolSpec`. The stable schema Loop committed (m3-02) holds `DynamicToolSpec`, but its `ThreadStartParams` has no `dynamicTools`: the member is experimental. The experimental schema of the same codex 0.167.0 (`generate-json-schema --experimental`) has `dynamicTools: [DynamicToolSpec] | null` on `ThreadStartParams`, with the same `DynamicToolSpec`, and nothing on `turn/start`. The real app server, with a temporary `CODEX_HOME`, no login and no model call, answered `thread/start` with `dynamicTools` by the error `-32600 thread/start.dynamicTools requires experimentalApi capability` under `experimentalApi: false`, and started the thread under `true`. So when the runner declares its tools, `initialize` asks for the experimental API and `thread/start` carries `dynamicTools`, added to the JSON of the stable type; without tools both stay as m3-18 wrote them. This is a deviation from m3-18's stable protocol only: with the experimental API on, the app server may send members and messages the generated types do not know. Responses are read ignoring unknown keys, unknown notifications are passed over and an unknown server request gets the method not found error of m3-20, so none of them can stall or end a turn. The schema stays the stable one; regenerating it with `--experimental` would change every generated type for one member.
- **When:** the attempt env has `LOOP_LAUNCHER` and `LOOP_TOOL_CONFIG`, which `loop run` gives every agent (m1-23), as the Claude Code runner writes its MCP config only then. Outside an attempt Loop started, the thread declares nothing and a dynamic tool call gets m3-20's unsupported answer. A tool config snapshot that cannot be read declares nothing either, with one `loop:` line on stderr, `the thread declares no Loop tool: the attempt's tool config snapshot cannot be read (<reason>)`.
- **Only the selected tracker's tools.** The tools and their argument schemas are built from the snapshot, which names the workflow's tracker kind and its defined states and holds no credential: `move` takes a required string `state` whose `enum` is exactly the tracker's defined states (`todo`, `in_progress`, `review`, `needs_info`, `done`, `canceled` for `files`; `open`, `closed`, `review`, `needs_info` for `github`), and the descriptions of `move` and its `state` name the tracker kind. `comment` takes a required string `text`. `submit_result` takes the PLAN section 7 result, the schema `SubmitResultSchema` gives the MCP tool, field for field, `status` required. A build script change, a protected path in Loop mode, would be needed for the module to use the `mcp` module, so the schema is written in the module and a cli test keeps it equal to what `loop tool mcp` lists. The descriptions are the MCP tools' descriptions. The pinned app server checks the shape of each `DynamicToolSpec` at `thread/start` (a spec without `inputSchema` is refused), not the JSON schema inside it; it accepted the three specs of both trackers.
- **A call runs through `loop tool mcp`.** A call of a declared tool with an object as its arguments is run by spawning `<LOOP_LAUNCHER> tool mcp` in the workspace, with the agent env of the app server plus `LOOP_SESSION_ID` and `LOOP_TURN_ID` of the turn, as the Claude Code runner's MCP config passes them; the runner speaks the MCP handshake and one `tools/call` to it, then closes its stdin. So a Codex call is the same request as a Claude Code call: the same validation, the same queue in the attempt directory, the same events (`result_requested`, `issue_move_requested`, `issue_comment_requested`), applied at the same host side [drain](#host-side-tracker-writes-extension-m1-23) by Loop's tracker adapter with its own auth, and in Loop mode the same verdict path for a result and the same intercept of a move. The runner holds no credential and writes nothing to the tracker. One server per call keeps nothing alive across turns; a call costs one start of the launcher.
- **The answer:** the tool result's text as one `inputText` item, `success` true, or false when the result is a tool error, a refusal of `loop tool mcp` that queued nothing (a state the tracker does not define, an invalid result, an attempt that is not live). The agent reads the same reply as over MCP, so it can fix the call in the same turn. A call to a tool Loop does not declare, a namespaced call, or one whose arguments are not an object gets a failure result at once, without starting `loop tool mcp`: `Loop serves only submit_result, move and comment on this thread, so the tool <name> is not supported. Nothing queued.`, or `The arguments of <tool> must be a JSON object. Nothing queued.` A `loop tool mcp` that cannot start, exits before answering (with its last stderr line, the `loop tool` refusal, cut to 300 characters), answers with a JSON-RPC error or does not answer within 60 s gives `Loop could not run <tool>: <reason>.` with `success` false. The turn goes on in every case.
- **Trace:** each call adds `loop: ran the Loop tool <tool> for the tool call request <id>`, with `, refused` for a failure result, on stderr. It never quotes the arguments or the reply; the reply's events are `loop tool mcp`'s own.
- **Timing:** the read loop waits for the call, so the app server's messages queue meanwhile, and the time of Loop's own call does not count against the turn timeout: it restarts once the answer is written.

Not in m3-22: the current issue and `native_ref` as tool context (17.5 item 16, tools 3), Partial until m4-14 covered it, see the matrix and bl-153 (m3-26); and a recorded real session that calls a client tool, which needs a model turn, so the real integration profile (m3-24) checks that the pinned Codex sends `item/tool/call` for a declared tool.

Tests: `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/CodexClientToolsTest.kt` (against the fake server and a fake `loop` launcher: `17_5 item 15 the thread start carries submit_result, move and comment as client tools with valid schemas`, `17_5 item 16 tools 1 with the files tracker, move accepts the files tracker's states`, `17_5 item 16 tools 1 with the github tracker, move accepts the github tracker's states`, a `submit_result`, a `move` and a `comment` call, a call `loop tool mcp` refuses, arguments that are not an object, an undeclared tool, a `loop tool mcp` that exits before answering, no tool outside an attempt, an unreadable snapshot; every message checked against the pinned schema, the tools against `DynamicToolSpec`), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/CodexClientToolsRunTest.kt` (end to end through `loop run --once`, the real `loop tool mcp` and the drain with the `files` tracker: `17_5 item 16 a Codex session's comment and move act on the tracker host side, and a refused move gets a failure result`, `17_5 item 16 a Codex session's submit_result is recorded and verified, and the session gets the reply`, `m3-22 the client tools take the arguments loop tool mcp declares, move narrowed to the tracker's states`).

### Codex runner context occupancy and rotation (m3-23)

Story 42 with plan 8.3 and 8.4 on Codex. `CodexAppServerRunner` reports the occupancy of its thread as a `context_usage` runner event (m3-06), declares `occupancy`, and ends a running turn on `interrupt()`, so the soft limit rotation of [m3-11](#soft-limit-rotation-extension-m3-11), the hard limit rotation of [m3-12](#hard-limit-and-split-issue-extension-m3-12) and the peak per session of [m3-09](#peak-occupancy-and-runners-without-occupancy-extension-m3-09) work on Codex as on the claude-code runner, with no change to the orchestrator. The code is `Telemetry`, `StartupHandshake` and `CodexAppServerRunner` in `plugins/runner-codex-app-server`. The decisions:

- **How occupancy is computed.** From `thread/tokenUsage/updated`, the notification m3-21 reads for spend. Its `tokenUsage` carries `total`, the thread's cumulative usage, and `last`, the usage of the latest model request alone: the protocol's form of Codex's `last_token_usage`. The occupancy is `last.inputTokens`. The protocol's `inputTokens` already holds the cached input and the cache writes, as m3-21 found for `total`, so it is the input size of that one request, cached tokens included, the same measure as Claude Code's `input_tokens + cache_read_input_tokens + cache_creation_input_tokens` of [m3-08](#claude-code-context-occupancy-extension-m3-08). Output and reasoning tokens never count. `total` is never read for occupancy: it only grows across the requests of a thread, so it would cross the soft limit long before the context does. Spend keeps reading `total`, unchanged.
- **One report per change.** Each notification of the session's thread emits `ContextUsage` right after its `Usage`, with the turn's ids; a report equal to the last one, the same tokens on the same model, emits nothing, since occupancy replaces the last report and a repeat changes nothing. A smaller report after a larger one is emitted, as after Codex trimmed its context.
- **Main thread only.** The session's thread is the one of the `thread/start` answer. A notification of another thread, a subagent's, emits no occupancy: its window is its own, the gap m3-08 recorded for Claude Code, recorded here for Codex too and not enforced. Its spend still counts (m3-21).
- **The model and the window source.** `ContextUsage.model` is the `context.windows` key `<modelProvider>/<model>`, both from the `thread/start` answer, for example `openai/gpt-5.1-codex-max` on the recorded answer of codex 0.167.0. A `model/rerouted` notification of the thread switches the model to its `toModel`, on the same provider, until the next turn starts on the thread's model again: the schema gives a reroute a turn id, and the requests after it run on the new model. The window is the one `context.windows` gives that key, the defaults merged with the workflow's entries, the one source the orchestrator, the ledger and `loop status` read for every runner. The defaults hold no Codex model, so an operator running Codex adds the model's window under `context.windows`; without it the sessions report their occupancy in tokens, with no window and no fraction, never rotate, and Loop warns once per run as m3-08 does. The protocol's `modelContextWindow`, also on the notification, is not read: `ContextUsage` carries no window, and using it would change the plugin API and the orchestrator's window lookup, outside this issue's module; the operator can take the value from it.
- **The interrupt.** `AgentSession.interrupt()` writes one `turn/interrupt` request with the thread id and the turn id of the running turn and the next request id, at once when Codex has named the turn, else as soon as its `turn/start` answer arrives; a second call writes nothing. Codex then reports the turn's last usage and ends it with `turn/completed` of status `interrupted`, which the runner maps to `turn_cancelled` as before; the orchestrator, which asked for the interrupt, takes that terminal event as the end of the cut turn and records `turn_completed` with `interrupted` = `hard_limit`, not a failure. The usage reported after the interrupt counts in the ledger line, and so does its occupancy in the session's peak. The answer to `turn/interrupt` is passed over whenever it comes, so a late one never fails the next request's answer.
- **The rotation.** The orchestrator's, unchanged: it stops the session, which closes the app server's stdin and kills its group after the stop grace, and opens a new one with `openSession`, whose turn 1 starts a new app server and a new thread with the rotation prompt and the session brief (m3-10). The ledger's `thread_id`, `peak_tokens`, `window` and `peak_fraction` of each turn line, `session_rotated` with the old and new thread ids, and `loop trace` follow from the runner's events as for Claude Code.

Tests: `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/CodexOccupancyTest.kt` (against the fake server: `m3-23 context_usage follows the last request's input, cached tokens included, not the cumulative totals`, the declared capability, a subagent thread and a repeated report, a continuation turn, a rerouted request, `m3-23 interrupt asks Codex to end the running turn once, and its usage after the interrupt is still reported`, a late answer to an interrupt), `CodexAppServerRunnerTest` and `CodexSignalKindsTest` (the capability and the telemetry signals), and `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/CodexRotationRunTest.kt` (end to end through `loop run --once` with a scripted app server: `m3-23 a Codex session over the soft limit rotates at its turn end onto a new thread with the session brief` and `m3-23 a Codex session over the hard limit has its turn interrupted, then rotates onto a new thread`, each with `session_rotated`, `loop trace` and the peak of each session in the ledger).

### Real integration profile (m3-24)

Story 96, the spec's real integration profile (17.8), set up for Codex in m3-24. The GitHub half, m3-25, follows the same shape. Real integration tests are JUnit tests in each module's test sources, gated by assumptions, so the profile needs no Gradle change. The decisions:

- **The switch.** `LOOP_REAL_INTEGRATION` names the targets to run, a comma list: `codex` (m3-24), `github` (m3-25), or `codex,github`. When the variable is unset or does not name a target, that target's tests are aborted by a JUnit assumption, so the report shows them skipped, never passed. A name that is no known target fails the test, so a typo never reads as a skip. The gate is `RealIntegration` in `plugin-api/src/testFixtures/kotlin/loop/api/RealIntegration.kt`, on the test classpath of every plugin.
- **Failing the job.** Once its target is named, a test fails when anything it needs is missing, naming each missing item in one message, and when the real run fails. Either failure fails the Gradle task, so the job fails.
- **Names.** A target's settings are named `LOOP_REAL_<TARGET>_<NAME>`. Credentials keep the names their tool already reads, never a `LOOP_REAL_` name: the codex login for Codex, the playground token `LOOP_PLAYGROUND_GITHUB_TOKEN` for GitHub. Not `GITHUB_TOKEN`, which the `gh` CLI reads, so every shell would switch to the narrow token (`docs/self-hosting.md`, GitHub playground).
- **Isolation.** Each run takes a fresh `RealIntegration.runId()`, `loop-real-` and 8 hex characters, for its workspace and its identifiers, works in its own temporary folder, and removes what it created. The one exception is a GitHub issue: a fine grained token cannot delete one, so each `github` run leaves its issue closed on the playground.
- **The `codex` target.** `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/real/`. The credential is the codex login, checked with `codex login status` under the test's env, so `CODEX_HOME` and a keyring store count as codex counts them; an API key variable alone does not log codex 0.167.0 in, so it is not checked. The program must be on `PATH`. `LOOP_REAL_CODEX_COMMAND` overrides the command, `codex app-server` by default. It is also how the run picks its model, since the smoke test runs the runner with its defaults and so sends no `runners.codex.model` ([refused Codex model](#refused-codex-model-extension-bl-141)): a login refuses the model of an operator's `~/.codex/config.toml` with `modelNotFound`, so a ChatGPT login sets `LOOP_REAL_CODEX_COMMAND='codex app-server -c model=gpt-6.1-sol'` or another model of `model/list`. The smoke test makes one real model call: the `success` prompt of m3-03 in a new git repository under the system temporary folder, outside Loop's checkout, through `CodexAppServerRunner` with its defaults and a 3 minute turn timeout. It passes when the session starts, the turn completes, usage is reported, the workspace holds only its README, and the workspace is gone afterwards. Codex itself keeps a rollout of the session under `$CODEX_HOME/sessions`, which the runner cannot prevent.
- **The `github` target.** `plugins/tracker-github/src/test/kotlin/loop/tracker/github/real/` (m3-25). It runs against the playground, `Soules-Studio-Ltd/loop-playground`, with no setting of its own. The credential is `LOOP_PLAYGROUND_GITHUB_TOKEN`, read through `GitHubTrackerSettings.parse` as a `WORKFLOW.md` would, so an unset, blank or unusable token fails naming the variable and never its value. The smoke test creates two labels named from the run id, `loop-real-<hex>` and `loop-real-<hex>-ready`, and one issue carrying both, straight through the API, since the tracker cannot create either. Then, through `GitHubTracker` scoped to the first label with the second as its required label, it fetches the open candidates and finds only that issue, reads it by id, comments on it and finds the comment through `completeIssue`, moves it to `review` (the handoff label `needs-review` added, the required label removed), and moves it to `closed`. The required label is the run's own, never `ready-for-agent`, so a Loop pointed at the playground never takes the issue as a candidate. Reads after a write are retried up to 5 times, 1 second apart, since GitHub's lists and counts can lag. Whatever happened, the test then closes the issue and checks the close response reads `closed`, then deletes both labels, each step on its own, and a cleanup failure names what is left to remove by hand. Since bl-150 a write whose response is lost leaves nothing behind: each label is kept for deletion before its create is sent, and when the issue create got no answer the issues labelled with the run id are looked up (read again while the list is empty, since it can lag) and closed before the labels go; a failed lookup keeps the labels, the only way to find the issue by hand. The review read settles until `needs-review` is there and the required label is gone, not on the first label alone. The cleanup logic runs against a fake GitHub in `GitHubRealRunTest`. It prints its steps with the issue number, never the token.

Enable it from the repository root, after `codex login status` says you are logged in:

```bash
LOOP_REAL_INTEGRATION=codex LOOP_REAL_CODEX_COMMAND='codex app-server -c model=gpt-6.1-sol' ./gradlew --no-daemon :plugins:runner-codex-app-server:test --tests 'loop.runner.codex.real.*' --rerun
```

The `--tests` filter and `--rerun` both matter. The environment is not an input of the test task and the build cache is on, so without `--rerun` an earlier result can be reused; and without the filter, the enabled result would be stored under the same inputs as a plain run, whose report would then show the smoke test passed with the profile unset. The smoke test prints its runner events, which is the output to link in the issue. It leaves out the raw protocol lines, which carry the home folder, the host name and the installation id; a failure message shows them all, for a local look. The enabled run passed on 2026-11-17, see m3-24.

Enable the `github` target the same way, with `LOOP_PLAYGROUND_GITHUB_TOKEN` exported:

```bash
LOOP_REAL_INTEGRATION=github ./gradlew --no-daemon :plugins:tracker-github:test --tests 'loop.tracker.github.real.*' --rerun
```

The same `--tests` filter and `--rerun` apply. Its printed steps are the output to link in m3-25. The enabled run passed on 2026-11-17, see m3-25.

Tests: `plugin-api/src/test/kotlin/loop/api/RealIntegrationTest.kt` (the gate: skipped when unset or naming another target, a failure naming each missing item, an unknown target), `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/real/CodexRealProfileTest.kt` (the `codex` target against a fake `codex` script, in every build: skipped when off, a failure naming the program missing from `PATH`, a failure naming the missing login and `CODEX_HOME`, `LOOP_REAL_CODEX_COMMAND`) and `CodexRealSmokeTest.kt` (the real session, skipped unless enabled), `plugins/tracker-github/src/test/kotlin/loop/tracker/github/real/GitHubRealProfileTest.kt` (the `github` target with no real token, in every build: skipped when off or naming only `codex`, a failure naming `LOOP_PLAYGROUND_GITHUB_TOKEN` when it is unset or blank, a token with whitespace refused without showing it) and `GitHubRealSmokeTest.kt` (the real playground run, skipped unless enabled).

### Codex exit run (m3-28)

The M3 exit for the Codex runner, 2026-11-17. bl-63 was worked by the `codex-app-server` runner on the `files` tracker in Loop mode, from `WORKFLOW.codex.md`, a local file kept out of the repository: a copy of `WORKFLOW.md` with the Codex runner, `required_labels: [runner-codex]`, one agent, the `context` limits of m3-27 (soft 0.20, hard 0.21) and the window `openai/gpt-6.1-sol: 475000`. Build 5591d3d2e271, codex-cli 0.167.0, a ChatGPT Plus login, `codex app-server -c model=gpt-6.1-sol`. Attempt b9d9e179 of run a4e6b030 worked it in one turn of 250 s, commented with the `comment` client tool and closed with `submit_result`. Loop verified it (`diff_policy`, the full build in 23 minutes, the two acceptance commands), the operator approved it with `loop approve bl-63 --workflow WORKFLOW.codex.md`, and Loop merged it as pull request #113 (merge commit b8ee14b) after two merge checks, since `main` moved twice. What the run taught:

- **Occupancy.** One session, peak 66,436 tokens, 0.1399 of the 475,000 token window, no rotation. The window is the `modelContextWindow` codex reports in `thread/tokenUsage/updated`. The runner never reads it, so a workflow names it under `context.windows`, else the sessions get no fraction and never rotate (m3-23). Since m4-02 the window of `openai/gpt-6.1-sol` ships in the defaults, see [default limits and unknown windows](#default-limits-and-unknown-windows-extension-m4-02).
- **Spend.** 1,554,606 tokens: 65,342 input, 1,483,392 cache read, 5,872 output, and no cost, since Codex reports none. The rate limit notices ended at 3 percent of the 5 hour window. During a turn `loop status` shows the cumulative `tokens`, which grows by the whole cached context on every request, so it is no reading of occupancy. The peak per session is in `loop show <attempt> --cost` and the ledger.
- **Selecting the runner and the model.** `runner.kind: codex-app-server`, with `-c model=<model>` in `runners.codex.command`, since the runner sends no model (bl-141). A second workflow file next to `WORKFLOW.md` works: the hooks only read `dirname "$LOOP_WORKFLOW"`, and the project id is a hash of the workflow path, so its run file, ledger and status are its own, while the store, the tracker lock, `./workspaces` and the `loop/<id>` branches are shared. A label of its own keeps the two workflows off each other's issues. Every command then takes `--workflow WORKFLOW.codex.md`, since the default is `WORKFLOW.md`.
- **The prompt.** Codex gets the client tools `move`, `comment` and `submit_result`, not `mcp__loop__*`, so the copy names them. The agent closed with `submit_result` and Loop moved the result to review.
- **Delivery.** The auto merge fell back for two reasons. The auto merge `ci` condition reads the Actions runs of the merged head even under `merge.ci: none`, and Actions refused every job for billing that day (bl-146). And the merge risk reviewer exists only for the claude-code runner, so a Codex result never merges on its own (bl-145). The operator ran the merge risk review on Sonnet and its Fable 5.2 recheck by hand: band medium, from the safety code floor of `GitPush` and `WorkspaceGit`, and low findings only (bl-148).
- **The model in the ledger.** The turn lines read `"model": null`, as for the claude-code runner without a configured model, though the Codex thread named `openai/gpt-6.1-sol`. Since bl-147 a line holds the model the runtime reported, see [the model in the ledger](#the-model-in-the-ledger-extension-bl-147).
- **Compared with the claude-code runner.** On the same kind of issue, a small fix from Loop's own backlog in Loop mode, worked in one turn: the claude-code runner spent 3.55M tokens in 10 minutes on bl-19 (2.00 US dollars) and 7.21M in 14 minutes on bl-108 (3.45 US dollars); Codex spent 1.55M in 4 minutes on bl-63, with no cost reported. Those claude-code attempts ran before occupancy was recorded, so their peaks are not in the ledger. The occupancy comparison at the same limits is m3-27's run of bl-136. Verification and the merge check treated both runners alike: the verdict comes from the checks, never from the runner.

### Claude Code exit runs (m3-27)

The M3 exit for the claude-code runner, 2026-11-18, in two runs at the same limits: `context.soft_limit` 0.20 (the default), `context.hard_limit` 0.21, the window `anthropic/claude-opus-5-5: 1000000`, a rotation block and `loop.session_brief` in the prompt, and a commit after each passing step. The first run worked Loop's own bl-136 and bl-132 in Loop mode on the `files` tracker, build e010f034a940; the second run worked a pilot app issue on the GitHub tracker from the pilot app's `WORKFLOW.md`, build da123ea8d2b3. The traces are in the m3-27 comments. What the runs taught:

- **A rotation in practice.** bl-132's first session crossed the hard limit at 210,251 tokens (0.2103): Loop interrupted the turn, recorded `turn_completed` `interrupted=hard_limit` and `session_rotated` `reason=hard_limit`, and the second session went on from the workspace, peaking at 0.0704. The interrupt, the rotation prompt and the brief worked as m3-12 and m3-10 describe. The request that crosses 0.21 lands under 0.25 only because the largest jump between two requests stayed near 27k tokens; the default hard limit of 0.25 cannot keep that promise (bl-144).
- **The soft limit never fired.** It is checked at the end of a turn only when the attempt goes on, and every agent ended its last turn by handing off (a move to review, `submit_result`), which ends the attempt. In both runs the hard limit was the only guard that could act, so a workflow that wants the 25 percent rule sets the hard limit below 0.25 and keeps the soft limit as the early, cheap rotation for multi turn attempts.
- **Peaks.** No session went above 25 percent: bl-136 0.1844 in one turn, bl-132 0.2103 then 0.0704 and 0.0538, the second pilot app issue 0.1228 in attempt 1 and 0.0848 in attempt 2. Occupancy measures the main session only. On the pilot app the work runs in subagents (the app's expert subagent and its plan, code and spec reviewers), each in its own window, which Loop does not see, so the 25 percent figure holds for the main session and says nothing of the subagents (the subagent gap of m3-08). Their spend does count: the second pilot app issue used 5.97M tokens (4.25 US dollars) in attempt 1 and 1.66M (1.23 US dollars) in attempt 2.
- **A background wake is a turn.** The pilot app agent ends its turn while a subagent or a benchmark run works in the background, and the wake that reports back starts the next turn (m3-07). Attempt 1 used 6 of its 10 turns in 12 minutes, turns 2 to 5 lasting 10 to 50 seconds each. `agent.max_turns` therefore counts wakes, not units of work, and a workflow whose agent delegates needs a far higher cap than one turn per step (bl-155).
- **Sleep ends an attempt.** The Mac went to idle sleep on battery at 10:10Z during the mutation benchmark run; the turn failed at 10:21Z with "Can't reach the API server (ENOTFOUND)", and the error retry due 10 seconds later started at 12:48Z, after the wake. With `retries.max_attempts: 2` that left one attempt. Nothing in `loop run` holds the machine awake (bl-156). Since m4-08 such a failure takes no attempt, see [network loss](#network-loss-extension-m4-08), and since m4-09 `loop run` keeps the Mac awake while work runs.
- **A reload applies to the next attempt only.** The two workflow edits made during the pilot app run (a higher `max_turns`, a continuation text) were reloaded at 12:51Z, three minutes after attempt 2 had started on the old config. As [workflow reload](#workflow-reload-clarification-of-62-m1-15) says, an attempt keeps the workflow it started with.
- **Continuation on a reused workspace.** Attempt 2 of the second pilot app issue read the progress file the skill keeps under the gitignored `.scratch/`, kept the red mutation run attempt 1 had recorded, and ran the three clean benchmark runs and a fourth, watched one in one turn of 29 minutes. The skill's progress file did the work of a session brief across attempts; Loop's own brief added 307 tokens.
- **The merge check on an up to date head.** The pilot app's base had not moved, so the merge check ended `up_to_date` and reran only `diff_policy` (step 3 of the merge check), not the unit suite. The pilot app's `WORKFLOW.md` comment saying the merge check reruns the unit suite holds only when the base moved.
- **Delivery.** Both Loop issues waited for `loop approve`: the auto merge `ci` condition reads Actions runs even under `merge.ci: none`, and Actions refused every job for billing (bl-146). The pilot app delivers with `policy: human`, so its pull request merged after `loop approve`, as designed.

### The model in the ledger (extension, bl-147)

Found in the m3-28 Codex exit run: every turn line of a `codex-app-server` attempt read `"model": null`, though the `thread/start` answer named `openai/gpt-6.1-sol`, and so did the claude-code lines of a `WORKFLOW.md` that names no model. The ledger could not say which model a turn ran on, which a runner comparison (M8), the cost per model and `loop report` (m4-38) need. The decisions:

- **The source.** The runners already name the session's model on `context_usage`: Claude Code from the newest `system` `init` line ([m3-08](#claude-code-context-occupancy-extension-m3-08)), Codex from the `thread/start` answer, switched by `model/rerouted` ([m3-23](#codex-runner-context-occupancy-and-rotation-m3-23)). So the session's model reaches Loop through those reports, and the plugin API does not change.
- **A turn line.** `model` is the model of the latest `context_usage` report of the turn's session that named one, kept across the session's turns and dropped at a new session, as the session's occupancy is. A turn with no such report takes the runner's configured model (`AgentRunner.model`), null when it has none.
- **An attempt row.** `model` is the latest model a `context_usage` report of the attempt named, else the configured one. After a reroute or a rotation onto another model the row and some of its lines can differ, so `model` is the one setup key that can change between the lines of one attempt.
- **The form.** A reported model is written as reported, the `context.windows` key `<provider>/<model>`, for example `openai/gpt-6.1-sol` or `anthropic/claude-opus-5-5`, so the ledger, the `context_usage` event and the window lookup agree. The configured fallback is written as configured, with no provider added: Loop cannot tell which provider and model an alias such as `opus` resolves to, and guessing would mix two models under one name. A reader tells the two apart by the slash.
- **Older lines.** Lines and rows written before bl-147 keep the configured model, or null.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/OrchestratorLedgerTest.kt` (the `bl-147` tests: a Codex turn and a claude-code turn with no configured model holding the reported model on the line and the row, a turn with no report taking its session's model, a new session with no report or an unnamed one taking the configured model, an attempt with no report naming null), and end to end, `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/CodexRotationRunTest.kt` (each line of both threads with the model of the `thread/start` answer) and `ClaudeCodeRunCommandTest.kt` (each line of a rotated attempt with the model of its session's `init` line), both with workflows that name no model.

### Default limits and unknown windows (extension, m4-02)

Stories 101 and 102, plan 8.2 and 8.3, carrying bl-144, bl-125 and the window part of bl-138. The default hard limit keeps the 25 percent rule, a session whose model has no window is recorded and shown, and the pinned Codex model's window ships. The decisions:

- **The default hard limit is 0.21 (bl-144).** The hard limit fires on the first `context_usage` at or above it (`OccupancyMeter.crossing`), so a session always peaks at or above the limit, by up to one request. With the old default of 0.25 a session that reached the hard limit was over 25 percent by construction. Rebuilt from 171 attempts, the jump between two main session requests is about 1.3k tokens at the median, 9.7k at the 99th percentile and 27.1k at most. At 0.21 a 1,000,000 token window leaves 40,000 tokens between the hard limit and 25 percent, room for the largest jump seen. 0.21 is the value the M3 exit runs used ([Claude Code exit runs](#claude-code-exit-runs-m3-27)), and soule confirmed it on 2026-11-18.
- **The rule holds within one request.** A session stays under 25 percent of its window when one request adds less than the gap between the hard limit and 25 percent: 40,000 tokens on a 1,000,000 token window, about 19,000 (118,750 minus 99,750) on the 475,000 token Codex window, which is below the largest jump seen. On that window a jump over 19,000 tokens can still carry a session over 25 percent before the interrupt.
- **The soft limit stays 0.20.** It is the early rotation at a turn's end for multi turn attempts, and `soft_limit <= hard_limit` still holds with the defaults. The cost is known and accepted (soule, 2026-11-18): on the 475,000 token window the gap between the two limits is 4,750 tokens, below the 9.7k jump of the 99th percentile, so some Codex rotations become hard interrupts in the middle of a turn instead of soft rotations at a turn's end. On a 1,000,000 token window the gap is 10,000 tokens.
- **The pinned Codex window ships (bl-138, window part).** `ContextConfig.DEFAULT_WINDOWS` holds `openai/gpt-6.1-sol: 475000` beside `anthropic/claude-opus-5-5: 1000000`, the `modelContextWindow` codex reported in the [Codex exit run](#codex-exit-run-m3-28). A Codex session on that model gets a fraction and rotates with no `context.windows` in the workflow. Reading `modelContextWindow` from the runner instead of a shipped entry is left out.
- **`context_window_unknown` (bl-125).** A model that matches no `context.windows` key gives tokens with no fraction, so no soft or hard limit holds for its session. Before m4-02 that was one operator log warning per process, never cleared, and no event, so `loop trace` and `loop show` could not say why the fraction was missing. Now, in Loop mode with a runner that declares occupancy, the first `context_usage` of such a model in an attempt records `context_window_unknown` with `model` (absent when the runner named none) and `runner` (the workflow's `runner.kind`), at the report, on the turn's session. It is recorded once per attempt and model: a second attempt records it again, and a reroute onto another model with no window records it for that model. The operator log warning `event=context_window` follows the same rule, once per attempt and model, outside Loop mode too. The window logic lives in `ContextWindows`, beside `OccupancyMeter`, one instance per attempt.
- **Where it shows.** `loop trace` prints it in the attempt's timeline, `context_window_unknown model=openai/gpt-unknown-9 runner=codex-app-server`, and the human log writes `runs <model>, which has no window in context.windows, so no context limit holds for its session`. `loop status` shows a running session with no window as `window=none`, from its first report on, then a line under it, indented to the issue column: `no window for <model> in context.windows, so the soft and hard limits do not hold for this session` (`the runner named no model` when it named none). A session whose latest report has a window, after a reroute, shows its window and fraction as before. `loop show --cost` already printed `window unknown`.
- **The prompt limit of a runner with no known model.** It takes the smallest window of `context.windows` ([m3-13](#prompt-tokens-and-prompt-limit-extension-m3-13)), so with the Codex window shipped it is 23,750 tokens (0.05 of 475,000) instead of 50,000. This applies to the `command` runner and to a claude-code runner with no `runners.claude-code.model`, as Loop's own `WORKFLOW.md`. The rule is kept: it is the conservative one, and a workflow that wants the larger limit names its model or adds its window.
- **Release note.** A workflow that sets `context.soft_limit` above 0.21 and at most 0.25 with no `context.hard_limit` passed preflight before m4-02 and is now refused with `invalid_config`: `context.soft_limit (0.23) must not be above the default context.hard_limit (0.21): set context.hard_limit or lower context.soft_limit`. With `hard_limit` set, the old message stays. A workflow with no `context.windows` entry for its claude-code model and no `runners.claude-code.model` gets the lower prompt limit above.
- **Out of scope.** The other bl-138 findings (the token estimate, the reviewer window, the Codex `stop()`, an interrupt during a tool call, `MODEL_REROUTED` and `CodexRotationRunTest`) stay in their own issues.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workflow/ContextConfigTest.kt` (the `m4-02` tests: the new defaults, a soft limit of 0.23 with no hard limit refused by the loader and by preflight naming the default, the same soft limit passing with a hard limit set), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/ContextWindowsTest.kt` (the window logic, `context_window_unknown` once per attempt over two attempts with the model and the runner, none outside Loop mode or without occupancy, a Codex session on the pinned model rotating at the soft limit and interrupted at 0.21 with no `context.windows`), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/StatusCommandTest.kt` (`m4-02 status shows a running session whose model has no window as such, before and after its first turn ends`), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/TraceCommandTest.kt` (`m4-02 context_window_unknown shows the model and the runner in each attempt`).

### Wake turns (extension, m4-03)

Story 103, plan 8.4, carrying bl-155, bl-114 (its turn option) and the wake findings of bl-122. An agent that ends its turn while a subagent or a long command works in the background is woken by Claude Code when the task ends, and the next turn reads that wake first (m3-07). Before m4-03 each wake took one of the attempt's `agent.max_turns`: attempt 10c2f759 of the second pilot app issue used 6 of its 10 turns in 12 minutes, turns 2 to 5 lasting 10 to 50 seconds and holding one subagent report each. The decisions:

- **What a wake turn is (bl-170, option E, soule, 2026-11-21).** A turn that reads the `task_notification` line (`type` `system`, `subtype` `task_notification`) of a background task run whose `task_started` line an earlier turn of the same agent session read. Runs are matched by `task_id` and `run_id`, not by task: a restarted task keeps its `task_id` with a new `run_id`, as task `id_089` of the recording does. Where the turn reads the line does not matter: drained while idle before Loop's input, inside a wake's own `init` and `result`, or folded by Claude Code into the turn's answer. The rule depends neither on input timing nor on the `origin` field, and it does not change where a turn ends. Two notifications in one turn make one wake turn, and a notification of a run started in the same turn makes none.
- **The runner says it.** A new runner event, `WakeTurn(sessionId, turnId)` in `plugin-api`, sent at most once per turn, right after the first notification line that makes it one, before the terminal event. An event rather than a field of the terminal event, so a turn cut by a budget or interrupted at the hard limit, whose terminal event the orchestrator may never read, is still known as a wake turn, and the other runners change nothing. A runner that cannot tell never sends it: the Codex runner sends none (out of this issue). The Claude Code runner keeps, per agent session, the turn that read each run's `task_started` line; a rotation opens a new session, and the background runs of the old process die with it.
- **A wake turn is free.** It never counts against `agent.max_turns`, even when it then does real work: `turn_timeout_ms` bounds its length and `agent.max_wake_turns` their number. The counting lives in `TurnCount` (`loop.core.orchestrator`); `Orchestrator.kt` only calls it. The `turn` number in events and ledger lines stays the turn's place in the attempt, so after the six turns of attempt 10c2f759, of which 2, 3, 4 and 6 are wake turns, 2 of 10 turns are used and the continuation guidance of turn 7 reads `This is turn 3 of 10.`: the guidance names the next counted turn.
- **`agent.max_wake_turns`.** A positive integer under `agent:`, default 30 (soule, 2026-11-18); `agent.env` is the precedent for a Loop key in that section. Below 1, or not an integer, it fails preflight with `invalid_config`: `agent.max_wake_turns must be a positive integer, got 0`. Reaching it ends the attempt normally with exit reason `max_wake_turns`, like `max_turns`, so a chatty background task cannot keep an attempt alive forever; a refutation in the last turn turns it into a `refuted` failure as it does `max_turns` (m2-06). The human log reads `it used all its wake turns`. Budgets still apply.
- **Usage, ledger, events and the soft limit.** A wake turn's usage counts in the attempt totals and the budgets like any turn's. Its `turn_completed` or `turn_failed` carries `wake` = `true`, shown by `loop trace` and in the human log (`turn 2 finished, a wake turn`). Each ledger turn line has a `wake` key, `true` for a wake turn and `false` otherwise. The soft limit is checked at its end like at any turn's end, so a wake turn can rotate the session.
- **A turn stays open no longer (bl-114).** Its option "keep a turn open while a background task is pending" is not taken: one turn would then last as long as the subagent and defeat the turn timeout. Its option "let `stop()` report usage read while idle" landed in bl-132; the wake after the last turn is m4-04.
- **No wake from a subagent line (bl-122).** A subagent's `assistant` line (with a `parent_tool_use_id`) read while idle no longer marks the process as running a wake. Before m4-03 it did, so the next turn waited for a `result` that only a wake writes, up to its turn timeout. An `init` or a main session `assistant` line read while idle still marks one. Without that trigger, a wake's `result` read after the turn's input could end the turn early, the known limit under [Lines written while idle](#claude-code-long-lived-process-extension-of-103-m3-07). A `result` with `origin.kind` `task-notification`, Claude Code's own marker of a wake run, no longer ends the turn, unless it comes after an interrupt request, which always ends it. On attempt 10c2f759 turns 2 and 4 hold such a wake (lines 199 to 209 and 291 to 302), and each still ends where the recording says.
- **The idle queue is bounded (bl-122).** Between turns nothing reads the process, for example during a long verification, and its event queue was unlimited. While the process is idle it now queues at most 1,000 lines no turn needs, a subagent's messages or tool results; past that bound the newest such lines are dropped. Every line a later turn needs is always kept: `result` lines (their usage), `init` lines, `task_started` and `task_notification` lines, main session `assistant` lines (their occupancy) and Loop's own `loop:` stderr lines. The next turn starts with one stderr line, `loop: warning: the claude-code process wrote more than 1000 lines while idle, <n> of them dropped, its usage and result lines kept`. The newest lines are dropped rather than the oldest so that the queue stays a plain channel fed by the launch collector. A turn's own lines are never dropped.
- **Out of scope.** Usage after the stop grace (m4-04), the other bl-122 findings (a turn cancelled during the wake wait, `leftGroups` synchronisation, pid reuse at retire) and wakes for the Codex runner.
