Tests: `plugins/runner-claude-code/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/claudecode/WakeTurnTest.kt` (on the `background_wake` fixture with the `task_started` and `task_notification` lines Claude Code writes: a notification read while idle, inside a wake's own `init` and `result`, or folded into the answer makes a wake turn, a run started in the same turn and a restarted task decided by its `run_id`, two notifications making one wake turn, a subagent line read while idle that does not hold the next turn, a wake's result read after the input that does not end the turn, a result after an interrupt that does; and the replay of `wake_turns_10c2f759.jsonl` turn by turn as `wake_turns_10c2f759.turns.json` delimits it: turns 2, 3, 4 and 6 are wake turns, 1 and 5 are not), `ClaudeCodeRunnerTest` (the bounded idle queue keeping every usage and `result` line, a turn's own lines never dropped, each usage field and the cost of a resume after a wake), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/TurnCountTest.kt`, `OrchestratorWakeTurnsTest.kt` (wakes scripted in the fake runner: `max_turns` counting only ordinary turns, `max_wake_turns`, the 10c2f759 turns, a wake turn's usage and budget, the soft limit at its end), `JsonlTurnLedgerTest`, `PreflightTest` and `WorkflowConfigValidationTest`.

### Usage at the stop (extension, m4-04)

Story 104, carrying findings 1 to 4 of bl-152, from the reviews of bl-132. Since bl-132 the stop of a rotation read the usage of a wake that ended while the session was idle, but three gaps were left: a wake still running when the stop grace ended was killed and its usage lost with no trace, the stop at the attempt's end read a wake and discarded it, and the usage read at a stop no turn followed was in the attempt totals but in no ledger turn line. The decisions:

- **One report callback.** `AgentSession.stop(usage)` becomes `stop(report: suspend (RunnerEvent) -> Unit)`. It gives what the session read while it stopped, in the order it read it: `RunnerEvent.Usage` reports, absolute for the session like a turn's, and the new `RunnerEvent.StopGraceExpired(sessionId, turnId, graceMs, wake, wakeMessages)`, sent only through a stop. Reports come before a failed stop throws. A runner that reads nothing at its stop reports nothing: the default calls `stop()`.
- **Every stop passes it.** The stop of a rotation and the stop at the attempt's end, whatever the end (review, `max_turns`, a failure, a cancel), go through `StopUsage` (`loop.core.orchestrator`), beside `SessionSpend`; `Orchestrator.kt` only calls it. Its usage is recorded as `usage` events and counts in the spend, the budgets and the ledger, and a failed stop is logged and recorded as `session_stop_failed` (m3-07) after what it reported.
- **The stop grace (Claude Code).** The runner gives the process 5 seconds to exit after it closes stdin. While it reads the process then, a main session `init` or `assistant` line marks a running wake and keeps the usage of its model messages, one per message id as for the turn's estimate, and a `result` line covers them and clears the mark. When the grace ends with the process still there, the usage of the wake's messages is added to the session's and reported, then `StopGraceExpired`, and the process group is killed. An `assistant` line carries no cost, so only the tokens of those messages count; what the runtime had not written yet is lost.
- **`stop_grace_expired`.** The orchestrator records it after that usage, with `grace_ms` (5000), `wake` (`true` when a wake was running), `wake_messages` (how many of its messages counted) and the attempt's usage known after them, `attempt_tokens` and `total_cost_usd`, absolute as on `usage`; a field Loop does not know is absent. `loop trace` prints `stop_grace_expired grace_ms=5000 wake=true wake_messages=2 attempt_tokens=9000 total_cost_usd=0.09` in the attempt's timeline. The operator log adds a warn line with `event=session`, `outcome=failed` and `grace_ms=5000`. A process that exits within the grace records none.
- **The ledger.** Usage a rotation's stop read counts in the next turn's share, as since bl-132. Usage a stop read with no turn after it, at the attempt's end or at a rotation ended by a failed stop or a budget, gets a `stop` line: a copy of the attempt's last turn line with `outcome` = `stop`, the stop's share of the tokens and the cost, the attempt's totals, the wall time since the last line, neither `interrupted` nor a wake. The turn lines of an attempt then sum to its total. An attempt with no turn line writes none.
- **A budget passed at the stop.** At a rotation, a budget the stop's usage went past ends the attempt with `budget` instead of the new session, and wins over a failed stop: the attempt ends on the budget and the stop still recorded `session_stop_failed` (soule, in the issue). At the attempt's end, a budget the last stop's usage went past replaces the end the attempt reached with the budget end, `budget_reached` and the `budget` park, even when that stop failed. Two ends keep theirs: an end that already parks the issue keeps its own reason, and a `waiting_approval` end keeps its wait, so a verified result waiting for a human is never lost.
- **Out of scope.** The rest of bl-152 stays in the backlog: the rotated issue re-read outside a guard, the re-read fallback copy, and the `completeIssue` KDoc. They landed in [The rotated issue](#the-rotated-issue-extension-bl-152).

Tests: `plugins/runner-claude-code/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/claudecode/ClaudeCodeRunnerTest.kt` (the `m4-04` tests: a wake still running when the stop grace ends reports the usage its lines read, then `StopGraceExpired`; a process with no wake that outlives the grace reports `StopGraceExpired` alone, and a wake's `result` clears the mark), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/StopUsageTest.kt` (the order of the reports, a failed stop after usage, the rotation end, the attempt end, the `stop_grace_expired` data, the `stop` line), `OrchestratorStopUsageTest.kt` (wakes scripted in the fake runner: the grace at the attempt's end and at a rotation, a wake after a review end and after a `max_turns` end, a budget passed at the last stop, a stop that throws after usage past a budget at the attempt's end and at a rotation, the turn lines summing to the attempt total) and, in `cli`, `TraceCommandTest` (the `m4-04` test: the `stop_grace_expired` line).

### The rotated issue (extension, bl-152)

The rest of bl-152, the low findings of the bl-132 reviews that m4-04 left. The decisions:

- **A pending rotation is always recorded.** A rotation is pending from the old session's stop to the new session's first turn: the read by id and the completion of the issue, the brief and the render run in between, up to three GitHub requests. Before bl-152 a `loop stop` or a reconciliation stop that cancelled the attempt there lost `session_rotated` from the trace. The session's end, which runs on every end of the attempt, cancels included, now records a rotation still pending, without the new session's ids, before it stops the session. `session_rotated` then comes before `attempt_finished`, as on a template error (bl-132).
- **The fallback is the turn end's issue.** The rotated prompt falls back to the issue the turn end's refresh read, moments before the rotation, not the dispatch copy: that read decided the issue was still eligible, so it is the freshest copy Loop has. It is the tracker's issue as read, not completed (bl-06): on the `github` tracker a fallback prompt shows no `## Comments`. A failed read logs one warning, `event=rotation_issue`, no longer a second `event=tracker` line too.
- **What stays.** An issue that becomes ineligible between the turn end's read and the read at the rotation still gets its new session; reconciliation or the next turn end stops it. A failed refresh only warns, the documented fallback.
- **The contract.** `AgentRunnerContract` checks `stop(report)`: after one turn of a session, its stop reports only `Usage` and `StopGraceExpired`, no `Usage` when the runner declares none, each `Usage` of the session's thread and never below the last turn's, and at most one `StopGraceExpired`, last.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/OrchestratorRotationTest.kt` (the `bl-132` test of a failed read whose fallback, the issue the turn end read, is still the dispatched one; the `bl-152` tests: a failed read and a failed completion at the rotation, each falling back to the issue the turn end read with one warning; a `loop stop` and a reconciliation stop during the read, each keeping `session_rotated` before `attempt_finished`) and `plugin-api/src/test/kotlin/loop/api/AgentRunnerContractCapabilitiesTest.kt` (the stop case on an honest runner and on runners whose stop reports a turn event, usage below the last turn's, usage of another thread, or usage after `StopGraceExpired`).

### The reviewer apart from the runner (extension, m4-06)

Story 106, carrying bl-145 and bl-100. Until m4-06 `riskReviewerFor` built a reviewer only under `runner.kind: claude-code`, so a Codex result always fell back with "this Loop has no risk reviewer for its runner" and waited for `loop approve` (bl-63). The decisions:

- **How the reviewer is chosen.** The reviewer is a concern apart from the runner that did the work: the workflow names its model in `kinds.code.delivery.risk.reviewer`, and Loop runs it through the Claude Code command whatever `runner.kind` is, `codex-app-server` and `command` included. The command is the one of `runners.claude-code`: under `runner.kind: claude-code` its `command`, which the loader checks; under any other runner `runners.claude-code.command` when it is set and not blank, else `claude`. The section is not required then and nothing else of it is read. This is bl-145's first option, confirmed by soule on 2026-11-18. A Codex reviewer and a reviewer section of its own are not built.
- **Where the command is looked up.** A bare name is looked up in the folders of the host `PATH` and the reviewer starts the absolute path found, so the check and the run agree; an absolute path must be an executable file; a relative path with a slash resolves against the issue workspace, which does not exist at startup, so it is kept as written.
- **No reviewer can run.** When the command cannot be found, `riskReviewerFor` gives no reviewer and the gate falls back as before, the comment reading `Merge risk: not assessed, this Loop cannot run its merge risk reviewer, so the reviewer setup, not the result, is why the merge waits`. Under `policy: auto` with `pull_request.merge` and `risk.reviewer`, `loop run` warns once at startup: `loop run: warning: kinds.code.delivery.risk.reviewer is claude-sonnet-5-5, but its command claude cannot be found, so no merge risk reviewer can run and every result waits for loop approve. Install Claude Code or set runners.claude-code.command.` A command that is found but fails to start or crashes falls back as any reviewer crash.
- **Never in the operator's checkout (bl-100).** During the run of 2026-11-16 a merge risk reviewer started by hand ran `git checkout` in the operator's main checkout, which holds the live `WORKFLOW.md`. Loop's own reviewer runs with the issue workspace as its directory and without Bash; tests now pin both, so a change that moves it to the project root or gives it Bash fails. The `merge-risk` skill states the same rule for a reviewer started by hand: read only, never `git checkout`, `git switch`, `git reset` or `git stash` in the operator's checkout; read with `git diff` and `git show <ref>:<path>`, or in a separate `git worktree` of the pull request head.
- **Out of scope.** A Codex native reviewer, a reviewer section in the workflow and the merge risk scoring itself. Since bl-214 a Codex model in `risk.reviewer` gets a Codex reviewer, see [Codex merge risk reviewer](#codex-merge-risk-reviewer-extension-bl-214).

Tests: `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/DeliveryExecutorsTest.kt` (the `m4-06` tests: under `codex-app-server` the reviewer is Claude Code's, found on the host `PATH` and run with the model of `risk.reviewer` in the workspace; `runners.claude-code.command` when set; the `command` and `claude-code` runners; no reviewer when the command cannot be found), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/RiskReviewerWarningTest.kt` (the startup warning, its conditions and `loop run` printing it), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/AutoMergeOrchestratorTest.kt` (a Codex result with every condition held and a low band from a fake reviewer auto merges; and `a host with no reviewer falls back`, now naming the reviewer setup), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/merge/MergesTest.kt` (the fallback reason) and `plugins/runner-claude-code/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/claudecode/ClaudeCodeRiskReviewerTest.kt` (the `bl-100` tests: the directory is the issue workspace, never the project root or Loop's own directory, and Bash stays among the disallowed tools).

### Codex merge risk reviewer (extension, bl-214)

The Codex twin of bl-205. Since m4-06 every merge risk review ran through Claude Code, so a machine that runs only Codex could not use `policy: auto` with merge without installing Claude Code. The decisions:

- **The model picks the runtime.** `RiskReviewerRuntime.of` in `cli` reads the model of `kinds.code.delivery.risk.reviewer`, trimmed and in any case: a name that starts with `gpt-` or `codex-`, or an `o` series name such as `o3` or `o4-mini`, is a Codex model, and `riskReviewerFor` builds `CodexRiskReviewer`. Every other name, aliases such as `sonnet` included, keeps the Claude Code reviewer of m4-06, whatever `runner.kind` is. It is the one place the choice is made, so bl-205 adds Gemini there. No reviewer section and no runtime key were added: the model already names the runtime.
- **Its settings are `runners.codex`.** The section is read as the Codex runner reads it, with the holdout folder: `command` (default `codex app-server`), `read_timeout_ms` and `strict_mcp` apply; the model is the reviewer's and the turn timeout is `risk.timeout_ms`. No section is the defaults. The first word of the command is looked up on the host `PATH` as m4-06 looks up `claude`, and the reviewer starts the absolute path found, the other words kept, so the check and the run agree. A section the runner refuses gives no reviewer.
- **One turn of the runner.** `CodexRiskReviewer` runs one turn of `CodexAppServerRunner` in the issue workspace, so the startup, the version check, strict MCP, the exec policy rules check and the agent env policy are the runner's. Its attempt env is empty, so no Loop tool is declared.
- **Read only.** The settings' `readOnly`, which is not a profile key, starts the thread on the `read-only` sandbox (`thread/start`) and the `readOnly` policy with no network (`turn/start`), approval policy `untrusted`, and declines every command and file change approval, in both forms, with the reason `the merge risk reviewer runs read only` when no other rule refuses it first. Declining every command is needed: the recorded `approval.jsonl` shows an accepted command writing a file under the read-only sandbox. Codex still runs the commands it holds as known safe (`cat`, `rg`, `sed -n`, `git diff`, `git show`) without asking, inside the sandbox, which is all a reviewer reads with.
- **Holdout.** The runner's holdout rules stay on, so a request that names the holdout folder is declined as touching it, and every other request is declined anyway. Not covered, the gap of m3-20: a known safe command that reads the holdout folder runs without a request.
- **The answer.** The text of the turn's last completed `agentMessage` item, the one of phase `final_answer` when there is one, with the session's tokens from `thread/tokenUsage/updated` and no cost, which Codex does not report. A failed turn, a user input request, a launch failure or a turn with no agent message is a crash; a turn past `risk.timeout_ms` is a timeout and its app server is stopped. The orchestrator parses the answer as any reviewer's.
- **The warning.** Under `policy: auto` with merge, a Codex reviewer whose program cannot be found warns at startup with `... but its command codex cannot be found, so no merge risk reviewer can run and every result waits for loop approve. Install Codex or set runners.codex.command`; one whose `runners.codex` cannot be read warns with the reason.
- **Out of scope.** The Gemini reviewer (bl-205), a model list from the runtimes, and a read deny that holds without an approval request.

Tests: `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/CodexRiskReviewerTest.kt` (the answer and tokens, the read-only sandbox, model, prompt and workspace of the thread, every command, file change and older approval declined, a holdout read declined, a failed turn, no answer, the timeout, a command that cannot start, `commandOf`), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/RiskReviewerRuntimeTest.kt` (the model names), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/DeliveryExecutorsTest.kt` (the `bl-214` tests: a Codex model gets the Codex reviewer found on the host `PATH` and run in the workspace, `runners.codex.command` with its other words, a Claude model keeps Claude Code under `codex-app-server`, no reviewer when codex cannot be found) and `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/RiskReviewerWarningTest.kt` (the Codex warning, none when codex is found, and `loop run` printing it).

### Network loss (extension, m4-08)

Story 108, first half, carrying bl-123 and the sleep decision of bl-156. On 2026-11-16 between 12:32 and 12:48 UTC the machine lost its connection, and Claude Code ended the turn with `API Error: Can't reach the API server (...) check your internet or DNS (ENOTFOUND)`. Loop recorded an ordinary `turn_failed` and failed the attempt of both running issues, bl-117 after 7.3M tokens and 3.78 US dollars of work, m3-07 after 0.41 US dollars. A lost network says nothing about the issue, the same as an authentication failure, so it now goes through the [runner unavailable hold](#runner-unavailable-hold-extension-bl-39). The decisions:

- **Claude Code.** An error `result` line (`is_error` set) is `runner_unavailable` when its `api_error` is `connection_failed`, the typed field Claude Code writes when it gets no answer from the API, or when its text holds `ENOTFOUND`, `EAI_AGAIN`, `ECONNREFUSED`, `ECONNRESET`, `ETIMEDOUT`, `Can't reach the API server` or `API Error: Connection error`. As in bl-39, the same words in the agent's answer, a tool output or an ordinary error result never match. The recorded result of bl-123 is the fixture `network_enotfound.jsonl`, and the last line of `wake_turns_10c2f759.jsonl`, the failure after the sleep below, is pinned the same way.
- **Codex.** A `turn/completed` with status `failed` is `runner_unavailable` when its error's `codexErrorInfo` is a transport failure with no HTTP answer: `httpConnectionFailed`, `responseStreamConnectionFailed`, `responseStreamDisconnected` or `responseTooManyFailedAttempts` with no `httpStatusCode`. With a status code the server answered, so the failure stays ordinary, as do every other `codexErrorInfo` but `modelNotFound` (bl-141, see [refused Codex model](#refused-codex-model-extension-bl-141)), a turn with no typed error and an interrupted turn. Before m4-08 the Codex runner set no category at all. Its access failures (`unauthorized`, `usageLimitExceeded`, `rateLimitExceeded`, `serverOverloaded`) are not mapped here.
- **The hold is bl-39's.** The failure takes no `max_attempts` slot, records `runner_hold_started`, and the issue is requeued for the probe; the orchestrator reads only the category, so nothing in it changed. The backoff from 1 minute to 15 suits a network that comes back after minutes or hours, and a successful turn lifts the hold.
- **The next attempt starts fresh.** A new attempt never resumes the session of the one before: the probe, and any later retry, opens a new session, and since it is a retry its prompt gets the [session brief](#session-brief-extension-m3-10), as after a rotation. The work in the workspace and the progress file stay. Since m4-46 the Claude Code session is resumed when its last occupancy was below the soft limit, see [Resume after a network hold](#resume-after-a-network-hold-extension-m4-46).
- **Sleep rule, option (a) (bl-156, soule, 2026-11-18).** In the m3-27 run the Mac slept at 10:10Z, the turn failed at 10:21Z with `ENOTFOUND`, and the retry started at 12:48Z, after the wake, so the sleep spent one of two attempts. A failure after a sleep surfaces as a network error, because the machine wakes with no network yet and the connection the turn held is gone, so the classification above already makes it take no slot. No sleep detection is built: comparing the wall and monotonic clocks would add a second path for a failure the first one already covers, and would guess at a cause the runtime reports anyway. m4-09 keeps the Mac awake while work runs, which prevents most of these failures; this rule covers the ones it cannot, a lid close or a low battery. If a recorded sleep failure is ever not network shaped, a backlog issue reopens the question.
- **Out of scope.** The resume after the hold (m4-46), the sleep assertion (m4-09), sleep detection from the clocks, the wider resume question of bl-151, and a failed fetch of `main` on the next attempt, which already falls back to the last fetched `main` ([base fetch](#base-fetch-before-every-attempt-extension-bl-59)).

Tests: `plugins/runner-claude-code/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/claudecode/StreamJsonMapperTest.kt` (the `m4-08` tests: the recorded `ENOTFOUND` result of bl-123 and the recorded failure after the sleep of bl-156 are `runner_unavailable`, each network code and text in an error result, `api_error` `connection_failed` whatever the text, the same words outside an error result or in an ordinary error leave the category null), `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/CodexAppServerRunnerTest.kt` (`m4-08 a failed turn whose error is a transport failure with no HTTP answer is runner_unavailable`, and the null category of a transport failure the server answered, another typed error and an interrupted turn), and `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/OrchestratorRunnerHoldTest.kt` (`m4-08 a lost network holds the run, takes no slot, and the next attempt starts a fresh session with the brief`).

### Resume after a network hold (extension, m4-46)

Story 108, second half. After m4-08 the attempt that follows a network hold opens a new session, so the agent reads again what the lost session had already read: the tokens of a session at 15 percent of a 1,000,000 token window are spent twice. The resume is bounded as the M4 spec decided, since the wider question of bl-151 is deferred. The decisions:

- **The rule.** The next attempt of an issue continues the runtime session of its last attempt only when all of these hold: the [hold](#runner-unavailable-hold-extension-bl-39) took that attempt, its last turn failed as `runner_unavailable` and the hit was not the third one counted as an ordinary failure; its last session reported a runtime session id; that session's last `context_usage`, as a fraction of the window `context.windows` gives its model, is below `context.soft_limit`; and the runner kind is the same. The soft limit read is the next attempt's, after any reload. Anything else starts a fresh session with the session brief, as before m4-46: an ordinary failure, an occupancy at or above the soft limit, no occupancy report, an unknown window, a restart of `loop run` since (the choice is kept in memory, as the hold is) or a session whose rotation was pending, whose new session reported nothing yet. Each choice is used once: a second failure of the resumed attempt is decided again from its own last session.
- **A rotation never resumes.** A rotation opens a new session with `openSession`, always, including a rotation of a resumed session that crosses the soft limit. Only the first session of the attempt after a hold can be a resumed one.
- **The `plugin-api` member.** `AgentRunner.resumeSession(ctx, runtimeSessionId): AgentSession?`, with the runtime session id the earlier attempt's `SessionStarted` carried. Its default returns null, and Loop then opens the session with `openSession`, so a runner that knows nothing of it, a third party one included, compiles and runs as before. A runner may return a session only when it declares `continuation`; turn 1 of that session starts with `SessionStarted` under a new thread id like any session's. `AgentRunnerContract` checks both, and `AgentRunnerContractCapabilitiesTest` runs it with a runner that keeps the default and with one that resumes. A method on the runner rather than a field of `AttemptContext`, so Loop knows whether the session was resumed and no runner is handed an id it would silently ignore.
- **Claude Code.** `resumeSession` opens a session whose turn 1 spawns `claude -p --resume <runtime session id> ...`, after the stderr line `loop: warning: turn 1 resumes session <id> of an earlier attempt`. Its `SessionStarted` carries the id the resumed process's `init` line reports, and later turns and respawns behave as in any session. A resume Claude Code refuses ends the turn without a result line, an ordinary `port_exit` failure.
- **Codex.** The Codex runner keeps the default, so after a hold it starts a fresh session with the brief, whatever the occupancy. A Codex resume is out of scope.
- **The prompt is the same.** The resumed attempt gets the prompt of any retry: the template with `attempt`, `loop.retry_kind` and the session brief, built from the workspace as it is now. A runner that returns null needs the brief, and the resumed session reads the facts of the attempt that failed, its commits and the hold, which its own transcript ends before. The cost is the prompt once more, small beside a session rebuilt from nothing.
- **Where it lives.** `NetworkResume` in `loop.core.orchestrator`, beside `RunnerHold`, holds the rule and opens the session; `Orchestrator.kt` only reports each session's end (its runtime session id and last occupancy) and each attempt's end (whether the hold took it), and asks for the session at dispatch.
- **Out of scope.** A resume after any other failure, the wider resume question of bl-151 and a resume for Codex.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/NetworkResumeTest.kt` (the rule: below the soft limit resumes, at or above it starts fresh, the next dispatch's soft limit decides, an attempt the hold did not take, no runtime session id, no occupancy, another runner kind, a choice used once, a stale hold of another attempt; and the session it opens: `resumeSession` for a resume, `openSession` for a fresh choice or a runner that keeps the default), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/OrchestratorRunnerHoldTest.kt` (the `m4-46` tests: below the soft limit the probe resumes with the brief, at the soft limit it starts fresh, a runner that cannot resume starts fresh, the rotation of a resumed session opens a fresh one, an ordinary failure starts fresh), `plugins/runner-claude-code/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/claudecode/ClaudeCodeRunnerTest.kt` (the `m4-46` tests on the spawned command line: `--resume <id>` at turn 1 of a resumed session, none in a fresh one, and a respawn of a resumed session resumes the newest id), `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/CodexAppServerRunnerTest.kt` (`m4-46 it resumes no session across attempts`), `plugin-api/src/test/kotlin/loop/api/AgentRunnerContractCapabilitiesTest.kt` (the default passes the contract, a resume without continuation and a resumed session that starts with `TurnStarted` fail it) and `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/OccupancyMeterTest.kt` (the fraction).

### Refused Codex model (extension, bl-141)

Found while recording the m3-03 fixtures on 2026-11-17, codex-cli 0.167.0 with a ChatGPT Plus login. The runner sent no `model`, so the thread took the `model` of `$CODEX_HOME/config.toml`. The operator's config named `gpt-5.1-codex-max`, which a ChatGPT login refuses: a `warning` that its metadata is missing, an `error` with `codexErrorInfo` `modelNotFound` (`not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account`) and `turn/completed` `failed`. Every Codex turn on that machine failed at once, and each failure counted as the agent's.

- **The `model` key.** `runners.codex.model`, a string of the [profile](plugins/runner-codex-app-server.md#profile) with no default, trimmed; a value that is not a string fails preflight with `runners.codex.model must be a string`. When set, `thread/start` carries it as `model`, a member of the stable `ThreadStartParams`, so a workflow names the model it was checked with and the operator's Codex config no longer decides. `turn/start` sends none, so every turn keeps the thread's model. Without it nothing changes: no `model` is sent and Codex picks. The runner's `AgentRunner.model`, which the ledger and the prompt limit read, stays null, since the occupancy key of a Codex thread is `<modelProvider>/<model>` of the `thread/start` answer, not the configured name.
- **`modelNotFound` is `runner_unavailable`.** A `failed` turn whose error's `codexErrorInfo` is `modelNotFound` says nothing about the issue: Codex refused before any work. It gets the category of [bl-39](#runner-unavailable-hold-extension-bl-39), takes no `max_attempts` slot and holds the run with Codex's message as the hold's reason, until the operator sets a model the login accepts; the workflow reload of the next tick builds the next attempt's runner with it, and the probe lifts the hold. An interrupted turn keeps a null category, as in m4-08. Its reason ends with `(model_not_found: set runners.codex.model to a model the Codex login accepts)`.
- **No `model/list` check at session start.** The issue offered a check of the thread's model against `model/list` before the first turn. Not built: the key lets a workflow pin a model, the category already keeps the refusal from costing attempts, and the check would add a request to every session start. A refusal still costs one turn start per probe.
- **The workaround** before this issue, `command: codex app-server -c model=gpt-6.1-sol`, still works and is in the [runner's doc](plugins/runner-codex-app-server.md#a-refused-model).

Tests: `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/CodexAppServerRunnerTest.kt` (the `bl-141` tests: `thread/start` carries the configured model and validates against the pinned schema, none without it, `turn/start` none; `modelNotFound` on a failed turn is `runner_unavailable` with the hint in its reason; an interrupted turn keeps a null category), `RecordedSessionsTest.kt` (`bl-141 a recorded model refusal is runner_unavailable, and thread start carries the configured model`, the recorded `failure.jsonl`), `CodexAppServerRunnerProviderTest.kt` (the profile key, its parse and a refused value) and `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/PluginProfileDocsTest.kt` (the profile table).

### Codex guards after the m3-20 review (extension, bl-135)

The low findings of the merge risk review of pull request #104 (m3-20), from its three rounds.

- **Exec policy rules are checked before each session.** A Codex `prefix_rule` with the decision `allow` runs a matching command with no approval request, even under `untrusted`, so Loop never gets to decline it. The pinned codex 0.167.0 can skip the user and project rules files only in `codex exec` (`--ignore-rules`); the app server has no such flag and no thread config key for it, so ignoring them was not possible. So the runner reads the rules before the first turn of each session starts the app server: every `*.rules` file of `$CODEX_HOME/rules` (from the agent env, default `$HOME/.codex`), of `/etc/codex/rules`, and of each `.codex/rules` folder from the workspace up to the first folder holding `.git`, or up to the file system root. A rule whose decision is `allow`, or absent, which Codex reads as `allow`, and whose pattern can match a denied command (each word or one of its alternatives, the first also by a path to it, and a `git` rule whose second word is an option) fails the turn with `exec_policy_rule: <file> allows the denied command <command> with no approval request`, and nothing starts. A file read as literals only: a pattern or decision that is not a literal, a `prefix_rule` not called directly, or a file that does not parse fails it with `exec_policy_rule: <file> cannot be read as literal rules (<reason>), so it may allow a denied command`. It is a plain turn failure: an agent written rule stays in the workspace, so the issue is retried and then parked with that reason; an operator rule fails every Codex attempt until it is removed. The trust entry Codex writes for the workspace in `$CODEX_HOME/config.toml` stays, since each session reads the workspace's rules again. Not covered: a rule that allows another program that runs a denied command for it, such as an interpreter, and a rule written during a session, if Codex reads it before the session ends.
- **More of a denied command is caught.** A program run by its path (`/usr/bin/git push`; for `gh` a path ending in `/bin/gh`, so `cd /tmp/gh` is not one), git's own options before the subcommand (`git -c x=y push`, `git -C . push`, `git --git-dir=.git push`), a backslash a shell drops (`g\h`), and a git subcommand from a substitution or a variable (`git $(echo push)`, `git $SUB`), which may be `push`. The message of a `git commit -m`, `-am`, `--message` or `--message=` is left out before matching, also inside a quoted script, so a message that names `gh` no longer declines the commit; a message holding a substitution is kept, since it runs. The claude-code runner's `ClaudeSettingsFile.isDenied` is unchanged: it mirrors what Claude Code's own prefix rules deny, which Loop does not control.
- **A parity test.** `DeniedCommandsTest` reads the string literals of the `deniedCommands` list and of `OPERATOR_SUBCOMMANDS` from the source of `ClaudeSettingsFile.kt` in the sibling module, and fails when they differ from the Codex runner's list or cannot be found. It reads the source because the object is internal to its module and the Codex runner's build file, a protected path, gives it no dependency on that module. Gradle does not count that file as an input of the test task, so a change to the claude-code list alone reruns the test only in a clean build or with `--rerun`.
- **An env assignment as the program.** `FOO=bar codex app-server` fails preflight with `runners.codex.command starts with the env assignment FOO, which no shell runs: set it in agent.env instead`, never the value. Before, it ran `FOO=bar` as the program and failed at launch with `agent_not_found`.
- **No profile `PATH`.** The [runner's doc](plugins/runner-codex-app-server.md#configuration) now says that a `codex` found only through a `PATH` entry a login profile adds (nvm, Homebrew) is not found, and what to do.

Closed here, each with its reason:

- **Whether `untrusted` asks for approval on a real turn, whether codex honours `allow_login_shell` on a real turn, and whether a subagent thread inherits the override:** each needs a real model turn, which this issue does not run; they go to the next Codex exit run with the [real integration profile](#real-integration-profile-m3-24). The m3-28 exit run did not record them.
- **No trace event records `allow_login_shell`:** the setting is in every `thread/start`, which the tests check against the pinned schema; a trace line would only repeat a constant.
- **`~/.zshenv` exports still reach the agent's commands:** zsh reads it for every shell and Loop cannot stop that; it is documented in m3-20 and in the runner's doc, and the operator should not export a secret there.
- **The test name that still says "carries no config"** (`CodexServerRequestsTest`): renaming it would count as a deleted test for the `deleted_tests` guard, and its comment already says what it checks.

Tests: `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/ExecPolicyRulesTest.kt` (an allow rule in the workspace, in `CODEX_HOME` and in `HOME`'s codex folder, the parent folders up to the project root, every rule form that can allow a denied command, rules Loop cannot read as literals, rules that ask, forbid or allow another command, no rules folder, the folders read), `CodexServerRequestsTest.kt` (`bl-135 a workspace or operator exec policy rule that allows git push fails the session before the app server starts`), `DeniedCommandsTest.kt` (paths, git options, backslashes and substitutions, commit messages, a substitution or a command after a message, paths that only end in a denied name, the m3-20 cases, the parity with the claude-code runner), `CodexAppServerRunnerProviderTest.kt` (`bl-135 a command that starts with an env assignment is refused when the config is read, never run as a program`).

### Drain (extension, m4-10)

Story 110, carrying bl-110. Loop had `loop run` and `loop stop`, nothing between. On the night of 2026-11-15 (run 284454f4), to let two running issues finish while starting nothing new, the operator removed the `ready-for-agent` label of the one issue they would unblock and restored it by hand later. A zero per state cap does not work, `max_concurrent_agents_by_state` ignores values below 1. The need comes back at each session end, before a reinstall and before a planned stop. The decisions:

- **A run state.** `loop drain [path-to-WORKFLOW.md]` marks the live run as draining. It writes the drain marker `<LOOP_HOME>/runs/<project_id>.drain` next to the run file and the stop marker of `loop stop`, holding the id of the run it drains, and returns at once. The run reads the marker at the start of each dispatch, so it notices the drain within one `polling.interval_ms`. A marker that names another run asks nothing, and `loop run` removes it when it takes or releases the run file, so a drain ends with its run and a restart dispatches again.
- **What stops.** From the dispatch that notices it, no candidate is dispatched. The running attempts, their verifications, CI waits, merge checks and merges finish. An error or continuation retry of an issue whose attempt was running when the drain began still runs, it is the same work (confirmed by soule on 2026-11-18). A retry of any other issue stays queued and does not start, and it does not shorten the sleep between polls either.
- **What holds the drain.** A running attempt, a check wait, a merge wait, an approval already given (by a human or by the auto policy, delivered at the next tick) and a queued retry of an issue drained. A wait that needs a human does not: an approval not given yet, a `needs_info` issue, and a merge check behind a delivery queue paused after a CI timeout (bl-175), which waits for `loop approve` or a restart. These stay in the store and resume at the next `loop run`. A merge job already running always holds. Machine slots (m3-16) are released as usual, and nothing new claims one.
- **The end.** After the dispatch where nothing holds the drain any more, the run records `run_drained`, writes `run ended after loop drain` in the human log and stops by itself, with exit code 0, releasing its run file. No `run_stopped` is recorded, since nothing was canceled.
- **The long wait.** A drain waits for a running CI wait, which can last up to `ci_timeout_ms` (45 minutes by default). `loop stop` is the way out: during a drain it still stops the run at once, canceling what runs as it always does, and records `run_stopped` with `stop_requested`.
- **Events.** `run_draining` is recorded once, when the run notices the drain, run level, with what still runs: `attempts`, `waits` and `retries`, each a comma separated list of identifiers, absent when empty. `run_drained` is recorded when the run stops, with `drained_ms`, how long the drain lasted. `loop trace <issue>` prints both as their own lines for every run the issue has events in, and the human log writes `draining, nothing new starts, the run stops once bl-1, bl-2 end` and `drained in 4m 12s, the run stops`. bl-109's notifications (m4-30) will fire on `run_drained`.
- **`loop status`.** The run line ends with `draining`, and one line above the issues lists what still runs, by the rules above: `draining  run  since=2026-11-24T01:02:03Z still=bl-1,bl-3  stops once these end, loop stop stops it at once`. Before the run notices the drain the line reads `begins at the next poll`, with the issues running now.
- **Messages and exit codes.** `Run <run8> (pid <pid>) is draining: nothing new starts, the running attempts finish, then the run stops. loop status shows what still runs, loop stop stops it at once.`, exit 0. A second `loop drain` says `Run <run8> (pid <pid>) is already draining.`, exit 0. With no live run, `loop drain: no run in progress for project <id>. Nothing to drain.`, exit 1, or `loop drain: no run in progress for project <id>, removed a stale run file. Nothing to drain.` when it removed a stale one. An unreadable run file, a run file whose pid is not the run that holds it, or a drain marker that cannot be written exit 3.
- **Naming.** "Drain" already names the host side drain of the tool requests an agent queued, so the code says `RunDrain`, and the events `run_draining` and `run_drained`.
- **Where it lives.** `RunDrain` in `loop.core.orchestrator` holds the drain state and its rules, with `holdsDrain`, the wait rule `loop status` shares. `Orchestrator.kt` only asks it at its call sites: at the start of each dispatch, on the candidates, on the retries that may fire, on the sleep's next due time, and after each dispatch, whether the run stops.
- **Out of scope.** `loop stop --drain` as an alias, and a drain of one tracker only.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/RunDrainTest.kt` (no new candidate after the drain while the running attempt finishes and the run stops by itself, a drain with nothing running, a retry of an issue running when the drain began still runs while a due retry of another does not, an approval wait does not hold the drain and still waits at the next run, what still runs, a merge check behind a paused queue, no drain asked), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/DrainCommandTest.kt` (with the command runner, the running attempt finishes, verifies and delivers, the other issue never starts, the run exits 0, `loop status` and `loop trace` show the drain; `loop drain` with no live run exits 1), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/StopCommandTest.kt` (`m4-10 loop stop during a drain stops the run at once`), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/StatusCommandTest.kt` (the two `m4-10` tests), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/TraceCommandTest.kt` (`m4-10 the drain of a run the issue took part in prints as its own lines, the drain of another run does not`) and `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/log/HumanLogTest.kt`.

### Several trackers, the config part (extension, m4-11)

Story 111, config part, and PLAN 6.1. A project worked one tracker. This first issue of the multi tracker registry reads and checks `trackers:` and exposes the registry; nothing polls the extra trackers yet (m4-13). The decisions:

- **The primary tracker.** The spec's `tracker:` stays the primary tracker, with every key it had. Its tracker id is its `id` key, a Loop key in the spec's block (confirmed by soule on 2026-11-18), else `default`. A Loop `WORKFLOW.md` always keeps `tracker:`, so other Symphony implementations still pass preflight; they ignore `tracker.id` and `trackers:`.
- **Extra trackers.** `trackers:` is a list, each entry with `id`, `kind`, `provider`, and the optional `active_states`, `terminal_states` and `required_labels`, as in the PLAN 6.1 example. Each entry is read like `tracker:`: its `kind` selects a tracker plugin, which checks `provider` against its profile and reads it, with the same spec 11.4 categories, and `provider.dir` resolves like `tracker.provider.dir`, `.loop/issues` beside `WORKFLOW.md` when unset. States left unset take the plugin's defaults, and each state must be one the plugin defines. `required_labels` are the entry's own, empty when unset: an extra tracker never takes the primary's, and its handoff move removes its own. Another key of an entry is warned about once and ignored. An absent or empty `trackers:` changes nothing: such a workflow loads exactly as before.
- **Validation.** At preflight, with the entry named as `trackers[<index>]`: ids are unique across `tracker:` and `trackers:` (`trackers[0].id "gh" is already the id of the primary tracker`, `trackers[1].id "local" is already the id of trackers[0]`), match `[a-z][a-z0-9-]*` (`trackers[0].id "Local_1" must match [a-z][a-z0-9-]*`, and `tracker.id` likewise), a kind with no tracker plugin is refused (`trackers[0].kind "linear" is not supported, use files or github`), and so is a `provider` the plugin refuses, its message naming the entry's section (`trackers[0].provider.repo is required`, `missing_tracker_secret` for `trackers[0].provider.token uses $NAME, which is not set`). A plugin message about `tracker.provider` is renamed to the entry's section. A read command defers an extra tracker's unset secret like the primary's (bl-70).
- **Secrets.** Every `$NAME` value of an extra tracker's `provider`, other than `dir`, is a secret redacted like `tracker.provider`'s, and the startup redactor learns them from a best effort read before any load.
- **The registry.** `loop.core.trackers` holds it: `TrackerParser` reads the trackers for `WorkflowLoader`, `TrackersConfig` holds the primary `TrackerEntry` and the extras (`WorkflowFile.trackers`, `trackerId`, `extraTrackers`), and `TrackerRegistry` gives each tracker id its adapter, states and `required_labels`. The host builds it with `trackerRegistryFor`, each adapter by its own plugin. `WorkflowFile`'s `trackerKind`, `trackerDir`, `trackerSettings`, states and labels stay the primary's, so the orchestrator still takes the primary adapter until m4-13; `Orchestrator.kt` is not edited.
- **Not polled yet.** `loop run` with extra trackers said it once at startup, `loop run: warning: trackers lists local, which Loop does not poll yet (m4-13), it polls only the primary tracker gh.`, so a workflow that set them was not silently half read. m4-13 polls them and removed the line, see [Each tracker polled and reconciled on its own](#each-tracker-polled-and-reconciled-on-its-own-extension-m4-13).
- **Out of scope.** Namespaced ids (m4-12), polling and reconciliation per tracker (m4-13), routed writes (m4-14), per tracker limits (m4-15). Two `files` trackers reading one folder are not refused here; the reload of `trackers:` is not warned about, since nothing reads the extras live.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/trackers/TrackerRegistryTest.kt` (the primary id and each extra tracker's kind, settings and states, `default`, each tracker's own `required_labels`, the registry's adapters, a workflow with no `trackers:`, one preflight refusal each for a duplicate id, an id off the pattern, an unknown kind and a refused `provider`, an undefined state, the shape of `trackers:`, an unknown key, a deferred secret, the notice), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/SecretValuesTest.kt` (the two `m4-11` tests) and `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ExtraTrackersRunTest.kt` (the startup line of `loop run`, none without extra trackers, each adapter built from its own entry).

### Namespaced issue ids (extension, m4-12)

Story 112, first half. With several trackers (m4-11), two trackers may hand over the same id. Loop now names every issue with its tracker. The rule was decided by soule on 2026-11-18, so nothing in flight changes:

- **The namespaced id.** It is `<tracker id>:<native id>`, such as `gh:454` or `local:research-03`. `loop.core.trackers.NamespacedId` holds the type and the rule: the tracker part matches `[a-z][a-z0-9-]*`, so the first colon always ends it and a native id may hold more colons. The adapter keeps its native ids. The namespacing happens where the registry hands issues to the core: each adapter of `TrackerRegistry`, and the tracker `loop run` opens, is a `NamespacedTracker`, which namespaces every issue it hands over, gives the adapter the native id of each write and refuses a write for another tracker's issue. The namespaced id is what the store, the ledger, events and logs carry. The commands that take an issue id and the display of ids come with m4-47, see [Issue ids in commands and their display](#issue-ids-in-commands-and-their-display-extension-m4-47).
- **The reading rule.** Store rows and ledger lines written before m4-12 hold bare ids. They are read as the primary tracker's ids (`NamespacedId.read`), never rewritten: the event store is append only, and a by id read, write or delete of a primary tracker's issue matches both forms (`IssueIdForms` in the store). A row held under its bare id keeps it when it is updated; a new row gets the namespaced id. An extra tracker's issue never reads the primary's rows, since it had no rows before.
- **Kept primary workspace keys and branches.** The primary tracker's issues keep `WorkspaceKey.of(identifier)` and the branch `loop/<key>`, so no workspace folder or branch is renamed. A renamed folder would break the gitdir link of the worktrees `scripts/hooks/worktree-after-create.sh` makes, and an in flight workspace keeps its folder, its branch and its open pull request, with no `after_create` run again. An extra tracker's issue gets `WorkspaceKey.of("<tracker id>:<identifier>")` and the branch `loop/<that key>`, so two trackers with the same identifier never share a workspace. `NamespacedId.workspaceKey` gives the key; the orchestrator and the merge queue call it where they called `WorkspaceKey.of(identifier)`.
- **Variables.** `issue.id`, `issue.identifier`, `LOOP_ISSUE_ID` and `LOOP_ISSUE_IDENTIFIER` stay native: the prompt prints `{{ issue.identifier }}` and hooks read the `LOOP_*` names. The GitHub tracker's issue keeps the `id` `454` and the `identifier` `example-app-454`. The tracker id is a new prompt variable, `issue.tracker`, and a new variable of hooks, checks and the agent, `LOOP_TRACKER_ID`. The Claude Code runner passes `LOOP_TRACKER_ID` to the `loop` MCP server, and `loop tool` takes an attempt as its issue's when the `attempt_started` event holds `<LOOP_TRACKER_ID>:<LOOP_ISSUE_ID>`, or the bare id of an attempt started before m4-12. Since m4-47 a missing `LOOP_TRACKER_ID` means the primary tracker.
- **The ledger.** The ledger line carries the namespaced id in its issue id. The separate `tracker` field that `loop report` groups by came with m4-13.
- **Out of scope.** Commands taking a bare id and the display of ids (m4-47), polling several trackers (m4-13), routed writes (m4-14).

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/trackers/NamespacedIdTest.kt` (the form, the split at the first colon, the reading rule, the kept primary key and branch, the extra tracker's key and branch, two trackers with one identifier), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/trackers/NamespacedTrackerTest.kt`, `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workflow/PromptVariablesTest.kt` and `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workspace/HookEnvTest.kt` (the native variables and the tracker id), `store/src/test/kotlin/loop/store/SqliteIssueIdFormsTest.kt` (rows written before read as the primary's, never rewritten), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/NamespacedIdRunTest.kt` (a `files` tracker with `tracker.id: local`: events, rows and ledger lines carry `local:<id>`, the logs show the bare id since m4-47, and a store and ledger written before are read with their bare ids), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/WorktreeDispatchTest.kt` (`m4-12 an in flight primary workspace keeps its folder and branch, and after_create never runs again`) and `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ToolMoveCommandTest.kt`. `WorkspaceConformanceTest` is unchanged.

### Issue ids in commands and their display (extension, m4-47)

Story 112, second half. The store, the ledger and events carry the namespaced id `<tracker id>:<native id>` (m4-12). This decides how a person types an issue id and how Loop shows one. The display rule was decided by soule on 2026-11-18. `loop.core.trackers.IssueIdDisplay` holds the reading rule and the display rule, built from the project's `TrackersConfig`; the commands only call it, and `Orchestrator.kt` is not edited.

- **Reading a typed id.** `loop trace`, `loop approve` and `loop resume` take a bare id or a namespaced id. A bare id means the primary tracker, so `loop approve m4-12` works as before. A namespaced id names its tracker, the primary's included: `loop approve default:m4-12` is the same issue. One whose tracker is not in the registry is refused with exit 2 before anything is read, naming the tracker id: `loop approve: no tracker "gh" in this project, its tracker is default. Nothing changed.` The id splits at the first colon, so `local:a:b` is the native id `a:b` of `local`. A command reads only the primary tracker until m4-14: `loop trace` looks an extra tracker's issue up by its id in the store, without its title, like a tracker whose secret is unset (bl-70), and `loop approve` and `loop resume` find it among the waiting or parked issues, by its native id or its identifier within the tracker named.
- **The agent tools.** `loop tool move`, `loop tool comment` and the tools of `loop tool mcp` name no issue: their issue is `LOOP_TRACKER_ID` and `LOOP_ISSUE_ID`. With no `LOOP_TRACKER_ID`, `LOOP_ISSUE_ID` is read like a typed id, so a bare one is the primary tracker's. A `LOOP_TRACKER_ID` that is not a tracker of the project is refused with exit 5, naming it: `loop tool move: no tracker "gh" in this project, its tracker is default. Nothing queued.` The tool config snapshot carries `tracker_id`, the primary's, and `tracker_ids`, every tracker id, for this. A snapshot written before m4-47 has neither and keeps the m4-12 check.
- **Showing ids.** A project with no `trackers:`, or an empty one, shows bare ids everywhere, so a single tracker project reads as before m4-12: `issue_id=m4-12` in the `key=value` log, `Issue m4-12 "..."` in `loop trace`, `issue m4-12` and `"issue_id":"m4-12"` in `loop show`, `parked    m4-12` in `loop status`. A project with extra trackers in `trackers:` shows the namespaced id, the primary's included: `issue_id=default:m4-12`, and where Loop showed an identifier, `<tracker id>:<identifier>`, such as `gh:example-app-454` in `loop status`, the `loop trace` heading, the human log and the messages of `loop approve` and `loop resume`. An id of a tracker that is not the primary stays namespaced in any project, so a row written while `trackers:` was set never reads as the primary's. The stored form is unchanged: it always carries the tracker id.
- **Not shown by the rule.** The identifier field of the `key=value` log, `issue_identifier`, stays native, since `issue_id` names the tracker. The `still=` list of a draining run in `loop status` and the issue of a paused delivery queue show the identifiers recorded in their events.
- **Out of scope.** Polling several trackers (m4-13) and routed writes (m4-14). `loop show` takes an attempt id, not an issue id, so only the display applies to it.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/trackers/IssueIdDisplayTest.kt` (the reading rule, the refusal naming the tracker, bare ids with no extra tracker, namespaced ids with extra trackers, the agent tool's issue), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/log/KeyValueLogTest.kt` (`m4-47 issue_id is shown by the display rule given`), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/log/HumanLogTest.kt` (`m4-47 an issue line names the issue`), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/TraceCommandTest.kt` (the four `m4-47` tests), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/IssueIdCommandsTest.kt` (`loop approve`, `loop resume`, `loop status`, `loop show` and the agent tools) and `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/NamespacedIdRunTest.kt` (`m4-47 a project with trackers shows the namespaced id in its logs`).

### Each tracker polled and reconciled on its own (extension, m4-13)

Story 113, first half. `loop run` polls every tracker of the registry (m4-11), not the primary one alone. The per tracker loop lives in `loop.core.trackers.TrackerPoll`; `Orchestrator.kt` only replaces the single adapter's reads with its calls, and `RetryCoordinator` reads by id through it. The decisions:

- **The poll.** Each tracker is polled with its own `active_states`, and the candidates of all trackers are merged, then filtered and sorted by the spec's rule (8.2): priority, then creation time, then identifier, whatever their tracker. An issue is judged by the states of its own tracker, read from its namespaced id (m4-12): its eligibility, a due retry's refresh, reconciliation, a handoff and the checks of a move. An issue whose tracker a reload removed is no longer active.
- **A failing tracker.** A tracker that fails its poll is logged at error with its id, `event=poll outcome=failed tracker=gh`, and skipped for that tick; the other trackers' candidates still dispatch. Its issues are absent from that tick without having left: their retry counters are kept, their parks and waits stay as they are, and the waits a previous process left are restored at the first tick where every tracker answered. A tick where every tracker failed dispatches nothing, as a failed poll did before.
- **Reconciliation.** The running issues are read by id, each from its own tracker, and judged by that tracker's `terminal_states`. The attempts of a tracker whose read failed keep running until the next tick, logged with `tracker`, while the others are reconciled. The startup cleanup reads each tracker with its own terminal states, and a failing one does not stop the others. Due retries and parked issues are read the same way: a retry of a tracker whose read failed is rescheduled with no failure counted, the others go on.
- **Each tracker's `required_labels`.** A tracker's candidates are filtered by its own `required_labels`, never the primary's: a `files` issue in `todo` that carries only the GitHub tracker's label is not a candidate. A handoff move on an issue removes the labels of that issue's tracker, read at each move from the effective workflow, so a reload reaches every tracker. A tracker that a reload removed keeps the labels it had at startup.
- **Writes.** `TrackerPoll` gives each write to the issue's own tracker, which the routed writes of m4-14 build on. The tool config snapshot an agent reads kept the primary tracker's states and kind until m4-14. The trust of acceptance commands (m2-09) and the pointer to a cut description (m3-14) read the kind of the issue's own tracker, so a GitHub issue never runs commands from its text in a project whose primary tracker is `files`.
- **The ledger field.** Ledger lines and rows hold the id of the issue's tracker in their `tracker` field, beside the namespaced issue id, so `loop report` (m4-38) groups by tracker. It held `tracker.kind` before; rows and lines written before m4-13 keep it, and the store schema is unchanged. A single tracker project writes its primary tracker's id, `default` when `tracker.id` is unset.
- **Not covered.** The adapters are built once, at startup: a reload that adds a tracker takes effect at the next start, and a removed one is no longer polled. The shared GitHub rate limit hold (m4-48, see [One rate limit hold per GitHub origin and token](#one-rate-limit-hold-per-github-origin-and-token-extension-m4-48)), routed writes for the agent tools and the commands (m4-14), per tracker limits (m4-15) and cross tracker blockers (m4-16) are out of this issue.
- **The test names that still say "not polled yet"** (`ExtraTrackersRunTest`, `TrackerRegistryTest`): the m4-11 tests of the startup line now check that no such line is logged and that every tracker is there to poll. Renaming them would count as deleted tests for the `deleted_tests` guard, and their comments say what they check.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/trackers/TrackerPollTest.kt` (each tracker polled and read with its own states, a failing or throwing tracker reported by its id, by id reads and writes reaching each issue's tracker, each issue judged by its own tracker's states and labels), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/MultiTrackerOrchestratorTest.kt` (the merged order and the own labels, a failing tracker logged while the other's candidates dispatch and reconcile, reconciliation and the startup cleanup by each tracker's terminal states), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/OrchestratorLedgerTest.kt` (the tracker id in the setup), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/MultiTrackerRunCommandTest.kt` (a `files` tracker and a GitHub tracker on recorded answers: dispatch in the spec's order, each filtered by its own labels, a handoff removing the GitHub tracker's label only, the `tracker` field of ledger rows and lines, a failing GitHub poll) and `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ExtraTrackersRunTest.kt`.

### Event exporters (extension, m4-42)

Story 139. An exporter plugin sends the events Loop records to another system. The first one, `otel`, sends them to an OpenTelemetry backend as traces over OTLP. Its config, mapping and failure handling are in [`plugins/export-otel.md`](plugins/export-otel.md).

- **The plugin interface.** `EventExporterProvider` in `plugin-api`, found by `ServiceLoader` through `ExporterPlugins` in `cli`, like the runner and tracker plugins. Its exporter is an `EventSink`. `EventExporterContract` in the `plugin-api` test fixtures is the behavior every exporter must show.
- **Opt in.** Each section of `exporters:` in `WORKFLOW.md` turns on the plugin of its key, and nothing is exported without one. The preflight of startup and of every tick checks each section against its plugin's profile and the plugin's own check. The exporters are built once, at startup.
- **Redaction.** The store redacts inside its own `record`, so the host wraps each exporter in the run's redactor (`RedactingEventSink`), which redacts the issue identifier, the phase and every data value as the store does.
- **Never in the way.** `loop run` records each event in the store first, whose result is the only one the orchestrator sees, then hands it to each exporter through a bounded queue of its own, which drops an event when full. A failing exporter is logged at most once a minute, `event=export outcome=failed`, and the run goes on.
- **A shutdown budget.** When the run ends, the exporters get up to 1.5 seconds to send what their queues hold and 1 second to close, whatever their own timeout, then the run closes the store. What is left is dropped and counted in a last `event=export outcome=failed` line. A collector that never answers so keeps a stop within the 10 seconds of its shutdown hook, clean and exiting 0 (m1-34). Added after the merge risk reviews of pull request #44.
- **The ledger setup on `attempt_started`.** `attempt_started` carries `tracker`, `kind`, `runner`, `model` and `template_version`, the setup its ledger row names, so an exporter puts it on every span of the attempt. Attempts recorded before m4-42 have none.
- **The plugin list.** `loop run` logs `event=plugins` at startup, with the runners, trackers and exporters found and the exporters the workflow turns on.

Tests: `plugins/export-otel/src/test/kotlin/loop/export/otel/` (`OtelEventExporterTest`, the span tree, ids and attributes; `OtelRedactionTest`; `OtelSettingsTest`; `OtelEventExporterContractTest`), `plugin-api/src/test/kotlin/loop/api/RedactingEventSinkTest.kt`, `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ExporterPluginsTest.kt` (the loader, the preflight, the fan out and `loop run`, `loop stop` with a collector that never answers), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ExportingSinkTest.kt` (a full queue, the shutdown budget), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/OrchestratorRetryTest.kt` (`m4-42 attempt_started carries the ledger setup of the attempt`).

### One rate limit hold per GitHub origin and token (extension, m4-48)

Story 113, second half, carrying bl-50. With several trackers polled on their own (m4-13), the GitHub tracker and pull request delivery each kept their own rate limit hold, and m3-15 only kept the delivery's across ticks: a delivery step that spent the last request did not stop the tracker's next read, which GitHub may count against an integration that keeps sending while limited. The decision kept from the triage of bl-50:

- **One hold per API origin and token, for the life of the run.** GitHub counts the limit per token. `loop.tracker.github.GitHubClients` is built once by `loop run` and keeps one `GitHubClient` per API URL and token and one `RateLimitHold` per API origin (scheme, host and port, the default port filled in) and token. Every GitHub tracker the host builds (`trackerRegistryFor`, the GitHub plugin's `create` with the clients) and every delivery executor `DeliveryExecutors` keeps (m3-15) send through it, so the tracker's reads and writes, its blocker reads (m4-16 reads through the same hold), the pull request steps, the merge and the CI wait's polls of the Actions runs and jobs share one hold. Two tokens, or two origins, never hold each other. The hold lives in memory: a hold persisted across a restart is out of this issue.
- **Errors keep their kind.** A held tracker call stays `TrackerError.RateLimited`, handled like any failed poll or write (11.4). A held delivery step stays `DeliveryError.Unavailable`, so the wait keeps its approval and the next tick tries again; nothing is sent while the hold lasts, and the step goes out at the reset time.
- **A clear never overwrites a newer hold (from bl-22).** Each request takes a ticket from the hold before it is sent. An answer with requests to spare ends the hold only when no hold was set after its ticket, so a slow answer to a request sent before a rate limit answer never lifts that limit; of two holds, the later end is kept. The rest of bl-22, the GitHub client hardening that does not touch the shared hold, stays in the backlog.
- **The token stays out.** The registry keys clients and holds by a SHA-256 of the token; its `toString` names origins only, a client's its API URL, a hold's its end time. Messages of a held call name the reset time, never a header.
- **Where it plugs in.** The plugin API is unchanged: the classpath provider is one object shared by every project of the JVM, so the host calls the GitHub plugin's `create` overload that takes the run's clients, and any other tracker plugin's `create` as before. A tracker built outside `loop run`, such as by `loop approve`, `loop resume` or `loop trace`, gets its own clients. `Orchestrator.kt` is not edited.
- **Out of scope.** A hold persisted across a restart, the per tracker poll (m4-13), routed writes (m4-14).

Tests: `plugins/tracker-github/src/test/kotlin/loop/tracker/github/SharedRateLimitHoldTest.kt` (a tracker read that spends the last request holds the next pull request call with nothing sent, and the other way round, a rate limit answer holding delivery until its retry after, the hold ending at the reset time, two tokens and two origins apart, the CI wait's polls, one client per API URL and token, a late clear that leaves a newer hold, a later hold never shortened, the token in no `toString` or message) and `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/SharedGitHubHoldTest.kt` (the tracker and the delivery executors built by the host over one `GitHubClients`: a delivery step held at one tick sends nothing at the next tick while the hold lasts and delivers after it, a CI poll that holds the tracker's next poll, the token in no message or `toString`).

### Writes by the current issue, routed to its own tracker (extension, m4-14)

Story 114, carrying bl-153, the last Partial row of the conformance matrix (17.5 item 16, tools 3). SPEC 17.5 asks that the current normalized issue and its `native_ref` be available as tool context, and section 11 says the runtime SHOULD pass the current issue to the adapter. The decisions:

- **Writes by issue.** `TrackerAdapter` gains `moveIssue(issue, toState)` and `addComment(issue, comment)`. Their default bodies call the id versions, so a tracker plugin that overrides only those keeps working; a tracker whose writes need a provider id beyond `issue.id`, such as a GitHub Projects item id or a Linear issue uuid, overrides the `Issue` versions and reads `native_ref`. The core calls the `Issue` versions: the agent's `move` and `comment` applied at the drain (the move with the issue as the tracker shows it just before), the not ready comment, the split comment of a parked issue, the evidence summary, the verified move, the move of an approved delivery or a merged one, and pull request delivery, whose `DeliveryFacts` now holds the `Issue`. `NamespacedTracker` hands its native adapter the issue with its native id, `native_ref` kept.
- **Routing.** `loop.core.trackers.WriteRouting` sends every write, by issue or by id, to the tracker of the issue's namespaced id, a bare id being the primary's, never to the primary by default. `TrackerPoll` holds it, so `Orchestrator.kt` only changed its write call sites. A write for an issue whose tracker is not in the registry fails with `UnroutedWrite`, whose message names the tracker id, and nothing is written anywhere; pull request delivery treats it as permanent. It is a core exception, not a `TrackerError`, since that type is sealed in the plugin API. A lone adapter that is not namespaced, as in the orchestrator's tests, takes every id.
- **The tool config snapshot.** `tool-config.json` holds the kind, the states and the `provider` of the issue's own tracker, so `loop tool move`, `mcp__loop__move` and the Codex `move` client tool check a state against that tracker. `tracker_id` stays the primary's, by which bare ids are read (m4-47). `ToolConfigWriter.write` takes the issue's `TrackerEntry`.
- **Not changed.** The merge outcome comments of `loop.core.merge.Merges` (a refused merge, an auto merge fallback, a failed merge check) keep the id version, since most of their call sites hold the wait and not the issue; the id version is routed the same way. Label writes keep their id version (`files` labels and `file_followup` are m4-33).

Tests: `plugin-api/src/test/kotlin/loop/api/TrackerAdapterDefaultsTest.kt` (the defaults call the id versions, a tracker reads `native_ref`), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/trackers/WriteRoutingTest.kt` (each write reaches its own tracker with its native id and `native_ref`, a write for a tracker not in the registry fails naming it and writes nothing), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/WriteRoutingOrchestratorTest.kt` (the drained `move` and `comment`, the snapshot, the evidence summary, the verified move and an approved delivery's move, each on its own tracker), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/delivery/PullRequestDeliveryTest.kt`, and the two tracker cases of `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ClaudeCodeTrackerToolsTest.kt` and `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/CodexClientToolsRunTest.kt`, a `files` tracker and a GitHub tracker on recorded answers.

### Per tracker concurrency limit and a quieter retry requeue (extension, m4-15)

Story 115, carrying bl-77 findings 1, 2 and 4. With several trackers polled on their own (m4-13), one tracker's backlog could take every slot. The decisions, the limit confirmed by the maintainer on 2026-11-18:

- **The key.** `agent.max_concurrent_agents_by_tracker`, a map of tracker id to a positive count, sits under `agent:` beside the spec's `max_concurrent_agents` and `max_concurrent_agents_by_state`. A tracker with no entry has no limit of its own. The primary tracker is named by its id, `default` when `tracker.id` is unset, so a single tracker project may set it too, where it acts like a lower `max_concurrent_agents`.
- **Refused, not ignored.** Preflight refuses a key that is no tracker id of the workflow, a value below 1 or not an integer, and a key that is not a map, with `invalid_config` naming the key. Its sibling `max_concurrent_agents_by_state` ignores bad entries as the spec asks (17.1); a Loop key has no spec behavior to keep, and a typo in a tracker id would otherwise lift the limit without a word. A reload that removes a tracker still named by the key fails like any invalid reload, and the last good workflow stays.
- **The slot rule.** A dispatch needs a free slot under all three limits. The rule lives in `loop.core.trackers.TrackerSlots`, and `SlotPlan` asks it beside the total and the per state cap: a launch holds its tracker's slot for the rest of the tick, so a tracker at its limit skips its later issues (`candidate_skipped` with `no_available_slots`) while the other trackers' issues still take the free slots. A due retry at its tracker's limit is requeued with `no available orchestrator slots`, like one at its state's cap (bl-77 finding 2). The issue's tracker is read from its namespaced id (m4-12); an issue whose tracker a reload removed counts under no tracker limit. Like the per state caps, the limit counts attempts only: a merge check is never held by it. `Orchestrator.kt` only passes the tracker of each issue and of each running attempt to the plan.
- **The requeue volume (bl-77 findings 1 and 4).** While slots stayed full, every tick gave each waiting retry one `retry_scheduled`, one WARN line, one by id refresh and one store write, with no bound, and a per tracker limit makes more retries wait. Now a requeue for the reason the retry already waits for records no `retry_scheduled`, no WARN line and no store write: only its moved due time changes, in memory. A new reason, such as `no available machine slots` after `no available orchestrator slots`, records one event and one line and writes the retry state once. A waiting retry refreshed by id less than one poll interval ago is not read by id again: it is judged on this tick's polled copy of its issue, else on the copy its last refresh read, so on GitHub it spends at most one request per poll interval. An issue that left the active states meanwhile is dropped at the next refresh, at most one poll interval later. The firing of due retries writes only the entries it dropped or rescheduled.
- **In memory.** The reason a retry waits for and the time of its last refresh live on its queue entry, in memory, like its first due time (bl-34): a requeue keeps them, any other schedule forgets them, and a restart starts without them, so the first requeue after a restart is recorded again. The stored due time stays the one of the first recorded requeue, which is just as due after a restart.
- **Out of this issue.** bl-77 findings 3 and 5, the first due time lost on a restart and its early tick window.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workflow/TrackerConcurrencyConfigTest.kt` (the key and its refusals), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/SlotPlanTest.kt` (a tracker at its limit, the launches of one tick, a due retry blocked by its tracker's limit or its state's cap, the three limits together, no limit on the merge check), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/TrackerSlotsOrchestratorTest.kt` (two trackers: with a limit of 1 one issue runs and the other tracker's issue takes the next free slot, a due retry at its tracker's limit waits while the other tracker's issue takes the free slot), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/OrchestratorRetryTest.kt` (`a due retry blocked by its state's cap is requeued and another state's candidate takes the free slot (bl-77 finding 2)`), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/RetryRequeueVolumeTest.kt` (events, WARN lines, store writes and by id refreshes counted over fourteen ticks and one change of reason) and `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/RetryQueueTest.kt`.

### Cross tracker blockers from a body line (extension, m4-16)

Story 116, PLAN 6.1. Within a tracker, the native `blocked_by` of its adapter is unchanged (m1-09, bl-158). Across trackers, an issue names its blockers in a line of its description. The decisions:

- **The line.** A blocker line is a column 0 line of the description that reads `Blocked by ` followed only by one or more `<tracker>:<id>` items separated by commas, whatever the tracker ids:

  ```markdown
  Blocked by gh:12
  Blocked by gh:12, local:m4-03
  ```

  The phrase is read case insensitively, spaces around the commas are free, a period at the end of the line is not part of the last id, and several lines add up. Any other `Blocked by` line is prose and changes nothing, such as `Blocked by bl-54 so the two never run side by side` in Loop's own issues (`bl-61`, `bl-48`), or `Blocked by gh:12 and lin:3`. Lines in fenced code blocks, and indented or quoted lines, are ignored. The parser is `loop.core.trackers.BlockerLines`.
- **The check.** An issue is blocked while any blocker it names is not in a terminal state of its own tracker, read from the blocker's `terminal_states` in the workflow in effect: a `files` blocker in `done` or `canceled`, a GitHub one `closed`. Its `Issue.blockedReason` becomes `blocked_cross_tracker <blocker> (<why>)`, naming each blocker that holds it, comma separated, and the dispatch records it as the `candidate_skipped` reason once per change, as for every skip. `<why>` is the blocker's state, such as `open`.
- **Fail closed.** A tracker id that is not in the registry of the run, or not in the workflow in effect, keeps the issue blocked with `(unknown tracker <id>)`, so a typo, a case slip such as `Gh:12`, or a tracker removed from the workflow (`Blocked by lin:MKT-12` with no `lin`) never unblocks an issue in silence (decided by the maintainer on 2026-11-18). A single tracker project keeps an issue blocked by another tracker's id the same way, and the primary tracker never reads it. A blocker whose tracker fails its read keeps the issue blocked with `(unreadable: <error>)`, the error cut at 200 characters, and one its tracker does not find, or that a GitHub tracker leaves out of its scope, with `(not found)`.
- **The reads.** Blockers are read by id through the registry's adapters, so a GitHub blocker goes through the run's shared rate limit hold (m4-48). The check runs once per tick on the eligible candidates and reads each blocker at most once, all the blockers of one tracker in one by id read, whatever the number of issues naming them. An issue that its own tracker already blocks, or with no blocker line, reads nothing.
- **Where it lives.** `loop.core.trackers.CrossTrackerBlockers` holds the check and `TrackerPoll.withCrossTrackerBlockers` applies it; `Orchestrator.kt` has one call site, in the eligibility filter of the dispatch, before the blockers are read into skips. The rule is Loop's own and the same for every tracker, so the orchestrator still never branches on a provider's blocker semantics (11.2). A due retry goes through the same check.
- **Not covered.** A running attempt is not stopped when a blocker named in its issue's description leaves its terminal state: reconciliation reads only the native blockers. Blocker lines in the GitHub tracker's native fields and a Linear tracker (M7) are out of this issue.
