Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/trackers/CrossTrackerBlockersTest.kt` (the parser table, the prose lines of `bl-61` and `bl-48`, open, closed and own tracker blockers, an unknown tracker, a tracker the workflow no longer has, a single tracker project, an unreadable, missing or throwing blocker, every holding blocker named, one read per blocker counted over six issues) and `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/CrossTrackerBlockersRunTest.kt` (a `files` tracker and a GitHub tracker on recorded answers: a `files` issue skipped while its GitHub blocker is open and dispatched once it reads closed, each blocker read once per tick, an unknown tracker and a failing GitHub read keeping the issue blocked).

### Minimal area lock (extension, m4-17)

Story 117, carrying bl-10. Claims stop one issue running twice, but nothing stopped two issues that edit the same files: m1-17 and m1-34 conflicted when they merged out of order, and run 4672d0bf lost attempts to `Orchestrator.kt` conflicts. The M4 exit runs two code issues in parallel on one app, so two issues of one area must never run at once. The lock is deterministic code, no model (principle 2). The decisions, confirmed by the maintainer on 2026-11-18 as the minimal lock of M4; anything wider is for M6 triage:

- **The field.** `Issue.area`, a list of repository relative paths, each a folder or a file, empty by default. The `files` tracker reads the optional front matter `area`, a list or one path (`area: [spec-core, docs/agents]`, `area: cli`). A tracker without front matter carries it as a column 0 `Area:` line of the body, comma separated paths (`Area: spec-core, docs/agents`), which the GitHub tracker reads into the same field. The word is read as written, so `area:` in prose is no area line, lines in fenced code blocks and the text of HTML comments are ignored (a line that starts inside a comment is no area line, a comment after the paths is left out), several lines add up, and issue comments never carry it.
- **Normalizing.** `loop.api.IssueArea` puts each entry in Unicode NFC, trims it and each of its segments, and drops its `.` and empty segments and its trailing slash, so `./spec-core / src/` is `spec-core/src`; duplicates count once. An entry is malformed when it is blank, absolute, holds a `..` segment, a glob character (`*`, `?`, `[`, `]`, `{`, `}`), a backslash, a backtick, a control or format character (such as a zero width space) or whitespace inside a segment, or names the repository root (`.`). No globs: an area names paths. A path whose name holds a space cannot be an entry, its parent folder can. An area holds at most 64 entries, more is malformed. A malformed value makes a malformed issue on `files`, skipped by a read by states and failing a read by ids like any other malformed field; on GitHub, whose body people edit, it is ignored with a warning and the issue runs with no area.
- **The GitHub form.** The expected form is `Area: <path>, <path>`, paths only, such as `Area: spec-core/src/main/kotlin/loop/core/trackers, docs`. Loop's own issue style also has a column 0 `Area:` prose line, such as ``Area: the `Issue` model in ...``: on GitHub such a line holds a backtick or a space inside an entry, so it is a malformed line, never bogus entries. One malformed `Area:` line drops every `Area:` line of the body, as an area read in part could let two overlapping issues run at once. The warning names the issue and the line number, says that every `Area:` line of the body is ignored and that the issue runs with no area, and quotes nothing of the body, which an untrusted author may have written. It is given once per issue for its latest malformed line: an edit to the line warns again, a read with no malformed line forgets the issue, and the set of warned issues is bounded.
- **Overlap.** Two entries overlap when, normalized, they are equal or one is a parent folder of the other on whole path segments: `spec-core` overlaps `spec-core/src/main` but not `spec-core-x`. Paths compare case folded, so the lock fails closed: on a case insensitive file system `Docs` and `docs` are one folder, and on a case sensitive one two such issues are at worst kept apart for nothing. Two areas overlap when any of their entries do. Overlap is computed on declared areas only, never on the files an attempt changed so far. The rule is `loop.core.trackers.AreaOverlap`.
- **Busy while claimed.** An area is busy while its issue is claimed: running, waiting while its verification runs (also a verification a dead process left, which runs again at the start), or queued for a retry. It frees when the claim is released, so a verified result that waits for approval or for its merge holds nothing: conflicts after that are the merge check's job. The holder's area is the latest read of its issue; a holder whose tracker failed this tick's poll keeps the area of its last read.
- **The skip.** At the dispatch, after the blocker and readiness checks, a candidate whose area overlaps a holder's is skipped with `candidate_skipped` and the reason `area_busy <holder> (<entry>)`, naming the holder and its overlapping entry, such as `area_busy m4-03 (spec-core)`, recorded once per change like every skip. Candidates of one tick are taken in slot order, and the slot plan holds a candidate whose area overlaps a launch it planned earlier in that tick, with the same reason; a held candidate takes no slot, which goes to the next one. Only a candidate given a slot holds its area that way, so a candidate left with no slot never blocks a later one, and two candidates of one area are never launched by one tick even when neither is claimed yet. A due retry is a holder and a candidate at once: it holds its area against new candidates wherever they sort, and of two due retries of one area the first in slot order runs, so they never block each other for good. A skipped due retry stays queued and is requeued like one with no slot: its due time moves one poll interval ahead, with the error `area_busy` on its first `retry_scheduled`, so the daemon sleeps its interval instead of polling back to back while the holder runs, and it fires on the first tick its area is free. A due retry a cross tracker blocker (m4-16) holds is requeued the same way, with the error `blocked_cross_tracker`.
- **No reordering.** A skipped candidate keeps its place in the dispatch order and is dispatched on the first tick its area is free, after any candidate that sorts before it.
- **No area.** An issue with no `area` never blocks and is never blocked.
- **Where it lives.** `loop.core.trackers.AreaLock` holds the lock and `AreaOverlap` the rule; `Orchestrator.kt` has the call sites: the eligible candidates taken before the slot plan, passing the claimed, verifying and queued issues, the requeue of a held due retry, and `AreaLock.heldBy` given to `SlotPlan.attempts`, whose held candidates are skipped or requeued like those with no slot. `Waits.verifying` gives the issues whose verification runs.
- **Out of this issue.** Filling `area` on existing issues (the operator's call), areas suggested by M6 triage, and the fetch mutex of bl-67 (m4-49). The `CONTEXT.md` entry for the term is a human step after the merge.

Tests: `plugin-api/src/test/kotlin/loop/api/IssueAreaTest.kt` (normalizing and refusals), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/trackers/AreaOverlapTest.kt` (the overlap table, both ways round), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/trackers/AreaLockTest.kt` (a claimed holder, no area, an earlier candidate of the tick given a slot, two due retries, a queued retry sorted after a new candidate, the latest and the remembered read of a holder), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/AreaLockOrchestratorTest.kt` (busy while the holder runs, verifies, verifies again after a restart, or waits for a retry, freed by its release, the skipped candidates dispatched on the first free tick in their order, a due retry held by its area or a cross tracker blocker requeued for a poll interval with no back to back polls, a candidate with no slot holding nothing), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/SlotPlanTest.kt` (a held candidate takes no slot), `plugins/tracker-files/src/test/kotlin/loop/tracker/files/FilesTrackerTest.kt` and `plugins/tracker-github/src/test/kotlin/loop/tracker/github/GitHubTrackerTest.kt` (the field read, HTML comments, a malformed or prose line, the warning given again after an edit).

### Kind map and matcher (extension, m4-18)

Story 118 and PLAN 6.2. Until m4-18 a workflow in Loop mode had one kind, `code`, and the ledger wrote `code` for every attempt. This first issue of the kinds family makes `kinds:` a map of named kinds and matches each issue to one, with `code` unchanged. The decisions:

- **The kind map.** `kinds:` is a map of named kinds, read in the order written. Each kind holds the slots `code` holds (`verify`, `ready`, `delivery`), read and checked the same way under its own key, such as `kinds.content.verify.checks[0].run is required`, and a `match`. A kind name is lowercase letters, digits and `-`, starting with a letter, since it reaches `LOOP_KIND` and the ledger. `code` stays required: the paths that are code only so far read it. `KindsConfig.named` holds the kinds, and `KindsConfig.code` reads the kind named `code`, so the call sites and tests of `kinds.code` compile unchanged.
- **The match keys.** `match` is a map of `default`, `label`, `tracker` and `state`. `match: { default: true }` marks the default kind, and `default` is then alone in its match. Otherwise every key set must hold: the issue carries the `label` (compared lowercase, like the labels of an issue), comes from the tracker whose id is `tracker` (an id of the registry of m4-11, the primary's `default` when `tracker.id` is unset), or is in the `state` (compared trimmed and lowercased, the same comparison as the tracker's active and terminal states). A match on a front matter field waits for a field map in the issue model, so it is not in M4 (decided by soule on 2026-11-18).
- **`code` unchanged.** `code` with no `match` is the default kind, so a workflow whose only kind is `code` loads to the same config as before and behaves exactly as before. No existing workflow needs an edit. One change: a `kinds.code.match` key, ignored before m4-18, is now read and checked like any match, so a stray one fails preflight or makes `code` match only some issues. No workflow in this repository has one.
- **The matcher.** `loop.core.kinds.KindMatcher`, a pure function of the kinds, the issue and its tracker id: an issue gets the first kind whose match holds, in the order the kinds are written, and the default kind otherwise. The default kind may be written anywhere, and may be another kind when `code` has a `match`.
- **Preflight.** Each of these is `invalid_config`: two default kinds, `code` with no `match` counting as one (`kinds.code and kinds.content are both the default kind, keep one match: { default: true }, code is the default when it has no match`); no default kind; a kind other than `code` with no `match` (`kinds.content.match is required, only the code kind is the default without one`); a `match` that is not a map or holds none of the keys; an unknown match key, a front matter field key included (`kinds.content.match.field is not supported, use default, label, tracker or state`); `default` that is not `true` or `false`, or `true` beside another key; a blank `label`, `tracker` or `state`; a `tracker` that is not a tracker id of the workflow. The m2-26 check that `delivery.on_delivered.move_to` is a terminal state runs for every kind, named by its own key.
- **One lookup.** `loop.core.kinds.KindLookup.of(workflow, issue)` gives the issue's kind, its name and its settings, through the matcher with the id of the issue's own tracker. An attempt resolves its kind once, from the issue as dispatched, like the workflow it started with. The orchestrator reads the issue's kind where it read `kinds.code`: the readiness check, the checks and holdouts of a verification (also one a dead process left, rerun at the start), the acceptance commands frozen at dispatch, the base of the session brief, the base fetch before an attempt, and the delivery (`policy`, approval, the delivery of an approved result). `Orchestrator.kt` only calls the lookup.
- **The kind pinned on the waits.** The `Waiting(verify)` and `Waiting(approval)` of an attempt hold the name of the kind the attempt was dispatched under (the wait data key `kind`), and the merge wait that follows an approval keeps it. The delivery of an approved wait, the rerun of a verify wait a dead process left, and `loop approve` resolve the kind by that name (`KindLookup.pinned`), never from the issue as it reads now: a move to another active state or a label edit during the wait, by a human or by the agent, would otherwise skip the pull request of a `code` issue, or deliver a pull request for work the code kind never checked. A wait stored before m4-18, without the key, falls back to the kind the issue matches now. A wait whose kind left the workflow keeps waiting, with one warning per issue in the log for the run, until the kind is back: its checks do not rerun and nothing is delivered; `loop approve` still approves it and says so on stderr.
- **An agent cannot change its own kind.** A move the agent queues to another active state, which Loop applies as is, is refused with `tool_request_failed` when it would put the issue under another kind (`the move to in_progress would change the kind of the issue from code to quick`), so an agent never picks a kind with laxer checks for its next attempt. The agent has no tool that edits labels. A human may still move or relabel the issue: the current attempt and its waits keep their kind, and the next attempt is dispatched under the kind the issue matches then.
- **Code only paths.** The merge check and the auto merge (`Merges`, `AutoMergeGate`, the pull request delivery's `merge` setting) keep reading the code kind's settings; an issue of another kind takes them up when its kind lands (m4-20 to m4-23). Only a `code` issue reaches them: a kind other than `code` may not declare `delivery.pull_request` yet (`kinds.content.delivery.pull_request is not supported yet, only the code kind delivers a pull request`), so it delivers by a move, under `policy: human` or an opted in `auto`, and the approval wait pins the kind, so a later match cannot send an issue of another kind into a pull request delivery. The pinned diff base of a verification and the base fetch before an attempt read the issue's own kind, so an issue of another kind, with no pull request, fetches nothing and diffs against its `guards.base`.
- **Every kind in the preflight checks.** The holdout folder check and the acceptance commands trust check (m2-09) read every kind, in the order written, each named by its own key; for `code` their sentences are unchanged.
- **`LOOP_KIND`.** Every hook of an issue gets `LOOP_KIND`, the name of its kind, beside the other `LOOP_*` variables (`hookEnv`), inside an attempt or not, so a hook can branch on the kind. It is absent in Symphony mode, which has no kind.
- **The ledger.** Ledger lines and rows hold the kind the issue matched, not a constant, so `loop report` (m4-38) can group by kind. The `attempt_started` event carries it too. The row of the merge risk review of an auto merge, a code only path, holds `code`.
- **Out of this issue.** A match on a front matter field, context sources and per kind templates (m4-19), verification without git (m4-20), the `content` and `research` kinds themselves (m4-21 to m4-23), and a per kind runner.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/kinds/KindMatcherTest.kt` (the table of default, label, tracker id and state matches, every key of a match, first match wins, no match falling to the default, a default written anywhere), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workflow/WorkflowFileTest.kt` (only `code` and no `match` loads to the same config, a second kind accepted with its slots, two defaults, no match nor default, an unknown match key with a front matter field, an unreadable match, no default, the slots of another kind and its pull request refused), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workflow/KindsConfigTest.kt` (a kind name), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workspace/HookEnvTest.kt` (`LOOP_KIND`), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/kinds/KindLookupTest.kt` (a pinned name wins, a wait without one falls back, a name gone, a move that changes the kind), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/waiting/WaitsTest.kt` (the `kind` key through the merge wait and back), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/KindPinOrchestratorTest.kt` (two kinds with a state match: the state moved by a human before submit, delivery follows the dispatched kind both ways; a restored verify wait reruns its pinned kind's checks, falls back without a name, waits when its kind is gone; an approved wait whose kind is gone; an agent move into another kind refused), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ApproveCommandTest.kt` (`loop approve` follows the pinned kind, warns on a kind gone), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/KindsRunCommandTest.kt` (a project with two kinds: the labelled issue runs under the second kind, the other under `code`, each verified by its kind's checks, its hooks given its kind, and its ledger rows and lines holding it).

### Context sources and per kind templates (extension, m4-19)

Story 119, PLAN 8.6 and 8.9. Until m4-19 every issue got the `WORKFLOW.md` body, and the `context_sources` slot of the prompt budget measured 0. A kind now declares where its context comes from, as pointers the agent reads on demand, and may set its own template. The decisions:

- **`kinds.<kind>.context.files`.** A list of paths relative to the workspace, in the order written. `context` is a map whose only key is `files`; any other key is refused (`kinds.content.context.file is not supported, use files`), so a misspelled key never drops the sources silently. A path is trimmed, never blank (`kinds.content.context.files must not hold a blank path`), never absolute and never through `..` (`kinds.content.context.files[0] is /etc/voice.md, use a path relative to the workspace`), so a pointer stays in the workspace. Absent, the kind declares no sources.
- **The list.** `loop.core.kinds.ContextSources` renders the paths as one `- <path>` line each, never their text. Each path is checked against the attempt's workspace when the prompt renders, the first prompt and the first prompt of a rotated session alike. A path not there is listed as `- <path> (missing from the workspace)`, and the orchestrator records `context_source_missing` with `path` and `kind`, once per missing path and render, shown in the trace timeline and on the human log. It never fails the attempt: the agent may create the path, or a hook may populate it later. An empty or absent list is null, so it renders nothing.
- **`loop.context_sources`.** The list is the prompt variable `loop.context_sources`, null when the kind declares no sources (PLAN 8.9 named it `loop.context_files` before m4-19). Sources appear only where the template places the variable: the prompt is the template, the `WORKFLOW.md` body or the kind's own file, and Loop never inserts the list by itself. A template writes it as `{% if loop.context_sources %}Read these when you need them:\n{{ loop.context_sources }}{% endif %}`.
- **The slot.** The list counts in the `context_sources` slot of `BuildingPrompt` (`slot_context_sources_chars`, `slot_context_sources_tokens`), measured like the other slots as the difference between the prompt and the same template rendered without the sources. It is the last slot of PLAN 8.5, so a prompt over its limit cuts it first: it keeps its first lines that fit, one source each, then `(N more context sources are left out to fit the prompt limit.)`, and is null, its branch gone, when not even that line fits. `cut_context_sources_tokens` records what it lost.
- **`kinds.<kind>.template`.** A path relative to the folder of `WORKFLOW.md` (or absolute, or under `~`), such as `.loop/templates/content.md`, rendered instead of the `WORKFLOW.md` body for the issues of that kind (confirmed by soule on 2026-11-18). Without it the body is used, so `code` is unchanged. The file is read when `WORKFLOW.md` is loaded and trimmed like the body; a path that does not exist is refused (`kinds.content.template is .loop/templates/nope.md, which does not exist`), so preflight fails with `invalid_config`. An attempt renders the template of the kind it was dispatched under for its whole life, its rotations included. The ledger's `template_version` is the hash of the template the kind renders.
- **A reload.** Loop reloads the workflow when the hash of `WORKFLOW.md` changes (m1-15), so an edit of a kind's template file alone reaches attempts at the next change of `WORKFLOW.md` or the next start. Preflight still reads the file on every tick, so a template file that goes missing or breaks fails preflight at once.
- **Preflight.** It checks the template of every kind, in the order written, its own `template` or the body; the body alone in Symphony mode. Each is parsed with the static variable check and rendered against every sample of m3-11 with `loop.context_sources` null and set, whatever the kind declares, so every branch on the variable renders. A kind that declares `context.files` with a template that never uses `loop.context_sources` is `invalid_config` (`kinds.content.context.files is set, but the WORKFLOW.md body never uses loop.context_sources, place {{ loop.context_sources }} where the sources go`, or `but kinds.content.template (.loop/templates/content.md) never uses ...`), using the m2-06 rule of `uses`: a read in any branch counts. A failure of a kind's own template names it (`the prompt template of kinds.content (.loop/templates/content.md) uses "issue["nope"]", which is not a variable`); failures of the body keep their sentences. The m3-13 check that the template alone fits the prompt limit runs for each template.
- **`code` unchanged.** Loop's own `code` kind declares no `context.files` and no `template`, so its `WORKFLOW.md` needs no human edit and renders byte for byte as before.
- **The orchestrator.** `loop.core.kinds.KindPrompt` gives the template text and the rendered sources of the attempt's kind; `Orchestrator.kt` only passes the kind and the workspace to it, and records the missing paths.
- **Out of this issue.** Reading the sources for the agent or inlining small ones, lessons (M6), and a per kind runner.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/kinds/ContextSourcesTest.kt` (one line per path in order, an empty list renders nothing, a missing path listed and named, the list rendered only where the template places it, the `context_sources` slot, a kind's own template and the body for `code`, the loader reading `context.files` and `template`, refused context paths and keys, preflight refusing a missing template path, preflight refusing unused sources, preflight rendering the variable for every kind), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workflow/PromptFillTest.kt` (the sources cut first, then left out), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/ContextSourcesOrchestratorTest.kt` (the sources in the prompt, `context_source_missing` recorded and the attempt succeeding, the slot on `BuildingPrompt`; a kind's own template for its issue and the body for a `code` issue).

### Verification without git and the empty workspace (extension, m4-20)

Finding M7 of the m3-29 review. Verification, `diff_policy`, the restore and the pinned base assumed a git work tree, while an empty research workspace has none. This lands before any non code kind. The decisions:

- **`kinds.<kind>.workspace`.** `git`, the default and the behavior before m4-20, or `plain` (confirmed by soule on 2026-11-18). Any other value is refused (`kinds.notes.workspace must be git or plain, got "plian"`), so a typo never falls back to `git`.
- **The declaration decides, never detection.** The workspace is populated by the workflow's global hooks, not per kind, so a `plain` kind in a project with worktree hooks can still get a git work tree. Loop never looks: a `plain` kind is fingerprinted `tree:<hash>` even inside a work tree, and a `git` kind in a folder that is not a work tree stays `unverified` as before, its `diff_policy` saying the workspace is not the top of a git work tree.
- **Preflight refuses git only settings on a `plain` kind,** so the mistake is caught before dispatch, not found as a park of every issue: the deny guards (`kinds.notes.verify.guards.deny reads a git diff, and kinds.notes is workspace: plain`), a `diff_policy` or `changed_line_coverage` check, written in `checks` or `holdout`, any `verify.guards`, which add a `diff_policy` check, and `delivery.pull_request`, which pushes a commit. The refusal is in the loader, so `loop run` and every tick's preflight fail with `invalid_config`; `loop.core.kinds.KindWorkspaces` holds the rules.
- **`kinds.<kind>.populate`.** `hooks`, the default, or `empty`, moved here from m4-23 so a plain workspace can exist outside tests. A kind that declares `empty` gets the folder Loop creates under `workspace.root`, and Loop runs none of `after_create`, `before_run`, `after_run` and `before_remove` for it: the global hooks assume a worktree, and `before_remove` would fail on a plain folder. `on_token_threshold` and `on_budget_warning` still run. `LOOP_KIND` (m4-18) stays for workflows that prefer one set of hooks that branch on the kind. Preflight refuses `populate: empty` on a `git` kind. The removal of a workspace (reconciliation, terminal cleanup) reads the kind the issue matches at that time, like `LOOP_KIND` there, so a kind matched by state can differ from the one the issue was dispatched under; a kind matched by label keeps it.
- **The `tree:` fingerprint.** `Fingerprinter.tree` gives `tree:<hash>` of any folder, the hash of the path and content of every file in path order, the result file aside, as for a folder that is not a work tree. The `.git` entry at the top of the folder is never part of it, for either form of `tree:`: it is git's own state, which a read only git command can change, so the fingerprint of a plain kind inside a work tree moves only with the files.
- **What verification does without git.** On a `plain` kind the verdict, the evidence, the waits and the evidence summary carry the `tree:` fingerprint. The listing of uncommitted files, the restore after the checks and the pinned base are skipped, whatever the folder holds. `command`, `file_exists`, `file_matches`, `acceptance_commands` and holdouts run as for any kind; a failed check gives the usual findings. `changed_line_coverage` outside a work tree says it could not run, never throws; on a `plain` kind preflight refuses it. The holdout folder check finds no repository for a project that is not one and compares against the project folder.
- **The verdict compared again before delivery.** The delivery of an approved `plain` result takes the `tree:` fingerprint again and delivers only when it equals the verified one. A file changed after the verdict, a workspace that is gone, or a fingerprint that cannot be taken records `delivery_failed` with step `fingerprint` and the reason, and clears the approval, as a pull request delivery does when its fingerprint does not hold: the issue waits for a new `loop approve`, which delivers only once the workspace is back at the verified fingerprint. Nothing restores a plain workspace, so a check of a `plain` kind must leave the folder as it found it, or its result is refused at delivery. Under `policy: auto` the move follows the verdict in the same attempt, as for git.
- **`pinnedBase`.** It reads the issue's own kind since m4-18 and returns no base for a `plain` kind, so a plain kind never fetches one.
- **The orchestrator.** `Orchestrator.kt` passes the kind to `KindWorkspaces` (the workspace manager without the four hooks, the `plain` test) and to `loop.core.verify.PlainWorkspace` (the fingerprint by declaration, the stale check before delivery).
- **Out of this issue.** The content and research deliveries (m4-22, m4-23).

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/verify/PlainWorkspaceVerifyTest.kt` (a command check verifies a plain workspace with a `tree:` verdict and evidence, a file changed after the verdict refuses the delivery, an unchanged one is delivered, preflight refuses `diff_policy`, the deny guards, `delivery.pull_request` and `populate: empty` on a git kind, the pinned base of the issue's own kind and none for a plain kind, `tree:` inside a work tree without `.git`, a git kind outside a work tree `unverified`, `populate: empty` with no workspace hook, `changed_line_coverage`, holdouts and the findings on a plain workspace with no uncommitted listing or restore), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/PlainWorkspaceRunTest.kt` (the run end to end on a workspace that is not a git repository). `WorkspaceGitTest` passes unchanged.

### Content kind, the files deliverable (extension, m4-21)

Story 120, first half, PLAN 6.2. Split from the attach delivery (m4-22) by finding H1 of the m3-29 review. A kind's deliverable can be files, checked by the workflow's own `command` checks. The decisions:

- **Behavior follows the keys, not the kind's name.** `kinds:` is a free map (m4-18) and custom kinds are allowed, so `deliverable.files` gives the files deliverable whatever the kind is called, and "a content kind" means a kind that declares it. A kind named `content` with no `deliverable.files` is verified as any kind, with nothing listed. Key names settled by soule on 2026-11-18: `deliverable.files` and `deliverable.report` for the deliverables, `delivery.attach: true` and `delivery.attach_auto.enabled: true` for the attach delivery (m4-22).
- **`kinds.<kind>.deliverable`.** A map whose only key so far is `files`, a list of at least one glob relative to the workspace, never absolute, never through `..`. `deliverable.report` is refused until the research kind lands (`kinds.posts.deliverable.report is not supported yet, use files`, m4-23), any other key is refused, and so is an empty list, so a misspelled key never turns the deliverable off. The globs are anchored at the workspace root with the matcher of `guards.protect` and of the `changed_line_coverage` reports (`ProtectedPaths` without folder cover): `*` and `?` stay in one folder, `**` crosses any number of them, so `out/**/*.md` matches `out/post.md` and `out/2026/post.md`, and a glob that names a folder matches nothing under it.
- **The listing.** `loop.core.kinds.DeliverableFiles` lists the regular files of the workspace that match, in path order, each with its size and sha256 digest. It never follows a symlink, so a link cannot pull a file from outside the workspace into the record, and never lists a `.git` entry, git's own state. Each glob is walked from its literal folder (`out` for `out/**/*.md`), so a large repository is not walked whole, and a folder that is not there lists nothing. The listing is taken with the workspace fingerprint, before the first check, like the m2-25 uncommitted listing, so a file a check writes is never taken for the deliverable.
- **Recorded with the verdict.** The `verdict` event carries `deliverable`, the number of files listed, and `deliverable_evidence`, an evidence file beside the check runs (`<attempt_id>.evidence-<n>.log`, numbered with them like the judgment calls) whose header holds `attempt`, `deliverable` and `fingerprint`, then one `- <path>, <size> bytes, sha256 <digest>` line per file. Neither is recorded for a kind with no `deliverable.files`. The human log adds `, 2 deliverable files listed` to the verdict line. m4-22 attaches the files from this record.
- **No deliverable file.** When every check passed and no file matches, the verdict is `refuted` and the findings start `Your last result was refuted: no deliverable file`, then name the globs, so the next turn knows where the files go; the human log reads `verdict refuted (no deliverable file)`. The rule follows the m2-25 uncommitted refusal: a failed check refutes with its own findings first, and the missing deliverable is counted among the failing checks of the progress note. It matters for the dash check: `! grep ... out/` passes when `out/` is missing. A workspace that cannot be listed gives `unverified` with the reason `cannot list the deliverable files: ...`.
- **The workspace.** A content kind works in a git worktree populated by `after_create`, like `code` (`workspace: git`, m4-20). On a `workspace: plain` kind the listing is the same and the verdict carries the `tree:` fingerprint. `delivery.pull_request` stays refused on a kind other than `code` (m4-18).
- **The checks are `command` checks the workflow declares,** banned words and dashes, length limits, markdown lint. No new check type: bl-14, bl-116 and bl-118 are deferred after M4. The worked example, which runs on BSD and GNU grep, exits 1 on a file with an em or en dash and prints its path and line, which reach the findings (checked on macOS with `/usr/bin/grep` on 2026-11-25):

  ```yaml
  verify:
    checks:
      - type: command
        run: |
          ! grep -rnE "$(printf '\342\200\224|\342\200\223')" out/
  ```

  `printf` writes the UTF-8 bytes of the two dashes, so the pattern needs neither `-P` nor a locale. PLAN 13's former `grep -rnP '\x{2014}|\x{2013}'` form was wrong: macOS grep has no `-P`, the usage error exits 2, and the `!` turned it into a pass on a file full of dashes. PLAN 13 now shows the example above.
- **Delivery policy.** A content kind keeps the default `delivery.policy: human` of every kind (`CONTEXT.md`, Delivery policy). Preflight refused `policy: auto` on a kind with `deliverable.files`, checked before the `auto_merge` opt in so the message names the real reason, until m4-22 defined what auto means for it: since m4-22 it needs `delivery.attach: true` and `delivery.attach_auto.enabled: true` (see the attach delivery below). The kind's template and context sources come from m4-19.
- **The orchestrator.** `Orchestrator.kt` calls `DeliverableFiles.listing` next to the uncommitted listing, refutes on a missing deliverable next to the uncommitted refusal, saves the listing as evidence like the judgment calls and adds the two verdict fields; the rules are in `loop.core.kinds`.
- **Out of this issue.** Attaching the files to the issue (m4-22), a review rubric (M6), new check types.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/kinds/DeliverableFilesTest.kt` (the glob matching, a pure function; the listing in path order with sizes and digests, nothing for a missing folder, no `.git` entry, folder or symlink, a file matched twice listed once; the listing of a kind by its key, a failed listing never a throw; the preflight rule), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/kinds/ContentKindVerifyTest.kt` (the files recorded with the verdict and the default approval wait, no deliverable file refuted with its finding, both for a kind named `content` and one named `posts`; a kind named `content` without the key lists nothing; a failed check keeps its own findings; the banned dash example with the real command check runner, an em dash naming `out/post.md:3:` in the findings, an en dash refuted, a clean file verified; preflight refusing `policy: auto` with and without the opt in; the loader reading the key and the example check, and refusing a bad deliverable; the verdict sentence), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ContentKindRunTest.kt` (a `content` issue on a temporary project with git worktrees and the command runner, its kind in the ledger, its checks run in the worktree, its file recorded with the verdict, waiting for approval).

### Content kind, the attach delivery (extension, m4-22)

Story 120, second half, PLAN 6.2 and 6.5. A kind whose deliverable is files can be delivered on the issue itself, so the operator reads the work where they approve it. The decisions:

- **`kinds.<kind>.delivery.attach: true`.** Key name settled by soule on 2026-11-18. `true` or `false`, anything else refused (`kinds.posts.delivery.attach must be true or false, got "yes"`). It needs `deliverable.files` (`kinds.posts.delivery.attach needs kinds.posts.deliverable.files, the files it posts`), so behavior follows the keys, not the kind's name (m4-21). It is not combined with `delivery.pull_request` (`kinds.posts.delivery.attach and kinds.posts.delivery.pull_request cannot both deliver a kind, use one of them`); a kind that delivers by pull request keeps that path unchanged.
- **The comment.** `loop.core.delivery.AttachComment.build`, a pure function, writes one comment: a first line, `Loop attached the deliverable of <identifier>, verified in attempt <attempt_id>: <n> files.`, or `verified: <n> files.` when a restored check has no attempt id, then each file in path order under its path and size (`` `out/post.md`, 812 bytes: ``), in a fenced block. A path is shown on one line, its newlines joined by spaces, so it adds no markdown to the comment. The fence is three backticks, or one more than the longest backtick run the file holds, so a file holding a code fence never closes the comment's block early. A path is a code span whose delimiter is longer than any backtick run it holds.
- **The cap.** `AttachComment.MAX_CHARS` is 60,000 characters, counted in UTF-16 units, below GitHub's limit of 65,536 characters per comment; a UTF-16 count is never below GitHub's. Files are inlined in order while every later file still fits as a listing line. A file that does not fit is listed after the blocks, under `Listed, not inlined, the workspace is kept until the issue is delivered:`, as `` - `out/big.md`, 70000 bytes, sha256 <digest>, over the 60000 character cap of a comment `` (or `no room left under the 60000 character cap of a comment` when only the files before it filled the comment). At most 100 files are listed one a line, the others counted on a last line, `listed in the deliverable evidence of the verdict`, or `not listed here, the workspace holds them` when that evidence could not be saved. Nothing is cut in silence, and the workspace stays until the issue is delivered, like every workspace.
- **Read again at delivery.** `AttachDelivery` reads each file listed at verification from the workspace again, never through a symlink, and inlines it only when its size and sha256 digest are still the listed ones and it is valid UTF-8. Otherwise it is listed with `changed since its verification`, `not UTF-8 text` or `cannot be read: <reason>`. A file whose size alone puts it over the cap is not read.
- **Through `addComment`.** The comment goes through the tracker's `addComment`, author `loop`, so it works on `files` and GitHub alike, redacted like every Loop comment, file contents included. The file texts and paths are redacted before the cap is applied, so a `[redacted:NAME]` marker longer than its secret still fits; a comment that redaction of the whole still puts over the cap lists every file instead. It is recorded as `issue_commented` with `reason` = `deliverable`, and the human log reads `commented the deliverable files of its verified result`.
- **`policy: human`, the default.** PLAN 6.5: kinds with weak checks default to human approval. The verified result posts the deliverable comment, then the evidence summary, so the approval sentence stays the last comment, and waits as `Waiting(approval)`. A failed deliverable comment is logged at warn and the wait still starts, the evidence summary adding `The deliverable could not be posted: <reason>.` A deliverable that cannot be built, no files listed by the verification, starts no wait and is reported like a failed move. The wait's target is `delivery.on_delivered.move_to`, or the `on_verified.move_to` handoff state when absent, so `loop approve` says `Loop moves it to done on its next tick.` with no change to `ApproveCommand.kt`, and the next tick moves the issue there. The comment is posted once, at the drain, never again at the delivery.
- **`policy: auto`, opt in.** `delivery.attach_auto.enabled: true` is the opt in, as `delivery.auto_merge.enabled` is for code; `attach_auto` must be a map and `enabled` `true` or `false`. Preflight refuses `policy: auto` on a kind with `deliverable.files` without `attach` (`kinds.posts.delivery.policy is auto, which a kind with deliverable.files supports only with kinds.posts.delivery.attach: true and the opt in kinds.posts.delivery.attach_auto.enabled: true, or use policy: human`) and without the opt in (`kinds.posts.delivery.policy is auto, which needs the opt in kinds.posts.delivery.attach_auto.enabled: true`), and refuses the opt in without `attach`. These are checked before the `auto_merge` opt in, which an attach kind does not need. Opted in, the drain reads the issue again (a human move cancels the delivery), posts the comment and moves the issue to its target at once, with no approval, but only when every deliverable file was inlined. A file only listed (over the cap, changed since its verification, for example by a check that rewrites it, not UTF-8 or unreadable) falls back to the approval wait of `human`, as an auto merge falls back to human, so the workspace stays: the comment is posted, then the evidence summary with `<n> deliverable file is only listed, not inlined, so Loop did not move it on its own.` and `The delivery policy is auto: ...`. A comment that cannot be built or posted moves nothing and is reported like a failed move. A config built past the loader with auto and no opt in waits for approval. A second result in the same drain (a tool move and a `done` result file) is delivered from the verification of the first, never from a fresh one, so it posts the same deliverable. The same deliverable (issue, attempt, fingerprint) is posted once per process: a move that failed after the post retries the move alone. A crash between the post and the move or the wait can post it again, which is not stored.
- **The orchestrator.** `Orchestrator.kt` keeps the files the last verification listed and the verification of a drain's first result, posts them before the approval wait's evidence summary, and under auto prepares the comment, then posts and moves or falls back to the wait; the comment and its rules are in `loop.core.delivery`.
- **Out of this issue.** The research report (m4-23), uploads as real attachments (GitHub has no issue attachment API), a review rubric.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/delivery/AttachDeliveryTest.kt` (the comment builder, a pure function: the cap below 65,536, files in order under their paths, a fence longer than any in the file, a file over the cap listed by path, size and digest, files inlined in order until the cap with the rest listed, a file that cannot be inlined listed with its reason, the listing ending with how many more there are, a path holding a backtick in a longer code span, a path holding a newline on one line, the count of files left over worded when the evidence was not saved, the count of listed files, no attempt id), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/delivery/AttachDeliveryOrchestratorTest.kt` (the comment before the evidence summary and the approval wait under human, an approved wait moving to `on_delivered.move_to` with no second comment, the handoff state kept without `on_delivered`, auto with the opt in posting and moving with no approval, a file a check changed listed by its verified digest, the comment redacted, preflight refusing auto without the opt in and accepting it with it, the loader rules; under auto a second result in one drain with a failing comment moving nothing, a file over the cap and a file a check changed falling back to the wait, a move that failed after the post posting once, auto without the opt in waiting; under human a failed comment named in the evidence summary; the cap applied to the redacted comment), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/AttachDeliveryRunTest.kt` (on the `files` tracker, one deliverable comment with a fenced post and a file over the cap listed, `Waiting(approval)`, `loop approve` naming `done` and the next run moving it there; on the GitHub tracker on recorded responses, the same comment posted through `addComment`).

### Research kind, the report deliverable (extension, m4-23)

Story 121, PLAN 6.2. Builds on the plain workspace and `populate: empty` of m4-20 and the attach delivery of m4-22. A kind's deliverable can be one report, posted on the issue once its required sections are present and its sources reachable. The decisions:

- **Behavior follows the keys, not the kind's name.** As in m4-21, "a research kind" means a kind that declares `deliverable.report`; a kind of any name declaring it behaves the same.
- **The empty workspace.** A research kind declares `workspace: plain` and `populate: empty` (m4-20), so Loop creates the folder and runs none of `after_create`, `before_run`, `after_run` and `before_remove` for it, and verification fingerprints it `tree:<hash>`. Nothing new is built for this: `KindWorkspaces.manager` already skips the hooks, and `LOOP_KIND` (m4-18) stays for a workflow that prefers hooks. `deliverable.report` does not require the two keys, since a report can also live in a git workspace.
- **`kinds.<kind>.deliverable.report`.** Key settled by soule on 2026-11-18. One path relative to the workspace, trimmed: never blank, absolute or through `..` (`kinds.research.deliverable.report is ../report.md, use a path relative to the workspace`), never a glob (`kinds.research.deliverable.report is out/*.md, name one file, without * or ?`) or a folder. A kind declares `files` or `report` (`kinds.research.deliverable holds files and report, use one of them`), and an unknown key is refused with `use files or report`. `KindDeliverable.globs` gives verification the report path alone, so the listing, its evidence file and the attach delivery of m4-21 and m4-22 work unchanged for it.
- **Read once at verification.** `loop.core.kinds.DeliverableFiles.report` lists the report like a deliverable file, with the fingerprint and before the checks, never through a symlink, then reads it once, so the size, digest and text come from the same bytes. A missing report is refuted with the finding `report.md is missing`; an empty or blank one with `report.md is empty`; one that is not UTF-8 text or over 8 MiB (`REPORT_MAX_BYTES`) with its own finding. A report that cannot be read leaves the result `unverified`, like a listing that fails (m4-21).
- **`kinds.<kind>.verify.require_sections`.** A non empty list of non blank section names, allowed only beside `deliverable.report` (`kinds.research.verify.require_sections needs kinds.research.deliverable.report, the report it reads`), so it is never read on a text that is not there. `loop.core.kinds.RequiredSections.missing`, a pure function, reads the report with the heading reader of the readiness check (`MarkdownSections`): a section is present when a column 0 heading outside fences names it, trimmed and case insensitive, and holds at least one non blank line before the next heading of the same or a higher level. The readiness check (m2-11) now calls the same function, so the two rules cannot drift. Each missing section is a finding of its own, `report.md misses the section Sources`, in config order.
- **The findings and the verdict.** Checks run first, as for files: a failed check refutes with its own findings. When every check passed and the report refutes, the findings open with `Your last result was refuted: the report is not ready` and list one line a finding, redacted, at most 10. The verdict event carries `deliverable` as for files and `report_findings`, the number of findings reading the report gave, absent when none or when the report is missing, which `deliverable: 0` already says; the human log reads `verdict refuted (1 report finding), ...`. The refutation counts as one failing check.
- **Sources reachable, a documented example.** No new check type (bl-14, bl-116 and bl-118 stay after M4). `docs/examples/research/check-sources.sh`, under `docs/` since `scripts/` is protected, is a POSIX `sh` script with `awk` and `curl` that reads the Sources section (or the section its second argument names) with the same heading rules, requests each http or https link once with `curl -L --max-time "$SOURCES_TIMEOUT"` (10 seconds by default), and exits 1 on a final status of 400 or more, a link nobody answers, a section with no link or no section, printing one line a link. A workflow copies it into its own repository and runs it as a `command` check by an absolute path, since the workspace is empty and a check gets no workflow folder in its env. Its limits are written in its header: a link ends at a blank, a quote or one of `()<>[]` and loses trailing punctuation, and a site that refuses curl's user agent reads as unreachable.
- **Delivery.** The attach delivery of m4-22, `delivery.attach: true`, posts the report as one comment on the issue, read again and checked against its digest at delivery. The default stays `policy: human`, so the operator reads the report before `loop approve` moves it to `delivery.on_delivered.move_to`, or `on_verified.move_to` when absent. `policy: auto` follows the m4-22 rules, with `deliverable.report` named in the refusal (`kinds.research.delivery.policy is auto, which a kind with deliverable.report supports only with ...`).
- **The orchestrator.** `Orchestrator.kt` changes at its call sites only: the deliverable refutes when `DeliverableListing.Listed.refuted`, the findings come from `VerifyFindings.deliverable`, and the verdict records `report_findings`.
- **Out of this issue.** A review of the report's content (M6), the `ops` kind, a sources check type.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/kinds/RequiredSectionsTest.kt` (the section check, a pure function: present sections, each missing one in config order, any heading level trimmed and case insensitive, an empty section missing, a deeper heading inside its section, fences and indented headings ignored, a heading that only opens with the name), `ResearchKindVerifyTest.kt` (a verified report listed as the one deliverable file with a `tree:` fingerprint and waiting for approval; a missing report, whatever the kind is called, and a report missing a section, each refuted with a finding naming what is missing; an empty report and one that is not UTF-8 text; the loader's reading and refusals; the verdict sentence), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ResearchKindRunTest.kt` (end to end on the files tracker with the command runner writing `report.md`: an empty workspace, no workspace hook run, a `tree:` fingerprint, the example sources check passing on a local server, the report posted as one comment, `Waiting(approval)`, and `loop approve` moving it to done), and `SourcesCheckExampleTest.kt` (the example script against a local test server: reachable links and a redirect pass, a 404 and a closed port fail and are named, a section with no link and no section fail).

### Resources config and the provider interface (extension, m4-24)

Story 122, the config and provider part, PLAN 6.9 and 12. The locks and the queue (m4-50), the tools that request and release (m4-25, m4-51), the limits and the read only check after release (m4-26) and the real providers (m4-27, m4-28) build on it. The decisions:

- **`resources:`.** A list in `WORKFLOW.md`, read by `loop.core.resources.ResourcesParser` into `WorkflowConfig.resources`, in the order written. Each entry has `kind` (`android-device`, `avd`, `ios-simulator`, `mac-app`, `display` or another string) and `id`, both required and trimmed, `can` and `cannot` (lists of free text shown to the agent), `restore` (a list of shell commands) and `read_only_check` (one shell command), all optional. A refusal names the entry: `resources must be a list`, `resources[1] must be a map`, `resources[1].kind is required`, `resources[1].id is required`, `resources[1].id "iphone-16" is already the id of resources[0]` (an id is unique in the workflow, whatever the kind), `resources[1].can must be a list of strings`, `resources[1].read_only_check must be a string`. An unknown key of an entry is a warning, `resources[0].restor is not a key of a resource, Loop ignores it`, as for a tracker entry.
- **The plain lock.** A kind no provider serves loads as a plain lock: Loop will grant and release it in queue order ([m4-50](#resource-locks-and-their-queue-extension-m4-50)) and `prepare` returns nothing. The preflight logs one line per such kind, `resources kind "avd" has no resource provider, Loop holds its resources as plain locks`, so a typo such as `ios-simulater` shows. The line is a config warning, logged at startup and on a reload that adds it.
- **The seam.** spec-core never depends on a resource plugin: the loader asks a `ResourceCheck` (`loop.core.resources`) whether a provider serves each kind. A loader built without one sees no provider, so every kind is a plain lock. Resource plugins that could not be found fail the load with the reason, like runner and tracker plugins, but only when `resources:` declares a resource.
- **`ResourceProvider`.** In `plugin-api`, the smallest set m4-27 and m4-28 need: `kinds`, a set, since one provider serves `android-device` and `avd`; `prepare(resource)`, which makes the resource usable and returns what the agent needs as a small map of non blank strings, such as `udid` or `serial`; `release(resource)`, which undoes what `prepare` started; `readOnlyCheck(resource)`, the provider's own check, run after each release beside the declared `read_only_check` command, one line per difference and empty when the resource came back as lent; and `recover(liveHolders)`, called once at startup with the holders of every live lock on the machine (`ResourceHolder`: project, kind, resource id, holder), so the provider removes what a crashed run left on a resource no live holder has, and ignores holders of kinds it does not serve. The startup call is m4-50's. Since bl-208, in m4-28, `prepare` also receives the attempt id of the holder and must be idempotent ([provider calls](#provider-calls-their-timeout-and-the-attempt-id-extension-bl-208-in-m4-28)). A provider receives the whole entry as a `ResourceDeclaration`, the type `WorkflowConfig.resources` holds, and fails with `ResourceException`. The lock, the queue and the limits stay Loop's.
- **The loader.** `ResourcePlugins` in `cli`, like `RunnerPlugins` and `TrackerPlugins`, finds providers with `ServiceLoader` on `loop.api.ResourceProvider`, on the classpath then in each JAR of `<LOOP_HOME>/plugins/`. Two providers serving one kind fail startup naming both (`two resource plugins serve the resource kind "avd": ... and ...`), and so does a provider serving no kind. None registers on the classpath before m4-27 and m4-28.
- **The contract.** `ResourceProviderContract` in the `plugin-api` test fixtures, next to `TrackerAdapterContract` and `AgentRunnerContract`, covers `prepare`, `release`, the read only check and `recover`. A provider's test supplies providers over one machine, a fake one for providers tested against fake `xcrun simctl` and `adb` scripts, so a second provider plays a restart after a crash, and `leftBehind`, read from that machine, never through the provider, tells whether `recover` removed what the crash left. `dirty` is optional: a fixture that cannot change a resource skips the case of a difference left by the holder.
- **The glossary.** `CONTEXT.md` is protected, so the term for Resource is proposed under `residual_human_steps` of the submitted result, for the maintainer to add after the merge.
- **The orchestrator.** `Orchestrator.kt` is not edited.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/workflow/WorkflowFileTest.kt` (the `m4-24` tests: every field parsed in order, a duplicate id and a missing kind refused, a kind with no provider loading as a plain lock with one line per kind, the other refusals and the unknown key warning, plugins that could not be found), `plugin-api/src/test/kotlin/loop/api/FakeResourceProviderContractTest.kt` (the contract against an in memory provider over a fake machine, and six wrong providers that each fail it), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ResourcePluginsTest.kt` (a fake provider registered through `cli/src/test/resources/META-INF/services/loop.api.ResourceProvider`, loaded and serving its two kinds; two providers of one kind and a provider of no kind refused; the host's loader naming the kind no provider serves).

### Resource locks and their queue (extension, m4-50)

Story 122, the locks part, PLAN 6.9 and 12, on the config and the provider of m4-24. The tools that request and release (m4-25, m4-51), the limits and the read only check after release (m4-26) and the real providers (m4-27, m4-28) build on it. The decisions:

- **The rows.** Two tables of the machine level store (schema version 8), shared by every project of the machine (ADR 0001). `resource_locks` holds one row per held resource: `kind`, `resource_id`, the holder's `project_id`, `issue_id`, `identifier` and `attempt_id`, `granted_at`, and `prepared`, the JSON map the provider's `prepare` returned, null until m4-25 writes it. `resource_queue` holds one row per waiting request: the same ids, `requested_at` and `place`, its rank in the queue.
- **The key.** A lock is keyed by `(kind, resource_id)`, never by project, so two projects of one machine asking for one kind and id share one lock. A queue row is keyed by `(kind, resource_id, attempt_id)`, so an attempt waits at most once per resource.
- **The queue order.** First requested, first granted. A request for a free resource with nobody waiting is granted at once, else it joins the end of the queue; a second request of the same attempt keeps its place. A freed lock goes to the head of its queue in the same transaction, so a grant never waits on the waiter acting, and `place` keeps the order when two `requested_at` are equal. The rules are a pure function, `loop.core.resources.ResourceQueue`, never the store's.
- **One API for two processes.** `loop.core.resources.ResourceLocks` holds the calls (`request`, `release`, `freeAttempt`, `freeProject`), for `loop run` and the short lived `loop tool mcp` (m4-25) alike. It sits on `ResourceLockStore` in `plugin-api`, whose one change reads every row of the machine, hands them to the queue rules and writes what they return, in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction, so of two processes changing the rows at once one waits for the other and a resource is never granted twice. Each grant records `resource_granted` and each freed lock `resource_released` (data `kind`, `resource_id`, `reason`), with the holder's project, issue and attempt.
- **No leak.** Every lock and queue row of an attempt is freed when the attempt ends, whatever its end (success, failure, stop or cancel), in the `finally` of the attempt in `Orchestrator.kt`, with the reason `attempt_ended`. A crash frees nothing, so the startup of the next `loop run`, once it holds the run lock and the abandoned attempts are closed (m1-36), frees every lock and queue row of its project with the reason `abandoned`: with the run lock held no live process of the project runs an attempt, so these rows cover the attempts the sweep finds and any attempt whose free at its end failed. The same startup call then calls each provider's `recover(liveHolders)` once, with the holders of every lock left on the machine, of every project. A failed free or `recover` logs a warning and the run goes on; a lock left by a failed free goes at the next startup. `release` records the reason `released`.
- **Visible.** `loop status` prints one line per resource of the workflow, in the order written, then one per other resource an issue of the project holds or waits for: `Resource avd api35  held_by=t-1 attempt=a1b2c3d4 since=2026-11-25T09:30:00Z queue=01234567/o-9,t-2`, or `free`. A holder or a request of another project shows with that project's short id before its identifier.
- **The orchestrator.** `Orchestrator.kt` changes at one call site, the free at the end of each attempt, plus its constructor parameter. The startup call is in `RunCommand`, next to the abandoned attempt sweep (`cli/.../AbandonedAttempts.kt`).

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/resources/ResourceQueueTest.kt` (the table test of request order, never one resource granted twice, a promoted request, rows a crash left), `ResourceLocksTest.kt` (the calls record their grants and releases, a refused event never undoes the change), `store/src/test/kotlin/loop/store/SqliteResourceLocksTest.kt` (rows survive a reopen, two handles see one lock, two projects share one lock and the key refuses a second, concurrent requests of several handles, the redactor, the migration from version 7), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ResourceLocksTest.kt` (an attempt that succeeds, fails or is stopped frees its rows and records `resource_released`; after a kill -9 the next run frees the dead attempt's rows and calls the fake provider's `recover` once with the live holders), `StatusCommandTest.kt` (the `m4-50` test: each resource with its holder and queue).

### Resource tools on the Claude Code runner (extension, m4-25)

Story 122, the tools part, PLAN 6.9 and 6.10, on the locks of [m4-50](#resource-locks-and-their-queue-extension-m4-50). The Codex half is [m4-51](#resource-tools-on-the-codex-runner-extension-m4-51); the limits, the read only check after release and the `resource:` key of checks are m4-26's; the real providers are m4-27's and m4-28's. The decisions:

- **Answered inside the turn.** `request_resource(name)` and `release_resource(name)` are the first tools of `loop tool mcp` answered inside the turn: they never use the request queue of `submit_result`, `move` and `comment`, which Loop applies when the turn ends. The tool process (`cli/.../ResourceTools.kt`) opens the machine's store, checks the attempt is live, and takes, queues or marks the lock itself through `ResourceLocks`, then answers at once. `name` is the `id` of an entry of `resources:`.
- **Two processes.** `loop tool mcp` reads the resources from the attempt's tool config snapshot, which since m4-25 carries `resources`, the `kind`, `id`, `can` and `cannot` of each entry, and since m4-26 its `restore` and `read_only_check` too, so the release can run the check ([m4-26](#resource-limits-the-read-only-check-after-release-and-resources-for-checks-extension-m4-26)), and never reads `WORKFLOW.md`. It never runs a provider. `loop run` runs every provider call: a coroutine next to the orchestrator ticks every 500 ms while the workflow declares resources (`ResourceGrants`, in `loop.core.resources`). On each tick it gives back each lock its holder released, through the provider's `release`, then frees it, which grants it to the head of its queue; then it runs the provider's `prepare` for each lock of its project not prepared yet and writes the result on the lock row. A kind no provider serves prepares to an empty map. So a booted simulator or a background emulator is a child of `loop run`, never of the short lived tool process. At the end of an attempt the provider's `release` runs before the free, so the next holder is never prepared first.
- **The rows.** Schema version 9 adds three columns to `resource_locks`: `prepare_error`, why the prepare failed; `answered`, whether the holder was answered granted; and `release_requested_at`, set by `release_resource`. `prepared` stays null until the prepare ran, and an empty map is a prepare that returned nothing. A store of version 8 gets the columns added on open. The answers are pure rules on the rows, `ResourceAnswers`, like `ResourceQueue`.
- **The answers.** Every answer is one JSON object with `status`, `resource`, `kind` and a `message`. `granted` carries `prepared`, what the provider's `prepare` returned (for example a `udid` or a `serial`), and the entry's `can` and `cannot`. `preparing` is granted, preparing: the lock is the attempt's and `loop run` has not prepared it yet. `queued` carries `position`, 1 being the next one granted. `release_resource` answers `released`. The agent calls `request_resource` again to check; a waiting request keeps its place, and a call that only checks records nothing. The tool descriptions say so.
- **Structured failures.** A tool error whose `status` is `error`, with an `error` code and a `message`, and the turn goes on: `unknown_resource` (a name not in the snapshot, with the names listed), `already_held` (a request after the attempt was answered granted), `not_held` (a release of a resource the attempt does not hold, or released already, with its queue position when it waits), `releasing` (a request while `loop run` gives the resource back), `prepare_failed` (the prepare failed, said once, and the lock is marked for release), and `store` (the store refused the change; nothing changed). A call from an attempt that is not live is refused like the other tools.
- **Events.** `resource_requested` on a new request, then `resource_granted` or `resource_queued` with its `position`; `resource_granted` again when a freed lock goes to a waiting request, and `resource_released` with its `reason` (`released` after `release_resource`, `attempt_ended`, `abandoned`). All four show in the `loop trace` timeline with their `kind` and `resource_id`.
- **Advertised only with resources.** `LoopMcpServer` takes an optional `ResourceHandler`; `loop tool mcp` gives one only when the snapshot holds resources, so a workflow without `resources:` lists `submit_result`, `move` and `comment` alone. Both tools take one required string, `name`, whose description lists the declared ids.
- **Dead holders (bl-204).** A lock or queue row whose project has no live run file is freed with the reason `abandoned`, on each tick of `loop run` and, in the same store change, before a `request_resource` answers. The check reads `RunFiles(loopHome, projectId).peek(retry = false)` like `slotLive`: it never peeks the caller's own project, since a `loop run` holds that run file's lock and reading the file would drop it, and an unreadable run file counts as live. So a killed run never keeps a resource from another project until it restarts. A free that failed at the end of an attempt is tried again on the next tick, without a second provider `release`. A lock freed as abandoned gets no `release` from this run: its prepare ran in the dead process, and the provider's `recover` at the next startup of that project cleans what it left. No schema change.
- **The orchestrator.** `Orchestrator.kt` changes at the free at the end of each attempt, which goes through `ResourceGrants.endAttempt` when `loop run` gives it one, plus that constructor parameter.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/resources/ResourceAnswersTest.kt` (preparing then granted once then a double request, queued with its position and a kept place, the release marks, a failed prepare, a stale prepare result, a dead holder freed before the answer), `ResourceGrantsTest.kt` (the tick prepares and gives back with the provider, the attempt end releases before the free, the abandoned rows of a project with no live run, the failed free tried again), `store/src/test/kotlin/loop/store/SqliteResourceLocksTest.kt` (the new columns and the migration from version 8), `mcp/src/test/kotlin/loop/mcp/LoopMcpServerTest.kt` (the `m4-25` tests: no resource tool without a handler, both tools with a required string `name`, a call reaching the handler), and `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ResourceToolsTest.kt` (two attempts of the command runner over `loop tool mcp` on stdio, preparing then granted with the map, queued at 1 then granted after the release, and the four events in `loop trace`; `prepare` and `release` in the `loop run` process, by the pid the fake provider records; the snapshot with no `WORKFLOW.md`; the three structured failures, each followed by a served call; no resource tool without `resources:`; a killed project's lock granted to another project's queued request).

### Resource tools on the Codex runner (extension, m4-51)

Story 122, the Codex half of the tools, PLAN 6.10, on [m4-25](#resource-tools-on-the-claude-code-runner-extension-m4-25). The decisions:

- **Client tools from the snapshot.** `LoopClientTools` reads the ids of the snapshot's `resources` (m4-25), and the thread start adds `request_resource` and `release_resource` after `submit_result`, `move` and `comment` only when there is at least one. A snapshot without the key, one written before m4-25, gives none. A `resources` that is not a list of maps with a string `id` makes the snapshot unreadable, like any other bad field: the thread declares no Loop tool and says so on stderr.
- **Nothing answered in the plugin.** A call goes to `loop tool mcp` through `LoopToolCall`, like the other client tools, and its reply goes back to the turn unchanged: `granted`, `preparing` and `queued` as a success, a structured failure as a failure result, and the turn goes on. So the `name` argument has no `enum`: an unknown name reaches `loop tool mcp`, which answers `unknown_resource` with the names listed. A resource tool called on a thread that did not declare it is refused by the runner's request policy, without running `loop tool mcp`, like any tool Loop does not declare.
- **Same texts.** The descriptions and the `name` argument, whose description lists the ids, are those of `LoopMcpServer`. The runner module cannot depend on the `mcp` module, so the texts are copied, and the cli test `CodexClientToolsRunTest` keeps them equal to what `loop tool mcp` lists for the same snapshot.

Tests: `plugins/runner-codex-app-server/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/codex/CodexClientToolsTest.kt` (the `m4-51` tests against the fake app server and a fake `loop tool mcp`: both tools with `resources:`, none without it or with an empty list, an unreadable `resources`, a granted, a queued and a released answer and a structured failure reaching the turn unchanged, a resource tool refused without `resources:`), and `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/CodexClientToolsRunTest.kt` (the `m4-51` test: the tools of a real snapshot equal to the ones the real `loop tool mcp` lists, descriptions included).

### Resource limits, the read only check after release and resources for checks (extension, m4-26)

Story 123, PLAN 6.9, on the locks of [m4-50](#resource-locks-and-their-queue-extension-m4-50) and the release path of [m4-25](#resource-tools-on-the-claude-code-runner-extension-m4-25). The real providers are m4-27's and m4-28's. The decisions:

- **The limits.** Constants of `loop.core.resources.ResourceLimits`: `ANDROID`, at most 2 Android devices and emulators together, the kinds `android-device` and `avd` counted as one pool named `android`, and `IOS_SIMULATORS`, at most 2 self managed iOS simulators, the pool `ios-simulator`. Both are machine wide: a pool counts every lock of its kinds in the shared store, of every project. A kind of no pool has no limit. Declaring more resources than a limit is valid: the extra ones wait their turn.
- **The queue rules.** A request now joins its queue first, then `ResourceQueue.settle` grants, in queue order, each request whose resource is free, not dirty, and whose pool is under its limit. With no limit and no dirty mark in play this gives the m4-50 order unchanged. `resource_queued` gains `reason`: `held` (another holder has it), `limit` (a grant would pass the pool's limit) or `dirty`. The `queued` answer of `request_resource` carries the same `reason` and says it in its message. A freed lock of a pool can grant a request for another resource of the same pool, so a third Android request waits with `reason=limit` and is granted as soon as one of the two held is released.
- **The order on `release_resource`.** 1. `loop tool mcp` runs the resource's `read_only_check` itself while the resource is still up, with what its prepare returned as `LOOP_RESOURCE_<KEY>` env, in the folder of `WORKFLOW.md` (`workflow_dir` of the snapshot) and with the tool process's env, the agent's own. A failure is the tool error `read_only_check_failed`, with `difference` (the exit code and the end of the output, at most 2,000 characters) and `restore`, the entries the agent must run; the lock stays with the attempt, and the agent calls `release_resource` again. A resource not prepared yet, or one with no `read_only_check`, skips the check. So the snapshot of the tool config now carries each entry's `restore` and `read_only_check` too. 2. On a pass the lock is marked released as before, and `loop run` runs the provider's `release`, then the provider's own `readOnlyCheck`. A difference there marks the resource dirty in the same store change that frees the lock, so the next holder is never granted in between; the agent is not asked, since the resource is gone. 3. At the end of an attempt, a lock it still holds gets its `read_only_check` from `loop run` first, while the resource is up: a failure runs the `restore` entries once, in order, then the check again, and a second failure marks the resource dirty. Then the provider's `release` and its own check run as for a release. On the `loop run` side these commands run in the same folder, with the agent env policy instead of the agent's own env. Each command may run 60 seconds (`ShellResourceCommands.TIMEOUT_MS`), since `loop run` runs it under the lock of its resource calls.
- **A lock freed as abandoned is not checked.** A lock the bl-204 sweep frees as `abandoned`, because its run died, skips the `read_only_check`, the `restore` and the dirty mark: the process that could run them is gone, and the provider's `recover` is the hook that brings such a resource back.
- **The dirty mark.** A row of a new machine level table, `resource_dirty` (schema version 10), keyed by kind and resource id, with the project it came back from, `check` (`read_only_check` or `provider`), the `difference`, redacted, and `marked_at`. Each mark records `resource_check_failed` with `kind`, `resource_id`, `check` and `difference`, shown in the `loop trace` timeline. A dirty resource is never lent: requests for it wait with `reason=dirty`. `loop status` shows it as `Resource avd api35  dirty check=provider since=... difference=<its first line, cut at 120 characters> queue=...`. While a request waits for it, `loop run` checks it again on a tick, at most once every 10 seconds after the last recheck ended. The recheck holds a real lock: its holder, `recheck-<uuid>`, is the only one the queue rules grant on a dirty resource, so a recheck is exclusive across the processes of the machine and counts in the pool of its kind; a resource held or at its limit waits for a later tick. Under that lock run the provider's `prepare` (a failed one is released, as for a lock), the declared `read_only_check`, the provider's `release` and its own check. A pass clears the mark in the change that frees the recheck lock, and the queue rules grant it on the same change. The events of a recheck lock carry no issue and no attempt. No new command; a mark stays when its project's rows are freed.
- **A check may hold a resource.** A `command` check of a kind, visible or holdout, may declare `resource: <kind>`. For the verification and for the merge check, which both run through the `CheckRunner` that `loop run` builds, the check asks for any resource of that kind that `resources:` declares: its holder, `check-<uuid>`, waits on every resource of the kind at once (the queue rows carry `any_of_kind`, schema version 10, one `resource_requested` and one `resource_queued` per resource id), in queue order and under the limits like any grant, and the first one granted drops the others. `loop run` prepares it, the check gets the map as `LOOP_RESOURCE_<KEY>` env (the key upper cased, every character other than a letter or a digit as `_`, so `serial` is `LOOP_RESOURCE_SERIAL`), and the resource is released after the check whatever its end, through the provider's `release` and its own check like any release. The wait comes before the check runs, so the check's `timeout_ms` starts when it runs: a wait in the queue is never a failed check and never takes a `max_attempts` slot. A lock that cannot be prepared is a check that could not run, with the reason. The holder is not the attempt, and the events of a check's lock carry no attempt id. A check of the verification runs inside its attempt, whose locks are freed only at its end, after the check, so a lock the attempt still holds in the pool of the check's kind (the agent forgot to release it, or its `read_only_check` failed and it kept it) would make the check wait forever. So first, with the attempt read from the check's `LOOP_ATTEMPT_ID`, `loop run` gives back the locks that attempt holds in that pool, or of that kind when it has no pool, and drops its requests there, as at the end of an attempt: the `read_only_check`, the `restore` and the dirty mark, then the provider's `release` and its own check. Each freed lock records `resource_released` with `reason=check`. The merge check has no attempt. The tool descriptions of `request_resource` and `release_resource` say it. While a check waits, `loop run` logs a `WARN` line every 5 minutes. When a check is canceled, its resource is still given back through the provider, but its declared check and `restore` are skipped, so a cancellation never waits on them. This is why the pilot app check can stop claiming its own simulator (m4-43).
- **Preflight.** A blank `resource` is refused, `kinds.<kind>.verify.checks[0].resource must not be blank`, and so is a kind no entry of `resources:` declares: `kinds.<kind>.verify.checks[0].resource is "avd", which no entry of resources declares` (`holdout` for a holdout check).
- **The limit answer.** The `queued` answer with `reason=limit` tells the agent to release a resource it holds if it does not need both at once, since two attempts each holding one resource of a pool of 2 and each asking for a second can wait on each other.
- **The upgrade.** A `loop run` of schema version 9 left open beside one of version 10 writes queue rows without `any_of_kind`, which drops the groups of the checks' requests. Restart every `loop run` of the machine after the upgrade.
- **The orchestrator.** `Orchestrator.kt` is not changed: the check holder wraps the `CheckRunner` in `RunCommand`, and the attempt end goes through `ResourceGrants.endAttempt`, the call site m4-25 added.
