Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/resources/ResourceLimitsTest.kt` (the table test: a third Android grant, `android-device` and `avd` together, and a third iOS simulator grant queued with `reason=limit` while two are held and granted when one releases, a dirty resource not granted until its mark clears, the requests of checks in request order, a recheck holder granted on a dirty resource and counted in its pool, the locks and requests of an attempt of one pool given back for its check; the limits as named constants; a dirty mark kept when its project is freed), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/resources/ResourceGrantsTest.kt` (a recheck under a real lock, a failed prepare released, a recheck left for later at the limit, a check taking back its attempt's lock), `store/src/test/kotlin/loop/store/SqliteResourceLocksTest.kt` (the dirty marks and `any_of_kind`, and the migration from version 9), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ResourceReleaseCheckTest.kt` (with the fake runner and the fake provider: a failing `read_only_check` keeps the lock and answers the difference and the `restore` entries, a passing one runs the provider's `release` then its check; a provider check failing after `release` marks the resource dirty without asking the agent; an attempt ending with a failing check runs `restore` once, then marks it dirty, records `resource_check_failed` and `loop status` shows it; a restore that fixes it leaves it clean; a dirty resource granted only after its check passes again), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/CheckResourceTest.kt` (two verifications each holding their own resource with its map as `LOOP_RESOURCE_<KEY>` env and a third waiting in the queue without a failed check, the merge check holding one the same way, a check whose attempt still holds the only resource of its kind taking it back and running, the preflight refusal).

### The Android resource provider (extension, m4-28)

Story 125, PLAN 6.9 and 12, on the provider interface of [m4-24](#resources-config-and-the-provider-interface-extension-m4-24), the release path of [m4-25](#resource-tools-on-the-claude-code-runner-extension-m4-25) and the limits of [m4-26](#resource-limits-the-read-only-check-after-release-and-resources-for-checks-extension-m4-26). The profile, with every default and message, is [`docs/plugins/resource-android.md`](plugins/resource-android.md). The decisions:

- **The id names the device.** `ResourceDeclaration` has no other field, so the `id` of an `android-device` entry is its serial and the `id` of an `avd` entry is the AVD name, as in the PLAN 13 example `{ kind: avd, id: wallpilates_api35 }`.
- **"No device of the entry connected".** For an `avd`, the device of the entry is an emulator that runs that AVD, read with `adb -s <serial> emu avd name` for each connected `emulator-<port>`. One already running is granted as it is, and left running on release, since Loop did not boot it. A real device connected does not stop the boot: it is not the entry's device.
- **A known serial.** The emulator is started with `-port <port>`, the first free even port from 5554 to 5584 that no connected emulator, no record and no other local process uses, so its serial is `emulator-<port>` before it starts and no other emulator is mistaken for it.
- **Records on disk.** `recover` runs in a new process, after a crash, so what the provider booted is written under `<LOOP_HOME>/resources/android/booted/`, one file per AVD. The provider has no host to ask, since `ServiceLoader` builds it with no argument, so it reads `LOOP_HOME`, else `HOME`, from the environment, as the host does. The choice of a port and the write of its record run under a file lock, since every process of the machine shares the folder.
- **Recover spares a live booter.** A record names the process of Loop that booted it, as `<pid>@<start time>`. `recover` stops an emulator only when no live lock of kind `avd` holds its AVD and that process is dead. A live `loop run` releases its own emulators, and a test that loads the provider inside its own JVM, whose `LOOP_HOME` may not be the store it recovers, never stops the emulator of a live run.
- **Release never throws for an emulator that stays.** `release` throws when a command fails, after killing the emulator's process for a failing `emu kill`. An emulator still connected after `emu kill` and after its process is killed is kept in its record, and the provider's read only check reports it, which marks the resource dirty (m4-26), so the next holder never gets it.
- **The attempt names the boot (bl-208).** The record of a boot carries the attempt id `prepare` received, and the emulator's log is named `emulator-<port>-<attempt>.log`, the attempt id made safe for a file name. `recover` still decides by the AVD and the booting process, not by the attempt: a later holder of the AVD may have taken over an emulator of a dead attempt without a record of its own, and an attempt rule would stop it under that live holder.
- **An idempotent prepare (bl-208).** A prepare of an `avd` run again for the same attempt, its first outcome lost, grants an emulator of the AVD already connected, as any prepare does, and when none is ready yet waits for the one its record names, if this process started it for that attempt and it still runs. Any other record is stale and its emulator is stopped before a new boot. An `adb` call interrupted by Loop's timeout kills its `adb` child, keeps the interrupt and fails. One `release` gives back what both calls made, and the contract checks it.
- **Boot output to a file.** The emulator's output goes to `<LOOP_HOME>/resources/android/logs/emulator-<port>-<attempt>.log`, and each `adb` call's to a temporary file, never a pipe, so an `adb` server that `adb devices` starts in the background never holds the call open. The last line is quoted in a failure.
- **The cli tests.** The provider is on the runtime classpath of `cli` since m4-01, so registering it makes `avd` and `android-device` served kinds in the cli tests too. `ResourcePluginsTest` names `display` as the kind no provider serves, and `ResourceLocksTest` takes its locks on the plain lock kind `phone`, so no test of `loop run` calls a real `adb`.
- **Not here.** Installing the app, the iOS provider (m4-27) and the limit of 2 (m4-26).

Tests: `plugins/resource-android/src/test/kotlin/loop/resource/android/AndroidResourceProviderTest.kt` (against fake `adb` and `emulator` scripts on the provider's `PATH`, `FakeAndroid`: a connected device granted by serial with no emulator started, a device absent or unauthorized refused, the AVD booted on the next free port, waited for and granted, and `release` killing it, on the recorded arguments; an emulator of the AVD already running granted and left running; `recover` stopping an emulator booted for an AVD with no live lock and leaving a real device, an AVD a live lock holds and the emulator of a live booter; a boot that never completes failing after the timeout with the last `sys.boot_completed` value; a failing `adb devices` and a failing `emu kill` naming the command and the exit code; a hanging `adb` call timing out; an emulator exiting before it booted; an emulator left up after release reported by the read only check; the `ServiceLoader` registration; the `bl-208` tests: the attempt on the record and in the log name, a prepare of the same attempt after an interrupted boot waiting for that emulator and booting no other), `AndroidResourceProviderContractTest.kt` (`ResourceProviderContract` for an `avd` and for an `android-device`), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ResourcePluginsTest.kt` (the `m4-28` test: the provider found on the classpath serving both kinds).

### Provider calls, their timeout and the attempt id (extension, bl-208 in m4-28)

Findings 2, 4, 5 and 12 of bl-208, the low findings of the m4-25 review, moved into m4-27 and m4-28 by the operator, made in m4-28 since it merged first. They change [m4-24](#resources-config-and-the-provider-interface-extension-m4-24)'s interface and [m4-25](#resource-tools-on-the-claude-code-runner-extension-m4-25)'s `ResourceGrants`. The decisions:

- **No provider call holds the others up (finding 2).** `ResourceGrants` had one mutex held across every provider call, so a hung `prepare` stalled every grant and every attempt end of the run, and a healthy boot of a minute serialized everything behind it. The global mutex is gone. Each lock, by holder, kind and resource id, has a mutex of its own, so the provider calls on one lock never overlap. A tick starts the prepares, the provider releases of released locks and the rechecks of dirty resources in the background and returns: it never waits on a provider, and it skips a lock whose call still runs. The end of an attempt and a check of the verification or the merge check take the mutexes of their own locks only, so they wait for a call in flight on them and never for another lock's. The end of an attempt holds them until its free is written, so no tick starts a prepare on a lock being freed. A failed recheck fails the next tick, as it failed its own tick before.
- **A core timeout (finding 2).** Every provider `prepare`, `release` and read only check runs on a daemon thread of its own and may run 10 minutes (`ResourceGrants.PROVIDER_TIMEOUT`), longer than the 5 minute boot of the Android provider. Past it the call fails with `the provider did not answer in 10m`, recorded like any prepare or release failure, and its thread is interrupted and left to end on its own: a provider deaf to interrupts keeps a thread, never the run. The interrupt reaches the thread only while the call runs, so a later call on the same thread is never interrupted.
- **Idempotent prepare (finding 4).** A prepare whose outcome was lost, canceled on shutdown or not written because the store refused the change, runs again on a later tick. The interface now requires a prepare run again for the same holder to reuse or clean what the first made, so one `release` gives back everything, and `ResourceProviderContract` checks it: two prepares for one attempt, one release, nothing left behind. On the core side a lock whose provider `prepare` ran in this process is released at its end even when its outcome was never written, and a prepare that ends after its lock was freed is released at once. A prepare lost with the process is left to `recover` at the next startup, as before.
- **A plugin `Error` is a call's failure (finding 5).** A provider call that throws anything but a cancellation, an `Error` such as a `LinkageError` from a provider jar too, fails that call: the tick and the free go on. In `Orchestrator.kt`, the free at the end of an attempt has its own try, so whatever it throws, its requests are still dropped and its directory deleted.
- **The attempt id (finding 12).** `ResourceProvider.prepare(resource, attemptId)` receives the holder of the lock as an `AttemptId`: the attempt id, or `check-<uuid>` and `recheck-<uuid>` for the checks of Loop. This is a source breaking change of `plugin-api`: a provider built against m4-24's `prepare(resource)` must add the parameter. A provider uses it where it names what it creates: the Android provider writes it on the record of a boot and in the log name, and m4-27 names its simulator with it. `release` is unchanged.
- **One change for both providers.** m4-27 rebases on this change and does not make it again.

Tests: `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/resources/ResourceGrantsTest.kt` (the `bl-208` tests: a hung prepare, deaf to interrupts, holds neither the ticks nor the end of another attempt and fails after the core timeout; a hung release at the end of an attempt fails after it and the lock is freed; a prepare that stops on the interrupt fails the same way; the end of an attempt waits for the prepare in flight on its lock, then releases it; a lock whose prepare outcome was not written is released at its end; a provider `Error` fails its call, never the tick or the free; the holder passed to `prepare`), `plugin-api/src/test/kotlin/loop/api/FakeResourceProviderContractTest.kt` (the new contract case, and a provider whose second prepare boots another copy failing it), and the Android tests of [m4-28](#the-android-resource-provider-extension-m4-28).

### Antigravity CLI (`agy`) ACP and print mode spike (2026-11-24)

Spike 0 of `.loop/specs/gemini-support.md`, run on 2026-11-24 against `agy 1.5.16` (`/Users/soulesidibe/.local/bin/agy`, Google AI Pro login in the OS keyring) with `gemini-4-neon-high` and `gemini-3.8-flash-low`.

- **Pinned CLI and protocol:** `agy 1.5.16` (major `1`), ACP `protocolVersion: 1`, `agentInfo` `{"name": "Antigravity ACP Server", "version": "1.5.16"}`. `authenticate` is not required when the operator's Google login is already cached in the OS keyring. Cross-process `session/load` succeeds from a fresh `agy acp` process.
- **Model pinning (stop condition 1, passed):** `session/new` returns `models.availableModels`, `models.currentModelId` and `configOptions` (`model`, `thought_level`, `mode`). `session/set_model` (`{"sessionId", "modelId"}`) switches to exact ids (`gemini-4-neon-high`, `gemini-3.8-flash-low`), updates `config_option_update`, and rejects unknown ids with JSON-RPC error `-32602` (`Unknown model ID`). Context window reported in `usage_update.size` is `1048576` for both models.
- **Occupancy and usage (stop condition 2, passed):** during a multi-tool turn, `agy acp` emits live `usage_update` notifications (`{"sessionUpdate": "usage_update", "used": <tokens>, "size": 1048576}`) after every model request (`16220` to `17630`), giving per-request occupancy for `RunnerEvent.ContextUsage` and mid-turn hard-limit rotation. `session/prompt` returns the turn total in `result.usage` (`inputTokens`, `cachedReadTokens`, `outputTokens`, `thoughtTokens`, `totalTokens`, where `inputTokens` is non-cached input, `inputTokens + cachedReadTokens + outputTokens == totalTokens`, and `thoughtTokens` is inside `outputTokens`). No USD cost is reported (`cost = false`).
- **Config isolation and login:** `--app-data-dir` and `--gemini-dir` require relative paths under `$HOME` (absolute paths fail with `must not be absolute`); nested relative paths such as `.loop/attempts/...` work. `--include-default-customizations=false` alone does not isolate `~/.gemini/config/` (68 commands still load). Passing `--gemini-dir <relative>` to an empty folder keeps the OS keyring login working with zero credential copies, drops `available_commands` from 68 to 13 built-ins, isolates `config/mcp_config.json`, `config/hooks.json`, `antigravity-cli/settings.json`, transcripts and logs, and writes nothing under `<workspace>/.gemini/`.
- **Permissions and `toolPermission: strict`:** `agy acp` ignores advertised client `fs` capabilities (`readTextFile`, `writeTextFile`). Under `default` mode, `read_file` and read-only `run_command` (`ls -la`) run without `session/request_permission`. Setting `"toolPermission": "strict"` and `"allowNonWorkspaceAccess": false` in `<gemini-dir>/antigravity-cli/settings.json` forces `agy acp` to send `session/request_permission` for every tool action, `read_file` included, with structured `toolCall.name`, `args`, `rawInput`, `kind` and `locations`. Loop therefore gates every tool in process in Kotlin with zero JVM spawn latency, rather than spawning a `loop tool guard` hook per tool call (a JVM invocation of `loop --version` measured 326 ms). Note: `permissions.deny` in `settings.json` did not block `read_file` when the `strict` prompt answered `allow`, so denials must be enforced in Loop's `session/request_permission` handler.
- **PreToolUse hooks (fallback reference):** a `PreToolUse` hook in `<gemini-dir>/config/hooks.json` (keyed by hook name, camelCase stdin JSON) fires on `read_file` and `run_command`, blocks on `{"decision": "deny"}`, and fails closed on nonzero exit, invalid JSON and timeout (`signal: killed`). Not needed for the primary ACP preset now that `toolPermission: strict` routes every tool to Loop in process.
- **Workspace customizations (stop condition 3, hit, Option 1 accepted):** workspace `.agents/hooks.json` fires in `agy acp`, and a `.agents/hooks.json` written after turn 1 fires on turn 2 of the same session. `agy` has no flag or setting to disable workspace hooks. Decided on 2026-11-24 (Option 1): proceed with `runner-acp` (`preset: agy`) using `--gemini-dir` isolation, `"toolPermission": "strict"` blocking writes/edits/commands targeting `.agents/`, `.agent/`, `_agents/` and `_agent/`, `--sandbox` (Seatbelt) for terminal commands, and checking protected roots against `<base>` before every session and before every `session/prompt` turn. Documented as a guardrail until M5 sandboxing or an `agy` flag closes mid-turn script writes.
- **Process tree, cancel, MCP and slash commands:** both `agy acp` (1 process) and `agy acp --process-per-session` (2 processes, front and `--worker`) keep 0 descendants outside Loop's process group. `session/cancel` plus answering a pending `session/request_permission` with `{"outcome": {"outcome": "cancelled"}}` ends `session/prompt` cleanly with `stopReason: "cancelled"`, preserves `result.usage` (`totalTokens: 15489`), and does not execute the pending tool. MCP tools passed in `session/new` `mcpServers` are named with single underscores (`mcp_loop_submit_result`, `mcp_loop_move`, `mcp_loop_comment`). Leading `/` in `session/prompt` expands skills (`/tdd` triggered reading `tdd/SKILL.md`), so the `agy` preset escapes a leading `/`.
- **Sandbox:** `agy acp --sandbox` uses macOS Seatbelt (`sandbox-exec`) to block shell reads (`Operation not permitted`) and writes outside the workspace, but does not restrict built-in `read_file`. `RunnerCapabilities.sandbox` therefore stays `false`.
- **Print mode (`runner-agy` deferred):** running `agy --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --disable-slash-commands` (without `-p`, which takes a prompt argument) accepts `{"event":"user","message":{"content":"..."}}` lines on stdin, runs multiple turns on one process, and emits `init`, per-step `step_update` (with `usage`) and cumulative `result`. Because top-level `agy` has no `--gemini-dir` flag, print mode loads `~/.gemini/config/` and cannot enforce `strict_mcp` or interactive `session/request_permission`. `runner-agy` is not built; `runner-acp` (`preset: agy`) is the sole Gemini runner.

### Claude Code rate limits (extension, bl-209)

Conformance 17.5 item 13 asks a runner to extract rate limit telemetry; the Codex runner did since [m3-21](#codex-runner-usage-rate-limits-and-cost-m3-21), the Claude Code runner dropped its rate limit lines. Claude Code writes `{"type":"rate_limit_event","rate_limit_info":{...}}` lines in its stream: a `status` (`allowed`, `allowed_warning`, `rejected`), the `rateLimitType` and `resetsAt` of the window that status is about, the overage fields, and `unifiedWindows`, each window by name with its `utilization` (0 to 1), `resetsAt` (Unix seconds) and `status`. The recorded lines of `real_two_turns_one_process.jsonl` and `wake_turns_10c2f759.jsonl` name only `seven_day`. The decisions (`ClaudeRateLimits` in `plugins/runner-claude-code`):

- **Same event:** each line gives the [`rate_limit`](#codex-runner-usage-rate-limits-and-cost-m3-21) event of the Codex runner, `RunnerEvent.RateLimit`, with `limit_id` `claude` and no `limit_name`, since Claude Code names none. It is emitted right after its line, with the ids of the turn that read it. A line read while the process is idle is kept for the next turn, like a `result` line.
- **Windows:** the event has two windows, so the unified windows are ordered by length, shortest first, and the first two are the primary and the secondary, as Codex gives its five hour and weekly windows. The length is read from the name, `<count>_hour` or `<count>_day` with the count in digits or a number word, so `five_hour` is 300 minutes and `seven_day` 10080. A window whose length cannot be read goes last. A third window is not reported. The used percent is `utilization` times 100, rounded; a window without a utilization is left out.
- **Sparse lines:** the recorded lines carry only the window their `rateLimitType` names, so, as for Codex, a window a line leaves out keeps what an earlier line of the session said of it.
- **Reached:** the line's top level `status` when it is not `allowed`, taken as each line gives it, so it clears. `allowed_warning` counts: it is the runtime's own word that the limit is close.
- **Changes only:** a line whose windows and status give the snapshot already reported emits nothing, so a repeated line is recorded once.
- **Not read:** the overage fields and the per window `status`. The event reports, it never stops: a turn Claude Code refuses past its limit already fails as `runner_unavailable` (see [runner unavailable hold](#runner-unavailable-hold-extension-bl-39)).

Tests: `plugins/runner-claude-code/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/claudecode/StreamJsonMapperTest.kt` (the `bl-209` tests: the recorded line, a bare or odd line), `ClaudeRateLimitsTest.kt` (the recorded line as a `RateLimit` event, a line that changes nothing, the window order and the sparse merge, windows without a utilization or a readable length), `ClaudeCodeRunnerTest.kt` (`bl-209 the real rate limit line is reported once, right after its line, as the claude limit`), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/TraceCommandTest.kt` (`bl-209 a Claude Code rate limit shows in the same format as the Codex one`).

## Spec changes log

Each change of the pinned spec commit adds one entry, newest first: the date, the old and new commits, what changed upstream, and what Loop changed in response.

| Date | From | To | Upstream changes | Loop changes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-11-06 | none | `8001b52e3062495a16e520e4ceaf8f9de868c4d0` | Initial pin of Draft v1 (upstream commit of 2026-08-12) | Vendored `SPEC.md`, `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` in `docs/symphony/`; this document created (m1-01) |

## Conformance test matrix

This section maps every item of the spec's test matrix (section 17) to the Loop tests that cover it. Filled in issue m1-18. Rows follow the spec's order; an item quoted with "item n" is the n-th bullet of its subsection. New tests written for an item carry its number in their name, such as `17_3 an empty id list returns empty without a provider call`.

Paths are from the repository root. A test name in parentheses after a path is a test of that file; a path alone means the whole file covers the item. The adapter neutral tracker tests live in `plugin-api/src/testFixtures/kotlin/loop/api/TrackerAdapterContract.kt` and run through `plugins/tracker-files/src/test/kotlin/loop/tracker/files/FilesTrackerContractTest.kt` and `plugins/tracker-github/src/test/kotlin/loop/tracker/github/GitHubTrackerContractTest.kt` (m2-22).

`conformance/src/test/kotlin/loop/conformance/ConformanceMatrixTest.kt` keeps this section true in `./gradlew build` (bl-154): every backticked path of a Kotlin file must be a file, and every backticked text in the parentheses after a path must be part of the name of a test of that file, so a row may shorten a long name or name a group of tests by their common start. A file name without a folder is a file of the folder of the last full path before it in the same row. The `conformance` test task does not declare this file and the tests of the other modules as inputs yet, so a `:conformance:test` that Gradle finds up to date or in its build cache skips the check after a change to them alone; `./gradlew :conformance:test --rerun` forces it.

Statuses:

- **Covered:** the tests listed prove the item and pass in `./gradlew build`.
- **Partial:** the tests cover part of the item; the row says what is missing and where it goes.
- **M3 (Codex):** the item tests the Codex app-server protocol or the real integration profile, which arrive in M3. Where Loop's M1 runners have the same behavior, the tests of that analogue are listed, but they do not prove the Codex item.
- **Not shipped:** an `If ... is implemented` bullet (Extension Conformance) for an optional feature Loop does not ship, so the spec does not require it.
- **Not applicable to `files`:** a Core item about remote trackers that the only M1 tracker cannot exhibit, as its [adapter profile](plugins/tracker-files.md) states (no pages, no rate limit, no remote response, no native id apart from `id`). The GitHub tracker (m2-22) brought these items into scope: no row has this status any more, and the rows say what each tracker covers.

| Item 16, tools 3: the current Issue and `native_ref` are available as tool context | `plugin-api/src/test/kotlin/loop/api/TrackerAdapterDefaultsTest.kt` (`m4-14 a tracker that writes by id only gets the moves and comments by issue through its id versions`; `m4-14 a tracker that writes by issue reads the current issue's native_ref`), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/trackers/WriteRoutingTest.kt` (`a move and a comment by issue reach the issue's own tracker with its native id and native_ref`; `a write for an issue whose tracker is not in the registry fails naming the tracker id and writes nothing`), `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/WriteRoutingOrchestratorTest.kt` (`the agent's move and comment reach the issue's own tracker, with the issue and its native_ref`; `the tool config snapshot of each attempt holds the issue's own tracker and its states`), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ClaudeCodeTrackerToolsTest.kt` (`m4-14 with a files and a GitHub tracker each agent's comment and move reach its issue's own tracker only`), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/CodexClientToolsRunTest.kt` (`m4-14 with a files and a GitHub tracker each Codex session's comment and move reach its issue's own tracker only`), `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ToolMoveCommandTest.kt`, `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ToolConfigSnapshotTest.kt` | m1-23, m4-14 | Covered: `LOOP_ISSUE_ID` and the tool config snapshot, which holds the kind, states and provider of the issue's own tracker. The agent's `move` and `comment` act host side in the drain of the attempt's issue and reach the adapter through `moveIssue` and `addComment` taking the current `Issue`, `native_ref` included (bl-153). Their default bodies call the id versions, so a tracker plugin that overrides only those keeps working. With several trackers each write goes to the tracker of the issue's namespaced id, never to the primary by default, and a write for a tracker the registry does not have fails naming it (m4-14) |
| Item 16, tools 4: tracker secrets are not inherited by the coding agent child | `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ClaudeCodeRunCommandTest.kt`, `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/AgentEnvRemovedNamesTest.kt`, `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ToolConfigSnapshotTest.kt`, `plugins/runner-command/src/test/kotlin/loop/runner/command/CommandRunnerTest.kt` | m1-23 | Covered |
| Item 16, tools 5: invalid arguments, missing auth and transport failures return structured failure payloads | `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ToolMoveCommandTest.kt`, `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ToolCommentCommandTest.kt`, `spec-core/src/test/kotlin/loop/core/orchestrator/ToolRequestDrainTest.kt` (`a move the tracker refuses is named in the next turn's guidance (m2-07)`; `a failed comment and an undecodable request are named, and only in the very next turn (m2-07)`; `the target of a refused move reaches the guidance redacted, on one line and cut to 64 characters (m2-07)`; `an unreadable request file names its reason redacted, on one line and capped (m2-07)`) | m1-23, m2-07 | Covered: `loop tool` refuses invalid arguments at once; a host side failure is recorded as `tool_request_failed` and named to the agent in the next turn's continuation guidance, redacted, on one line and capped (see [host side tracker writes](#host-side-tracker-writes-extension-m1-23)). A failure in the drain after the last turn of an Attempt reaches no agent, since no turn follows |
| Item 16, tools 6: unsupported tool names fail without stalling the session | `cli/src/test/kotlin/loop/cli/ToolMoveCommandTest.kt` (`17_5 an unsupported tool name fails at once with a usage error and queues nothing`) | m1-18 | Covered |
